> **This is the open-source copy of Chord.** The website, its tooling (`scripts/site/`) and the legal texts are kept in the maintainers' private repository, so skip any step below that mentions them. Pull requests are welcome here; see CONTRIBUTING.md.

# Project Instructions for Claude Code

You are working on **Chord** — a mobile-first group-chat demo with an AI bot.
One codebase runs on iOS, Android, and web via Expo + React Native Web; the
server is Express + TypeScript + Postgres. This file orients you; the real
reference material is in [`docs/`](./docs/README.md). Read it.

## First things to load

If you don't already have context, read these **in this order**:

1. [`docs/architecture.md`](./docs/architecture.md) — system shape + request flow
2. [`docs/features.md`](./docs/features.md) — what the product does (mapped to the demo flows in [`docs/references/chord_demo_flows.html`](./docs/references/chord_demo_flows.html))
3. [`docs/subsystems/bot.md`](./docs/subsystems/bot.md) — how the agent works (the most-changed subsystem)
4. [`docs/tasks/README.md`](./docs/tasks/README.md) — current roadmap + Phase 4 plan
5. [`docs/notes/`](./docs/notes/) — ADRs for every non-obvious decision
6. [`docs/references/`](./docs/references/) — **external input docs** (feature briefs, brainstorms, discussion notes from colleagues). **Read for spirit, not as contract** — see the folder's README for how to treat them.

The original design brainstorm lives at
[`docs/references/chord_demo_flows.html`](./docs/references/chord_demo_flows.html).
It's not a spec — it's the inspiration; `docs/features.md` maps each flow
to what's built.

## Where things live

```
chord-demo/
├── mobile/          Expo SDK 54 + expo-router (primary client, iOS/Android/Web)
├── server/          Express 4 + TypeScript (tsx-dev, tsc-build), workspaces
├── client/          Vite + React admin UI — PHASE 4 placeholder, not implemented
├── kernel/          Shared TS types + zod schemas + API constants  ← NOT a workspace
├── data/            uploaded files (gitignored). DB is Postgres now — ADR 0038
├── logs/            Winston output (gitignored)
├── docs/            Full documentation — start there
├── CLAUDE.md        You are reading this
├── README.md        Human-oriented entry point
└── example.env      Environment variable reference
```

## npm workspaces + kernel — important

**Workspaces** are `server`, `mobile`, `client`. Always run workspace commands from the repo root:

```bash
npm install --workspace=server <pkg>
npm run dev --workspace=mobile
npx tsc -p server/tsconfig.json --noEmit
```

**`kernel/` is deliberately NOT a workspace.** It's a plain TypeScript folder
imported via tsconfig `paths` in both `server` and `mobile`, plus a metro
alias in mobile. The `kernel/package.json` exists only to mark the folder as
ESM (`"type": "module"`). See [ADR 0001](./docs/notes/0001-kernel-not-a-workspace.md)
and [`docs/ops/kernel-path.md`](./docs/ops/kernel-path.md) for why and the
pitfalls (most important: **no `.js` extensions in kernel internal imports**).

## Path aliases

Import shared types as `@chord/kernel`:

```typescript
import { API, type Message, CreateTaskRequest } from '@chord/kernel';
```

Within each workspace, `@/*` resolves to that workspace's `src/*`:

```typescript
// server/src/routes/foo.ts
import { logger } from '@/config/logging.js';
import { createTask } from '@/services/tasks.service.js';

// mobile/src/api.ts
import { colors } from '@/theme';
import { useApp } from '@/store';
```

Server uses `.js` on relative imports (NodeNext-compatible via Bundler resolution works). Mobile and kernel do not use `.js` extensions in internal imports.

## Server layering

Strict unidirectional dependency: `types → config → utils → db → services → middleware → routes`.
Never import upward. Services receive plain typed data (no `Request`/`Response`).
Membership-gate group-scoped routes via `middleware/require-member.ts`.

Canonical API paths + zod request schemas live in `kernel/src/`. Don't
hardcode paths on either side; use the `API.*` helpers.

## SSE event names

Declared in `kernel/src/sse.ts` as the `SSE` object with payload types in
`SseEventMap`. When adding a new broadcast, add it there first so both
server and client typecheck against the same contract. See
[`docs/subsystems/chat.md`](./docs/subsystems/chat.md).

## LLM providers

Factory in `server/src/services/llm/factory.ts`. Four values for `LLM_PROVIDER`:

- **`openai`** (default) — OpenAI + also Ollama / vLLM (set `OPENAI_BASE_URL`)
- **`anthropic`** — via `@anthropic-ai/sdk`
- **`gemini`** — via `@google/generative-ai`
- **`mock`** — deterministic regex-driven provider for demos + tests without an API key

Run end-to-end without any API key: set `LLM_PROVIDER=mock` in `.env`.
The bot's full flow (streaming → tool-fence parsing → tool execution →
SSE broadcast → cleaned persisted message) exercises correctly with mock.

## Bot architecture (most-edited subsystem)

Read [`docs/subsystems/bot.md`](./docs/subsystems/bot.md) first. Summary:

- `bot.service.ts` decides whether to react and orchestrates one turn.
- `context.ts` builds the LLM prompt (system + attachment summaries + a
  recency-aware message window — ADR 0035).
- `tools.ts` holds the tool registry (zod schemas).
- `parser.ts` extracts ` ```chord-tool ``` ` fences from the post-stream text.
- `executor.ts` dispatches each validated call to a service and broadcasts SSE.
- Providers in `llm/{openai,anthropic,gemini,mock}.service.ts` implement `LlmService`.

OpenAI, Anthropic + Gemini use **native function-calling** with an iterative loop
(ADR 0005, shipped — `runNativeLoop` in `bot.service.ts`). The **text-based
tool fences** (ADR 0003) remain only as the fallback for providers without
native tools (mock, text-only Ollama/vLLM).

### Every UI capability needs a bot-tool equivalent

Chord is a chat app first — a feature that can only be reached by tapping a
button in the mobile UI is a feature half the product can't use. When you add
a mutating capability (a new route/service action, a new gesture), also add
the matching bot tool in `tools.ts` + `executor.ts` (zod schema, description,
`TEXT_FENCE_TOOL_PROMPT` catalogue line) so the same thing is sayable in chat.
Example: the Trips tab's hard-press-and-drag-to-combine gesture
(`mergeTrips`/`mobile/app/catchup/[tab].tsx`) shipped alongside a `merge_trips`
bot tool so "combine this leg and this leg into a trip" works too — reuse the
service function the route already calls; don't fork the logic. Read-only
additions (a new screen that only displays existing data) don't need this —
only new ways to *change* something do. If a capability genuinely can't have
a chat equivalent (e.g. it's inherently a drag gesture with no sensible
verbal phrasing), say so explicitly in the PR/commit rather than silently
skipping it.

### Every iPhone change is an Android change

iOS and Android are one codebase, so anything iOS-specific - a
`Platform.OS === 'ios'` branch, a `Platform.select`, an iOS-only package or
native module, `app.json` `ios` keys, an Apple API - gets its Android
equivalent in the **same change**, not a follow-up (owner rule, 2026-10-20).
Watch for the shapes that broke before (ADR 0263 and its addendum): an
Alert with more than three buttons (Android draws three), keyboard avoidance
with no Android `behavior` (Android draws edge-to-edge), anything pinned to
the bottom without the Android nav-bar inset (`useAndroidBottomInset`),
CalDAV-only Google-calendar checks, `canOpenURL` for another app (Android 11+
says no unless declared), and iOS-only previews like Quick Look. When there
is genuinely no Android equivalent (Reminders sync), say so in the commit/PR
and add it to ADR 0263's "still different" list.

| [0006](./docs/notes/0006-keyword-retrieval.md) | Keyword retrieval over filenames + summaries, no embeddings |
| [0007](./docs/notes/0007-user-identity-in-turn.md) | Triggering user identity in the system prompt |
| [0014](./docs/notes/0014-google-only-auth.md) | Google-only auth for real users |
| [0015](./docs/notes/0015-server-side-google-calendar.md) | Server-side Google Calendar write (supersedes part of 0012) |
| [0016](./docs/notes/0016-family-graph-in-sqlite.md) | Family graph model (storage now Postgres, see 0038) |
| [0030](./docs/notes/0030-group-calendar-scope.md) | Group-chat calendar reads scoped to Chord-target calendar, default-deny (superseded by 0040) |
| [0031](./docs/notes/0031-uuid-signup-soft-link.md) | UUID-referrer soft-link from TG welcome DM (pair-code fallback when bot not DM'd first) |
| [0032](./docs/notes/0032-strict-per-group-scope.md) | Strict per-group scope: pre-filter SQL + post-filter result scrubber; supersedes ADR 0020 behaviorally |
| [0034](./docs/notes/0034-configurable-nudge-time.md) | User-configurable daily-briefing time (per-user `nudge_*` columns; UI + `set_nudge_time` chat tool) |
| [0035](./docs/notes/0035-recency-aware-context-window.md) | Recency-aware context window: 3-day age cutoff + 20-message floor, time-gap separators, triggering-message anchor |
| [0036](./docs/notes/0036-bot-sends-files-not-links.md) | Bot delivers files via `send_file` (re-posts the attachment as a `file` message); never fabricates a URL |
| [0037](./docs/notes/0037-file-management-and-delete.md) | Catchup "Files" tab (`/me/files`) + path-aware attachment delete (unlinks disk file only when no shared copy references the path) |
| [0038](./docs/notes/0038-sqlite-to-postgres.md) | SQLite → Postgres on same VM; tsvector for FTS; `DATABASE_URL` config |
| [0040](./docs/notes/0040-user-owned-two-way-calendar.md) | **Calendar redesign (in progress):** user-owned event copies (not group-owned) linked by `ical_uid`, versioned by `sequence`; two-way sync (LWW by `updated_at`, tombstoned deletes); selectable target calendar; `group_ids` JSONB visibility; <5-min events are free. Supersedes/extends 0015, 0024, 0030, 0033. **Addendum 2026-10-23**: `ical_uid` is Chord's shared key — a push or pull of our own event never replaces it with Google's iCalUID (it only fills an empty one); copies of one group event targeting the same real calendar get ONE Google event (organizer's copy, else lowest id, inserts; the rest adopt or wait; a reflection never patches or deletes it — `google/fanout-copies.ts`); a pull skips another member's copy of an event the puller already has; repeating series follow the same one-master-per-calendar rule (keyed on `series_uid`). Not covered: shared Outlook calendars, already-diverged rows |
| [0041](./docs/notes/0041-native-ios-oauth-client.md) | Native iOS uses its own (public) Google OAuth client + reversed-client-id redirect; refresh tokens are bound per-client (`users.refresh_token_client_id`, `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID_IOS`) |
| [0042](./docs/notes/0042-proactive-calendar-reconnect-nudge.md) | Proactive once-per-disconnect nudge (deep-link action card) when a Google token dies; `pushDirtyEvents` un-claims on dead token so offline-created events still sync on reconnect |
| [0043](./docs/notes/0043-account-deletion.md) | Account deletion (`DELETE /api/v1/me`): ordered teardown of solo-owned data + best-effort Google revoke; shared groups kept + anonymized, ownership/files reassigned to a remaining member |
| [0044](./docs/notes/0044-byok-failure-and-future-billing.md) | BYOK key-failure handling: **no** silent fallback to the system key; classify auth/credit/transient (`llm/errors.ts`), tell the user to fix/top up their key; validate the key on save. Future metered billing (credits/paid plan on our key) documented, not built |
| [0047](./docs/notes/0047-shareable-join-links.md) | Shareable group-invite links: `/join/<CODE>` web route joins on click; pending code stashed in AsyncStorage survives Google OAuth (mirrors ADR 0031); no server change |
| [0048](./docs/notes/0048-native-deeplinks-for-invite-links.md) | Native deep-linking: `https://chordapp.ai/join/<CODE>` opens the app via verified iOS Universal Links / Android App Links; `.well-known/*` shipped from `mobile/public/`; falls back to web when unverified/not installed |
| [0050](./docs/notes/0050-online-calendar-import.md) | `import_calendar` bot tool imports an **online** calendar feed (pasted ICS/webcal URL or a curated district registry); SSRF-guarded outbound fetch reuses `importIcs`; honesty gating so the bot never claims an import without an attached file or a successful tool call |
| [0051](./docs/notes/0051-post-invocation-followup-window.md) | Post-invocation follow-up window: after an `@`-mention, the bot answers the **group's** next `BOT_FOLLOWUP_TURNS` messages (any member, group-scoped) without a re-mention — in-memory, TTL-bounded, hard-capped at 20; distinct from ADR 0018 `active` mode |
| [0053](./docs/notes/0053-whatsapp-twilio-connector.md) | WhatsApp connector + "taster" allowance for unsigned strangers. **Addendum 2026-08-21**: the connect prompt now rides the taster's own replies (turns 1 / 3 / last, `whatsapp/taster.ts` → `outbound-publisher`) instead of appearing only at the wall — asking while the bot is useful, not when it refuses; cadence DERIVED from the allowance (first / every third / last, so it adapts to any `WHATSAPP_TASTER_TURNS`; stays 10 — the turns were never the problem, spending them silently was); copy addresses someone who ALREADY has Chord ("connect this chat", never "sign up" — a shadow is usually a signed-up user on an untied number) and the wall drops its paywall framing; `tasterAllowanceSpent` → `tasterState` so the turn count survives, counting the reply going out |
| [0054](./docs/notes/0054-expo-push-notifications.md) | Expo push notifications: a `push-publisher` mirrors the IM outbound-publisher on `sseHub.onBroadcast` so nudges + bot replies + new messages all push to a group's members (minus sender); per-device `push_tokens` table, token-presence = enabled (no `push_enabled` column), dead tokens pruned on the `DeviceNotRegistered` ticket; gated by `PUSH_ENABLED`. **Prefs** (addendum): per-recipient gating in `selectRecipientTokens` — per-category toggles + quiet-hours (briefing exempt) on `users`, per-group mute on `group_members.muted` (surfaced as `Group.muted`) |
| [0059](./docs/notes/0059-unified-search-tool.md) | Unified read-only `search` bot tool across chats/files/tasks/reminders/saves/events/lists/places/contacts/media; scope auto-follows ADR 0004 (personal chat → cross-group, shared group → this-group); cross-group hits carry a `group` NAME not `groupId` so ADR 0032's scrubber doesn't drop them; no new index/tables (files reuse the 0021 tsvector, the rest reuse the list services + in-memory filter); results render as **tappable chat cards** — new `BotCard.href` (in-app expo-router path) makes each hit a `router.push` in the mobile bubble (messages→their chat, files→/catchup/files, tasks→/catchup/tasks, etc.) |
| [0064](./docs/notes/0064-phone-as-attribute-identity.md) | Phone-as-attribute identity: verified E.164 `users.phone_number` (partial-unique) + indexed `phone_hash` + `phone_verified`, Google stays the login/calendar anchor; SMS OTP via an `OtpProvider` abstraction (**mock** DB-backed default ‖ **twilio** Verify), result-union errors; WhatsApp `backfillWhatsappPhones` trusts prior verification; `/me/phone{,/start,/verify}`; **addendum 2026-08-21**: a verified number CLAIMS its `whatsapp:+E164` identity (auto-bind on the next inbound, no taster/referrer hop) and `DELETE /me/phone` + transfer-on-fresh-proof give a recycled/mistyped number a way out — **mock OTP provider REMOVED** — a mock LLM fakes an answer, a mock OTP fakes an IDENTITY (contact discovery + WhatsApp claim + number transfer), and `000000` ran in prod for weeks; Twilio Verify is now the only provider, `getOtpProvider()` returns `OtpProvider|null` and NEVER falls back (the old soft-fallback quietly verified everyone when the service SID was empty), `unavailable` → **503**, `PHONE_VERIFY_{PROVIDER,MOCK_CODE,TTL_MINUTES,MAX_ATTEMPTS}` deleted (feature is ON iff the three Twilio creds are set). Consequence: the `!== 'mock'` gates are gone, transfer-on-proof is unconditional and `taken` is race-backstop-only; **both directions now hold** — a linked WhatsApp identity also GRANTS `phone_verified` (`adoptPhoneFromWhatsapp`, called from `mergeOrLinkExternalIdentity`; the ADR 0064 backfill is the same rule swept over old rows), since WhatsApp registration needs an OTP to that same line — but it never takes a held number (identity rows aren't FRESH proof; transfer stays with real-time `/me/phone/verify`) and never marks a shadow verified (that's what makes a row contact-discoverable) |
| [0065](./docs/notes/0065-deterministic-contact-discovery.md) | Deterministic contact discovery: client hashes device numbers (shared `CONTACT_HASH_PEPPER`, raw numbers never uploaded), server matches vs `phone_hash`; `contact_links` persists **only** resolved hits; `resolveContactToUser` is strictly sender-scoped (never a global user search) — distinct from group-scoped `resolveAssignee`; privacy posture explicitly *not* enclave-grade; `/me/contacts{,/resolve}` |
| [0066](./docs/notes/0066-in-repo-llm-email-classifier.md) | In-repo LLM email classifier (`EMAIL_CLASSIFIER=llm`): batch new/changed threads via `getLlmForUser`, merge into prior tree, snapshot-replace through `ingestClassification`; fail-closed on LLM error; Mode-B ingest accepts session or `x-chord-classifier-token`; mock gate unchanged. Addendum: `Social & professional` + `Events & invites` tiles, deterministic sender routing (`deterministicPathFor` — linkedin/evite/… skip the LLM), Gmail `CATEGORY_*` tab passed as an LLM hint |
| [0068](./docs/notes/0068-attention-weighted-email-map.md) | Attention-weighted nested email map: one persistent two-level treemap (`tiles?deep=1`; supersedes ADR 0061's drill-down), tile **area** ∝ `attention` (unread + pending-magic threads, additive floor keeps cold tiles tappable); a tapped category's emails open in a bottom sheet over the map; ADR 0063 suggestions dock beside their email row / pin in the thread view (`EmailThreadSummary.magics` via the `gmail:<threadId>` provenance join) and resolve through the normal proposal routes; `email/index` registered with `animation: 'none'` like the other tabs; addendum: keyword-emoji fallback for tile icons (`category-emoji.ts`, classifier-assigned `icon` wins) |
| [0069](./docs/notes/0069-reply-composer-recipients-html-signature.md) | Reply composer: optional `to`/`cc`/`bcc` on `EmailReplyRequest` (client computes reply-all prefills; bcc rides the raw header, never persisted); **Chord-managed** plain-text signature (`users.email_signature`, Settings) appended after `"-- "` — explicitly NOT imported from Gmail (extra sensitive scope); replies send `multipart/alternative` (text + `textToHtml` part) |
| [0070](./docs/notes/0070-todos-hub-action-items-first.md) | "To Do's" hub (rename of Catchup — label only, **route stays `/catchup/*`** so ADR 0059 hrefs survive): sub-tabs Action Items · Tasks · Lists · Saves · Trips · Finance · Attachments; **Action Items lands first** (pending proposals with `gmail:` provenance, confirm/reject + audit); Finance is a deliberate scaffold; Files renamed Attachments (key `files` unchanged) |
| [0071](./docs/notes/0071-email-attachments-into-knowledge-base.md) | Email attachments feed the knowledge base: post-sync pass (`email/attachment-import.ts`) saves real Gmail attachment parts into the **personal group** via the normal `saveFile` pipeline (extraction/FTS/search/send_file all inherit); once-per-message `attachments_imported_at` marker, batch-capped full fetches, >15 MB + tiny-image + dup filters; `EMAIL_ATTACHMENTS_ENABLED` default ON; mock mailbox seeds `UA512-itinerary.txt`. **Addendum 2026-10-29**: an image copied out of email gets no "in this screenshot" suggestions (`saveFile` `originEmailThreadId`, written at insert, also on the boot retry) - Informed Delivery scans made ~70 junk suggestions for one user in a night; still extracted, indexed and trip-filed. **Addendum 2026-11-09**: a file copied from email is filed under the email path's rules (`emailCopyFiling` + `isBeforeDay`): nothing from Trash/Spam/archived mail or a dangerous sender (ADR 0294), nothing already past (ADR 0158 §4; a trip under way keeps the legs ahead), events on the mailbox's calendar (ADR 0312); Trash is not imported at all; a file a person uploads still files past dates |
| [0072](./docs/notes/0072-unified-calendar-settings-and-one-briefing.md) | Unified calendar settings + one briefing: ONE Settings list — Block switch (default judged from source signals per ADR 0033: primary/owner/writer block, reader-only informational; device calendars via `allowsModifications`) + Mirror switch (absorbs the AppleSync card incl. Reminders toggle); EventKit per-event `availability` now flows through device sync ("free" → transparent, like Google `transparency`); Google-backed device calendars fold into their Google row so nothing imports twice; the email digest is an **attention-first section appended to the morning briefing** (suggestions → reply-worthy unread → one count line; `email_digest` kind is legacy-drain only). **Addendum 2026-08-13:** the on-demand `summarize_email` tool is a *sibling*, not a reuse — its own `email/summary.service.ts` returning **structure** (sections by the ADR 0086 action axis, complete counts + capped examples) rather than the digest's rendered string; personal-1:1 only; the window resolves in the **user's zone** (`zonedDayStartUtc`, same drift technique as `nextScanDate`) and defaults to their yesterday so the model never computes a date. **Addendum 2026-11-02**: the phone's "Possible conflict" popup on bot-created events (`getDeviceBusyForSlot`) is removed - it read every phone calendar and ignored the Block switch, so a read-only school feed raised it; the server check (Block-set calendars Chord syncs, phone-only via the Mirror) is the one rule |
| [0073](./docs/notes/0073-email-delete-archive-convergence.md) | Email delete/archive convergence: archived (incl. trashed) mail hidden from **every** listing surface; a whole-thread trash or permanent delete **retracts the thread's pending magic proposals** (`retractByProvenance` → `expired`; decided proposals + created entities never touched; plain archive keeps the proposal); full re-backfill reverse-deletes in-window messages absent from a complete snapshot so stale-token deletes converge (mirrors ADR 0040's calendar fix) |
| [0074](./docs/notes/0074-timezone-anchoring-and-invite-reconciliation.md) | Timezone anchoring + invite reconciliation: a bare datetime ALWAYS gets a real user's offset (`utils/tz.ts`; proposal confirm uses the confirmer's `last_tz`, magics prompt carries user zone + preserved email `Date` header + sent-date, classifier restaurant/movie use uploader tz w/ `last_tz` fallback, ICS date-only → true all-day + DTEND); briefing/digest render in the recipient's zone; **calendar-invite emails skip the LLM** — the `.ics` is parsed deterministically and the proposal keyed on the invite UID so it reconciles with a Google-side accept in either order (owner-scoped `ical_uid` dedup + pull-side bridge), plus a narrow same-group/title/±60min fuzzy net; **addendum: "invite wins"** — a real calendar item arriving for a slot Chord filled from a plain-email agreement ADOPTS the keyless email-derived event (`event-convergence.service.ts`, wired into the Google pull, the invite-confirm path, and invite staging) and retracts pending email proposals for the slot, so agreed-then-invited meetings never twin; **addendum 2026-07-25**: probe errors DEFER the thread (no LLM fallthrough, no permanent mark), adopt/retract windows widen to ±12h on a title match, the magics sweep retracts proposals already covered by a real calendar event, `METHOD:CANCEL` retracts/cancels by UID instead of creating, and `.ics` parts are excluded from ADR 0071 attachment import; **addendum 2026-10-23**: a twin Chord already PUSHED is adopted too (`gcal_event_id` with our `chord_uid` is not an identity of its own) and Chord's Google copy is deleted through a tombstone row (`queueRemovalOfChordCopy`, unconditional, same transaction on the pull); an `alreadyInGoogle` confirm unbinds the adopted row (ADR 0103 shape, never pushed); a decline turns a pushed twin into a tombstone (`removeEmailTwin`); a remote carrying our `chordUid` never adopts. **Narrowed by ADR 0344**: nothing is taken over or removed any more - the match needs a title or a person in common, and Chord asks in chat before removing the email-made event |
| [0075](./docs/notes/0075-stripe-checkout-without-the-sdk.md) | Stripe checkout **without the SDK** (completes ADR 0063's purchase slice, F&F release blocker): raw REST session-create + hand-rolled HMAC `v1` signature verify over `req.rawBody` (Slack-verify shape, keyless tests via `signStripePayload`); `POST /me/billing/checkout` (503 until keys set) + `POST /webhooks/stripe` (**registered before the `/:platform` catch-all**; paid-guard; 200-on-unprocessable; `grantCredits` idempotent by session id); `checkoutAvailable` on BillingStatus; `WEB_ORIGIN` env (falls back to SIGNUP_URL origin); card-only price, refunds + IAP deferred |
| [0076](./docs/notes/0076-email-derived-people-and-family-facts.md) | Email-derived people + family facts (`EMAIL_PERSONS_ENABLED`, default OFF): afterSync pass over **all non-bulk mail** (bulk-domain + noreply skipped pre-LLM) → two existing consent sinks — `add_person`/`add_important_date` **proposals** (provenance `gmail:<threadId>`; ADR 0073 retraction + Action Items apply) and persona **memory proposals** (`spouse_name`/`kid_name` vocabulary); `people.email` sender anchor so repeat senders accrue only new facts; **bounded auto-write bootstrap** on a user's first pass (confidence ≥0.8, ≤12 people, owner decision) then propose-only per D1/D8; addendum: once-ever extraction marks live in `email_extraction_marks` keyed by `gmail_thread_id` (both `magic` + `person` kinds) so a mirror wipe/reconnect never re-mines or re-proposes |
| [0077](./docs/notes/0077-user-declared-email-routing.md) | User-declared email routing ("school email → family group"): `email_routing_rules` (sender_domain suffix ‖ category-title substring → target group, membership enforced at write AND match) consulted **before** the ADR 0063 LLM matcher; personal-only `set_email_route`/`list_email_routes` bot tools with **exact-name-or-ask** group resolution (zero/multiple matches → candidates returned; the bot asks until the name is exactly clear — never fuzzy); LLM-matcher fallback grounded with confirmed persona memories (`kid_school`); `EmailMagicSuggestion.targetGroupName` renders a "→ group" chip on the card + briefing line so routing is visible pre-confirm |
| [0078](./docs/notes/0078-settings-redesign-five-sections.md) | Settings redesign: 16 flat cards → five sections (Profile · AI Plan · Connected accounts · Briefings & notifications · Account) of collapsed `Disclosure` cards with status summaries (`openWhen` force-opens on reconnect deep links); ONE Google card owns Calendar + Gmail + the ADR 0072 Calendars list; Listening picker retired (ADR 0051 supersedes `active`); trust list renamed **Chordinators** under Profile; delete-account behind a danger disclosure + type-DELETE. Addendum: People screen shows learned facts (confirmed-memory chips + `people.email`) and a pending add_person/add_important_date strip (`/me/people/pending`); About-Me relation facts stage `add_person` proposals via shared `stagePersonProposal` — one consent shape for typed/chatted/emailed facts |
| [0079](./docs/notes/0079-thirty-day-trial-then-gate.md) | **30-day trial, then the AI surface gates** (supersedes ADR 0063's never-blocked posture post-trial): `getEntitlement` (BYOK → trial → credits; trial runs premium free/undebited, `TRIAL_DAYS` env); gates when `BILLING_ENABLED` — bot turns post a Settings→AI Plan nudge instead of an LLM turn, email afterSync AI passes skip (mirror/reader keeps working), digests/conflict-nudges retire un-rendered, but the **daily briefing keeps arriving as a deterministic no-LLM teaser** (few real lines + "Read more… → AI Plan", `buildGatedBriefingTeaser`) and self-reseeds so the full briefing resumes on payment; `task_remind` stays; `BillingStatus.entitlement`/`trialEndsAt` power the trial-countdown card |
| [0080](./docs/notes/0080-multi-account-email-providers.md) | **Multi-account email: Outlook/M365 + Yahoo (generic IMAP)**: additive `email_accounts` (sealed creds, per-account cursor/status; Gmail does NOT move — `account_id NULL` = the primary Gmail mailbox); provider-native ids stored ACCOUNT-PREFIXED in the existing `gmail_*_id` columns so classifier/magics/marks/provenance work unchanged; Outlook = bare-fetch Graph (Inbox delta, `@removed`→archived, `reply` action, rotating refresh token, `MS_CLIENT_ID/SECRET`), Yahoo/AOL/iCloud = one preset-driven IMAP provider (app password, References-threading, `\Seen` two-way, SMTP send via imapflow/mailparser/nodemailer); thread routes dispatch on `account_id`; `OUTLOOK_PROVIDER`/`IMAP_PROVIDER` mock seams mirror `GMAIL_PROVIDER`; attachment import stays Gmail-only in v1; **addendum: secondary Gmail mailboxes** (provider `'gmail'`, migration 034) — same Google client + `gmail.modify` w/ `prompt=consent select_account`, sealed `{refreshToken,clientId}` on the account row (users-row token untouched), account-scoped historyId cursor, full label API pass-through, primary-address re-attach rejected; **addendum 2026-08-21**: a flagged mailbox RETRIES with its stored credential before re-asking — Yahoo throttles with an auth-*shaped* error, so `isAuthFailure` now lets transient markers beat the auth shape, and `POST /accounts/:id/reconnect` (`retryAccount`) re-syncs with the sealed blob (the mobile slide-on calls it first, app-password form only on `needsReconnect`); `openAuthBlob` raises `AccountAuthError` on a decrypt failure so a rotated `MASTER_KEY` stops failing silently; root cause was `isInTrash` **inside** the vanished-message loop (every `ImapApi` method opens its own connection — the seam was modelled on the stateless Gmail/Graph adapters), so a mass-archive fired one login per message into exactly the metric Yahoo throttles — now `findInTrash(ids)` resolves the batch in one connection, capped at 200/tick with overflow left unapplied (a wrong "archived" is sticky). **IMAP is now a stateful session**: `ImapConnection` holds one lazily-opened login (re-established when `client.usable` drops), all methods written once in `imapApiOn(auth, conn)`, `withImapSession` wraps a whole sync tick (3+N logins → **1**) while `realImapApi` stays one-shot for single-operation routes; login counts are pinned by tests against a faked `imapflow`. No cross-tick pool — providers cap simultaneous connections too. Audit found one more per-item-call-in-a-loop: `probeImap` re-fetched each thread's message for its `.ics`, so migration `040` adds `email_messages.ics_text` captured at sync (NULL = never parsed ‖ `''` = no invite ‖ text = VCALENDAR — the `''` state is load-bearing, else every ordinary message goes to the wire); remaining sites (`setSeen`/`fetchAttachment`/`rescueJunk`/`verify` single-op, `send` is SMTP, `listJunk` one per account) stay one-shot by design |
| [0081](./docs/notes/0081-android-oauth-via-app-link-redirect.md) | **Android Google sign-in has no Android client**: Google dropped custom-scheme redirects for that type (it now needs Play services' Authorization API), so Android runs the same `expo-auth-session` browser flow as web against the **web** client and catches the redirect at `<EXPO_PUBLIC_WEB_BASE>/nativeauth` via a verified App Link — a path deliberately distinct from the web flow's `/oauthredirect` so a mobile-browser login isn't hijacked into the app; **zero server change** (https → web client already), `EXPO_PUBLIC_WEB_BASE` now required for Android builds (`GOOGLE_AUTH_ERROR` says so), and Android login is blocked until `assetlinks.json` carries the release SHA-256 (ADR 0048's tracked prerequisite, now a login blocker) |
| [0082](./docs/notes/0082-sse-streams-follow-app-foreground.md) | **SSE streams follow the app's foreground state** (iOS background-battery fix): `manageStream` (mobile/src/api.ts) parks every live stream on `AppState` → `background` and reopens on `active` — fresh token + the caller's `onOpen` backfill makes park/resume lossless against ADR 0002's no-replay hub; the group stream moved `useEffect` → `useFocusEffect` so a pushed-away-but-still-mounted chat holds no socket; `attachBackoff` mutates react-native-sse's live `interval` (3s → 60s ceiling) instead of replacing its reconnect driver — a clean server close dispatches **no** `error`, only a silent re-poll, so a hand-rolled driver would miss it; `'inactive'` deliberately does NOT tear down (app switcher / Control Centre), web is exempt. **Addendum — refresh**: `mobile/src/refresh.tsx` adds `useForegroundRefresh` (focused screen only, real background round-trip only) + `useScreenRefresh` (that plus a `refreshControl` for the pull gesture, 350ms floor) — wired into Chats, Email, Calendar, People, To Do's; Email pulls a real mailbox sync and Calendar a real gcal pull; the group chat is excluded (inverted list + `onOpen` backfill already covers it) |
| [0083](./docs/notes/0083-email-stage-1-shell-decisions-on-tiles.md) | **Email Stage 1 shell**: `GET /me/email/decisions`; tile tap opens decisions (not threads); badge/area ∝ open decisions (amends ADR 0068 weighting); urgent strip; Unsorted + coverage footer; Email-tab long-press folders; **addendum 2026-10-20**: the strip's ✕ now really dismisses (was a local Not now, so the card stayed in Feed/Action Items), and Action Items / strip / Feed cards get an archive-or-trash icon for the source email (swipe setting picks which); ~~thin Forward-to-Chord hatch~~ (**removed 2026-08-21** — `ingest_email` is minted at `@chord.app`, a domain we don't own; an inert address that forwards family mail to a stranger's MX is worse than no address. Column + DTO field kept for Workstream K) |
| [0084](./docs/notes/0084-email-and-todos-in-search.md) | **Email + pending to-dos join search** (both the ADR 0062 user path and the ADR 0059 bot tool): migration `038` adds a generated `search_tsv` + GIN on `email_threads` (subject/snippet/sender, threads only — bodies are lazy); **email is searched ONLY from the personal 1:1** (`crossGroup`), since `email_threads` has no `group_id` and answering a shared-group search with private mail is the ADR 0032 leak; to-dos are pending proposals matched **in memory** (their text is inside `args_json`, so a tsvector would index JSON keys; the set is bounded because proposals expire) and carry a group NAME not an id; email hits include their ADR 0086 `action` |
| [0085](./docs/notes/0085-one-search-matching-rule.md) | **One matching rule for both search surfaces**: a row matches when the query's FTS tokens hit its `search_tsv` **OR** the raw query appears literally — a union, not a fallback, because FTS gives stemming/multi-word recall while the literal arm recovers what `extractKeywords` can't tokenize (tokens <3 chars, stopwords, and `512` inside the single token `ua512` — flight/confirmation codes). Applied in place on both paths (`searchHits` gains an `OR … LIKE` arm in its existing SQL; `searchEverything` gains `ftsMatchIds()`, deliberately **unscoped** since callers intersect it with an already group-scoped set); shapes stay separate on purpose (flat ranked `SearchHit[]` vs per-category DTO). A null tsquery becomes a **sentinel that matches nothing** instead of an early return, and the literal arm is **floored** (disabled under 3 chars or all-stopwords) so `%a%`/`%the%` can't match everything. Parity test asserts both paths return identical id sets, floor included |
| [0086](./docs/notes/0086-email-action-axis.md) | **Email action axis** orthogonal to the category tree: `email_threads.action` (`to_do`\|`reply_needed`\|`informational`\|`other`, null = unclassified), judged in the SAME LLM call but explicitly independent of the path; `ThreadAssignment.action` stays **optional** so the ADR 0061 seam still validates, ingest defaults a missing value to `other` (never null) and `loadBaselinePaths` carries the prior action forward so snapshot-replace can't wipe it; bulk senders skip the LLM on both axes; `?action=a,b` filters the thread list (unknown values dropped, not 400); only `to_do`/`reply_needed` get a row badge |
| [0087](./docs/notes/0087-email-tile-archetype-facts.md) | **Email Stage 2 facts**: tiles carry Timeline / State board / Ledger payloads from a path-prefix map + fail-closed extract (dates already on proposals/events; status keywords; `$` amounts). Unconverted tiles keep magic previews. Tap is still decisions (ADR 0083) with Current state above the batch. Amends 0068 previews on converted tiles only |
| [0088](./docs/notes/0088-email-magics-world-context.md) | **Email Stage 3a context**: magics extractor gets a bounded calendar/tasks/trips snapshot (names+dates, no group ids) so it can emit `none` for twins and resolve dates the world already knows. Conflict-on-card is the next slice. Bounded in **bytes** as well as rows (names clip at 80, block ceilings at 2k) and deliberately **not** gated to date-bearing threads — the resolving date is on the calendar, not in the mail; magics' real cost exit is the Stage 4 yield signal, never a sender-domain skip (the bulk list is Evite/Eventbrite/Meetup) |
| [0089](./docs/notes/0089-whatsapp-session-window-briefing.md) | **The briefing survives WhatsApp's 24h window**: WhatsApp only delivers free-form text within 24h of the *user's* last message, so the 07:00 briefing is undeliverable (and silently so — Twilio still returns a SID) for anyone who didn't write yesterday. `group_external_channels` gains `last_inbound_at` / `briefing_opted_out` / `nudge_prompt_sent_at` / `pending_briefing_message_id`; only `is_proactive=1` sends are gated (a reply is in-window by definition). Window closed → send the **approved** `TWILIO_NUDGE_TEMPLATE_SID` quick-reply asking if they still want it and park the briefing; `n+` delivers the parked one (the tap reopens the window), `n-` mutes **this channel only** (`users.nudge_enabled` untouched, so in-app + push keep working). Unanswered → quiet for `WHATSAPP_NUDGE_REASK_DAYS` (7), then ask again — **addendum 2026-08-25**: §6's "silence means stop" was right about the cadence and wrong about the duration, since a reachable opted-in user who simply does not tap got nothing forever (prod: one channel skipped `already_asked` with a day-old briefing still parked); each send is a billed business-initiated conversation, so the bound is weekly not daily, an *answered* ask never re-asks, a re-ask replaces the stale park, and `<= 0` restores ask-once-forever. Opt-out is sticky and has no re-enable affordance yet. **Addendum 2026-11-14**: a `Full briefing` tap delivers TODAY's briefing - a park from an earlier day (user's zone) is swapped for today's posted one, else a fresh one runs (`runDailyScanForUserNow`) and is forwarded |

| [0090](./docs/notes/0090-whatsapp-cloud-api-direct.md) | **WhatsApp direct via Meta, not Twilio**: a number belongs to exactly ONE WABA, so Twilio's Embedded Signup could never claim `+15555550142` (already attached) — that conflict, not the since-cleared business verification, is what the generic "Oops" was hiding. New `WhatsAppCloudConnector` behind a `WHATSAPP_PROVIDER=twilio\|cloud` seam (Twilio stays: its sandbox is the local-dev path). Channel ids are **normalised to Twilio's `whatsapp:+E164` shape** so existing bindings/identities/session-window rows survive a provider switch. In-session quick-reply buttons need **no template** on Meta (only ADR 0089's out-of-window nudge does, addressed by NAME + language). Webhook differs on every axis: GET `hub.challenge` echoed as **text/plain** (`VerificationChallenge.text`; JSON would quote it and fail), `X-Hub-Signature-256` HMAC over `req.rawBody`, `statuses` receipts ignored, and batched notifications handled by a new optional `parseInboundBatch` so no message is dropped. Twilio Verify (`phone/otp.ts`, ADR 0064) is a separate product and unaffected |
| [0091](./docs/notes/0091-briefing-as-a-whatsapp-template.md) | **The out-of-window nudge carries the briefing**: ADR 0089's template asked "want your briefing?" and carried nothing; it now carries the day's facts + links, reversing 0089's rejected alternative (being "gutted into a stub" is fine if the stub is TRUE and has a way in — same one billed conversation). A Meta body variable cannot hold a newline/tab/4+ spaces and cannot be empty, so the headers live in the APPROVED body with one variable per line; five variables, and the three `chordapp.ai` links are LITERAL text (identical per user, and dynamic URLs review worse). New `services/briefing/facts.ts` is a deterministic **sibling** of the briefing, never a parse of it — the Conflicts/This week/Heads-up sections come from `SCAN_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, not code, so a regex over the prose would rot silently into an undelivered message; `buildGatedBriefingTeaser` (ADR 0079) now renders from the same facts so the two abbreviated surfaces can't drift. **Ask-once is untouched** (`gateProactiveSend` unchanged) — only the content of that one send. Twilio deliberately keeps its one-slot prompt (its Content API interleaves body/button numbering, so slots 2..N would collide). **Weather** ships with it behind `WEATHER_PROVIDER` (`open-meteo`\|`mock`\|''), located WITHOUT a GPS prompt: IP via a **local** GeoLite2 `.mmdb` (the address never leaves the server and is never stored — only the derived city) → IANA-zone city → Settings override, which always wins and survives travel; `app.set('trust proxy', 1)` — **not `true`**, or a client forges `X-Forwarded-For` and picks its own location. Settings gains the weather field (shows the RESOLVED place) + a per-channel briefing mute, the first way back from `n-`; `n+` delivery now passes `moreUrl` (a full briefing exceeds WhatsApp's 1600 cap and was clipped to a bare `…`) |
| [0092](./docs/notes/0092-closing-out-stale-todos.md) | **Closing out stale to-dos**: the pile-up was never email (a thread is mined ONCE ever — `markThreadExtracted` fires unconditionally per iteration and marks are never deleted) nor proposals (72h TTL, swept on every list read); it was that a confirmed suggestion becomes an ordinary task and a task had exactly two exits, `done` or hard delete. Implements spec v4 §6.2, split by whether the task carries a **due date** — the user's own statement that it has a shelf life. Dated: `archiveStaleTasks` retires it `TASK_OVERDUE_ARCHIVE_DAYS` (7) past due, deferred while `remind_at` is pending and gated on `archived_at IS NULL` so it fires **at most once** — that guard is what stops the open↔archived oscillation on reopen (the `completed_at` bug's twin) and removes any need for an `updated_at` column `tasks` doesn't have. Undated: **never** auto-archived however old, so "renew passport" survives. Briefing stops nagging — context orders `due_at ASC NULLS LAST` (the 30-row budget went to newest-created, starving dated work), rows carry `[OVERDUE]`/`[STALE]` tags like events/trips already do, and `[STALE]` collapses to one counted line; "already overdue" is now bounded to a week by construction. Dropping becomes first-class (`complete_task` `drop:true` → `archived`, ⋯ sheet "Drop") since forcing abandonment through `done` corrupts the completion signal; `superseded` stays unwritten. The row finally renders `dueAt` at all, and >48h-stale rows (`TASK_STALE_MS` in kernel) mute and sink to the bottom. Rejected: age-based archive regardless of due date, a batch cleanup proposal (answers a daily nag with a weekly one), `updated_at` (a task you reread and dread weekly is "untouched"). **Addendum 2026-08-22**: the *Tabs* sort too — `applySort` gains `due-asc` and Tasks defaults to it (chip "Due"), undated rows below every dated one in their own newest-first order (same NULLS LAST reasoning); the stale sink still runs AFTER the sort, so "closest first" orders live work while a week-old pile stays at the bottom. Lists/Saves were already creation-date desc; Trips already hid finished trips (`isTripArchived`, a trip ending *today* is live) but rendered them above the live ones behind a best-effort `measureLayout` auto-scroll that is allowed to decline — past trips now collapse behind a `▸ Earlier trips · N` disclosure. **Addendum 2026-08-23**: a ticked **list item** sinks too — it has no status and no exit, so on a list the bot keeps appending to the finished half is also the older half and piled up at the TOP of the card. Checked rows sort to the bottom, each half in the list's own order (oldest first); deliberately **no fold** (a task is a paragraph worth hiding, a list item is one line and seeing what got done is half of why people keep the list). Header reads `N left · M done`, and the Lists badge counts unchecked rows only — same rule that made the Tasks badge count live work only. On an ADR 0094 clustered card the merged row's checked state is the **AND** across copies, not the lead's (a row still open in another group is still to do, so it must neither strike through nor sink), and the toggle takes its target from that merged value — which is also what stops two opposite-state copies swapping on every tap |

| [0093](./docs/notes/0093-ordinal-references-to-enumerated-tasks.md) | **"Mark 3-4-5-6 done" resolves server-side**: tool results don't survive a turn (`buildConvo` replays prose only), so a position in a list the bot just showed has **no referent** on the next turn — the model re-derived the set from remembered titles and closed all six. `list_tasks` now returns an explicit `n` and records the ordered ids under (group, user) in `bot/enumeration.ts` (in-memory, TTL-bounded, ADR 0051's shape); `complete_task` gains `ordinals: number[]` resolved against that record, **all-or-nothing** (one unresolvable position refuses the whole call — a stale list is exactly when the wrong subset gets picked). Plurality is **structural**: `taskId`/`titleContains` stay single-item, so a SET is only expressible through `ordinals`, and at most **one** id/title-resolved task mutation is allowed per turn (`turnId` = `botMsgId`; a second returns `batch_requires_ordinals`) — `list_tasks` records an enumeration the moment it runs, so the ADR 0005 loop can read-then-act inside the same turn. Losing the map costs a re-list, never a wrong write |
| [0094](./docs/notes/0094-symmetric-list-name-resolution.md) | **One list-name rule shared by reads and writes** (`kernel/src/list-name.ts`): `listNameKey` is canonical identity (case, punctuation, the filler word "list" and trailing plurals all collapse), so "restaurant list" appends to `Restaurants` instead of forking it; `getListByName`'s exact `=` is **deleted**, `resolveOrCreateListByName` replaces `findOrCreateListByName`, and `get_list`/`rename_list`/`add_list_item` share the resolver. An exact key beats a longer prefix match ("books" → `Books`, not ambiguous vs `Books to read`); several loose matches return `candidates` so the bot **asks** rather than minting a third list. Ties resolve oldest-first. A **rename that collides merges** rather than stranding the other list (`renameList` → `mergeListInto`, oldest keeps the rows and takes the typed name; the result carries `merged` so the bot reports it and the To Do's tab refetches instead of patching a row that may no longer be the survivor). Migration `042` (`mergeDuplicateLists`, extracted like `hardenCalendarIdentity`) folds existing forks into the oldest, moving only items the keeper lacks and clearing their FK-less `catchup_assignments` rows. **Addendum 2026-08-22**: the stemmer folded the wrong way — `(es|s)$` took two characters off an `e` stem, so "Notes"→`not` never met "Note"→`note` and `ies→y` made "Movies"→`movy`; the rule is unwritable as "strip the plural suffix" (nothing says whether `es` is the suffix or an `e` stem plus `s`), so `foldWord` pushes BOTH forms to one lossy stem instead (drop `s`, then `e`, then `y`→`i`; note/notes→`not`, movie/movies→`movi`) — no length guard on the `s` step because "To-do"/"To-dos" must fold, and migration `043` re-runs the merge under the corrected key since 042 folded under the broken one. "watchlist" vs "watch list" stays unmerged on purpose (stripping a trailing `list` would also eat "Specialists"). **Addendum 2026-08-22 (UI)**: the Lists tab clusters same-named lists across groups into ONE card by `listNameKey` — a view, never a write (`mergeListInto` still never crosses a group; ADR 0004/0097). Items dedup on `normalizeText` and carry a chip per group holding them (suppressed on single-group cards); toggle/edit/delete **fan out** across copies like the Tasks tab, the toggle coercing one target state from the lead so opposite-state copies can't oscillate, and a cross-group delete confirms by naming the groups. Rename + delete-list move out of the header into a per-list footer when the card spans >1 list, and adding an item to a spanning card **picks its target** (defaults to the naming list) rather than inferring a group. Card is named by its oldest member, sorted/dated by its newest; the tab badge counts merged rows |
| [0095](./docs/notes/0095-tasks-fold-instead-of-multiplying.md) | **A restated to-do folds instead of multiplying**: `create_task` had NO dedup (unlike `saves`/`list_items`), so every restatement minted a row and the briefing read them all back, prompting more — amplified by ADR 0051's follow-up window. `createTaskWithDedup` matches same group + same assignee + `normalizeText` title + status `open`/`active` with `archived_at IS NULL` (**done/archived are NOT duplicates** — that would lose the new intention and corrupt ADR 0092's completion signal). A hit is **folded, not dropped**: with no `update_task` tool a reschedule arrives as another `create_task`, so supplied fields fill gaps and a different due/remind time replaces the old one, while omitted fields are never cleared; `created`/`updated` ride the result and SSE fires only on a real change. The REST route still creates unconditionally — a person tapping twice is looking at their list, the LLM is not |
| [0096](./docs/notes/0096-personal-chat-reads-across-groups.md) | **Precise read tools follow ADR 0059's scope rule**: `get_list`/`list_tasks`/`search_saves` were pinned to the turn's group while `search` and `find_events` already spanned the user's groups — and the `search` description tells the model to PREFER the precise tools, so "what's on my restaurants list" from the personal 1:1 went blind to a list in a shared group. One `isPersonalTurn` helper (`search` now uses it too); cross-group arm reads via the `*ForUser` helpers (membership-bounded, ADR 0032 unchanged) and rows carry a group **NAME**, never an id. **`complete_task` widened with `list_tasks`** — ADR 0093 ordinals resolve to ids that a group-scoped lookup would silently skip — and `taskUpdated` now broadcasts to the task's own group. Reads widened; writes still target the turn's group. Also: **`get_list` falls back to `saves`** when no list matches (a restaurant filed by `save_place` is not on any list, and the user cannot know which container the bot picked) — token match over title/note/tags, capped at 20, `list: null` retained so the bot never claims a list exists |
| [0097](./docs/notes/0097-email-routing-is-a-choice.md) | **The model no longer picks which group your email lands in**: ADR 0063's `llmGroupMatcher`/`parseGroupMatch`/`GroupMatcher` seam **deleted** (dormant = re-enablable). Staging sets `target_group_id` ONLY from a user-declared ADR 0077 rule; everything else stays personal and a plain confirm creates it there. `confirmProposal` takes an explicit `targetGroupId` (membership-enforced — the check now guards a client value, not one we generated) and the Action Items row renders **Just me** (default) + a chip per shared group. Standalone path only — an ADR 0029 bundle resolves many entities in one tap. The old posture WAS confirm-gated; what it lacked is that one yes bundled "this is real" with "share this with my family", and the `→ group` chip was display-only |
| [0098](./docs/notes/0098-briefings-need-a-real-recipient-and-a-real-zone.md) | **A briefing needs a real recipient and a real zone**: a signed-up user's WhatsApp briefing arrived at midnight Pacific because he had TWO rows — his real account (`last_tz` correct, firing 07:00 Pacific into the app) and an ADR 0053 taster shadow minted when he first messaged the bot, which owns the personal group the WhatsApp channel is actually bound to. Three failures compose: `seedDailyScan` qualified on "owns a personal group" with **no `shadow` filter** (the taster path hands every stranger one via `ensurePersonalGroup`); a shadow's `last_tz` is **structurally** NULL (it never opens a client, so `recordUserTimezone` can never fire); and `nextScanDate`'s null-tz fallback was **server-local**, so on a UTC VM a 07:00 briefing was queued for 07:00Z = midnight Los Angeles. Fix: the shadow gate lives in `enqueueDailyScanForUser` — the choke point `seedDailyScan` AND `rescheduleDailyScanForUser` share, so a nudge-settings change can't put the row back — while `enqueueDailyScanForUserNow` stays ungated (that's the user asking); `seedDailyScan` also **purges pending shadow scans** so the deploy reconciles existing rows instead of needing a hand-run DELETE; and `BRIEFING_DEFAULT_TZ` (default `America/New_York`) replaces the server clock, an invalid `last_tz` now landing on it rather than falling through. **No new auto-merge** — `linkWhatsappIdentityForPhone` already folds a taster shadow into the real account on OTP proof and already collapses the duplicate briefing; this user has two rows only because he signed up in May and tasted in August (no signup intent left, no verified number), and matching him by display name would be the evidence-free identity claim ADR 0064's addendum refused. Rejected: deferring until a zone is known (a real non-shadow user in prod has never reported one — that silences rather than mistimes him), and deriving the zone from the bound number's country code (`+1` spans six zones, so it cannot tell New York from Los Angeles — the exact distinction the bug is about) |
| [0099](./docs/notes/0099-asking-for-the-briefing-runs-the-briefing.md) | **Asking for the briefing runs the briefing, and a rejected template says so**: a user's 07:00 WhatsApp briefing was generated and posted, then Meta refused the out-of-window template with **132001** (`daily_briefing` is approved as `en`; the deploy said `META_NUDGE_TEMPLATE_LANG=en_US`) — and the throw landed in the publisher's generic per-binding `catch` as a **warn**, indistinguishable from a transport hiccup, so it ran two days across two numbers before anyone noticed. She then asked three times in words and got three improvised summaries, because the registry had `set_nudge_time` (which only SCHEDULES) and nothing that RUNS a briefing — `runDailyScanForUserNow` was wired only to a demo route. New **`send_briefing`** tool calls that same function, so the on-demand briefing cannot drift from the scheduled one; it **posts** the briefing and returns only a confirmation (ADR 0036's posture — the bot delivers the artifact, never a paraphrase of it), since a made-up briefing is indistinguishable from a real one to the reader. Personal 1:1 only via `PERSONAL_ONLY_TOOL_NAMES` **plus** an executor `isPersonalTurn` check (a briefing spans every group the asker belongs to), and **once per turn** (`claimOncePerTurn`, ADR 0093's mechanism — the ADR 0005 loop re-streams up to 20 times and each briefing costs an LLM scan plus a mailbox sync). `sendBriefingTemplate` now catches its own failure, logs at **error** naming template/language/provider/channel with a remediation hint, and **returns without `markPrompted`** — `nudge_prompt_sent_at` means "asked and unanswered", which ADR 0089 §6 reads as permission to stop asking forever, so recording it would turn a config typo into a permanent silent opt-out. It parks nothing either (no prompt arrived, so no button exists to tap). Rejected: validating the template at startup (the deploy's Meta token is phone-number-scoped — `message_templates` and `whatsapp_business_account` both return `#100`, so we cannot enumerate templates with the credential we have), and delivering the parked briefing on the next inbound (when the template WORKS, a user who replies about something else has implicitly not asked — and the unspent ask already covers the config-error case at the next retry) |
| [0100](./docs/notes/0100-a-past-event-is-a-typo-until-confirmed.md) | **A past calendar event is a typo until confirmed**: a calendar entry is something the family still has to show up for, and nothing in the product needs the bot to file history — so a `create_event` (or an `update_event` reschedule) whose `startsAt` already went by is nearly always a stale year, a weekday that already passed ("add dinner Friday" / "move it to Friday" said on a Saturday), or a date read off a document describing something already over. Both now **refuse and ask** (`created: false`, `reason: 'past'`) instead of writing, the shape ADR 0094 uses for an ambiguous list name and 0093 for an unresolvable ordinal; the user re-confirms and the bot calls again with `confirmPast: true`. Deliberately **not** a silent roll-forward — guessing which way the typo went turns a visible refusal into an invisible wrong answer. The boundary is the **start of the user's local day**, not `now`: all-day events arrive as floating midnight (ADR 0040) and a strict `< now` test would reject every one of them, while "lunch at noon" typed at 2pm is someone recording a live day. Gated on `turnId` (ADR 0093) plus `externalUid` (ADR 0013), so ICS import, the Google pull and proposal confirm keep filing history — which is also why the guard lives in the executor and not in `createEvent`, whose callers would otherwise each need an `allowPast` opt-out. Prompt-only was rejected on the timezone precedent (a STRICT description plus `normalizeDatetimes`, because the instruction alone was not enough). On `update_event` only a **supplied `startsAt`** trips it — the guard is about the date being moved TO, so renaming or relocating an event that already happened moves nothing and stays silent. Consequence: every hardcoded event date in the fixtures had to go relative, since a literal rots into a silently non-writing test |
| [0101](./docs/notes/0101-a-two-person-chat-is-a-person.md) | **A two-person chat is a person, until someone names it**: a shared chat with exactly TWO members whose name nobody chose displays the other member's full name (chat list, header, catchup chips — they share `groupTitle`). The signal is new `groups.name_is_custom` (migration `045`), set by `renameGroup` and by the create-group **route** (a typed name is intent) but NOT by service-internal creations — `ensurePairGroup` mints `'Direct message'`, WhatsApp/connector imports take the platform title, and none of those is the user's name for the chat. The server resolves the rule (`counterpartNames()` returns a name ONLY for groups that should show one) so the client is `counterpartName || name`; membership lives server-side, and the chat-list endpoint carries no members. `fullName()` joins `greetingName()` in kernel — the latter is "what do I call you", this is "who is this". **Backfilled to 0 for every existing row** (owner decision): a name sticks only from the next rename on. A group **shrinking** 3→2 claims its name first (`keepNameOnShrink` in `leaveGroup`, pair groups excluded) — the backfill leaves a long-standing name like "UWS Mazaa" protected only by its member count, and losing a name silently is worse than keeping a dull one. Rejected: sentinel-matching the known default names (guesses, and every new auto-naming path must remember to extend the list), keying off `pair_key` (narrower than the question — a group that drops to two members reads the same way), and resolving client-side (an extra round trip on the app's hottest read) |
| [0102](./docs/notes/0102-a-failed-tool-does-not-get-to-explain-itself.md) | **A failed tool does not get to explain itself**: prod asked "add meeting Sharon at 330p for Wednesday" and was told "You already have a meeting with Sharon scheduled at 3:30 PM on Wednesday" — there was no meeting, and none was written. Two defects compose. (1) The model filled the optional `externalUid` with `""`; empty string is falsy so the ADR 0013 dedup branch skipped it, but `?? null` stored it as a REAL value, and `events_external_uid_per_group` is partial (`WHERE external_uid IS NOT NULL`) — NULL is exempt, `''` is not, so ONE `''` row wedges every later event in the group. The field's own comment claimed it was "not advertised to the bot", but `buildToolDefs` ships every zod field to the provider — an intent nothing enforced. (2) `executeAndBroadcast` returned `(err as Error).message` verbatim, so the model read `duplicate key value violates unique constraint` and relayed it as a fact about her calendar, inventing the confirming detail by echoing the request back. Fix: `INTERNAL_TOOL_FIELDS` strips such fields in `buildToolDefs` (**not** from the zod schema — .ics import, the Google pull and the email-invite path synthesize these calls and still need validation); `emptyToUndefined` + `|| null` normalize `''` to absent at BOTH ends (the schema covers the LLM, the write covers ingest, which can produce `''` from an .ics with no UID line); and `toolErrorForModel` returns ONE fixed sentence for every throw except `TRUST_DENIED` (restated, not forwarded — its own message carries raw user ids), with an ADR 0100-shaped `instruction` forbidding the model from claiming success or explaining a failure as a fact about the user's data. The real error still logs at `error` with its SQLSTATE. Rejected: `.min(1)` (a rejected call lands the model back in the same confabulation), deleting the field (four internal callers need it), widening the index to `<> ''` (leaves `''` meaning "none" everywhere else), and a prompt-only rule (0100's precedent — the STRICT timezone rule still needed `normalizeDatetimes` as a server-side choke point) |
| [0103](./docs/notes/0103-an-invitation-is-not-ours-to-file.md) | **An invitation is not ours to file**: a calendar invite produced TWO events — Chord's confirmed copy and the organizer's, both real, one of them a Google event Chord minted on the family calendar. The obvious reading (dedup failed, match harder) is wrong: **Google had not yet put the invite on the calendar** — it holds one from an unfamiliar sender until the user answers, and the logs show four pull passes between the invite and the confirm inserting nothing, then the first pass after the accept inserting immediately. So at confirm time there was nothing to dedup against and the window is bounded by how long a human takes to click Accept. The real mistake is that two different questions both got a yes — Google's "accept this?" and Chord's "add this event?" — and Chord's yes minted a rival copy rather than RSVPing. Fix: `createEvent` takes `alreadyInGoogle` (set only by the invite path, stripped from the model's schema per ADR 0102) → `local_dirty=0`, no `chord_uid`, no push, and **no `google_calendar_id`** (Google decides where an accepted invite lands, and a claimed calendar also blocks the later adopt). Explicit, never inferred from UID shape — an imported `.ics` has the same bare RFC-5545 UID and must still push. `findLocalCopy` gains `external_uid = remote.iCalUID` (the push had overwritten `ical_uid` with Google's UID for OUR copy, so the invite UID survived only there — the create side already checked both columns). **Two live bugs found underneath**: the family calendar had stopped syncing ENTIRELY (`findLocalCopy` matched `ical_uid` with no calendar scope, so one event on two calendars made a pull steal the other calendar's row and `applyRemoteToLocal` collided on `uq_events_gcal_event`, aborting the whole pass every time — now every key except `chord_uid` is scoped to this-calendar-or-unbound, and `reconcileOne` catches per item into a new `PullResult.failed`); and one event created 18 June had been re-pushed on every pass for two months on `events.insert 400`, because the catch restored `local_dirty` unconditionally — a 4xx other than 408/429 now gives up until a local edit re-arms it. Consequence: confirming an invite no longer puts it on Google at all; the remaining gap is that the card should offer an **RSVP**, not "add to calendar" (not built — needs the Google RSVP write). Rejected: matching harder on the create side (nothing to match yet), inferring from UID shape, push-then-delete-on-adopt, scoping `chord_uid` (it identifies our event wherever Google moved it) |
| [0104](./docs/notes/0104-an-invitation-is-answered-not-filed.md) | **An invitation is answered, not filed**: ADR 0103 stopped Chord publishing a rival copy but left the invitation *unanswered* — confirming recorded it in Chord ONLY, so the meeting sat where the user doesn't look, the organizer learned nothing, and the Google calendar stayed empty. Chord was answering the wrong question (Google asks "accept this?", Chord asked "add this event?" — and accepting is what MAKES Google place it). Constraint: the Calendar API has **no RSVP endpoint**; the only route is patching your own `attendees` entry, which needs the event addressable — and Google's docs say a held-back invitation "isn't added to their calendar until they click Add to calendar or they RSVP", so the event to patch doesn't exist until the RSVP already happened. Decision: **patch when we can, mail when we cannot** — `respondToInvite` finds it by `events.list?iCalUID=` (the only path from an emailed UID to an API id), patches with `sendUpdates=all` (and sends the WHOLE attendees array back, since Google *replaces* it and a partial would un-invite everyone), else sends an iTIP `METHOD:REPLY` (RFC 5546, what Gmail's own buttons do). Card asks the invitation's question — **Yes / Maybe / No** wired to `POST /proposals/:id/rsvp`, wire values `accepted`/`tentative`/`declined` (the PARTSTAT names, so nothing translates); ADR 0097 routing chips are dropped (an RSVP is one person's, not routable). **Organizer answered first, THEN recorded**: decline resolves and creates nothing; accept/tentative creates the `alreadyInGoogle` row the pull adopts; a failed RSVP leaves the proposal **pending** with a row-level reason (ADR 0102 posture) since an unanswered invitation must keep looking like one. **Answered anywhere = answered everywhere**: the pull retracts by UID on any `responseStatus` other than `needsAction` (decline already did), so answering in Gmail clears the Chord card — `needsAction` deliberately does NOT retract, that card is one of the places it can be answered from. The REPLY carries **no DTSTART** (restating the time is how a reply "corrects" a meeting nobody asked us to change); `ParsedIcsEvent` gains `organizer` because the fallback needs it exactly when the calendar lookup can't supply it, and the email sender is not a substitute (invites get relayed by robot addresses). Rejected: patch-only (fails in the very case that prompted this), always-email (spends a send on the majority a patch handles), `events.import` then patch (docs call it *a private copy* — the organizer never hears, which is the half that matters), confirm-means-accept-plus-dismiss ("no" is a real answer). **Addendum 2026-11-10**: the invitation is the only card - Graph `eventMessageRequest`/`eventMessageResponse` now count as meeting messages (`isGraphEventMessageType`; only the base type did, so the user's own "Accepted:" reply staged a second event that invited the organizer), `.ics` REPLY/COUNTER/DECLINECOUNTER are handled with no event, and on a thread holding an iTIP `.ics` or an invite card the model's events and RSVP to-dos are dropped (`threadHoldsInvite`). **Addendum 2026-11-19**: the Outlook probe reads Graph's `organizer` (it never did, so every Outlook invitation was `no_organizer` when its calendar could not answer); the answer goes to the invitation's organizer, else the calendar event's, else the email's sender when that is a person (`senderCouldBeOrganizer`: never the user, no-reply, `calendar@`/`invitations@`/`notifications@`-style robots or `calendar.google.com`; newest other sender only); every refused answer is logged with its reason |
| [0105](./docs/notes/0105-a-cross-group-screen-needs-a-cross-group-stream.md) | **A cross-group screen needs a cross-group stream**: "my mobile app has everything still needing to do" — the To Do's hub **subscribed to nothing**. Tasks/lists/saves broadcast on their GROUP channel (right for a chat screen; the hub is every group the user belongs to), so the only refreshes were mount, pull-to-refresh, a real background→foreground round trip, or a tab change after the 30s `CACHE_FRESH_MS` module cache. Nothing pushed — worth naming precisely, because "the stream is slow" / "the cache is too long" are both wrong: there WAS no stream. Fix: `todos-mirror.ts` registers on the ADR 0054 `onBroadcast` seam, watches the four events the hub renders (`task.created`/`task.updated`/`list.updated`/`save.added`), resolves `group_members` and re-emits one `todos.changed` on each member's `user:<id>` channel; the hub opens ONE `/me/stream` (shared with the email screens). Three consequences of the shape, not taste: **one stream not one-per-group** (per-socket battery is what ADR 0082 exists to reduce, ×N memberships); the event carries **only `{ groupId }`** (the hub refetches whichever tab shows, so a per-entity payload is work nobody reads — and a payload nobody reads can leak; the four tabs also render four shapes, so client-side merging would be written four times and drift, while a refetch is definitionally consistent); and it hangs off `onBroadcast` rather than the **~15** write sites, which would be 15 chances to forget forever. Fan-out is members-only (ADR 0004 — everyone told is already entitled to read what changed). Client: refetch **coalesced at 250ms** (one bot turn fires several writes), subscription in `useFocusEffect` so a pushed-away screen holds no socket, and a refetch on open since a parked stream missed what happened while parked. A user-channel broadcast is skipped by the mirror or the fan-out observes its own output and loops. Rejected: subscribing to every group's stream (the ADR 0082 battery profile), shortening `CACHE_FRESH_MS` (treats a missing subscription as a tuning problem — polling still shows the wrong thing between polls) |
| [0106](./docs/notes/0106-classification-ids-belong-to-a-user.md) | **A classification id belongs to a user**: `email_categories.id` is a GLOBAL `text PRIMARY KEY`, but the id came from the title path alone (`mk:newsletters`) and `ingestClassification` deletes only the caller's rows — so every user minted the SAME id, the first to classify owned it, and every later user's ingest died on the pkey, rolled back the whole snapshot (one transaction, not one row), and was caught at **`warn`**, reading as a flaky LLM call. Nothing recovered: the next sync retried the identical insert. In prod ONE account held all 48 category rows and worked, while **three mailboxes — 1,288 threads — had never been classified at all**, failing every sync since 22 Aug. Classification was structurally single-user, unnoticed because the one user it worked for was the one looking. Fix: `storageCategoryId(userId, id)` applied at the three write sites **inside ingest** — snapshot ids stay user-local, only storage is scoped, and no reader changes (the client already treats the id as opaque and echoes it back into `?category=`). **Deliberately NOT in `categoryIdForPath`**, which was the first attempt: ADR 0061 Mode B (`POST /me/email/classification`, service-token) takes a snapshot from an EXTERNAL classifier that never touches `classifier-tree.ts` and cannot know a Chord user id, so that path would have stayed just as wedged; ingest is the one choke point every classifier crosses, and it also keeps the id under the kernel's 128-char cap since validation runs pre-prefix. Migration `046` (`scopeEmailCategoryIds`, extracted for testing like `mergeDuplicateLists`) re-keys existing rows — it must **drop both FKs** (they're immediate, so id-first strands children and children-first dangles) and **open its own transaction** (`applyMigrations` runs each `up` bare), and every statement is prefix-guarded so a re-run can't produce `u1:u1:mk:…`. The catch moves `warn` → **`error`**: a pkey violation inside ingest repeats forever. Rows keep their classification (re-key, not reclassify) and the wedged mailboxes drain at 40/tick on their own. Rejected: composite `(user_id, id)` PK (six sites key on `id` alone — one with no `user_id` predicate — so it trades a loud crash for a silent cross-user match), opaque ids + unique `(user_id, path_key)` (delete-and-reinsert would need a read-back every transaction to keep tile ids stable), and drop-and-reclassify (empties a working map, refills 40/tick). A `CHECK (left(id, length(user_id)+1) = user_id \|\| ':')` would guard future direct writers but trips four unrelated fixtures — noted, not added |
| [0107](./docs/notes/0107-chord-reads-and-answers-gmail-manages.md) | **Chord reads and answers; the provider manages**: holding the mail surface to "everything any other mail app can do" is ~35 gaps and most of a year against a free incumbent, to win *filing* - and every management verb is a second writer against a mailbox the provider also writes, which we were already losing in three places. So **answering is ours** (read, reply, reply-all, RSVP, forward, compose, search, read state) and **managing is theirs** (trash, star, snooze, move, folders, filters), with a per-thread deep link out - Gmail exact via `#all/` + `authuser`, Outlook/IMAP-preset marked **inexact** (mailbox root, labelled by product), custom IMAP `null` so no dead button renders. The mirror stays COMPLETE: this narrows writing, never reading. The three mirror bugs are **fixed, not deleted with the feature** - archive is the one management verb load-bearing for *answering* (ADR 0073 convergence, the decision queue and `markSourceThreadsRead` all sit on it), so IMAP archive became a real `\Archive` folder move (never falling back to Trash) and Outlook un-archive became symmetric. General rule: **a mailbox write we do not really perform must not report that we performed it** - labels on Outlook/IMAP now 422 instead of being silently dropped. Consequence: compose lands, and a sent copy mirrors as `SENT` with no `INBOX` → `is_archived=1` → invisible to every listing and to search, so **you can send mail you cannot find**; Sent/archive browsing moves to the front of Wave 3. Rejected: full parity, deleting archive with the rest of management, keeping the silent drops (the ADR 0102 failure), and a read-only mirror (mark-read must be real or the decision queue and the mailbox disagree about what was dealt with) |
| [0109](./docs/notes/0109-bulk-is-how-mail-was-sent.md) | **Bulk is how mail was SENT, not what it is about**: a third axis beside ADR 0086's action, because *Money Stuff* genuinely IS about finance AND is a blast — and forcing one axis to carry both is what filed a ski retailer's sale under **Newsletters › Recipes & food** while `Coupons` held three threads in the whole database. The two real signals were on the wire and discarded: Gmail metadata returns only the headers you name, and the allow-list never named `List-Unsubscribe` (RFC 8058, mandatory for high-volume senders since Feb 2024). `email_threads.bulk` is derived on **`recomputeThread`** — the one choke point every provider funnels through, so it survives snapshot-replace and costs no LLM call — from `List-Unsubscribe` ‖ `List-Id` ‖ `CATEGORY_PROMOTIONS`; `Precedence: bulk` is deliberately NOT captured (it can only corroborate a decision already made, so it would change no outcome). **A thread is bulk only when EVERY message is** — one ordinary message makes it a conversation, and under-marking is the safe error. The classifier is now TOLD (`bulk` in the payload, present only when true) instead of being asked to infer it: the `"promotions" → Coupons ‖ Newsletters` fork is gone, `Coupons` gets real leaves, and Newsletters means *editorial writing someone subscribed to*. Governing rule, inherited from the backlog: `List-Unsubscribe` is a **strong positive and a weak negative** — transactional mass mail omits it by design, and Evite/Eventbrite/ParentSquare all set it — so `bulk` is a **signal, never a verdict**: nothing demotes on it alone and `action IN (to_do, reply_needed)` overrides it everywhere. Outlook carries `bulk = 0` (Graph exposes headers only outside the delta `$select`), which fails safe. Migration `047` backfills from the stored promotions label |
| [0110](./docs/notes/0110-the-ai-passes-must-be-shown-the-email.md) | **The AI passes must be shown the email**: ADR 0109 shipped and the two emails that prompted it still would not be caught — both sit in an Outlook mailbox where every header arm is blind (0 `List-Unsubscribe`, **0 of 173** `CATEGORY_PROMOTIONS`). Checking what the model actually receives reframed it: **we were reading ~78 characters of the email.** A marketing preheader pads with U+200C, so **464 of 2,432** prod threads lose 40+ chars to invisible text; and bodies barely existed (**47 of 2,718** on gmail-primary, **0** on Outlook) because both Gmail paths synced `format=metadata` — while `magics` and `person-extraction` are both written to read `bodyText.slice(0, 2000)` and got `null` **98%** of the time. Sync now keeps `body_excerpt` (2,000 chars, the number those passes already used); `body_text` stays lazy and reader-owned so **one writer each** can never truncate the other; `cleanSnippet` strips the padding at sync (migration `048` backfills) and `htmlToText` derives text where Graph returns HTML only. The classifier now RETURNS `bulk` (~5 output tokens, works on every provider) into a **separate `bulk_inferred`** column — merged with 0109's `bulk` it would be clobbered, since `recomputeThread` runs after every sync. **Reading further is the extractor's call**, not a keyword rule: one boolean in the JSON it already returns, bounded by "only if more exists", `EMAIL_DEEP_READ_PER_TICK`, and once-per-thread by construction. Attachment import unpinned from Gmail (ADR 0080's reason died when its own addendum built the cross-provider fetcher — BitGo's invoice PDF was the one legible copy of the amount). `classifier_version` tops up each batch **after** live mail so a prompt change re-runs the mailbox without starving new arrivals, and a pass that ANSWERED stamps even an empty answer — only a pass that THREW retries, else a thread the model declines wedges the queue forever. Tiles gain a `recent` preview so acting on mail no longer blanks them (attention still counts open work only), and `stripContextTags` stops "Onam tomorrow **[INFO_ONLY]**" reaching the user. Cost ~4x on the AI passes (~$0.78 → ~$2.01/user/month); whole-body reading rejected at ~$7.35. Existing magics marks deliberately NOT cleared — the mark is the only guard against re-proposing what was already rejected |
| [0134](./docs/notes/0134-a-count-is-a-place.md) | **A count is a place, not a number**: the ADR 0083 coverage footer put all three counts inside ONE `Pressable` whose handler only fired when `unreadable > 0` — so two of the three led nowhere, and the third opened the unclassified list whichever one you tapped.
