Client-side filtering (how `unreadable` does it) cannot work: `listThreads` pages at 50 and *still owed* means an EXPIRED proposal, so those threads are ≥ 3 days old and routinely past page one — and both sets come from `proposals` joined by `gmail:<id>` provenance, which the client cannot derive. Coverage now carries `handledThreadIds`/`owedThreadIds` (capped at 200; **counts stay exact**) and `?ids=` returns exactly those. **One deliberate exception to ADR 0073**: `?ids=` includes ARCHIVED threads — not because deciding archives (it does not; `markSourceThreadsRead` only clears UNREAD) but because `handledToday` counts what the user DID, so filtering makes the footer claim 3 over a list of 2. Asymmetric on purpose: `stillOwed` still drops archived, since archiving cancels an obligation but does not undo an action. `countStillOwed` → `stillOwedThreadIds`, length IS the count, so number and list cannot drift |
| [0135](./docs/notes/0135-a-shipping-update-says-when.md) | **A shipping update says when it arrives**: the tile rendered `Package · shipped` — the least informative sentence in the email — while magics staged a to-do saying "track this". A tracking number names its own carrier by SHAPE (UPS `1Z`+16, USPS 20-22 digits, FedEx 12/15/20, DHL 10; sender domain breaks the FedEx/USPS 20-digit tie), so carrier + URL are a parser and a template, never a model call — and the URL is **rebuilt from carrier+number**, not stored, so a changed template cannot strand old rows. The ETA is prose ("arriving Thursday") so it is READ: owner decision to read delivery mail greedily, which ADR 0110's excerpt made possible and which costs **no extra LLM call** — the magics extractor returns an optional `delivery` block read by `parseDelivery`, the `parseNeedMore` shape, so `EmailExtractor` still returns `EmailSuggestion|null` and every stub compiles. **Stored (`delivery_json`), not proposed** — tile facts are computed at RENDER time where no model can run, and a proposal is something you confirm while an arrival date is not a decision. A calendar event IS proposed when someone must be present (signature/window/perishable — ADR 0100's test), with a TTL to the day after arrival: ADR 0009's flat 72h would expire it before the parcel lands and drop it into ADR 0134's "expired, still owed", which is for obligations not parcels. A date-only ETA becomes midnight (ADR 0040 all-day) rather than an invented 9am. Also: a stated date makes a row even with NO status keyword (the keyword only ever DETECTED a delivery), and `dropArrivedDeliveries` retires a parcel once its day passes, since `extractStateStatus` only drops threads that literally say "delivered". **We render the tracking link; we never fetch the carrier page** — ADR 0107's ours/theirs does not decide this (a carrier is neither), and without the line "show delivery status" drifts into scraping. Migration `049` |
| [0138](./docs/notes/0138-a-flight-we-cannot-name-gets-no-link.md) | **A flight we cannot name gets no link**: the sibling of 0135 for flights, under 0133's corroboration rule. Two constraints decided it, and both were worth CHECKING rather than assuming: FlightAware addresses a flight by its **ICAO** ident (`DAL1231`, not `DL1231` — the IATA form redirects only for a handful of hand-entered majors), so `kernel/src/flight-ident.ts`'s ~80-airline IATA→ICAO table is not a formatting nicety but the **recognition step**, and an airline outside it gets no ident and no URL (the number still renders as text); and a URL in the bot's **prose is dead text** (the bubble is a plain `<Text>` — no markdown, no linkifier), so the only tappable path is `BotCard { kind:'action', url }`, which is why `tools.ts` already says *"The system builds the URL — do not assemble URLs yourself"*. Identity is **parsed** (`findFlight`) and the times/route/status are **read** off the same magics reply through the `onDelivery`-shaped side channel, so `EmailExtractor` still returns `EmailSuggestion|null`; **the model is not the authority on identity** — a flight block over mail with no recognisable designator writes NOTHING, since naming a flight wrong yields a link to somebody else's aeroplane, not a vaguer answer. `[A-Z]{2}\d{1,4}` is a MORE ambiguous shape than 0133's digit run (order number, room number, part number), so corroboration is asymmetric: a code **in** the table takes sender-is-that-airline ‖ airline-named-in-text ‖ cue word within 40 chars; a code **outside** it needs the cue word specifically (nothing else can speak for a designator we cannot name, and the sender arm is useless there by construction). `confirmation`/`order`/`booking`/`PNR` are deliberately NOT cues — they precede a record locator (`KX8QP2`), which is the `Order` mistake 0133 made a rule about — and matching is **case-sensitive**, or `to 500` becomes a designator and a sentence corroborates itself. Airline names match in FULL: "United" is not an alias precisely so "United States" cannot vouch for a `UA1234` order number (same for Delta/American/Alaska/Frontier/Spirit; JetBlue/Ryanair/Qantas are distinctive enough to alias). Sender is a strong positive, never a requirement — most flight mail arrives via Expedia/TripIt (0133's own reasoning). Stored in `flight_json` (migration `061`), **not proposed** — but unlike a delivery the `create_event` is **KEPT**, because a parcel is left at the door and a flight is somewhere a person has to be (ADR 0100's test). Arrival reuses `etaAt` so `dropArrivedDeliveries` → **`dropLandedRows`** retires it on the rule it already applied (nobody emails to say a flight landed). **Pays 0135's debt**: `tracking.url` had been computed server-side and DROPPED by the client ever since — `factLines` can only return strings for a treemap label — so the decisions sheet now renders facts through one extracted `LinkChip` and the delivery link works for the first time, which is most of why this was built once instead of twice. Bot side: a byte-capped `upcomingFlightsBlock` in `context.ts` (that group's legs; on a personal turn, legs across all groups plus email-derived flights per 0084/0096) carries the **designator and never a URL** — a URL in the prompt is one waiting to be copied into prose — and the link comes from `suggest_external_action` `kind:'flight_status'` (distinct from `flight`, which searches Google Flights for one to BUY); unknown airline → builder returns null, **no card**, and an ADR 0102-shaped instruction forbidding the model to dress that up as one. **We render the link; we never fetch it** (0135's rule, restated because a flight-status API exists and `chord-spec-v4.md` lists live tracking as Phase 3). Calendar grid deliberately untouched: `LegBlock` is sized by duration, collapses to one line, has no detail view, and one tappable block among inert ones on a scrolling surface is a mis-tap that launches a browser. Rejected: a best-effort IATA URL (dead-ends read as Chord's mistake), a search-page fallback (spends a tap to say we didn't know), requiring an airline sender, creating a travel leg from email (needs trip matching + dedup + a consent posture — its own ADR), letting the model write the URL, and storing it |
| [0139](./docs/notes/0139-a-link-is-a-button-only-when-it-is-the-only-one.md) | **A link is a button, but only when it is the only one**: 0138 put the FlightAware URL on a `BotCard` and WhatsApp rendered it as a bare appended URL, because `whatsapp.renderer.ts` said *"URL buttons can't be quick-replies (they'd need the CTA content type)"* — true of **Twilio**, where a URL button is a separate approved content template (0053 declined it), and obsolete since **0090** moved prod to Meta, where it is one field (`interactive.type:'cta_url'`) needing no template. The comment outlived its reason by a year — the shape of 0116's attachment-import constraint that had quietly stopped being true. **Verified, not assumed**, since the cost of being wrong is a message that fails to send: `cta_url` is a *service message* (free-form, no pre-approval, deliverable only inside the 24h window — the identical constraint `sendButtons` already carried), **ONE** button, `display_text` ≤ 20; no WABA eligibility gate is documented and ours is `CLOUD_API` / `GREEN` / `STANDARD`. There is **no read-only probe** — the token is phone-number-scoped (0099), `whatsapp_business_account` still returns `#100`, and no Graph field enumerates which interactive types a number may use — so confirming it took **one live send**, which returned a `wamid` **and** rendered as a button on the handset; both halves matter, because 0137 is the record of Meta accepting a message and then failing delivery into a `warn` nobody read. Rule: `cta_url` only when the turn carries **exactly one URL and zero callbacks**. **Callbacks win** when both are present — `interactive.type` is a single value so the two cannot coexist, and the tie breaks on what is lost either way: a confirm/reject is the ONLY affordance WhatsApp has for it, while a URL survives as a linkified bare URL in the text. **Two links stay as text** — promoting one of several silently drops the rest (the 0107 failure), and N links as text is lossier per link but complete. `text` **keeps** the appended URL lines and only the Meta `ctaUrl.body` omits them: the **Twilio** connector reads `quickReply` alone, so a `ctaUrl`-only slot falls through to `text` there and stripping the URL to avoid duplication would delete the link entirely on that path — which is also what makes Meta-only a property of the slot rather than a flag (Twilio degrades by doing nothing). Button label is `card.title`, which for `suggest_external_action` is the model's `label`, already written to read as a button; `BotActionButton.url` counts toward the "exactly one" test even though nothing emits one today, or a future producer loses its link to a rule that only looked at `card.url`. **One existing test FLIPPED** rather than being deleted (0108's treatment). Unrelated but larger, found here: service messages have been free since Nov 2024 and Meta **begins charging 1 Oct 2026** at utility/auth rates (published by 1 Sept) — that prices *every* WhatsApp bot reply, so the 0053 taster allowance and the 0089 briefing path both need re-costing; noted, not addressed. Rejected: replacing the text fallback wholesale, promoting the first of N, stripping the URL from `text`, building the Twilio CTA content template too, `cta_url`'s optional header/footer, and trusting the docs without the live send |
| [0140](./docs/notes/0140-a-deferral-outlives-the-provider-it-was-for.md) | **A deferral outlives the provider it was for**: 0139 fixed one stale Twilio-era comment; sweeping for others found three more, all deferrals taken for **Twilio's** reasons, carried onto the Meta connector and left after 0090 made the reason obsolete. Worth naming as a pattern, because each was honest when written — what rots is the REASON, and nothing links a deferral to the constraint it cited (the cloud connector's own comment defers media *"matching the Twilio connector's current scope"*, a reason about a sibling rather than about Meta). **(1) A file is really sent, and when it cannot be we say so.** `send_file` (0036) posts `kind:'file'` with body `note || filename`, `forward()` has no `kind` filter, so both connectors logged a warn and sent the BODY — asking for the school calendar over WhatsApp returned the literal string `LakesideCalendar2026.pdf` and no file, with 0036 correctly forbidding the bot from inventing a URL to compensate: 0107's failure on the OUTBOUND path, invisible to the sender. Meta takes the **bytes** (`POST /<phone-id>/media` → id → send by id); Twilio pulls from a **public URL**, which is the real objection 0053 had and which never applied to Meta. So media send is built on cloud and **Twilio's deferral stands — but its silence does not**: both fall back to one sentence naming the file and pointing at Chord (`file-fallback.ts`, one rule two callers for 0122's reason — the transports fail for unrelated causes but what the recipient deserves to hear is identical). Size **refused, never truncated** (0111) with **per-kind** caps because Meta's are (image 5MB, video/audio 16MB, document 100MB), checked BEFORE the upload; kind is not cosmetic — only `document` takes `filename`, `audio` takes no `caption`, and Meta rejects a payload carrying a field its type does not accept — and a caption equal to the filename is dropped (that is what `send_file` posts with no note, and a document already shows its name). **(2) Inbound media is a message, not a drop**: Twilio logged "dropping", cloud had NO handling so a photo hit the unsupported-type branch — a photographed permission slip got nothing while the same photo in-app feeds the whole 0071 pipeline. Retrieval is two hops (the media node returns a short-lived URL that STILL needs the bearer token) and a failed download **keeps the caption, omits the attachment** rather than claiming a file arrived; `voice` is deliberately absent from the type set (a voice note is `audio` with `voice:true`, so listing it matches nothing) and a filename is derived from the mime type since WhatsApp gives a photo none. **(3) The length cap belongs to the transport**: `WHATSAPP_LIMIT=1600` is **Twilio's** (it rejects with 21617 rather than truncating), WhatsApp's own is **4096**, and the constant sat in the SHARED renderer so Meta sends were clipped at Twilio's number — 0091 had already worked around the symptom, calling 1600 "WhatsApp's cap", which is where the wrong number became folklore. `RenderInput.textLimit` lets the connector state its own; the default stays **1600** as the fail-safe direction (a forgotten call site clips early = degraded but sent, where defaulting to 4096 hands Twilio a body it refuses outright = no send). `INTERACTIVE_BODY_MAX=1024` is real on Meta, untouched. Consequences: `toInbound` became async (batches were already handled one at a time, so ordering is unchanged); **untested against the live API** like 0118 and unlike 0139 — the shapes are pinned against Meta's documented fields but no real photo has round-tripped, and the FAILURE paths are the ones with tests, which is the right way round since they run when a shape is wrong; media are service messages so 0139's **1 Oct 2026** billing applies. Rejected: leaving Twilio's file behaviour alone because it is now local-dev only (the dishonesty is the defect, not the missing capability, and it costs one sentence), hosting files at a signed public URL for Twilio, silently falling back on oversize (the behaviour being removed), ONE shared media cap (0116's named trap — it would refuse on Meta a document Meta would take), reading `WHATSAPP_PROVIDER` inside the renderer (couples a pure function to env), and raising the default to 4096 (same information, failure inverted) |
| [0141](./docs/notes/0141-chord-labels-are-usage-derived-and-ours.md) | **Chord labels are usage-derived and ours**: local `email_threads.chord_label_ids` (migration `062`) that `recomputeThread` never touches — writing into `gmail_label_ids` is silently wiped on the next sync. Safe vs ADR 0107 because a Chord label has exactly **one writer**; the set is derived from usage (no `email_labels` table; empty labels do not exist). Browse ORs both columns under ADR 0125's searchable reach; honesty copy mirrors ADR 0129. Wave 2 provider push is out of this ADR |
| [0142](./docs/notes/0142-chord-label-push-is-one-way-and-optional.md) | **Chord label push is one-way and optional**: opt-in per mailbox (`chord_label_push_enabled` / `email_accounts.label_push_*`, migration `063`); provider form `Chord/<name>`; failed push never rolls back local `chord_label_ids`; never read categories/keywords back (keeps ADR 0141's one writer). IMAP only when `PERMANENTFLAGS` allows `\*`. `label:` search on both paths (ADR 0126/0085). Distinct from `gmail_sync_labels_enabled` (tile sync) |
| [0143](./docs/notes/0143-a-to-do-dies-with-the-email-it-came-from.md) | **A to-do dies with the email it came from**: two defects behind "my to-dos never clear". (1) ADR 0073 retracted a deleted thread's PENDING proposals and left anything a confirm had created - right for a saved place or an event (a RECORD of something, which outlives its source) and wrong for a to-do, which is an outstanding OBLIGATION whose whole value is being still owed; deleting the mail is how someone says it is over. The link already existed and nothing read it (`create_task` writes `sourceRawContext: provenance`, so email-derived tasks carry `gmail:<threadId>` in `tasks.source_raw_context`). `retireTasksByProvenance` sets them **`archived`, not `done`** - deliberately against the request's wording, since ADR 0092 made dropping first-class precisely because routing abandonment through `done` corrupts the completion signal; identical to the user, different in what the row MEANS to the briefing and the archive policy. Narrow by construction - `open` + null `archived_at` only: `active` means a person picked it up, `done` is history, and a non-null `archived_at` is 0092's fire-once marker whose re-stamping is the open/archived oscillation that ADR documents. Scoped by `created_by` too (thread ids are opaque per mailbox, so collision is unlikely not impossible, and this writes into groups others can see). Archive still keeps everything, as 0073 decided - archiving is filing, not doneness. (2) The magics query filtered `is_archived = 0` and nothing else, with **no age bound and no read-state filter** while `GMAIL_BACKFILL_DAYS` is 90 - so a connect mined a quarter-year of already-handled inbox (read-but-not-archived is how most people leave mail) into fresh to-dos. Same shape as ADR 0137's past-dated events, but that guard keys on `startsAt` and an ADR 0132 `create_task` often has no date at all. Now minable = **unread OR newer than `EMAIL_MINE_READ_MAX_AGE_DAYS`** (7): the COMBINATION is the rule - unread is mined at ANY age (nobody has looked, so it is still owed - an age-only cutoff, the obvious version, drops exactly that), read-but-recent is still mined, read-and-old is history. Magics only, NOT person extraction (ADR 0076): an obligation goes stale, a fact does not. The skipped thread is **not** marked extracted, or widening the window later would never revisit it. Missing a still-owed obligation read 8 days ago is accepted on ADR 0133's asymmetry (a missed suggestion costs a search, a false one costs trust). Found in passing: `minablePredicate` was applied TWICE in the same query. Rejected: `done` (corrupts 0092's signal), retiring `active`/`done` too, a new `source_thread_id` column, reacting to archive as well as delete, an age-only cutoff, and retro-retiring the existing pile (their source mail still exists, so there is no signal to act on) |
| [0144](./docs/notes/0144-the-mirror-goes-stale-when-nobody-is-looking.md) | **The mirror goes stale when nobody is looking**: "mail I already read in Gmail still shows unread" reads like a convergence bug and is not - EVERY provider path converges correctly (Gmail backfill upserts `is_unread` from the UNREAD label, History applies `labelsRemoved`, an expired `historyId` falls back to a full backfill; attached Gmail the same incl. the expiry fallback; Outlook maps `isRead`; `aggregateThread` is `some(m => m.isUnread)`, matching Gmail's own thread semantics). The defect is not HOW Chord learns but **WHEN**: mail synced in exactly two places - the Email tab force-syncing on focus, and once a day inside the morning briefing (both `maybeSyncGmail` calls in `proactive.service.ts` sit in the daily-scan path) - so every other surface (tiles + their attention weighting, unread counts, the decisions queue, To Do's) rendered whatever was true when that ONE screen was last open. Sharpest consequence, previously unnoticed: **ADR 0117's new-mail notification could effectively only fire while the user was already looking at their mail** (it triggers from `afterSync`, which ran from a sync, which ran from the Email tab) - structurally near-dead. Fix is a bounded sweep in the proactive tick at new **`mirror` depth**, and the seam ALREADY EXISTED: `runAfterSyncPasses` was written in two tiers because ADR 0079 had to decide what an unentitled user still gets - above the gate (body excerpts, new-mail notify, vacation responder: provider fetches, no LLM) and below it (classification, attachment import, magics, person extraction). A sweep wants the same line for a different reason, so `MailSyncDepth` reuses it rather than inventing a second. Keeping notify + responder ABOVE is load-bearing: both are time-sensitive, both are free, and the sweep is the first thing that ever made either work with the app closed. **Depth deepens, never shallows** - rounds are keyed per user, so a route sync arriving while the sweep holds that user's round JOINS it and must not be downgraded to passes it did not ask for; the mirror image bit immediately (the sweep's own `maybeSyncAccounts` opened a `full` round by default and deepened the sweep back into the AI passes), fixed by threading `depth` through that function rather than weakening a rule protecting a real case, with a test pinning both directions. `MAIL_SWEEP_BATCH` (20) users/tick **stalest-first** so it round-robins rather than starving anyone; `MAIL_SWEEP_MINUTES` (5); shadows excluded (ADR 0098 - nobody for a fresh mirror to be fresh FOR); never throws (ADR 0080's Yahoo throttle arrives as an ordinary rejection and must not stop the tick's other work). Also: the Email tab ran status -> sync -> refresh, so for the SECONDS a full sync takes it painted the AsyncStorage cache from whenever the tab was last open - it now refreshes from the mirror BEFORE the sync too (the server copy is already fresher than that cache and is one local read away). Consequence: swept mail is mirrored but NOT classified until the user opens the mail surface or the briefing runs - the deliberate trade, since the mirror is what was stale. Rejected: full-depth background sync (multiplies per-user AI cost by sweep frequency - the spend 0137 had just bounded), turning on ADR 0118 push instead (right long-run answer, but ships off + unverified and needs a public webhook/Pub-Sub topic/Azure app - and 0118 says push pre-empts the poll rather than replacing it, so the poll must exist first), only widening IMAP's 200-UID `fetchFlags` bound (fixes one provider's tail and nothing about cadence - still worth doing separately), and shortening/dropping the Email tab cache (treats a missing background sync as a rendering problem) |
| [0145](./docs/notes/0145-a-mailbox-can-be-given-a-name.md) | **A mailbox can be given a name**: ADR 0119 labelled the filter chips with the address LOCAL PART - true for two mailboxes, and with five the row reads `Gmail · crookbond · john_doe · jdoe · john`, four spellings of one person's name. The local part identifies the ADDRESS; a chip needs to say what the mailbox is FOR. New user-chosen `nickname` (migration `064`) winning over the local part - and deliberately **not** `display_name`, which is provider-supplied and REWRITTEN by `insertAccount` on every re-attach, so a nickname stored there would be silently erased by reconnecting: the ADR 0141 failure (writing into a column the sync owns) in a second place, and that ADR's rule generalises to **a value a user chose needs a column no sync writes**. Pinned by a test that renames, reconnects, and asserts the name survived. The primary Gmail has no `email_accounts` row (ADR 0080: `account_id NULL` IS the primary), so its name is `users.gmail_nickname` - the same asymmetry ADR 0142 already established for label-push opt-in, followed rather than papered over with a synthetic account row that much of the mail code would then mis-key. Fallback rule lives in `kernel/src/mailbox-name.ts` (`mailboxDisplayName` / `primaryMailboxDisplayName`) because THREE surfaces render the same mailbox - chip row, per-row account badge, Settings - and three copies of a fallback chain is how they stop agreeing (ADR 0085/0127's reasoning; also 0120/0121's, since kernel is the only place with a test harness). Whitespace-only falls back rather than rendering an invisible chip, and `normalizeMailboxNickname` turns an empty field into `null` - that is how a mailbox goes BACK to its default, so "cleared" must not store a nickname that renders blank. `SetEmailAccountPrefsRequest` became a partial patch (`setAccountLabelPushEnabled` -> `setAccountPrefs`): an absent field is LEFT ALONE, so renaming cannot silently switch label push off, and both directions have a test. The row badge RESOLVES the name from the account list in the parent rather than putting the nickname on every `EmailThreadSummary`, which would ship the same string on every row of every page to save one lookup. No uniqueness check - two mailboxes may share a name, that is the user's call. Rejected: reusing `display_name`, a synthetic account row for the primary, client-side storage (a recognisable label that does not follow you to another device is worse than none), and defaulting the nickname to the provider's `display_name` (a never-renamed mailbox would still change name on reconnect) |
| [0146](./docs/notes/0146-the-email-map-is-a-feed.md) | **The email map is a feed**: the owner didn't like the treemap tiles' look. `EmailFeed` replaces `EmailTiles` under the "AI" toggle — same `decisions` prop, one-line swap — and drops category grouping for one urgency-ranked list: dated items soonest-first (`sortFeedUnits`), undated items after, ranked by the same family-first/marketing-last weighting the tiles used for area (`categoryUsefulness`, now shared instead of duplicated). List items sharing a name still batch into one "Add all" card (`feedUnits`); a lone event or task no longer gets lumped with every other event or task across every category, which `batchDecisions` did and which would have erased the ordering the feed exists to show. Found in passing: `batchDecisions` never handled `kind: 'task'` (ADR 0132 postdates it) — a task-only decision fell through both filters and vanished from its sheet, so a tile badge could read "1" over "Nothing owed." `BatchCard` moved to its own file so the feed and the (now unwired) tile sheet can share it. Consequences stated, not silently absorbed: "Unsorted" disappears as a concept (nothing is off-map when there's no map), and the per-category "Current state" fact boards (ADR 0087/0135/0138 — delivery/flight/ledger rollups) have no home in a flat feed; `email-tiles.tsx` is kept in the tree, unwired, rather than deleted, so that UI isn't lost with no path back. Rejected: restyling the treemap in place (the objection was to the shape, not the palette), grouping by the ADR 0086 action axis instead (still a grouping), deleting the tile view outright (destroys the facts board with nothing to replace it) |
| [0148](./docs/notes/0148-a-stale-weather-location-should-ask.md) | **A stale weather location should ask, not guess**: ADR 0091 resolves the briefing's weather location (`ip`→`tz`→`user`) once and then never re-examines it — `seedLocationFromIp` freezes an `ip` fix after the first foreground, a `user` override is untouched by design, and there was no chat path to it at all (prod: a real user with `weather_place=Seville`, `source=user`, `last_tz=Europe/Madrid`, and five failed `PUT /me/weather-location` tries). No GPS to check against (0091 rejected the prompt and this doesn't revisit that), so the only signals are the same two automatic tiers. **New `set_weather_location` bot tool** (personal-only, like `set_my_name`) → the same `setUserWeatherLocation`/`clearUserWeatherLocation` the Settings route uses; empty `place` clears, `keepCurrent:true` answers a drift question "no", geocode-fail/`WEATHER_PROVIDER` unset → `ok:false` + write nothing + description forbids claiming success (0102). **Drift detection**: `detectWeatherDrift` compares stored coords vs a fresh fix — the IP city stashed on the user row (`PUT /me/timezone` is the only place with a request IP; `stashIpObservation` writes it unconditionally) if <3 days old, else the `cityFromTimezone` city geocoded — and `>WEATHER_DRIFT_KM` (100) apart is drift. **The ask**: the morning briefing appends one line ("set to X, you seem near Y — yes / a city / keep it") via `weatherDriftQuestion`, best-effort like the weather/email/proposals sections beside it; the user answers in the 1:1 and the normal bot turn calls the tool. **Not nagging**: `weather_prompt_{lat,lon,at,declined}` — don't re-ask within `WEATHER_DRIFT_KM` of the last spot for `WEATHER_DRIFT_REASK_DAYS` (7) if unanswered, or ever if declined, until a fresh fix lands a real distance away; any explicit set/clear clears the episode in `persist`. Applies to **all sources**, not just `user` (one rule — Chord never moves your weather without asking — over a per-source split; also un-freezes the `ip` tier). Migration 065 (8 nullable `users` columns, no backfill). **Distinct from** the ADR 0091 addendum's deploy fix (`MAXMIND_LICENSE_KEY` was in `.env.prod` not `deploy.env`, so the mmdb never shipped and the `ip` tier was dead in prod) — independent: this notices staleness whatever tier produced it. Rejected: silent auto-follow for `ip`/`tz` (the surprise this avoids), a buttoned card (0042 shape — but plain-message needs zero new answering machinery and a wrong guess invites a free-text fix), signup prompt (0091 already rejected as default), re-geocode every foreground (silent change + network on the hot path), a dedicated `proactive_kind` (another insert/broadcast/dedup surface vs a briefing section). Coarse-`tz` caveat: a correctly-set Barcelona user (same tz as Madrid) gets one prompt/week until the `ip` arm can tell them apart — mitigated by IP-preferred + decline + weekly cap + one-tap "no" |
| [0147](./docs/notes/0147-let-people-choose-their-email-view.md) | **Let people choose their email view**: "I don't like this one" and "this is the right one for everybody" are different claims, so instead of picking a second time, all four layouts sketched for 0146 — Map, Cards, Sections, Feed — ship as a per-device chip picker (`email.aiViewStyle` in AsyncStorage, same mechanism as the AI/Traditional toggle) under the Urgent Strip. Two new views make the choice real: **Cards** (`email-cards.tsx`) — one card per category ranked the same way the treemap ranked tile area (`groupByCategory`, sharing `categoryUsefulness`), expanding in place with no sheet or drag gesture; **Sections** (`email-sections.tsx`) — grouped by decision KIND (To-dos/Events/Lists), a deliberate substitution for the mockup's "Reply needed/To-dos/FYI" since that's ADR 0086's action axis living on `email_threads`, not on a decision — a decision only exists once a magic proposal was staged, so there's no "reply needed" bucket among decisions to group by. All four take the identical `{decisions, onDecisionsChanged}` props (Map alone still fetches its own category tree) and share `BatchCard` + the ranking helpers, so a fix to confirm/dismiss or to ranking lands in all four at once. | [0149](./docs/notes/0149-the-mail-surface-is-bounded-to-coordination.md) | **The mail surface is bounded to family coordination**: an audit found the ~40 ADRs since 0107 (folders, keyset pagination, own-drafts, undo-send, recipient autocomplete, image proxy + fetch-through, unsubscribe ×3, vacation responder, send-as aliases, markdown, quote-folding, snooze, bulk triage, label browse + push, mailbox nicknames, four AI-view layouts) each had a sound LOCAL reason and collectively built a second product — `services/email/` is now ~8.5k non-test LOC, the size of the whole agent — while the coordination pillars and the HARNESS-shaped email work (people/family extraction, group routing, the briefing digest) got a fraction of it; several shipped over an explicit "recommend deferring" (0129, 0146/0147). 0107 drew answering-vs-managing and left "answering" read generously; this draws a second, tighter line INSIDE it. **Decision**: a mail feature earns a place only if it produces or improves a **shared family artifact** (task, event, list item, person/date, routing outcome, briefing line, or a proposal that becomes one); reading + replying stay because they are that pipeline's input and response. Net-new mail-client parity/polish is **frozen** — folders, labels, snooze, draft UX, formatting, send-as, vacation, image handling, triage gestures, view layouts get no new work unless it is a correctness fix to something shipped or it directly feeds the pipeline. **The gate** (every email-touching change, one line in the commit/PR body): name the shared family artifact it produces — "a nicer inbox" is a rejection, that belongs in Gmail (ADR 0102/0107's say-it-in-the-product rule, turned on the roadmap). **Sanctioned lane** ahead of any further mail surface: (1) email→people/family facts (`person-extraction.ts`, ADR 0076) verified against a real mailbox; (2) email→group routing reaching shared groups through the ADR 0097 consent surface, not just personal; (3) the daily-briefing email section (`digest.service.ts`, ADR 0072/0091). `email-parity-audit.md` reframed not resumed — Wave 2 outbound is "enough", Wave 3 continues only where it serves recall of family context. Rejected: continue parity-by-ADR (the mechanism that caused the drift — another judgement call is not the fix), a hard freeze on ALL email work (throws out the differentiated classification→magics→proposals pipeline with the polish), a static allowed/banned checklist (goes stale; the gate is a question), deleting the shipped parity features to match the line (they work — the line is about what we ADD), folding into 0107 as an addendum (orthogonal line, and burying it makes it as skippable as the "recommend deferring" notes were) |
| [0150](./docs/notes/0150-a-routing-rule-is-learned-from-a-correction.md) | **A routing rule is learned from a correction, never inferred**: implements the half of ADR 0077 it deferred ("learning rules implicitly from confirm/reject — deferred"), under ADR 0097 ("only the user decides which group an email lands in") and ADR 0149's sanctioned lane. Owner constraint: **no auto-routing — a recipe for security leaks**; nothing reaches a shared group without a per-item confirm. `confirmProposal` returns an optional `routeSuggestion` on `{ ok: true }`, computed **only** from the explicit `targetGroupId` the user passed on THIS confirm (the correction), never the staged fallback, and gated: standalone (no `bundle_id` — ADR 0097 bundles carry no override), `gmail:<threadId>` provenance resolving to a parseable sender host, destination a **shared** group the user still belongs to, and **no `sender_domain` rule already routes that host there** (no nag; a rule pointing the host elsewhere yields `replaces`). Mobile Action Items renders it as a one-tap offer after the confirm ("Always send mail from `pack42.scouting.org` to `Doe Family`?"); Yes calls the existing `POST /me/email/routes` (`setRoutingRule`). A learned rule's ENTIRE effect is that the next proposal from that sender is staged with `target_group_id` set — ADR 0077's confirm ✓ is still required; it never auto-executes/auto-confirms/fans out. Pattern is the **exact sender host** (`from_email.split('@')[1]`), not a guessed registrable domain — narrower is the safe error for a rule the user didn't type; broaden in Settings. **Honesty fix (required)**: the chip strip initialised to "Just me" regardless of a proposal's staged `target_group_id`, so a rule-routed proposal showed "Just me" active while a bare ✓ sent it to the rule's group — the strip now starts on the staged group when visible, so the pre-selection matches where ✓ sends (bare-✓ behaviour unchanged). `email-magic.tsx` docked card has no override strip → no offer (follow-up). Rejected: inferring from confirm/reject STATISTICS (0077 rejected implicit learning; this is one hand-made correction = one offer), auto-applying a learned rule to already-pending proposals (rewriting a decision under the user), a "don't ask again" flag (suppression + resolved threads don't re-propose already cover it), a public-suffix list (a dependency + table to widen what one Settings edit widens — and a too-broad auto-created routing rule is the exact failure this ADR guards) |
| [0151](./docs/notes/0151-a-managed-persona-can-be-given-work.md) | **A managed persona can be given work** (built 2026-09-10): family-profile Phase 3b. A managed persona (a kid, a grandparent) cannot be the subject of a task or calendar event because `tasks.assignee_id` / `events.assignee_id` are `text REFERENCES users(id)` and every reader (`resolveAssignee`, `isTrusted`, `ownerOf`, `displayNameFor`) assumes a real account — the same `users`-FK wall Phase 2 hit with `memories.persona_id`. Fix mirrors Phase 2's: a parallel FK-free **`assignee_persona_id text REFERENCES personas(id) ON DELETE SET NULL`** on both tables (migration `074`), mutually exclusive with `assignee_id` (service-enforced, no CHECK), no backfill. Trust is **indirected through `personas.managed_by`** — `isTrustedForPersona(db, personaId, sender, scope)` (in `trust.service.ts`, own `managed_by` lookup) = `sender === managed_by || isTrusted(managed_by, sender, scope)`, returns `{trusted, managedBy}` so a refusal throws `TrustError(scope, sender, managedBy)` and the existing executor path opens a 0026 permission request against the adult. A persona's calendar item is **owned by `managed_by`** (`createEvent` writes `owner_user_id = managed_by` explicitly, so `ownerOf` is untouched) and its stored title is prefixed `<persona> — <title>` **at create, not push-only** (`applyRemoteToLocal` round-trips `title` from Google, so a push-only prefix would be stripped/doubled); no fan-out. A new `resolveAssigneeTarget` in `bot/executor.ts` (member match first, managed-persona fallback) feeds `create_task` / `create_event` / `update_task`; `groupRosterBlock` lists managed personas. REST task/event routes stay user-only (mobile assign-to-persona is a fast-follow). Out of scope: a persona getting its own login, RSVP/`message_user` subject, auto-promotion on kid signup. Rejected: polymorphic `assignee_kind` + ref (bigger blast radius, loses a constraint that catches real bugs on the common path), a shadow `users` row per persona (heavy identity object that leaks into auth/sessions/contact-discovery), Chord-only persona events (the adult wouldn't see them where they look), a `trusted_senders` row keyed on a persona (a second trust surface `managed_by` already answers), widening `resolveAssignee` in place (~8 unrelated callers) |
| [0153](./docs/notes/0153-an-external-action-link-is-family-aware.md) | **An external-action link is family-aware**: `suggest_external_action` (ADR 0039) already does the right thing — a tappable deep-link card, no transaction (ADR 0107). Two fixes. **(1) A named restaurant links to the place, not a search.** `buildRestaurant` hardcoded OpenTable, which 0-results for a Resy/SevenRooms/Reserve-with-Google-only place — a dead card reads as Chord's mistake (ADR 0102/0135/0138). Now: `restaurantName` present → a Google Maps place URL, whose "Reserve a table" button routes to whatever system that restaurant actually uses (no provider-selector field — the place page *is* the router); no name → the OpenTable search keeps its covers/date filters for genuine discovery. **(2) `buildFlight` carries the query** — was `q=Flights from BOS` with destination + dates dropped; now a natural-language `q=` Google Flights parses. **(3) `enrichExternalActionQuery`** (new `bot/external-actions-enrich.ts`) — an async step in the executor's `suggest_external_action` case, before the (still pure) `buildExternalAction`, filling **only blank** fields from the **triggering group** (ADR 0032 — no cross-group reads): `partySize` ← persona roster count (≥2, cap 20; personal 1:1 has no roster so it no-ops); flight `origin` ← a `home_airport` memory (household first, else the asker's own); `destination`/`departDate`/`returnDate`/`city` ← the soonest open `trips` row (`end_date >= today`); `restaurantName` ← a single strong token match in the group's saved places (`saves` kind `place`) → canonical title. Never overrides a supplied field; best-effort (any throw returns the query untouched). The shared-artifact gate (ADR 0149) is met downstream: tap → book → confirmation email → the magics pipeline (ADR 0074/0088/0100) makes the calendar event / trip leg. Rejected: a `provider` enum on the query (puts a routing call on the model that the restaurant's Google listing already answers), scraping the restaurant page for its booking provider (ADR 0135/0138 — we render the link, never fetch it), the `tfs` base64 Google Flights token (undocumented, brittle), cross-group enrichment (ADR 0032; a wrong-group trip is worse than a blank field), a booking-API integration (Chord answers, the provider transacts), enriching inside `buildExternalAction` (it would need `db` and stop being unit-testable) |
| [0154](./docs/notes/0154-the-settings-surface-is-pruned-to-what-a-family-sets.md) | **The settings surface is pruned to what a family sets**: three cleanups, extends ADR 0078. **(1) "About me" deleted.** The free-text bio's extraction leak was already closed (`extractMemoriesFromAboutMe` hard-forced every fact to the author's private personal group; person proposals only to their People screen), leaving pure redundancy with "About this family" (shared facts), the People screen (private people), and telling the bot in the 1:1. Removed the Settings disclosure + the To Do's "About you" card + `PUT /me/about-me` + the extractor + `ABOUT_ME_RELATION_KEYS` + the raw-text system-prompt block + kernel surface (`SetAboutMeRequest`/`API.meAboutMe`/`MeSettings.aboutMe`). **`users.about_me` kept dormant** — dropping it needs a migration and orphans real typed text; a nullable column nobody reads is inert (follow-up may null it or migrate to persona memories). **(2) Slack/Discord dropped from the connector UI + deploy-wide env** — Settings card + per-group bridge picker offer Telegram only; `platformList` = telegram + any platform with an existing binding (a legacy bridge stays disconnectable). `SLACK_BOT_TOKEN`/`SLACK_SIGNING_SECRET`/`DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` removed from `env.ts` + `example.env`; `registerEnabledConnectors` no longer wires them. Connector classes, routes, `StartPairingRequest` enum, inbound-pipeline branches, and `makeConnector`'s BYO Discord path (ADR 0025) left in place — fuller removal is its own change; ops runbooks get a deprecation banner. **(3) Per-group "Mirror all messages" switch** — `groups.mirror_inbound` (ADR 0055) was set by connection method (bot-added-to-group → on; bridge-after-the-fact → off) and frozen, with `PATCH /groups/:id {mirrorInbound}` existing but unused. A membership-gated toggle in group settings (shown when the group has ≥1 bridge), optimistic like the mute switch beside it. **No global default** — a small household sets this once per bridged group; a user-level column is plumbing for a knob nobody re-tunes. Rejected: gating the About-Me context block to personal turns instead of deleting the field (fixes the residual exposure, keeps the redundancy), dropping `about_me` now (destroys data, no sign-off), full Slack/Discord server removal in this change (large, orthogonal), a global mirror default, a per-group-vs-blanket mode slider (unusual control; a plain switch matches the screen) |
| [0165](./docs/notes/0165-one-bot-turn-per-group-at-a-time.md) | **One bot turn per group at a time**: prod — three "add to the trader joes list" messages in a shared group within seconds; only the first item landed, the bot said it added all three. `reactToMessage` is fire-and-forget (the POST returns before the turn runs) with **no per-group serialisation**, so the three `runBotTurn`s ran concurrently, and `resolveOrCreateListByName` is a check-then-create (`SELECT` names → if none, `INSERT`) with **no lock and no unique constraint** on `lists(group_id, <name key>)`. All three `SELECT`s ran before any `INSERT` committed → three lists all named "Trader Joes", one item each. Each turn's `addItem` returned `created: true`, so "added it" was truthful *for that fork*. Two layers: **(1)** `reactToMessage` chains a group's turns onto a per-`groupId` promise tail (`groupTurnTails`, in-memory + per-node like the follow-up window / SSE hub, ADR 0002) — same-group turns run in arrival order, cross-group turns still concurrent; the tail slot is cleared only by the turn still holding it so a queued-behind turn is never orphaned. **(2)** `resolveOrCreateListByName` runs inside `db.transaction()` and takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext(groupId), hashtext(listNameKey(name)))` before the SELECT — `_xact_` is the only advisory-lock form safe under transaction-mode pooling (ADR 0038/0137), two-int form because a NUL text separator is rejected (ADR 0126) and a printable one collides across the split. Layer 1 covers the class (task fold ADR 0095, save dedup) on one node; layer 2 is defence in depth for the connector path, a future second node, and any non-`reactToMessage` caller. No schema change. Rejected: a unique index on a `listNameKey` generated column (the JS stemmer would have to be ported to SQL and kept byte-identical forever — the ADR 0085/0106 drift trap); a global turn queue (head-of-line blocking across families); debounce/coalesce (changes product behaviour to hide a correctness bug). Consequence: a slow turn now delays that group's next reply — the intended trade, matching ADR 0051's sequential mental model. Migration `073` re-runs `mergeDuplicateLists` to heal the forks the race already made (idempotent, no-op on a fresh DB) |

| [0108](./docs/notes/0108-a-folder-is-a-predicate-not-a-sync.md) | **A folder is a predicate, not a sync**: ADR 0107 shipped compose, and a sent copy mirrors as `SENT` with no `INBOX` — `aggregateThread` reads absent-INBOX as archived, and ADR 0073 hid archived mail from **every** listing, so **you could send a message you could not find** (and everything you had archived in Gmail was equally unreachable, in the lists AND in both search paths). The tempting reading — we mirror the inbox, other folders need another sync — is wrong, and checking it is what kept this small: the backfill lists **all mail** (`newer_than:Nd`, no `in:inbox`) and an archived thread keeps its row and merely loses INBOX, so those threads were in the mirror all along behind ONE where-clause. So a folder is a PREDICATE over the label set (`email/folders.ts`): inbox = `is_archived=0`, sent = SENT not TRASH, archive = archived + not SENT + not TRASH — no column, no second sync, no backfill, nothing that can drift from the labels. `listThreads` **defaults to `inbox`** so tiles/categories/decisions are untouched, and `?folder=` takes ADR 0086's posture (unknown value — inbox, not 400). Excluding SENT from archive is load-bearing (a reply you sent is not a thread you dealt with). **Search stops filtering by folder entirely** — both paths exclude only TRASH: this narrows 0073 on the READING surfaces only (every AI-layer query keeps `is_archived=0`), because search is the surface you reach for exactly when you don't know where a message went. Drafts + Trash stay stubs for reasons, not time — no draft store exists, and trashed rows are reaped by 0073's reverse-delete, so a Trash folder would be right one day and empty the next (empty reads as "nothing was deleted"); SPAM gets no predicate since junk is never mirrored. **Coverage is stated, not implied**: only Gmail mirrors a whole mailbox, so Outlook/IMAP Sent holds just what Chord sent — the banner says so rather than let a short list read as lost mail (0107's rule pointed the other way). One existing test FLIPPED rather than being deleted. Rejected: a `folder` column (a second source of truth that can disagree with the labels), a separate Sent sync (the assumption that made this look like a feature), folders without the search change (only helps someone who thinks to open Sent first), and Trash as a fourth folder (nearly free to build, which is the trap) |

| [0110](./docs/notes/0110-the-mirror-was-buying-half-a-message.md) | **The mirror was buying half a message**: `email_threads.search_tsv` (038) indexes subject + snippet + sender, and a snippet is ~100 chars, so everything an email actually SAYS — the flight number, the amount, the one sentence you remember — was unfindable on both surfaces. Cause was one argument: the Gmail sync fetched `format=metadata`, `normalizeMessage` called `extractBodies` on a payload with nothing in it, stored `null`, and bodies arrived ONLY when the user opened a thread. So body search was structurally impossible, not merely unbuilt — and indexing what we had would have covered 'threads you already opened' with the user unable to tell which, the search-shaped version of the ADR 0102/0107 dishonesty. Two findings settled it: **Outlook and IMAP already store bodies at sync** (lazy bodies were a Gmail-path artifact, not a design), and **a body is nearly free** — `messages.get` is one call and one quota charge whether you ask for `metadata` or `full`, so the sync was paying per message and taking half of what it paid for. Fix: all five `'metadata'` fetches (backfill, incremental add, spam rescue, primary + attached Gmail) become `'full'` via **`normalizeFullMessage`** — which exists because on a full payload 'no attachment parts' honestly means NONE and can be stored as `'[]'`, while on metadata the same emptiness means 'nobody looked' (the `ics_text = ''` distinction from ADR 0080). Migration `048` adds a generated tsv + GIN on `email_messages`, where **`left(body_text, 200000)` is load-bearing**: `to_tsvector` RAISES past the 1MB tsvector limit and this is a GENERATED column, so the error would land on INSERT inside the sync and wedge that mailbox forever — exactly how 0106 happened. **HTML-only mail gets a derived text body** (`htmlToPlainText`) since `extractBodies` only read `text/plain` and most receipts/newsletters have none — they would have stored null and dropped back out of the index, in exactly the mail people search; indexing raw HTML instead would make `style`/`table` match half the mailbox. Both search paths gain the body arm through ONE shared helper (`email/message-search.ts`), since ADR 0085 makes them promise identical matching and two copies of a predicate is how that stops being true; the literal arm matters MORE in a body than in a subject (a confirmation code is unindexable as a token and lives there), and the matching body feeds `ts_headline` so a body-only hit doesn't render unbolded like a mis-hit. `backfillMessageBodies` drains 40/tick newest-first for pre-existing rows and marks a provider-deleted message with an **empty** body, not null (null = 'never asked'; without the distinction the pass re-asks the same dead rows forever and never reaches a live one) — and it runs ABOVE the ADR 0079 gate, because the sentence justifying that gate (*it's their mail*) is the same one that says an unentitled user still gets to find their own message. Consequences: opening a thread now fetches nothing, and **the AI passes see a body for the first time** — magics and person extraction both prefer a message with `body_text` and on an unopened Gmail mailbox that was ALWAYS null, so they have been extracting from a subject line and 100 chars of preview (prompt still caps at 2000 chars, so the token cost is bounded). Rejected: indexing the bodies we happen to have (invisible coverage), a second pass after sync (doubles `messages.get` to buy what the first call returns free), longer snippets (the provider decides, and no preview length finds a code at the bottom of a receipt), a separate bodies table (a join on the hottest read), and truncating STORED bodies (loses the reader's text to keep the index tidy) |

| [0111](./docs/notes/0111-a-file-is-sent-by-id-not-by-bytes.md) | **A file is sent by id, not by bytes**: compose/reply could send text only — a real gap (half of what a family emails is a photo of a form) and a hard blocker for **forward**, since a forward that silently drops the attachment is the ADR 0107 failure. The obvious wire shape is a multipart upload to the send endpoint; instead the request carries **Chord attachment ids**, the file going in through the attachment route that already exists and a device pick uploading FIRST. One shape covers 'send the itinerary you have' and 'send this photo', the send endpoint never learns multipart, and the consequence is the argument: a file sent this way **stays** — an ordinary searchable re-sendable attachment rather than a byte stream that lived for one request. Picked files land in the **personal** group, where ADR 0071 already puts files arriving BY email. **An id is not permission**: `getAttachment` has NO scope check, so a request-supplied id resolved straight through it would let anyone mail themselves any file in the system by guessing — every id goes through `loadOutgoingAttachments` (membership or personal ownership), which is ONE helper because three routes × four provider paths is four chances to forget; absent and forbidden return **the same sentence**, since telling them apart is the reconnaissance half of that attack. **Size is refused, never truncated**, and caps are **per provider** (Graph inline `fileAttachment` ~3MB vs Gmail's tens of MB) — one shared floor would refuse on Gmail a file Gmail would take, to keep a single number true for Outlook. Over the limit is a **400 before anything is sent**; same for a row whose blob is gone (a zero-byte attachment is the same failure in miniature). MIME becomes `multipart/mixed` with the existing body as the first part, and is **byte-for-byte unchanged without files** since every send goes through that builder. Two details are correctness, not tidiness, and both have tests: **base64 wraps at 76 chars** (RFC 2045 — some servers reject one long line) and **CR/LF are stripped from filenames** (a filename carrying one ends the `Content-Disposition` line and the rest reads as headers — header injection through a file picker). Rejected: multipart-to-send (the file vanishes after sending), a shared cap, drop-oversize-and-warn (a warning does not un-send an incomplete message), and uploading into the thread's group (mail is personal — ADR 0084) |

| [0112](./docs/notes/0112-a-forward-is-a-new-conversation.md) | **A forward is a new conversation**: the last of the three ways a person sends mail, and it sat behind ADR 0111 because passing on the attachment IS the forward — one arriving without the file has failed in the one way the sender cannot see. Shape question was variant-of-reply vs its own thing; **its own thing, because the audience is new**, and everything follows: **no threading headers** (`In-Reply-To` pointing at a message the recipient cannot see does nothing), the original **quoted** under the twenty-year-old `---------- Forwarded message ----------` block rather than appended to, **plain text from `body_text`** (a re-rendered HTML quote would silently differ from what the sender read — and ADR 0110 means that column now exists for HTML-only mail too), its **own screen** with the recipient field focused and the note empty (a reply is the reverse), and the **note optional** since "here, look at this" is the whole message most of the time. Forwards the **newest** message: "forward this email" means the thing on screen, and a per-message control is a bigger UI than anyone has asked for. **A file we cannot fetch stops the send** — originals are pulled from the provider at forward time (never stored, ADR 0080), and a missing one refuses the whole forward rather than thinning it; BOTH failure shapes are caught, since `fetchEmailAttachmentData` returns null for an unaddressable part but **throws** on a provider 404 — only the null case was handled at first and the test caught it, a raw error would have hit the route as a 500 where the sender needed a sentence (ADR 0102). `includeAttachments: false` is the sender's way out, a switch, never a silent decision. `checkOutgoingSize` had to come OUT of `loadOutgoingAttachments`: forward's files never pass through the loader, and a limit enforced on one of two paths is not a limit. Consequence: a forward is not mirrored into the thread (it is not part of it) — Gmail shows it in Sent next sync, Outlook/IMAP do not, the same partial-Sent the ADR 0108 banner already states. Rejected: forward-as-a-reply-mode (the recipient field is the first thing a forward needs and the last a reply does), keeping threading headers (tidy for the sender, confusing for a recipient referencing mail they never got), quoting the HTML, and send-what-we-can-and-warn (the ADR 0107 failure with extra steps). **Addendum 2026-10-27**: the composer shows the forwarded email under the note - header, full body, files (struck through when not included) - from `GET /me/email/threads/:id/forward/preview`, which reads the same `loadForwardSource` as the send and builds with the same kernel `forwardHeaderLines`/`buildForwardBody` (`email-forward.ts`), so what is shown is what goes (tested against the sent MIME). The quoted body is now the visible text (`bodyForModel`: rendered HTML, else the plain part; ADR 0268), not `body_text`; still plain text, shown through `EmailBody` with no HTML/images/links; Date line readable in the forwarder's zone (`forwardDateLabel`). **Forward passes on the message selected in the thread** (opened last, else the newest) via optional `messageId` on request and preview; an id not in the thread = 404, never the newest. A message nobody opened is fetched first (`getThread`/`getAccountThread`); before, it was forwarded with an empty body and no files. Note grows with its text in a `KeyboardScrollView`. A forward draft stores its message (`email_drafts.forward_message_id`, migration `132`, null = newest; omitted on save = unchanged); the outbox forwards it and the ADR 0303 Drafts-folder copy quotes it (`forwardDraftContent`, same loader). Chat `forward_email` card shows the email (sender, date, opening text, files) built by `forwardPreview` at staging and pins that message (hidden fields, overwritten at staging); description gives "forward from <sender> to <person>" = `search` `from`, newest hit |

| [0113](./docs/notes/0113-unsent-mail-is-ours.md) | **Unsent mail is ours**: every composer lost its contents on the way out — type half a reply, get a phone call, gone. ADR 0108 disabled the Drafts folder because *we keep no draft store*; that half is now fixed, while its OTHER reason still holds (listing the PROVIDER's drafts means reading a mailbox we don't mirror). So the question was never drafts-or-not but **whose**: a draft is an unfinished ANSWER, which ADR 0107 puts on our side. Decided independently by mechanism — autosave must be cheap, and a local row can be rewritten every 1.5s without a network round trip, a rate limit, or Gmail's permission. **Debounced, not per-keystroke** (a request per character) and **not only on unmount** (which loses the case autosave exists for, the app being KILLED rather than left); an **empty draft is never written**, or the list fills with blanks nobody wrote. The upsert rule makes it idempotent: **one draft per (user, kind, thread)** enforced by a partial unique index, not hopeful code, so a reply typed across two sittings is ONE draft and the composer finds it from the thread alone having forgotten the id; a save whose row was deleted in another window **falls through to an insert** rather than throwing at someone mid-sentence. `draftId` rides the send/reply/forward request and is cleared **server-side after the provider took it** — a client-side cleanup leaves a draft of an already-sent message whenever that second call fails, and deleting an absent draft is a success (a send must never fail on its own tidying). The folder **says what it holds** ("Drafts you started in Chord. Anything you began in your mail app stays there.") — the same debt 0108's Sent folder pays, since a list quietly holding half of what "Drafts" means is worse than no list. Resuming a reply/forward opens the **thread**, so a reply is written in one place, not two. Rejected: Gmail's drafts API (a round trip per pause, rate-limited, offline-hostile, and three implementations for four providers — Outlook has its own model, IMAP's `\Draft` is in a folder we don't sync), AsyncStorage (loses the draft across devices, and the web client can't list it), one row per save, and requiring a subject/body (loses a draft that is only recipients and an attachment, a real state on the way to sending a photo) |

| [0114](./docs/notes/0114-suggestions-come-from-your-own-mail.md) | **Suggestions come from your own mail**: recipient autocomplete, where the only real question is the source — and it is a privacy question, not a data one, because a field that answers a two-letter prefix with matching addresses **is a directory**. Candidates come **only** from the user's own mail and own `people` rows, the same rule ADR 0065 put on `resolveContactToUser` (strictly sender-scoped, never a global user search): every row returned is one they have already seen in their own mailbox. Three sources merged by address, weighted — a To-line address counts **double** a sender's (mail arrives unbidden; an address you chose was chosen), and a `people.email` counts 25 (someone the user told us about). **Frequency, not recency**: ranking on recency puts yesterday's newsletter above the person you mail weekly, and the one you mailed an hour ago is already on screen — recency stays as the tiebreaker. Two-character floor (below that a prefix matches most of a mailbox: a slow scan for a list nobody can use, the same floor ADR 0085 put on the literal search arm), and the sender arm matches `from_name` too, since people search for "chen" not an address they never memorised. Client fetches per **token** ("ruth@x.com, ch" asks about `ch`) with sequenced requests so a stale response can't repopulate a list already typed past. Rejected: a global address index (a directory reachable by prefix from a text field), device contacts (ADR 0065 deliberately never uploads raw ones), recency-only, and a materialised contacts table (a second copy of what the mailbox knows, needing invalidation nobody would write) **Addendum 2026-10-27**: on iPhone/Android the field also suggests the phone's contacts, matched ON the device (`suggestDeviceRecipients` / `mergeRecipientSuggestions` in kernel `device-contact.ts`, server rows first, tagged Contacts; `mobile/src/device-contacts.ts` reads Emails only, 5-min memory cache, permission asked once per run on field focus) - nothing from the address book reaches the server (0065 holds); web keeps mail-only |

| [0115](./docs/notes/0115-a-queued-message-is-a-draft-with-a-time-on-it.md) | **A queued message is a draft with a time on it**: undo send, and the tempting build — a timer in the composer, no server change — is wrong for one reason: **an app killed during the window never sends at all and the sender is never told**. They tapped Send; nothing said otherwise. That is the ADR 0107 failure (a thing we did not do, presented as done) moved onto the OUTBOUND path, where it is worse. So the hold is server-side, and `email_drafts` gains `send_after` — a model that earns its keep three times: **undo is clearing one timestamp** (which is also what undo MEANS to the user: put it back where I was writing it), **a failed send does the same thing plus `last_error`** so a message that cannot go lands back in Drafts with the reason rather than vanishing, and **the content is already there** (recipients, body, attachment IDS per ADR 0111, all autosaved before Send was tapped — an outbox table would be a second copy of every column). A row with `send_after` set is **absent from the Drafts list**: it is on its way out, not something to edit. Attachments resolve at **send** time, not queue time (a file deleted during the window must fail loudly, not send captured bytes). The row is **claimed before the provider is called** — two overlapping ticks would otherwise send twice, and a duplicate email is not something an apology fixes. **No retry**: ADR 0103 had a retry loop re-push the same failure for two months, and the same loop outbound mails the same thing five times when a transient error clears. A losing undo returns **409, not 404**, so the client says "already sent" instead of implying it stopped something. The countdown is **cosmetic** (the deadline is the server's; a bar that looks like it drives invites someone to make it drive), and `EMAIL_UNDO_SECONDS=0` sends immediately with no Undo button rather than one that does nothing. Own 2s tick, since the 15s proactive scheduler would turn an 8s window into 8-23s. Rejected: the client timer, a separate outbox table, retrying, Gmail's own undo (a client feature, no API), and a per-user setting (needs a Settings surface this does not otherwise need) |

| [0116](./docs/notes/0116-parity-is-what-a-mailbox-can-do.md) | **Parity is what a mailbox can do**: four mailbox kinds and a growing set of features that quietly meant GMAIL's features — each arrived for the provider it was easiest on, with a comment promising the others later, and the comments outlived their reasons. The rule: parity is about what a mailbox can **DO**, NOT about flattening limits — holding Gmail's 24MB attachment to Graph's ~3MB would not be parity, it would be constraining one provider by another's ceiling, so ADR 0111's per-provider caps stay per-provider. Three real gaps, found by reading: **(1) Sent was inbox-only on Outlook and IMAP** (Graph synced `mailFolders/inbox/messages/delta`, IMAP opened `INBOX`), so mail sent from any other client did not exist in Chord — now a second Graph delta stream on `sentitems` and an IMAP pass over the `\Sent` special-use box, stored as SENT-without-INBOX so ADR 0108's folder predicates work unchanged. Both cursors were ALREADY JSON blobs, so this extends a shape rather than needing a migration, and an old cursor still parses (inbox keeps its position, Sent starts fresh). Two load-bearing details: the **mock's `deltaPage` ignored the folder argument**, so both streams would have returned the same messages and imported everything twice while looking like it worked (a test against that mock is worse than no test); and **no Sent folder is a real state**, not an error — `listSentWindow` returns null rather than guessing a mailbox name and syncing somebody's Drafts into their Sent, and a Sent folder we cannot read must not fail an inbox sync that already succeeded. Consequence: **ADR 0108's Sent banner loses its apology** (it said "your other mailboxes only mirror what Chord sent" because that was true; it isn't now, so the sentence goes rather than being reworded). **(2) Attachment import was Gmail-only for a reason that had STOPPED being true** — the code claimed attached accounts have no fetchable parts, but the part list lives on the row since ADR 0110 and the bytes come from `fetchEmailAttachmentData`, which has dispatched per provider since the download route needed it (ADR 0112 already used it for forward). Widening it **deleted** code; a stale constraint is worth re-reading before working around it. **(3) Body backfill skipped secondary Gmail** — moved out of `gmail.service.ts` into `email/body-backfill.ts`, because reaching an attached account's API imports `gmail-account.service` which imports `gmail.service` back (the cycle `attachment-fetch.ts` sits above). Outlook/IMAP are absent from it ON PURPOSE: both store bodies inline at sync. Still deliberately asymmetric: **labels** — ADR 0107 already put managing on the provider's side, Outlook categories and IMAP keywords are the equivalents if revisited, and the route 422s honestly meanwhile. Rejected: one shared attachment cap (the trap the parity framing invites), `/me/messages/delta` for Outlook (drags in Deleted Items, Junk and Drafts), guessing a Sent folder beyond the well-known names, and leaving import Gmail-only until asked |

| [0117](./docs/notes/0117-new-mail-can-reach-the-phone.md) | **New mail can reach the phone**: mail arrived and nothing told you. The audit filed this under freshness beside `users.watch`/Graph subscriptions/IDLE, but those are about LATENCY and this was the notification not existing — one line explains it, `if (event !== SSE.message) return;` in the ADR 0054 publisher, and `email.synced` is not `SSE.message`, so email had never pushed on any provider however fast the sync noticed. Done FIRST on purpose: no public webhook, no Pub/Sub, no IDLE management, and identical on all four mailbox kinds — 5b makes it SOONER, this makes it HAPPEN. **Notify from the mirror, not from the sync**: the pass asks the DB what is new rather than reading a return value, which is what makes it provider-agnostic (all three land rows in one table) and which sidesteps a real trap — `EmailSyncResult.inserted` counts UPSERTS, so notifying on it announces mail that arrived last week. `users.email_notified_at` is the high-water mark, with three rules that each earn their place: the **first pass adopts it and stays silent** (a NULL mark means a just-connected mailbox, and announcing its backfill window fires a push per message — how people learn to turn notifications off); the **mark moves even when nobody was notified** (pushes off, quiet hours, no device — else enabling notifications fires a backlog about mail read days ago); and **one push per pass, not per message** (a sync pulling twenty must not buzz twenty times — one thread is named, several become a count plus the newest sender). Skips read, archived and **bulk** mail, ADR 0109's signal used for what it is most useful for: a push per newsletter is indistinguishable from spam to the person holding the phone. **Its own toggle** (`notify_email`, not a reuse of `notify_messages`) since wanting chat but not mail is ordinary, and it respects quiet hours because mail is the category most likely to arrive at 3am. `eligibleTokens` is **extracted** so both callers share one rule — quiet hours is exactly what would drift if reimplemented, and the drift would be invisible until 3am; group membership and the per-group mute stay on the group path, which email has neither of. Runs **above** the ADR 0079 gate (same argument as 0110's backfill: being told your own mail arrived is not the paid layer). Rejected: pushing on the `email.synced` event (fires on every sync, payload of counts), enriching `EmailSyncResult` (puts notification concerns in a type the client uses for a spinner, and still needs every provider to populate it), reusing `notify_messages`, notifying per message (truthful and unusable), and waiting for real push (conflates "the notification is late" with "there is no notification") |

| [0118](./docs/notes/0118-three-ways-to-be-told-one-way-to-react.md) | **Three ways to be told, one way to react**: ADR 0117 made new mail notify; this makes it notify SOON. Each provider offers something different — Gmail `users.watch` —> Pub/Sub —> our URL (7-day expiry, payload carries NO secret), Graph a subscription on the inbox (~3 days, echoes `clientState`), IMAP `IDLE` on a held-open connection (no expiry, the connection IS the auth). Two inbound webhooks with a registration to keep alive, one outbound socket simply held — and that asymmetry is the whole design problem. They **converge before anything provider-specific happens**: each mechanism's only job is to say WHICH MAILBOX, then `wakeMailbox` runs the sync the poll already runs. **None carries mail** (Gmail's has no content, Graph's is a change token, IDLE's is a message count), so every one is a HINT and we re-read regardless — which is what makes a forged notification boring: the worst it buys is an early sync. Load-bearing rule: **push pre-empts polling, never replaces it** — both registrations expire on a days-long timer so one WILL lapse unnoticed, and because the poll still runs that is a latency regression rather than an outage (also the only reason it is defensible to ship something unverifiable here). Coalescing at 2s is not an optimisation: Gmail publishes per history change, so five messages together is five notifications for one sync's work, and a nudge landing mid-sync **re-arms** rather than racing the same cursor. ONE `mail_subscriptions` table because a watch and a subscription are the same object (an external registration with an id and a deadline), keyed by mailbox with `account_id NULL` = primary Gmail; `clientState` is **per subscription** (a leak is worth one mailbox's early sync) and **reused across renewals** (in-flight notifications carry the old one). The two webhooks authenticate differently ON PURPOSE — Graph echoes `clientState`, Gmail's URL carries a token that protects no secret but stops the endpoint being a free way to make us work — and Graph's `validationToken` must come back as **plain text** (ADR 0090's trap: JSON would quote it). Both answer BEFORE working, since Pub/Sub retries on non-2xx and Graph drops a failing subscription. **IMAP IDLE does not contradict ADR 0080**: that ADR's concern was LOGIN RATE (Yahoo throttles on it), and IDLE is one login held open vs one per tick forever — it REDUCES the metric 0080 protected; the new cost is a long-lived resource, so it is capped, reconnects with doubling backoff, and resets that backoff only on a successful connect. **Unverified end-to-end and ships OFF** (`MAIL_PUSH_ENABLED=false`): no public URL, no Pub/Sub topic, no Azure app here, so what is tested is everything up to the wire (envelope parsing, clientState rejection, renewal windows, coalescing, a sync that throws). Route mounts BEFORE the connector router, which owns `/webhooks/:platform` as a catch-all (ADR 0075's ordering care). Rejected: polling faster (linear in mailboxes — frequency, and it is the login-rate pressure 0080 was about), one deploy-wide clientState, trusting the notification's `historyId` (lets a forged one steer our cursor to save a round trip), letting push replace polling once proven (converts every expiry bug into silence), and deferring IMAP (the asymmetry ADR 0116 just removed) |

| [0119](./docs/notes/0119-a-list-that-ends-and-a-row-that-says-where-it-came-from.md) | **A list that ends, and a row that says where it came from**: three small gaps on one surface. The Traditional list showed the newest 50 and stopped — the server had accepted a `cursor` since it was written and no client ever sent one. Pagination is **keyset, not offset**: the cursor is the last row's `last_message_at`, because mail arriving mid-scroll shifts every row down and an `OFFSET 50` then repeats the row that moved or skips the one it displaced (a keyset names a position in the data, not a count from the top — and there is a test that inserts newer mail between two pages). The client dedups on id when appending, since a thread whose newest message lands between the requests can legitimately satisfy both. "Is there more" is inferred from a full page rather than tracked: a short page is the end, one fewer thing for the server to be right about. With ADR 0116 mirroring Outlook and IMAP properly, a merged list finally holds mail from several mailboxes with no way to tell which — so the summary carries `accountId` and `accountEmail` (left join, so no second request or client-side lookup table) and the chip shows the **local part**, which is what tells two of someone's own mailboxes apart in the space a row has. Chip and filter appear **only with >1 mailbox**: otherwise every row carries the same label, which says nothing. The filter needs the literal **`'primary'`** because `account_id IS NULL` is ADR 0080's convention for the Gmail on the user row, and an empty string already means no-filter; an unknown value matches nothing rather than 400ing, the posture ADR 0086 took for `?action=` and 0108 for `?folder=`. Rejected: offset pagination, a server `hasMore` flag (one more thing to keep true across every filter combination), the full address on a row (pushes the subject out), and always showing the chip |

| [0120](./docs/notes/0120-a-remote-image-is-a-read-receipt.md) | **A remote image is a read receipt**: the reader loaded every image, with the sanitizer setting `referrerpolicy` and its own comment admitting "browsers may still load the pixel". Not a small leak — a remote image in marketing mail carries a per-recipient token, so fetching it tells the sender the address is live, when it was opened, how often and roughly from where (the mechanism behind every "they opened it 3 times" sales tool). **Block by default, allow per sender.** The default IS the decision: Gmail proxies images and Apple preloads through a relay, both of which cost infrastructure we do not have, so without a proxy the honest choices are load-and-be-tracked or block-and-look-plainer — and only one of those does not silently spend the reader's privacy. `cid:` is NOT blocked (those are the message's own attachments; blocking them hides the photo someone sent and stops no tracker). **The `url()` hole**: rewriting `src` is the obvious half and is not enough — `<td style="background-image:url(...)">` fetches on render exactly as an `<img>` does, and inline `style` is allowed on most tags, so `stripRemoteCssUrls` runs on EVERY tag's style; it has its own tests for quoted, unquoted, spaced, protocol-relative and uppercase `URL(`. The helpers live in **kernel/** rather than beside the renderer because `mobile/` has no test harness, and security-relevant logic that cannot be tested where it lives should move somewhere it can be. An **allow list keyed on the sender** (not a block list — the empty table is the safe state; not the thread — agreeing to one newsletter's images is a decision about that sender), stored server-side so it holds across devices, resolved into `EmailThreadDetail.imagesAllowed` on read, and case-insensitive because senders are not. The block is **visible and reversible in one tap**: one banner per THREAD ("3 images not loaded — Show — Always"), counted across messages or a five-message thread offers the same decision five times. Rejected: keeping `referrerpolicy` (hides who is asking, still confirms the address), proxying (the best answer for the reader, but a service to run/cache/secure/pay for, and it makes us the party fetching arbitrary URLs — revisit, do not block on), blocking only known trackers (a list wrong the day a sender changes domain, and any per-recipient URL is one), and a single global switch (the answer genuinely differs per sender) |

| [0121](./docs/notes/0121-a-thread-should-not-repeat-itself.md) | **A thread should not repeat itself**: a five-message thread rendered five full messages, each ending with a quoted copy of the ones above — the last card held the whole thread a second time. Fold the quoted history, fold the older messages, leave the newest open. The **html split runs on the RAW body, before the sanitizer**, because what identifies a quote container is the class the sender's client put there (`gmail_quote`/`yahoo_quoted`/`moz-cite-prefix`/`divRplyFwdMsg`) and `class` is not in the allowed-attribute list — sanitize first and only a bare `<blockquote>` survives, which is Gmail but not Outlook; the two halves are left with unbalanced tags on purpose (the sanitizer closes them next). Text side reads attributions (incl. the two-line form Gmail wraps into), `-----Original Message-----`, the underscore rule, an Outlook `From:`/`Sent:`/`To:` block, or a run of `>` lines.
