Two rules keep the guess honest: **one `>` line is not a quote** (that is someone quoting a phrase), and **a split leaving nothing visible collapses nothing** — top-posted mail is common and folding it renders a blank message, so every uncertain case resolves to show everything. Helpers live in **kernel/** for the ADR 0120 reason (`mobile/` has no test harness) with 15 cases pinned. A folded message shows sender/date/paperclip/preview — **the fold shows what is behind it**, which is what separates folding from hiding — and an explicit tap outranks the default both ways. **Print on web, Share on native**: `printableThreadHtml` builds a standalone document (no `<link>`, no `<script>`) from **the same sanitized html on screen**, so printing cannot become the request ADR 0120 prevented; its param is `sanitizedHtml` because it passes markup through unescaped by definition, everything around it escaped. Native print needs `expo-print` = a new native module and build, so the button says **Share** rather than opening a share sheet under a Print label — two honest verbs beat one true on one platform. Rejected: splitting at sync (freezes the heuristic at arrival; improving it later means a backfill), sanitize-then-split (loses exactly the Outlook/Yahoo cases), first-paragraph-only collapse (compounds the guess), adding `expo-print` |

| [0122](./docs/notes/0122-unsubscribe-is-three-different-promises.md) | **Unsubscribe is three different promises**: `List-Unsubscribe` has been captured since ADR 0109 as a bulk signal and never offered to the user. It looks like one capability and is three, differing in the only way that matters — what is TRUE after the tap: https + `List-Unsubscribe-Post` (RFC 8058) = POST and it is done ("Unsubscribed"); `mailto:` = we send the request but cannot observe whether the list honours it ("Request sent"); https alone = a page for a human (we open it and claim nothing). Collapsing them into one "Unsubscribed" toast is the ADR 0102 failure in a new place. **One-click needs BOTH headers** — an https URI alone is a link, and POSTing anyway is a guess about someone else's endpoint (RFC 8058 exists to say the endpoint accepts an unattended POST); `http://` is never one-click. A **mailto beats a link** when both are offered, sent from **the account that received the mail** (a list keys on the subscribed address, so the primary mailbox silently fails). The URL came out of an email, so the POST goes through the **same SSRF guard** as the ADR 0050 calendar fetch — which moved to `utils/ssrf.ts` on gaining its second caller, one guard imported rather than a copy that drifts (a test proves a URL resolving to `10.0.0.5` never reaches `fetch`). Gmail/IMAP carry headers at sync for free; **Graph's `internetMessageHeaders` rides the thread-open fetch, NOT the delta** (every header of every message on the hot path, to answer a question only asked about an opened thread) — which makes an *unobserved* header different from an *absent* one, so the upsert now writes `list_unsubscribe` only when the provider supplied it (`undefined` leaves it alone, like `body_excerpt`), else every delta tick wipes what the open discovered. `email_unsubscribes` records (user, sender, kind, when) — it keeps the button honest on a second visit and is the first real signal for the bulk-mail gate. Rejected: POSTing any https URI, link-only (useless in exactly the case the standard designed away), reporting every kind as "Unsubscribed", headers on the delta, and auto-unsubscribing from unread bulk senders (decides on someone's behalf that a relationship is over) |

| [0123](./docs/notes/0123-a-rule-says-where-mail-goes.md) | **A rule says where mail goes, not what happens to it**: the audit asked for a "filters/rules editor", and the obvious reading is wrong. In every other client "filters" means match-then-**archive/label/mark-read/delete** — all of which are FILING, which ADR 0107 gave to the provider on purpose (a filter that auto-archives is a second writer racing Gmail's own filters over the same mailbox, and the loser is whoever looks last). So Settings edits the ADR 0077 **routing** rules and only those: one question, *which group does this mail belong to*. The **kind is shown, not asked** (`sender_domain` vs `category` is inferred from the pattern; making the user pick between two words that mean nothing without the schema buys nothing, and the fix for a wrong inference is a different pattern either way). **Every write returns the whole list**, so the client replaces rather than patches — the set is bounded by how many groups a person has, and a client-side merge is four lines that can drift from what the server did. Membership is enforced **on the way in**, which is new: the bot path resolved a group from a name it had just listed for that user, so it never handed over an id the user could pick, and a client-supplied `targetGroupId` is exactly the case the write-side check exists for. Rejected: a full filter editor (violates 0107, duplicates the provider worse, races it), letting the user choose the kind, and leaving it bot-only (the user cannot answer "what rules do I have" without a conversation) |

| [0124](./docs/notes/0124-the-vacation-responder-is-ours.md) | **The vacation responder is ours**: the most provider-native-looking item on the list, and going native means a **new OAuth scope** on two providers (`gmail.settings.basic`, `MailboxSettings.ReadWrite`) — a re-consent prompt for every existing user — three implementations with three behaviours, and IMAP still unserved (Sieve is a different protocol). That last part settles it: a *parity* feature that structurally cannot reach one of the four mailbox kinds is not parity (ADR 0116), and an auto-reply is a REPLY, which ADR 0107 puts on our side. So Chord sends them, off the mirror in `afterSync` beside ADR 0117's notifier — one implementation, no new consent, identical on all three. RFC 3834 is mostly a list of things never to answer, so `autoReplySkipReason` (kernel, tested) refuses self / already-automatic / list mail (`Precedence`, `List-Unsubscribe`, ADR 0109 `bulk`) / no-reply + MAILER-DAEMON / **mail not addressed to you** (a bcc or list copy: answering tells a room of strangers) / a sender already told — and returns the REASON, since "why did nobody get my auto-reply" is otherwise unanswerable. **One reply per sender per holiday IS the loop guard**: `Auto-Submitted` is deliberately not captured, on ADR 0109's own precedent (a signal that can only corroborate a decision already made changes no outcome) — the cap bounds any exchange with a non-compliant responder at ONE message, which is the storm the RFC exists to prevent; the kernel function takes the header today so capturing it later is a drop-in. Turning it ON starts a fresh holiday (`vacation_since` bounds what gets answered, else switching on replies to a backfill window of already-read mail; the told-list clears, else a second trip tells nobody). **An auto-reply is not reading** — `sendReply` marks its thread read because a person replying HAS read it, and nobody read this one; marking it read hides the thread from the decision queue and the 0117 notification, so all four reply paths gained `markRead` (default true). The sender is **claimed before the provider is called** (ADR 0115's rule: a failed send costs one reply, never four copies of "I'm away"), and `MAX_PER_TICK` is a burst cap — a full inbox answered at once looks like a mail storm to every receiving server. Consequence stated plainly: replies ride the sync, so **if Chord is not syncing, no reply goes out** — a provider-side responder keeps working when we are down. Rejected: native everywhere, native-plus-IMAP-fallback (the scopes AND the fallback AND a behaviour difference by mailbox), capturing `Auto-Submitted` first, and replying to everything (how an auto-responder gets a domain blocked) |

| [0125](./docs/notes/0125-a-label-is-not-inside-a-folder.md) | **A label is not inside a folder**: two audit rows (list labels, browse them) that look like they need a provider call per mailbox — and the list does not. Every provider lands its label set in the same `gmail_label_ids` column, so ONE `jsonb_array_elements_text` over the user's threads answers for all of them, with no per-provider branch and no way for the list to disagree with what browsing finds. Outlook and IMAP mirror only synthetic INBOX/UNREAD/SENT, so their list is honestly **short** and the sheet says why (ADR 0108's Sent-banner posture: state the coverage, never let a short list read as loss). Gmail ids are opaque, so names come from `labels.list` — Gmail only, only when the mirror holds user labels, and a failure downgrades to showing the id; telling user from system needs no API `type` field, since every Gmail system label is an uppercase constant. The part worth writing down: **browsing a label reads ACROSS folders**. In Gmail labelled and archived are orthogonal, and the mail most likely to carry a label is precisely the mail that left the inbox (filing IS labelling plus archiving), so an inbox-scoped label view shows almost nothing and reads as an empty label — a label query therefore uses `searchablePredicate` (everything but Trash), the same reach search has and for the same reason. Reading, never writing: ADR 0107 keeps labelling with the provider. Rejected: fetching each provider's list (per-provider branch, returns labels with no mirrored mail, and can disagree with browsing), scoping a label to the current folder, a `label` column (ADR 0108's second-source-of-truth objection), and syncing Outlook categories / IMAP keywords (a different feature, already noted in ADR 0116) |

| [0126](./docs/notes/0126-an-operator-is-a-question-only-mail-can-answer.md) | **An operator is a question only mail can answer**: `from:mom dinner` used to search for the literal string "from:mom", which matches nothing and reads as missing mail. `parseSearchQuery` lifts `from:` / `has:attachment` / `after:`-`before:` (ISO) / `newer_than:`-`older_than:` (`3d`,`2w`,`1m`) out of the query **on BOTH surfaces** — ADR 0085 makes the two paths agree on what *matches*, and parsing on only one would make them disagree about what the QUERY is, breaking the same promise one step earlier. **Any operator narrows the result to mail**: a task has no sender and a saved place no attachment, so returning unfiltered tasks beside filtered mail answers a question nobody asked (dates included, since no other arm filters by date). An **unrecognised** operator stays in the text (`has:cheese` is a search term; `after:tuesday` is not parsed into a guess about which Tuesday) — dropping it would silently widen the query, the ADR 0102 failure in a surface where it is invisible. An operator with **no free text** is a real query ("everything from the school"), which is what forces the text predicate to become conditional and the empty-query early-return to admit it. `from:` matches the display NAME as well as the address (people search for "the school"). Found a latent crash on the way: the unusable-literal sentinel was `'\0nomatch\0'` and **Postgres refuses a NUL byte in a text parameter** — previously unreachable (no FTS tokens implied no usable literal, so it returned early), and an operator-only query reaches it for the first time; it is `\x01` now. Rejected: parsing on one path only, applying operators to every category (`from:` on a task would have to mean "created by" — a different question in the same word), dropping unrecognised operators, and a structured filter UI (a different feature; people type these because Gmail taught them to) |

| [0127](./docs/notes/0127-a-gate-needs-two-signals-agreeing.md) | **A gate needs two signals agreeing**: ADR 0109 captured the bulk header and 0110 added the classifier's second opinion, and neither did anything — every newsletter and retail blast still ran the AI passes. Ten backlog questions, nine following from one: **deny-list**, not allow-list (owner call). Mine everything EXCEPT a blast, because transactional mass mail — the flight confirmation, the invoice — omits `List-Unsubscribe` **by design** and is exactly what the extraction passes exist for; an allow-list loses it silently, and nobody notices a suggestion never made. **Skipping requires BOTH signals** — load-bearing, not cautious: 0109's own rule is that the header is "a signal, never a verdict; nothing demotes on it alone", so a header-only gate would contradict the ADR that produced the header. One reads how the mail was SENT, the other what it LOOKS like, and their disagreement is the ambiguous case that should stay mined. Three overrides: `action IN (to_do, reply_needed)` (ADR 0086, 0109's stated rule), a sender in the user's own `people.email` (ADR 0076) — a known person is never suppressed, one stale row re-opening the firehose for that sender only — and **`Events & invites` by title** on magics, since Evite/Eventbrite are bulk by construction and precisely what ADR 0088 feared losing. **`contact_links` CANNOT be part of the exemption**: ADR 0065 stores phone hashes and never uploads raw contacts, so it structurally cannot answer "do I know this sender". **Stickiness is structural** (§11 q9) — every input is a stored column already carried across snapshot-replace, so no new column and no backfill. The rule exists twice (SQL for the passes, TS for the reasoning) and is **pinned by a parity test over all 20 combinations**, since two copies of a predicate is how ADR 0085's promise stopped being true once already. `EMAIL_BULK_GATE` is a flag, not a constant: a missing suggestion is otherwise indistinguishable from a model that declined. Rejected: allow-list, header-alone, `Precedence: bulk` as a third signal (0109 already declined it — corroborates a decision already made), shipping engagement-based promotion in the same slice (needs real `email_unsubscribes` data or it is a guess), and bundling the image work (orthogonal: what a message contains, not who sent it) |

| [0128](./docs/notes/0128-a-bulk-action-is-still-one-write-per-thread.md) | **A bulk action is still one write per thread**: the three unbuilt §1.4 rows (multi-select, swipe, long-press row menu) are one thing — **triage** — and their absence is why Traditional was a reader rather than a mailbox. `POST /me/email/threads/modify` takes up to 100 ids and **loops over the same per-thread path**: each thread is still its own provider call, because that is what modifying a thread costs on Gmail/Graph/IMAP, and an endpoint that pretended otherwise would misdescribe what it did (the cap exists for the same reason — a request fanning out to a thousand is a DoS against the user's own quota). **Partial failure is reported, never swallowed**: `{updated, failed}` and the client says "7 done, 2 could not be changed" — one bad id must not fail the batch, and a batch that half-worked must not read as success (ADR 0107 in a new shape); another user's thread lands in `failed` rather than an error, so the caller learns nothing about whether the id exists. The gesture set stops where 0107 draws its line — swipe archives and toggles read, full stop, since trash/star/snooze/move are FILING and a gesture for a verb we do not perform is worse than its absence. **`GestureHandlerRootView` was missing from the app root**: without it the swipes bind, render and do NOTHING on Android — built-looking and inert, the failure this codebase keeps writing ADRs about. Archive is optimistic with the refetch as the correction (waiting a round trip per row makes triaging forty threads feel broken; dropping without re-checking is how mail silently goes missing), and selection mode suppresses swipes on purpose since a half-swipe and a checkbox tap are the same gesture. **A vacuous test surfaced here**: the first fixture made threads with no messages, but every label write runs `recomputeThread`, which DELETES a message-less thread — the rows vanished mid-test, `rows.every(...)` ran over `[]`, and the archive test passed having proved nothing; fixtures now insert a message and assert the row count before the predicate. Rejected: real per-provider bulk writes (three implementations, three failure modes — revisit if quota bites), returning only a count (the client then cannot say WHICH failed, so it lies or stays silent), failing the whole batch on one bad id, and swipe-to-delete (Chord does not delete mail) |

| [0129](./docs/notes/0129-snooze-is-a-mailbox-state-and-only-ours.md) | **Snooze is a mailbox state, and only ours**: `chord_email.md` §5 excludes snooze from the AI view deliberately and says nothing about Traditional, which is a complete mailbox and in which "not now" is the commonest thing anyone wants to say. It is also the one item on ADR 0107's FILING list that is not obviously filing — trash/star/move say where mail LIVES, snooze says WHEN you want to deal with it, which is nearer the to-do surface Chord owns. Owner chose a real mailbox state over a dated to-do (against the recommendation to defer). `email_threads.snoozed_until` is a **predicate** in ADR 0108's model — inbox becomes `is_archived = 0 AND (snoozed_until IS NULL OR snoozed_until <= now)`, `snoozed` its complement, so "is it back yet" is a comparison rather than a job that has to run on time; ISO text sorts correctly and needs no clock on the database. **Two columns, because waking is not touching**: `recomputeThread` is the right choke point (every provider crosses it, the ADR 0106/0109 precedent) but it ALSO runs for a plain mark-read, so an unconditional clear would mean any touch un-snoozed the thread and the feature would look flaky rather than broken — hence `snoozed_at < lastMessageAt`, i.e. a message arrived AFTER you put it aside; a message OLDER than the snooze (backfill) does not wake it either, and both have tests. **The cost is stated, not discovered**: Gmail's snooze has no API, so this is local — a thread snoozed in Chord stays visible in Gmail, the two-writers problem 0107 avoids, in the one place we cannot see the other writer. Mitigation is honesty: the folder says "Snoozed in Chord only — your mail app still shows these." Choices are coarse on purpose (later today / tomorrow / next week): a date-time picker is a bigger UI than "not now" deserves. Partial index, since almost nothing is snoozed and the inbox predicate must stay cheap. Rejected: snooze-as-a-dated-to-do (the recommendation — nothing new stored, nothing to drift), deferring, writing through to the provider (no API; archive-now-relabel-later moves mail in a real mailbox on a schedule, a far larger promise), and one column with an unconditional wake |

| [0130](./docs/notes/0130-an-alias-is-only-offered-once-verified.md) | **An alias is only offered once it is verified**: the audit's send-as row was half stale — a From picker across connected MAILBOXES already existed (ADR 0119); the real gap was an alias WITHIN one mailbox. The expected blocker turned out not to exist: `users.settings.sendAs.list` is covered by **`gmail.modify`**, which we already hold, so unlike ADR 0124's vacation responder this costs no new scope and no re-consent — worth CHECKING rather than assuming, since the same feature under `gmail.settings.basic` would have been a different decision entirely. **Verification is the whole feature**: Gmail does not reject an unverified From, it **silently rewrites** it to the authenticated address, sends, and returns success — so listing unverified aliases would show one address, send from another, and report that it worked (the ADR 0102 failure with a 200). Filter is `verificationStatus === 'accepted'`, primary exempt because it IS the authenticated address. The From is **re-checked server-side and REFUSED with a 400**, never quietly replaced with the mailbox's own: a message sent from somewhere other than where the sender chose is not the message they wrote. **Gmail-only is what parity means here** (ADR 0116): Graph needs a SendAs permission we do not hold and cannot enumerate, IMAP has no alias concept — not a limit we impose but the shape of those mailboxes, and the picker simply does not render with nothing to offer. Read live, so an alias added in Gmail appears without a reconnect and a lapsed verification disappears; a failed lookup returns `[]` rather than erroring, because composing from your own address must keep working. Rejected: a Chord-side alias table the user types into (works everywhere, cannot know what is verified — exactly the failure this prevents), asking for `gmail.settings.basic` (broader than the feature needs is how consent screens become something users click past), offering unverified aliases with a warning ("this may not work" is unactionable at send time and invisible after), and setting From on Outlook/IMAP (SMTP usually accepts any From, which is the problem — SPF/DKIM misalignment, spam folder or a bounce hours later) |

| [0131](./docs/notes/0131-formatting-is-a-small-vocabulary.md) | **Formatting is a small vocabulary, deliberately**: ADR 0069 derived the html part from the plain text (escape + `<br>`), so nobody could bold a word or make a list. The obvious build — a WYSIWYG editor — is a new dependency and a new native build for a composer whose typical message is three sentences. Instead a **small markdown subset rendered on send**: `**bold**`, `[text](url)`, `-` and `1.` lists, bare-URL autolink; the TEXT part keeps the markers as typed, which is the point of markdown (it reads fine unrendered, so text-only clients lose nothing). **Small is the decision, not a first step** — every marker is a character that also occurs in prose, and mangling what someone typed is worse than not formatting it. **Italics are deliberately absent**: a single `*` occurs in maths, footnotes, `2 * 3` and `*sigh*`, `_` is worse (`snake_case`), while `**` does not happen by accident — that asymmetry is the point, not an oversight to fix later. **Nothing changes for an unformatted body**: `textToHtml` renders markdown only when `hasMarkdown` says there is some, else it produces the same bytes it always did (ADR 0111's no-attachment posture applied to formatting), so the change cannot regress a message nobody meant to format. Escaping runs FIRST so typed markup stays text, and `[text](url)` accepts **http/https/mailto only** — relaying a `javascript:` href that arrived in someone's mail is one thing, AUTHORING one into outgoing mail is another. Preview rather than a toolbar (a toolbar implies a selection model the composer lacks), rendered through the same `EmailBody` the reader uses, shown only when the body actually has formatting. The ADR 0124 vacation body is user-authored and formats too — checked, not assumed. Rejected: a WYSIWYG editor, full CommonMark (headings/tables/code fences serve nobody here and each adds a way to format prose by accident), italics, and rendering every body unconditionally |

| [0132](./docs/notes/0132-a-to-do-is-not-a-list-item.md) | **A to-do is not a list item**: the magics extractor emitted only `create_event` and `add_list_item`, so "sign and return the permission slip by Friday" was filed as a row on a list called *To-dos* — a to-do in name only. A list item has no due date, no status, no overdue handling and exactly two exits (tick or delete), while everything the product knows about obligations lives on **tasks**: ADR 0092's archive path and due-ordered briefing, ADR 0095's folding, its own tab in the hub. Mail — where household obligations actually arrive, deadlines attached — was the one source that could not produce one. New `task` kind staged as `create_task`. **The boundary is what the thing IS**: a list is a COLLECTION (buy, bring — "cupcakes for the bake sale"), a task is an OBLIGATION (return, pay, book, reply, renew); the prompt carries that pair as the worked example because it is the case that separates them. **`dueAt` only when the email states one** — inventing a deadline is worse than having none, since ADR 0092 archives a dated task 7 days past due and a fabricated date would quietly delete the to-do, while an undated task is never auto-archived (the right home for "renew the passport"). `shouldStageTaskProposal` mirrors its list-item twin and compares **title only, ignoring the due date**: the same obligation proposed twice with different deadlines is one obligation, and this guards the CARDS — once confirmed, ADR 0095 folds the tasks anyway. Card says "TO DO" / "Add to-do" and renders a due date as date-only, since a deadline is a day and an event is an instant. Rejected: adding trip/save/reservation/RSVP as the audit row listed (each is a judgement from a subject line and each multiplies the ways a card can be wrong — a timed reservation IS an event, and trips need their own ADR; same discipline as 0131's vocabulary), giving lists due dates instead (rebuilding tasks on the wrong table), and migrating existing *To-dos* rows (someone's list, some already ticked) |

| [0133](./docs/notes/0133-a-bare-number-is-not-a-tracking-number.md) | **A bare number is not a tracking number**: ADR 0112 reads the carrier off the number's shape, which is right for the two that name themselves (`1Z`+16 is UPS and nothing else; the `9[1-5]` prefix is assigned to USPS) and wrong for the rest — FedEx is `\d{12}|\d{15}|\d{20}`, DHL `\d{10}`, USPS also bare 20/22, and a run of digits that long is equally an order number, an invoice number or a phone number with its punctuation stripped. `Order 123456789012 confirmed` produced a FedEx link, and **a wrong carrier link is worse than no link because it looks like an answer** (the reader taps it, FedEx says not found, and the mistake is Chord's). So an unmistakable shape stands alone and a **bare run of digits needs corroboration**: the sender IS that carrier, or the carrier is named in the text, or the number is labelled (`tracking`, `AWB`, `waybill`, `label`, `shipment`, `parcel`, `package`) **within 40 chars before the digits** — `Order` deliberately absent, since that is the case this rejects. **Requiring the sender was the obvious fix and is the wrong shape**: most shipping mail comes from the RETAILER (Amazon, Etsy, a Shopify shop), not the carrier, so a sender requirement would reject the majority of real tracking emails to fix a minority of false ones — the sender stays 0112's tiebreak, now also one of three corroborations. A cue word suffices because real shipping mail LABELS the number (that is why it is printed — a human has to find it), and the distinction is already in the text for free, needing no model call, which is what 0112 was protecting. Nearness is load-bearing: a mail saying "tracking available once we ship" would otherwise vouch for every number below it. `USPS` becomes two rules — self-identifying IMpb prefix vs bare 20/22 — because collapsing them treats an assigned prefix as guesswork. Consequence accepted: a bare unlabelled number from a retailer is now MISSED, which is the deliberate direction (a missed link costs a search, a wrong link costs trust). Rejected: requiring a carrier sender, asking a model on ambiguity (the per-delivery LLM call 0112 exists to avoid), and check-digit validation (three algorithms with per-service variants, to reject what a cue word already rejects — revisit only if the cue rule proves leaky) |
| [0136](./docs/notes/0136-we-fetch-the-image-so-the-sender-never-meets-the-reader.md) | **We fetch the image, so the sender never meets the reader**: ADR 0120 blocked remote images and understated the trade — a retail or school email is *mostly* images, so blocking produces a broken-looking mailbox needing a tap per message, not a plainer one. That ADR named the fix (proxy, "the best answer for the reader") and declined it on three costs, two of which other work has since paid: ADR 0122 extracted `utils/ssrf.ts` for exactly the "we are now the party fetching arbitrary URLs" problem, leaving bandwidth, which is a number rather than an architecture. **The proxy is unconditional; the setting only decides whether to fetch** — once it exists there is no case where the device should fetch out of someone else's mail directly, so *whether* is the reader's call (`users.email_images_auto`, default on, plus 0120's per-sender list) and *how* is not a question. That split is what keeps the change small: 0120's blocking path is untouched and is now what you get with the default off. **What the sender learns is stated exactly** — IP, device and cross-sender correlation never leak (they would if we had simply unblocked); the address-is-live-and-roughly-when signal DOES, which is the honest cost and Gmail's posture too. Caching blunts repeat opens; Apple's preload-at-delivery would close it and is an order of magnitude more machine. `imagesAllowed` becomes `auto \|\| sender-allowed`, resolved server-side, so the client keeps ONE rule and default-on needs no new branch. **The token is a capability, not an identity**: an `<img>` cannot send the session header (which is why attachments never had this problem), so the URL carries a sealed `{u, exp}` (AES-GCM, 12h, minted per thread open) rather than the session token — a session credential in a query string lands in every access log, and sealing means the holder cannot read the user id either. Fenced against becoming an open relay: `assertPublicHost` per redirect hop (`redirect: 'manual'`, max 3), `image/*` only, 10MB checked **while streaming** (a lying content-length must not stream us out of memory), 8s timeout, no cookies/referrer either way. Failure renders a **1×1 transparent GIF, not a 502** — a broken-image glyph is indistinguishable from the blocking this removes. `EMAIL_IMAGE_PROXY=false` kills it deployment-wide. One `REMOTE_CSS_URL` pattern serves both strip and rewrite, since a second regex is a second chance to miss the `background-image` case the first was written for — with a test asserting the two agree. Rejected: just unblocking (hands back the three harms 0120 was written about), "safe-looking" image heuristics (a pixel can be any size, a logo can be on the tracking domain), preload-at-sync, client-side signing (a shipped secret is not one), and server-side HTML rewriting (a second copy of the is-this-remote predicate — the ADR 0085/0127 failure) |
| [0137](./docs/notes/0137-a-proactive-message-is-not-always-the-briefing.md) | **A proactive message is not always the briefing, and a mailbox is not the user**: one prod morning produced five complaints and two bugs, both the shape of a flag read as an answer to a question it does not ask. (1) `messages.is_proactive` means *Chord started this* and SIX things set it, but both publishers read it as *is this the briefing*. A 21:02 `event_created_nudge` found the WhatsApp window shut, so `gateProactiveSend` sent the BRIEFING's approved template - "Good morning! Want your daily briefing?" plus the day's facts (ADR 0091) - at 9pm, and parked a calendar-conflict warning behind its `Full briefing` button; the push categoriser had the identical defect, putting every nudge in `briefing`, the one category exempt from quiet hours *because it fires at the user's own nudge schedule*. Migration `060` adds nullable `proactive_kind` + `isScheduledBriefing()` (in `db/schema.ts`, at the column) answering the real question - **did the USER choose this moment?** (briefing, digest, `task_remind` yes; event echo and the two reconnect warnings no). Out of window a non-briefing now **skips** (`window_closed`) rather than borrowing an ask that is a billed weekly-cadence conversation, and deliberately does NOT stamp `nudge_prompt_sent_at`. Optional and **not backfilled**: an unrecorded kind must read as not-the-briefing, since resolving it the other way IS the bug. Her other WhatsApp messages that evening were accepted-then-failed by Meta (131047) and, per ADR 0090 ignoring `statuses`, logged nowhere - seven of eight never arrived. (2) `afterSync` is USER-scoped work called once per ACCOUNT, so a two-mailbox user ran the whole AI pipeline twice per tick and a three-mailbox user three times: N x the LLM spend, plus racing `ingestClassification` snapshot-replaces (`email_categories_pkey` + `deadlock detected`, three prod mailboxes unclassified all day - ADR 0106 scoped ids across USERS, never one user against themselves) and four IMAP logins to one Yahoo account in 1.3s hitting the login rate ADR 0080 exists to protect, escaping a route as a 500 `unhandled error`. `afterSync` needed TWO mechanisms, because it was over-called in two unrelated ways and coalescing alone (the first attempt) catches only one: the bulk loops are `for ... await`, so the N calls are strictly SEQUENTIAL and never overlap - a coalescer never fires and the three-mailbox user keeps paying three pipelines (prod timestamps are 0.9s apart, not concurrent). `withMailSyncRound(db, userId, fn)` collapses the sequential case (an `afterSync` inside an open round records work and returns; the round runs ONE pass on close), while the per-user coalescer handles the CONCURRENT case - two rounds overlapping, a route sync racing the tick, which is what put two snapshot-replaces in flight - and `syncAccount` coalesces per **account id** (two mailboxes are different servers). Scoping the ROUND rather than moving the call is deliberate: stripping `afterSync` from the five providers and adding it at ~10 entry points is ten chances to forget forever, whereas a forgotten round degrades to today's behaviour rather than to silence. A mid-run caller joins the FOLLOW-UP run, not the in-flight one - a test caught the first attempt handing back a snapshot read before that caller's own mail landed (collapsing must not cost freshness: "sync now" means as of now, and sharing one follow-up still turns any pile-up into two logins); the briefing's round must CLOSE before `buildEmailDigestBody`, which reads what the pipeline produces. Pinned by a test that counts passes: 3 without the round, 1 with it. `ingestClassification` takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock` - `_xact_` being the only form that survives transaction-mode pooling, ADR 0038's reason for banning LISTEN/NOTIFY. The same double-mining is why one Amex email produced TWO to-dos 2.26s apart ("...WITH American Express" / "...FOR American Express credit card"): exact normalized-title dedup (ADR 0132) cannot catch a rephrase and nothing loose enough to would stay safe, so `threadAlreadyProposed` uses the invariant instead - one thread yields at most one suggestion, so a second pending proposal of the same tool from that thread is a duplicate by construction. (3) **Email no longer proposes a date already lived through**: the 30-day backfill mined a month of settled mail into `create_event` cards (a gym class from four weeks earlier), and ADR 0100's guard is gated on `turnId`, which a proposal confirm has not - `shouldStageEventProposal` now refuses (nobody to ask, nothing ambiguous - the date is simply over) and `retractPastEventProposals` sweeps per pass; `isPastEventStart` moves to `utils/tz.ts` since two copies of one predicate is how ADR 0085's promise broke once already. Also: `isTransientProviderFailure` gives routes a POSITIVE test (`isAuthFailure` false also covers every ordinary bug) so a throttle answers **503 + `retryable`**, and `stripNulls` at both extraction boundaries - Postgres `text` cannot hold U+0000 and PDF text layers emit them routinely (five prod receipts in one afternoon, each losing text/summary/chunks/FTS while the recovery pass retried forever); the email side matters more, where `body_text` feeds a GENERATED tsvector and one NUL would wedge that mailbox. Every hardcoded fixture date had to go relative, the same consequence ADR 0100 recorded. Rejected: a second approved template for echoes, backfilling `proactive_kind`, making it required, refusing the past event at confirm (leaves an untappable card and reopens 0100's ingest exemption), loosening title dedup, claiming the extraction mark before the LLM call (both passes had already SELECTed, and the invite probe defers WITHOUT marking on purpose), a composite `email_categories` PK (0106's reasons hold and it does not address a user racing themselves), and a DB lock around `afterSync` (right for the invariant, wrong for the waste - a joiner would block for a whole pipeline instead of sharing it) |

| [0136](./docs/notes/0136-a-device-calendar-is-not-a-push-target.md) | **A device calendar is not a push target**: a prod report that "some events show in Google Calendar online but never reach my phone" had two halves. The **reported** half works as designed — prod runs `GCAL_TARGET_CALENDAR_NAME=Family`, so Chord pushes to a SECONDARY calendar; the web shows every calendar, phones sync only the ones enabled on that device, so nine events were visible online and absent on the phone until the user ticked it. The **broken** half: four events (29—31 Aug) carried `google_calendar_id = BEE3A1B0-…`, an **EventKit** id, and died on `events.insert 404` having reached no calendar at all while looking fine in Chord. `calendar_sources` is multi-provider and a `device` row stores the phone's id in that same generic column (ADR 0057), so a phone mirroring the user's Google "Family" calendar produced **two writable rows with the same summary** — and `matchCalendarByName` filtered on `access_role`, never on `provider`. Worse, the result was CACHED: `setGroupDefaultCalendar` wrote the EventKit id to `groups.default_calendar_id`, so every later event in the group inherited it, and ADR 0103's new give-up-on-4xx rule (right for the 400 loop it was written for) meant each failed once and never retried. Fix has two parts because the bug had two lives: `matchCalendarByName` filters `provider = 'google'` (both branches), AND `resolveTargetCalendarId` **re-checks a cached group/user default** rather than trusting it — not belt-and-braces, since the defaults are caches of an earlier resolution and fixing only the matcher leaves anyone who already resolved once permanently broken. **Unknown is not invalid**: only a KNOWN device id is refused, because a calendar created in Google a minute ago is not in `calendar_sources` yet and refusing it would break a legitimate target to catch an illegitimate one. Falls back to `primary` — the one calendar that certainly exists, is writable, and that every device syncs by default. Rejected: detecting the id by SHAPE (a UUID-vs-email regex encodes an assumption neither vendor promised, when `provider` is stored and authoritative), refusing the write (strands the event), dropping the column from device rows (0057 needs it), and not pushing to secondary calendars at all (removes a feature families deliberately use) |

| [0137](./docs/notes/0137-an-ambiguous-calendar-name-is-a-question.md) | **An ambiguous calendar name is a question**: ADR 0136 stopped a DEVICE calendar being handed to Google; asked what happens when two calendars share a name, the honest answer was that only half the ambiguity was gone. `matchCalendarByName` ran `.find()` over a query with **no `ORDER BY`**, so with two writable Google calendars named "Family" (your own + one a spouse shared) the winner was a coin flip that can land differently between identical calls — and the result is CACHED by `setGroupDefaultCalendar`, so the flip becomes the group default and every later event inherits it, the same mechanism that made 0136 persist for three days. The substring fallback widens it further ("family" over "Family Trips" AND "Family Photos"). Quieter than 0136 — the event lands on a real calendar and syncs, just the wrong one, which is exactly why nobody would report it. Fix: **rank deterministically, ask when the ranking has no answer.** `rankSource` orders owner-before-writer, then primary, then id, so "my Family" beats "a Family shared with me" outright and repeatably; when the top candidates are EQUALLY yours, `matchCalendarsByName` returns `candidates` and no id and the executor returns `ambiguous_calendar` with an instruction not to claim the event was created — the exact-name-or-ask shape of ADR 0077 (email routing) and ADR 0094 (list names), and a stronger case than either since the wrong pick is also written to Google and cached. A loose match over several names ALWAYS asks. An ambiguous request deliberately does **not** fall through to the group default (answering with a calendar they did not name is answering a different question); it returns `primary` + candidates so a caller that ignores them still writes somewhere real. Rejected: order-by-id-and-move-on (determinism is not the same as being right), most-recently-used (needs history nothing stores, and would have confidently picked the DEVICE row in 0136), always-ask (interrupts the common own-one-shared-one case), and failing outright (ADR 0100's shape, but `primary` is a perfectly good answer for a caller that cannot ask) |

| [0152](./docs/notes/0152-email-search-trusts-the-classifier-on-a-category-match.md) | **Email search trusts the classifier on a category-name match**: searching "school" returned two false positives (a Shokz promo, a Matt Levine newsletter) alongside one real hit, because neither search surface (`searchEverything`/`searchHits`, ADR 0085) has any notion of topic — both match only via FTS/literal/body-text over subject/snippet/from/body (ADR 0110's full-body arm doing exactly what it was built for, on an ordinary word). Chord already runs an LLM classifier (ADR 0066/0086/0109/0110) that files each thread into a category path; new `resolveCategoryMatch` (`classification.service.ts`, next to `categorySubtreeIds` — refactored out of `loadTree`'s unrelated count join into shared `loadCategoryNodes`) matches the query against the user's own category **titles**, at any depth, case-insensitively — title-contains-query, never the reverse, so a short/generic title can't swallow a long sentence. **Replace, don't union**: a category match is authoritative for that query and drops the keyword/body arm entirely, since it fixes the false positive AND the mirror-image false negative a keyword search can never fix (a real "Picture day reminder" that never says "school"). ADR 0126 operators (`from:`/`after:`/`label:`/…) still AND on top unconditionally — only the free-text arm is replaced. Multiple matching branches union their subtrees (read-only recall, not a write-ambiguity case per ADR 0077/0094/0137). `CATCH_ALL_LEAVES` (classifier-tree.ts) categories are excluded from matching — a query for "notes" must not replace results with someone's generic bucket. `searchHits`'s `ts_rank_cd`/`ts_headline` legitimately score near-zero for a topic-only match, so a category hit gets the same synthetic rank-ordered band already used for `file`/`todo` hits, and a `Filed under "..."` snippet instead of a headline that would highlight nothing. No new env flag — a pure read of already-trusted classifier output with a safe null fallback. ADR 0149 framing: a correctness fix to the shipped ADR 0085/0059/0062 matching rule, not new mail-client polish. Rejected: union instead of replace (leaves the false positive in place), a per-query LLM relevance call (re-asks what 0066/0086/0109/0110 already paid to compute once), and query-contains-title matching (lets any long sentence get "matched" by a short/generic title) |
| [0155](./docs/notes/0155-a-past-date-in-a-file-is-history-not-a-question.md) | **A past date in a file is history, not a question**: narrows ADR 0100 for one source. That guard refuses a past `create_event`/`update_event` on a bot turn and tells the model to read the date back and **ask** — right for a typed "add dinner Friday" on a Saturday, wrong when the date came out of an uploaded file (a school calendar / term schedule lists dozens of gone-by dates beside the few ahead, and the answer to "did you mean this?" is always no). A file is also not a typo channel — a past date there is a fact the document states about something over. New `ApplyToolCallInput.triggerFromAttachment` (set by the bot turn from the same `triggerHasAttachment` signal that gates the trip/content auto-filers, threaded through `executeAndBroadcast`): a past create/update on such a turn still writes nothing but returns `reason: 'past', fromFile: true` with a summary telling the model NOT to create it and NOT to ask — only future rows become events. `confirmPast` still overrides; ADR 0100's exemptions (no `turnId`, any `externalUid`) unchanged; the file stays fully indexed (indexing is at upload). Prompt + choke point per ADR 0100: `tools.ts` + `context.ts` tell the model up front not to call `create_event` for a file's past dates, executor is the backstop. Rejected: suppressing `create_event` entirely for document uploads (a schedule's future rows are exactly what belongs on the calendar and have no other auto-filer), asking once for the whole batch (still noise every upload), silently rolling each past date forward (ADR 0100's rejected alternative — worse from a file, where the row has no intended future date) |
| [0156](./docs/notes/0156-sign-in-with-apple-beside-google.md) | **Sign in with Apple beside Google** (amends ADR 0014): the public App Store is bound by guideline 4.8 — Google sign-in obliges an equivalent privacy-preserving login, and the email-client exception is a weak fit (Chord works without mail, Gmail is optional, four providers). Native iOS only (`expo-apple-authentication`, `ios.usesAppleSignIn`); web/Android keep Google. **Server-side code exchange, no SDK, no JWKS**: the app posts Apple's single-use authorization code, the server exchanges it at `appleid.apple.com/auth/token` with a `client_secret` it signs itself (ES256 JWT from the `.p8`, `node:crypto`), and trusts the `id_token` in THAT response (arrived over TLS — OIDC Core §3.1.3.7) after checking `iss`/`aud`/`exp`/`sub`; the client's `identityToken` is never trusted, and the single-use code is the replay guard (no nonce). **On only when it can be undone**: Apple requires revocation on account deletion, which needs the refresh token + the same key, so `APPLE_TEAM_ID`/`APPLE_KEY_ID`/`APPLE_PRIVATE_KEY` + `AUTH_MODE=google` gate it (else 503, and `/health.appleSignIn=false` hides the button); refresh token sealed in `users.apple_refresh_token_*`, revoked best-effort before the ADR 0043 teardown. Identity `users.apple_sub` (migration `068`, partial-unique): by `apple_sub`, else bind to a non-shadow user whose email matches **only when Apple says verified and not private-relay** (both providers proved the mailbox — the Google route's pre-seeded-claim rule), else create. Name arrives on the first authorization only, never in a token — forwarded by the app, fills blanks, never overwrites; relay users get `Chord user`, not the relay local part. A Google connect that re-points an Apple-only session to an existing Google account **moves** `apple_sub` along (`moveAppleIdentity`) so the next Apple sign-in lands there. Shared sign-in tail extracted to `completeSignIn`. **Addendum 2026-09-11**: the re-point **folds the whole Apple account** into the Google one (`foldAppleAccountInto` in `apple-fold.service.ts`, one transaction, only when the identity actually moved) — profile blanks fill the Google row (its own values win, `Chord user` never copies); a **verified phone stays verified** (one person, so ADR 0064's fresh-proof rule doesn't apply; if the Google row has its own number that wins and the Apple one is released, never left verified on an unreachable row); `external_identities` + push tokens follow; the **personal chat folds** into the Google one via ADR 0053's `consolidatePersonalGroupInto`, as does a pair group the Google account already has with that person; other groups move membership (roster persona handed over by id); authored rows follow via the shadow merge's `reattributeUserFkColumns` (exported, not copied) + messages, `managed_by`, `contact_links`; briefing collapses to one. The emptied row stays (deletion is ADR 0043's list). Both consent routes share `finishGoogleConsent`, answer 400 on a missing refresh token **before** writing anything, and return `GoogleConsentResponse.user` only on a switch so the app drops the old account's cache and adopts the profile **Addendum 2026-10-01**: the **web** login offers it too when `APPLE_SERVICES_ID` is set - Apple issues web codes to a Services ID, so exchange/audience/revoke take a client (`ios`\|`web`, `AppleAuthRequest.client`); Apple form-posts to `POST /auth/apple/callback`, which only relays code + state + name to the web app in the URL **fragment** (never in a log), and the app redeems it through the same `POST /auth/apple`; `/health.appleWeb` is null unless a Services ID and an https origin exist; deletion revokes as both clients (no column for which issued the token). Rejected: phone as the only login (open product question, not the 4.8 fix), JWKS-verifying the client token (no refresh token to revoke), linking on any matching email (a relay/unverified address is not proof), web Apple sign-in in this change (Services ID + `form_post` callback, a surface 4.8 does not cover) |
| [0157](./docs/notes/0157-inviting-someone-to-an-event.md) | **Inviting someone to an event**: "add meeting with Kevin, invite guest.test@gmail.com" created the event on the asker's own calendar and silently dropped the invite — `create_event` had no attendee field and the Google push never emitted an `attendees` array (only ADR 0104's RSVP patch touched attendees, and only to set *your own* response). New `invitees: string[]` on `create_event` / `update_event` — each entry an email OR a name. The executor splits them: `@` → validated email; a name → resolved **sender-scoped** (ADR 0065) against the asker's `people.email` (ADR 0076) then their Chord contacts; unresolved → the event is **still created**, the name comes back in `unresolvedInvitees`, and a `kind: 'contact'` `BotCard` per name is attached. Resolved emails write to the dormant **`event_attendees`** table (migration 005, "rsvps successor" — first live writer; `user_id` NULL, `role` viewer); the event is re-dirtied and `pushDirtyEvents` sends the invitations — `localRowToGoogleBody` gains an `attendees` array and the insert/patch URL gets `?sendUpdates=all` **only when the event has attendees**. Mobile renders the `contact` card as "Add <name> from Contacts" → `expo-contacts` `presentContactPickerAsync()` (already wired for ADR 0065) → the chosen email is posted back as a plain chat message and the next bot turn calls `update_event`. **Also fixed alongside:** `eventBlocksTime` knew only `gcal:<calId>:<uid>` and "everything else → Chord-native → always blocks", so a `device:<calId>:<uid>` copy (ADR 0057/0136) of a read-only subscribed calendar counted as busy and triggered a conflict nudge — it now has a `device:` branch mirroring `gcal:` (`ctx.busy` already holds device calendar ids). Rejected: an emails-only field (forces the model to guess an address), blocking the create until every guest resolves, a new `event_invitees` table, `sendUpdates=all` on every push, a custom in-app contact list **Addendum 2026-10-27**: a name the server cannot place is asked of the asker's phone (ADR 0212 `requestDeviceContact`) on a live app turn, any chat (none for ingest/confirm/WhatsApp-Telegram); `pickInviteeFromDevice` (kernel): exact full name, else the only contact with an address, invited at its first address; several = bot asks; `contact.lookup` carries `forUserId` and only that user's app answers. Event editor gets a Guests box (composer recipient field, so phone contacts too) via `GET`/`POST /api/v1/events/:id/guests` -> same `changeEventGuests` as `update_event` invitees (organizer only, `reason` on refusal) |
| [0158](./docs/notes/0158-email-body-through-the-trip-classifier.md) | **An itinerary email is an itinerary, wherever it lives**: a Booking.com / Expedia confirmation never became a trip — not because trips are hard (Chord has imported them from documents for a year) but because **two pipelines do the same job and only one was fed**. An attachment → `saveFile` → `classifyAndAutoFileAttachment` (a plain `classify(text)` → flight/hotel → `createTrip`/`addTravelLeg`/`addAccommodation`). An email body → `magics.service.ts`, a separate pass whose vocabulary is only `event | task | list`. So an Expedia email *with a PDF itinerary* already made a trip (ADR 0071); Booking.com / modern Expedia put it in the **HTML body with no attachment** → fell to magics → a stray `create_event` or (for a hotel) an `add_list_item`. Prod (`ownertest@gmail.com`): 73 travel threads, **0** trip proposals ever. Fix: a new `afterSync` step runs the **existing** trip classifier on the email body and **stages a proposal bundle** (`create_trip` + `add_travel_leg`/`add_accommodation`, one `bundle_id`, `provenance = gmail:<threadId>`) — it does **not** auto-commit like the attachment path, because an email arrives unbidden and one classifier misfire is a phantom trip. `bot/trip_classifier.ts` exports `classifyTravelDocument` + `zonedDatetime`/`toLocalDatetime` (no behaviour change to the attachment path); `email/trip-extraction.ts` `stageEmailTripProposal` matches an existing trip by date (`findTripByDateRange` → children only, no `create_trip`) and dedups legs/stays; hooked into the `runEmailMagics` per-thread loop as a short-circuit for `travel/*`-category threads, **after** the invite `.ics` probe, marking the thread (`kind: 'magic'`, ADR 0137 one-outcome-per-thread) so no `create_event`/`flight_json` twin. The whole consumer side — bundle confirm (`create_trip` first, id injected into children, atomic), Action Items bundle card, briefing line — was already built (ADR 0009/0029); nothing read trip proposals only because nothing wrote them. `EMAIL_TRIPS_ENABLED` (default on). Rejected: a `trip` suggestion kind in magics (duplicates the classifier + its schemas + trip-matching), auto-committing like attachments, running `classify` on every thread (an LLM call per thread to catch mail the classifier already labelled `travel/`), importing the itinerary as a synthetic attachment. **Addendum (prod test):** (1) rail is a trip — `FlightLeg.mode` (`flight`\|`train`\|`ferry`\|`car`\|`other`; coach→`other`) + broadened prompt, so an Amtrak e-ticket is held; `syncFlightLegEvent` also fires for `train`. (2) the leg's calendar event now carries the carrier PNR, the booking-site itinerary/order number (`Flight.itineraryNumber` → new `travel_legs`/`accommodations.notes`, migration `069`), the passenger, and a one-tap **link back to the source email** (`providerThreadLink` via `email/thread-link.ts`, from the `gmail:<threadId>` provenance) — `createEvent` gains a `description` field (events row + Google push already had the column). `addTravelLeg`'s calendar sync is now **awaited** (a bundle confirm runs it inside a transaction). (3) a non-owner `passengerName` also stages an `add_person` proposal via ADR 0076's shared `stagePersonProposal` (extracted to `email/person-proposal.ts` to break a `magics→trip-extraction→person-extraction→magics` cycle). (4) **no past-dated proposal ever surfaces** — a flight change for a finished trip stages nothing, a mid-trip one keeps only legs `departAt ≥ local today`, a past-checkout hotel is skipped; and the deployed `create_task` magics path gains the same guard (`shouldStageTaskProposal` refuses a past `dueAt`, mirroring `shouldStageEventProposal`) so "confirm attendance for the Sep 5 reservation" doesn't arrive on Sep 11 |
| [0159](./docs/notes/0159-the-pipeline-runs-as-mail-arrives-not-when-you-look.md) | **The pipeline runs as mail arrives, not when you look**: ADR 0144 fixed the mailbox *mirror* going stale between app opens but stopped short of the AI layer, reasoning "a background sweep would multiply LLM spend by however often it runs" — so `sweepMailboxes` ran at `mirror` depth and the trip/task/person pipeline still only ran on Email-tab focus or the 7am briefing, meaning a day could pass with a real actionable email sitting unmined. The reasoning doesn't hold: every AI pass below the sweep line (`runLlmClassifier`'s `selectBatch`, magics'/person-extraction's `NOT EXISTS (extraction mark)` queries) makes **zero LLM calls** on a tick with nothing new — the per-thread one-time extraction mark is the real spend multiplier, not sweep frequency. **`sweepMailboxes` now opens at `full` depth**, not `mirror` — same batch size, same 5-minute cadence, still stops at the ADR 0079 entitlement gate; only *when* each thread's one-time pass runs changes. The two existing mining filters stay untouched (owner call): ADR 0127's bulk/marketing deny-list and ADR 0143's unread-or-recent window answer *what's worth proposing*, a different question from *when the pipeline runs* — widening them would flood a fresh connect with settled history and put sale blasts back in front of users. **"Nothing past ever surfaces" (ADR 0158) generalizes past trips**: `shouldStageTaskProposal` had **no** past-date guard at all (unlike its event counterpart) — the real cause of a prod "Confirm attendance for the Sep 5 reservation" surfacing on Sep 11 on `spouse.test@gmail.com` — fixed the same way ADR 0137 fixed events (refuses an already-past `dueAt`; undated tasks untouched, ADR 0092's reasoning). New bundle-aware `retractPastTaskProposals`/`retractPastTripProposals` (`event-convergence.service.ts`, run every magics pass alongside `retractPastEventProposals`) sweep proposals that go stale **after** staging but before confirm: a trip bundle retracts wholesale when every leg/stay is past, or drops only its stale members when some are still ahead (a straddling itinerary keeps its future return leg), mirroring the rule `stageEmailTripProposal` already applies at staging time. Rejected: widening the mining filters too, a third "just check for new mail" depth tier between `mirror` and `full` (the AI passes' own mark-gating already is that tier), retracting a straddling trip bundle wholesale (loses a still-ahead return leg — the common round-trip case) |
| [0160](./docs/notes/0160-a-receipt-is-settled-not-owed.md) | **A receipt is settled, not owed**: mail already classifies into a real `Receipts` category leaf (ADR 0109) that already maps to the Ledger tile (ADR 0087), but nothing ever extracted a structured fact from one — `extractLedgerFact`'s fail-closed regex explicitly treats "receipt / thank you for your order" as noise unless a balance is also due, since that function's job is money OWED or COMING BACK, and a settled purchase is neither. Owner direction: extract now and store for later, since Finance (ADR 0070's scaffold) doesn't exist yet but the per-thread extraction mark (ADR 0076/0110) fires once ever — mining only once Finance ships loses every receipt mined-and-marked before that date. **One more optional block on the SAME magics LLM call, not a second one** — `EXTRACT_SYSTEM` gains a `"receipt":{merchant,amount,currency,purchasedAt,orderNumber}` block read off the same reply as event/task/list, mirroring `onDelivery`/`onFlight`'s side-channel shape exactly (`parseReceipt`, `onReceipt`); a bill with a balance due stays a `task`, untouched. **Stored, never proposed** (`email_threads.receipt_json`, migration `070`, `recordReceipt`) — nothing to confirm about a purchase already settled, ADR 0135's reasoning. **Rendered through the Ledger board that was already there** as a genuinely new state: `EmailLedgerFact.direction` gains `'paid'` (misusing `debit`/`credit` would show it as owed or refunded — the exact confusion `PAID_NOISE` exists to prevent), `receiptToLedgerFact` converts at render time, `capFacts` ranks it strictly last (owed, then coming back, then settled) so an old receipt can never bump a due bill off the tile. Coupons explicitly deferred (owner: "ignore for now") — bulk mail by ADR 0109's own definition, deny-listed by ADR 0127; whether that needs a carve-out is a separate decision. Rejected: a second dedicated LLM pass (the magics call already has the body), reusing debit/credit for a receipt, waiting for Finance to exist before mining |
| [0161](./docs/notes/0161-a-briefing-can-be-told-what-to-skip.md) | **A briefing can be told what to skip**: prod (ownertest@gmail.com) got 17–19 `notifications@rippling.com` birthday/work-anniversary threads in a month, `informational`/`Social & professional`, inconsistently `bulk`-flagged so ADR 0127's gate doesn't reliably catch them — and the ONLY place email reaches the briefing, `digest.service.ts`'s deterministic "Worth a look" list, has no exclusion concept (only a hardcoded `LOW_SIGNAL_ROOTS` set). New `email_briefing_rules` (migration `071`, modeled on ADR 0077's routing rules but with no target — a hit just means "leave this out of the digest," never a route): `sender_domain`/`sender_email`/`category`/`subject_keyword`, matched by pure `matchBriefingRule` pinned by a case table. **Free text resolves deterministically, no LLM** (`resolveBriefingPattern`): `@` → address, dotted-no-space → domain, else ADR 0152's `resolveCategoryMatch` → category, else a sender-name search over the user's OWN mail (ADR 0065/0114's sender-scoped-only rule) — one matching domain auto-resolves, several return `candidates` for the bot to ask about (same shape `set_email_route` uses), none falls through to `subject_keyword`. **Scoped to what's rendered, not extraction** (owner call) — classification/magics/person-extraction run exactly as before; only `digest.service.ts`'s "Worth a look" list, pending-suggestion filtering (resolved via each proposal's `gmail:` provenance rather than widening the shared `EmailMagicSuggestion` wire type), and the closing count line (`unreadCountExcluding`, local to the digest — app-wide unread badges untouched) respect a rule. **Chat-only** (owner decision): `set_briefing_exclusion`/`list_briefing_exclusions`, `PERSONAL_ONLY_TOOL_NAMES`-gated, no Settings screen — the ask-until-clear back-and-forth rides the same personal-1:1 mechanism ADR 0148 already established, no new state machine. Rejected: gating extraction too (separate, bigger decision; moot today since `informational` mail never proposes anything), LLM topic matching (substring stays testable), a Settings editor now (chat answers "what have I hidden" already) |
| [0162](./docs/notes/0162-an-event-remembers-what-made-it.md) | **An event remembers what made it**: `events.description` plumbing (column, `createEvent` param, Google push/pull) existed end-to-end but had exactly one writer — ADR 0158's `syncFlightLegEvent`, travel legs only — and `rowToEvent` never returned it to any client, so even a travel leg's own description was invisible. The bot's `create_event`/`update_event` schemas had no description field, and live chat carried **no provenance at all** (`bot.service.ts` hardcoded `provenance: null`), so there was no source text to excerpt from even in principle; email-triggered events already carried `source_raw_context = gmail:<threadId>` through to `createEvent` but nothing turned it into a description. Fix: expose `description` on `GroupEvent`/`rowToEvent`; new `server/src/services/events/context-excerpt.ts` generalizes ADR 0158's `emailThreadLinkFromProvenance` to build `gmail:` (subject+snippet+link) or `chat:` (the triggering message's own text+sender) excerpts; called **once, inside `createEvent`**, only when the caller left `description` `undefined` (an explicit `null` — a travel leg with nothing to say — is respected, never second-guessed) so every `createEvent` caller gets it for free. `bot.service.ts` now threads `chat:<messageId>` instead of hardcoded `null` — checked, not assumed, that every existing `source_raw_context` reader matches by prefix (`LIKE 'gmail:%'`, never null-equality), so this is safe beyond `create_event` too. **Never model-authored** (ADR 0102's posture) — `description` is not a `create_event`/`update_event` tool parameter; always composed server-side from stored data. `EVENT_CONTEXT_EXCERPT_ENABLED` (default on) — touches the Google-pushed description of every future event, so it gets a kill-switch. Rejected: model-authored descriptions (confabulation risk), re-deriving on every `update_event` (creation-time fact only), restricting the new chat provenance to `create_event` only (broadening it is safe and useful plumbing) **Addendum 2026-10-29**: `update_event` that changes a field appends `Changed from chat: "<message>" — <sender>` (or `Changed by email: <subject>` on an email change card) via `buildEventEditNote`/`appendEditNote`, single events and whole series, once per message; app edits and edits with no message behind them leave it alone |
| [0163](./docs/notes/0163-a-meeting-remembers-why.md) | **A meeting remembers why**: sibling of ADR 0162, not a variant — 0162 only excerpts the ONE message that triggered `create_event`; this searches the user's mailbox broadly for prior correspondence with an ADR 0157 invitee, since the negotiation that produced a meeting is usually a different thread than whatever chat message told the bot to add it. Owner's worked example: "you set up meeting with Bill Mills on x, to discuss y, z and w." New `server/src/services/events/meeting-context.ts`, hooked in after `addEventAttendees` (attendee identity isn't known inside `createEvent` yet) in both `create_event`/`update_event`. Searches the user's OWN mail only (ADR 0065/0114), either direction (`from_email` or `to_emails`/`cc_emails` via `jsonb_array_elements_text`, ADR 0114's own technique), 60-day window, capped at 3 attendees × 2 messages — **no match found → zero LLM calls**, which keeps the common case (no attendees, or no mail on file) free. Only when correspondence IS found: one small LLM call ("name what it's about in 3-8 words, or NONE"), deterministic parse, injectable (`CorrespondenceSummarizer`, ADR 0158's `TravelClassifier` pattern) for testability. **Computed once, stored on `events.meeting_context`** (migration `072`, own column like `delivery_json`/`flight_json`/`receipt_json` — never folded into `description`), fire-and-forget beside the existing `maybeSyncCalendar` best-effort call so it never blocks the reply or fails the create. **Rendered by folding into the existing scan**, not a new section: `loadScanContext`'s per-event line appends `(context: …)` when present, one added prompt sentence (work it in naturally when given, invent nothing when absent) — the SAME LLM call that already writes "This week" produces the owner's example as one bullet. Own flag, `MEETING_CONTEXT_ENABLED` (default on, separate from ADR 0162's — this one has a real if usually-zero LLM cost). Scope: only the ADR 0157 invite path; email-magics-created events don't stage `invitees` yet. Rejected: search+summarize as a SCAN-loop tool call (breaks the compute-once-at-creation discipline every other fact-extraction ADR follows), folding into `description` (loses it as a distinct fact), one LLM call per attendee (one bounded call over whatever was found) |
| [0164](./docs/notes/0164-memory-search-and-named-group-writes.md) | **Explicit memory writes, live web search, and named-group targeting from the personal 1:1**: three bundled gaps. New `remember_fact` tool direct-writes a confirmed persona fact in-turn (no proposal round-trip — the user stating it IS the confirmation, same reasoning as `add_person`'s direct write), and `list_memories`'s description now coaches an interactive "what do you know about me" → thin result → one follow-up question → `remember_fact` loop. New `search_web` tool performs a REAL external fetch (distinct from `suggest_external_action`'s deep-link-only cards) behind a `SEARCH_PROVIDER` seam (`''`\|`mock`\|`google`) mirroring `WEATHER_PROVIDER` (ADR 0091) — app-orchestrated rather than provider-native so it works under every `LLM_PROVIDER` including `mock`; unconfigured → `getSearchProvider() === null` and the tool description forbids claiming a search happened (ADR 0102 posture). New optional `withGroupName` on `create_task`/`add_list_item`/`save_place`, honored only on a personal turn (ADR 0096 widened reads across groups but left writes pinned — this is the write half), resolved by a new shared `resolveGroupByName` helper extracted from `set_email_route`'s exact-name-or-ask query (ADR 0077) and upgraded to also match the ADR 0101 counterpart display name, so an unnamed two-person chat resolves on "Alice" even though its stored name is still "Direct message"; `set_email_route` itself refactored onto the shared helper. `resolveGroupByName`'s `reason` matches the original exactly: `'not_found'` whenever there isn't exactly one EXACT match (partial matches still ride along in `candidates`), `'ambiguous'` reserved for two exact ties — reads as a bug on first glance, is not. No schema/migration changes. Rejected: a provider-native web-search tool (Anthropic-only, breaks under the default `openai` provider and the mock demo flow), training-knowledge-only search, extending `withGroupName` to shared-group turns (breaks ADR 0004's strict scope). **Addendum 2026-09-11**: checked whether a search-affiliate/ad-revenue-share deal (Google AdSense for Search, Bing/Yahoo Syndicated Search Partners) could offset the API cost — both pay only on AD CLICKS, meaning ads inside the family's private chat, plus Google's own minimum-traffic gate a small family app won't clear; wrong shape for this product at any volume. Owner call: skip the paid path — `SEARCH_PROVIDER` stays dormant, and the no-provider case (the expected steady state) is upgraded from a dead-end apology to the real answer: `search_web`'s fallback now returns a zero-cost `https://www.google.com/search?q=…` link (never fetched by us, ADR 0135/0138's posture) as a tappable card, and the tool description now splits on evergreen-vs-business rather than the model's self-judged confidence (a confidently-recognized restaurant still gets the staleness caveat + link, since recognizing a place and knowing it's still open/good are different claims) **Addendum 2026-10-05**: "Reminder to email milo tomorrow at 9a" failed with `group "John Doe" did not resolve` - the model put the ASKER's own name in `withGroupName`. `resolveWriteGroup` now fronts `create_task` / `add_list_item` / `save_place`: a real group is tried first, and only then a name that is the asker (display/first/preferred/full name, username, "me", "myself") falls back to the turn's own group; any other unmatched name is still refused with candidates, and `set_email_route` keeps the strict resolver. Tool descriptions tell the model not to do it. The chat's receipt line shows a warning mark, not a tick, when every tool call in the turn was refused or failed. **Addendum 2026-10-25**: the named-group write (`withGroupName`) is removed; an item is created in the chat it was asked in (ADR 0232 addendum) |
| [0166](./docs/notes/0166-a-pending-question-needs-a-server-side-referent.md) | **A pending question needs a server-side referent**: prod — "Invite Karl dale" created the event and asked for Karl's email (ADR 0157's "pick from Contacts" card); a later plain "Karl is at karl@media.example" did nothing, only an explicit "update the invite to include X" worked. Same insight as ADR 0093 (tool results don't survive a turn — `buildConvo` replays prose only) applied to 0157's question instead of a numbered task list: 0157 shipped the instruction telling the model to call `update_event` once given an email, but nothing told it, turn after turn, WHICH event was still waiting — that fact lived only in the bot's own long-past chat sentence. New `server/src/services/bot/pending-invites.ts` (TTL-bounded map, mirrors `enumeration.ts`) records `{eventId, eventTitle, name}` whenever `create_event`/`update_event` returns `unresolvedInvitees`; a new `context.ts` block reads it into the system prompt every turn (personal or group) until resolved, naming the event id explicitly. Also reinforced the tool-firing trigger lists (answering a pending reminder counts even with no imperative verb), added `.describe()` to `invitees` (the block comment explaining it was stripped by `zodToJsonSchema` before reaching the model), and fixed `TEXT_FENCE_TOOL_PROMPT`'s tool catalogue, which was missing `update_event` entirely. **Audit**: the same "candidate id dies with the turn" shape recurs, unfixed, in `delete_event`/`update_event` ambiguous-title matches, `complete_task`/`update_task` ambiguous-title matches (0093 knowingly left this one), and `set_leg_passenger`/`set_trip_passengers` — deferred as follow-up rather than bundled into this fix. "Exact name or ask" resolvers (ADR 0077/0094/0161) are lower-risk: the user retypes a name, not an id, so a late reply just means "ask again," never a silent wrong write. Rejected: a deterministic pre-LLM email-address interceptor (ambiguous with >1 pending invite, and an email in a message isn't always answering one), fixing every audited surface in this same change (bundles unrelated fixes into one incident response) |
| [0167](./docs/notes/0167-a-year-less-ticket-needs-a-today-to-resolve-against.md) | **A year-less ticket needs a "today" to resolve against**: prod (`ownertest@gmail.com`) — three real, upcoming bookings (Denver round-trip, Toronto flight, Vail hotel) never became trips, verified live: the threads are minable by every gate ADR 0158 checks, but each already carries an `email_extraction_marks` row so `runEmailMagics` will never look again, and one left behind a `create_event` "Flight to Denver" — correctly dated a year ahead — that expired unconfirmed and was never a trip anyway. The email itself is a complete, legible Booking.com itinerary with **no year on the departure** ("Thu, Feb 11 · 8:55 PM") — ordinary for a ticket, since the reader already knows "now." `bot/trip_classifier.ts`'s shared prompt (`buildTextPrompt`/`buildVisionPrompt`, used for every kind — flight/hotel/restaurant/movie/calendar/recipe) carried no `Today:` line, unlike `magics.service.ts`'s sibling extractor which does — so the model's year guess is unconstrained, and `stageFlight`'s correct-by-design "nothing past ever surfaces" filter (ADR 0158 §4) cannot tell a genuinely past booking from a wrong-year guess; a bad guess gets silently discarded, `legArgs.length === 0` → `'none'`. Fix: `today` (`YYYY-MM-DD`, traveller's own zone — ADR 0074's rule) becomes a **required** param of `classifyTravelDocument` and the injectable `TravelClassifier` seam, threaded from both callers (`stageEmailTripProposal` via the same `dayOf` helper `stageFlight`/`stageHotel` already use for their own filter; `classifyAndAutoFileAttachment` via `uploaderTzOffsetMinutes`) — required, not optional-with-a-silent-fallback-to-the-server-clock, the exact mistake ADR 0098 diagnosed for `nextScanDate`. Existing `TravelClassifier` test doubles (ignoring all arguments) stay valid — TS allows a function with fewer declared params to satisfy a type expecting more. **Does not retroactively fix the three already-burned threads** — their `'magic'` mark is never cleared by design (ADR 0110), and re-mining them is a deliberate one-off operational step against real prod data, left as a follow-up rather than bundled in. Rejected: a silent server-clock fallback (reintroduces ADR 0098's bug), fixing only the flight/hotel path (leaves restaurant/movie/calendar dates with the identical ambiguity), clearing extraction marks as part of this change (an unconditional prompt fix and a one-time operational decision on real user data are independent) |
| [0168](./docs/notes/0168-a-cancelled-trip-is-cancelled-not-silent.md) | **A cancelled trip is cancelled, not silent**: prod — an American Express Travel "Cancellation: your trip to Vail" email arrived for a hotel stay already on the Trips tab. Traced against the code: the trip classifier's `Classification` schema (`bot/trip_classifier.ts`) has exactly seven kinds — flight/hotel/restaurant/movie/recipe/calendar/other — **none of them "cancellation."** Given this email's content (property, address, room type, dates) the model returns `kind:"hotel"` with the same property/dates as the original booking; `findExistingAccommodation` happens to dup-match it (same property+dates), so nothing gets re-proposed — but nothing ever marks the EXISTING booking cancelled either, since no status column exists on `trips`/`travel_legs`/`accommodations` at all. The Vail trip stays in the database looking exactly as live as before Amex cancelled it. That "no duplicate" outcome is coincidental (identical property/dates restated) — a cancellation with a different confirmation format would instead stage a duplicate proposal for a trip that no longer exists. Distinct from ADR 0074's `.ics METHOD:CANCEL` handling (calendar invites, keyed on UID — this is a marketing-style email with no UID) and from ADR 0138's flight-status side channel (an airline's own delay/cancel notice, stored in `flight_json`, never touches `travel_legs`). Fix: an eighth kind, `cancellation` (`cancelsKind:"hotel"|"flight"` + whichever identifying fields — propertyName/dates or legs/confirmation — the email restates, reusing exactly the fields `findExistingAccommodation`/`findExistingLeg` already match on). `stageEmailTripProposal` routes it to new `applyCancellation` (`email/trip-extraction.ts`) — **applied directly, never staged as a proposal** (the notice IS the confirmation, ADR 0074's `applyInviteCancellation` posture) — which locates the trip via `findTripByDateRange`, matches the specific accommodation/leg via new looser matchers (`findAccommodationToCancel`/`findLegToCancel`, confirmation-or-property+dates / confirmation-or-route+date, cancelled rows excluded), and sets `cancelled_at` (migration `075`, nullable on `trips`/`travel_legs`/`accommodations` — `trip_reservations` untouched, nothing cancels one today). A trip auto-cancels (`maybeCancelTrip`) only when it has at least one leg/accommodation and every one is now cancelled — a still-live return leg keeps the trip alive even after its outbound cancels. **No match found is a safe no-op** (ADR 0133's asymmetry: a missed cancellation costs nothing extra, a wrong match cancels a real trip). `findTripByDateRange` now excludes cancelled trips (`cancelled_at IS NULL`) so a rebooking for the same dates lands on a fresh trip, never the dead one; `findUpcomingFlights` and `magics-context.ts`'s world snapshot exclude cancelled legs/trips so the bot never casually mentions one as upcoming. Surfaced, not just stored: `Trip.cancelledAt`/`TravelLeg.cancelledAt`/`Accommodation.cancelledAt` on the existing DTOs, a "CANCELLED" badge on the Trips tab card, folded into the existing "▸ Earlier trips" disclosure alongside past trips — visible and searchable, never deleted. **Live-verified**: the real prod Amex email now classifies as `kind:"cancellation"` with the exact matching property/dates/confirmation. **Email-body path only** — `classifyAndAutoFileAttachment` (uploaded screenshots) explicitly no-ops on `kind:"cancellation"` rather than falling into its hotel `else` branch (which would have crashed the type, and would have mis-filed it as a new booking). Does not retract a still-pending (unconfirmed) proposal for the same booking if a cancellation arrives before confirm — left to the 72h TTL; does not retroactively fix a trip already mined stale before this shipped (its `'magic'` mark needs a one-off clear, same follow-up shape as 0167). Rejected: reusing `applyInviteCancellation`'s UID keying (no UID on a marketing email), staging the cancellation as a confirmable proposal (nothing left to decide), hard-deleting the row (loses the record — ADR 0092's reasoning), teaching the classifier to also detect a *changed* (not cancelled) booking in the same change (a harder, separate problem) |
