| [0169](./docs/notes/0169-a-partial-cancellation-leaves-a-gap-to-flag.md) | **A partial cancellation leaves a gap to flag**: extends 0168 — cancelling only the hotel on a trip that still has flights booked (or the reverse) leaves a real gap nobody is told about; a badge on the Trips tab only helps someone who happens to open it. `applyCancellation` now enqueues a proactive nudge (new `ProactiveMessageKind` `trip_cancellation`) after marking the match cancelled, reusing the EXISTING proactive-message machinery (ADR 0042/0137) rather than a bare `postIndexed`-style chat insert — this fires from a background magics pass with no user turn triggering it, so it needs the same WhatsApp-window/quiet-hours gating `event_created_nudge` and the reconnect warnings already get, or it repeats the exact bug ADR 0137 fixed. `renderTripCancellationNudge` is **deterministic** (no DB read, no LLM call — everything needed was already known when `applyCancellation` ran), following `renderEventConflictNudge`'s template pattern rather than `event_created_nudge`'s LLM-rendered trip half, since a coordination gap is a plain fact, not a phrasing judgment. `TripCancellationPayload` carries pre-formatted `cancelledItems`/`remainingItems` strings; empty `remainingItems` still fires (a full-trip cancellation is news too — *"Your Vail trip is now cancelled"*), non-empty fires the gap message (*"Heads up — the hotel was cancelled, but you still have [flights] booked. Want me to update anything?"*) — ends with an open question rather than guessing rebook-vs-drop, per the owner's framing ("user can act as they want"). Gating: **not** `isScheduledBriefing` (WhatsApp-window-gated, not quiet-hours-exempt, same as `event_created_nudge`), bypasses the cross-kind rate limit (ADR 0012's reasoning — as real-time-triggered as a create), **not** gated behind ADR 0079 billing entitlement (zero LLM cost, ADR 0117's "being told your own event arrived" posture), 2-hour staleness cutoff. Posted to the trip's own `groupId` (not always the actor's personal group, unlike `enqueueEventNudge` — a bundle-confirmed trip can live in a shared family group per ADR 0097). Best-effort: an enqueue failure is caught and logged, never fails the cancellation itself. Rejected: a bare chat message bypassing the proactive queue (skips every ADR 0137 gating rule), LLM-rendered text (restates already-structured data for cost and latency), silence on a full-trip cancellation (still news worth one line), a buttoned action card (real value, bigger surface — noted as a follow-up) |
| [0170](./docs/notes/0170-sse-fanout-over-redis-pubsub.md) | **SSE fanout over Redis pub/sub**: `sseHub` (`services/sse.hub.ts`) was in-process only (ADR 0002's known limitation) — a hard wall against ever running more than one Node process, surfaced while planning ahead of growth (not an active incident). `REDIS_URL` unset (default) keeps it byte-for-byte identical to before — dev/CI/`npm run sim` need no Redis. Set, and `broadcast()` still writes local subscribers directly AND `PUBLISH`es `{origin: nodeId, channel, event, data}` on a fixed `sse:fanout` channel; a second dedicated `SUBSCRIBE` connection (ioredis can't multiplex commands on a subscribing connection) replays it into local delivery on every OTHER process, origin-tag echo suppression rather than a mutable "just sent this" flag (correct under concurrent broadcasts by construction). Public API of `sseHub` unchanged — none of the ~90 existing `broadcast()`/`broadcastToUser()` call sites moved. **Fixes only the transport half** of running >1 process: the four `onBroadcast` singleton consumers (push-publisher, todos-mirror, IM outbound-publisher, proposal-resolution-notifier), the proactive scheduler, and IMAP IDLE all still assume exactly one process and would duplicate (pushes, outbound sends, mailbox connections) if simply started on every node — a web/worker process split (pinning those to one worker instance) plus a BullMQ-backed job queue for bot-turn/mail-sync/proactive AI work are the follow-up, not yet their own ADR. Rejected: Postgres `LISTEN`/`NOTIFY` (ADR 0038 already bans it — doesn't survive transaction-mode pooling), Redis-only delivery even for local-node events (makes Redis a hard dependency for dev/CI/sim and adds a round-trip to latency-sensitive bot-token streaming). |
| [0171](./docs/notes/0171-a-deleted-trip-is-recoverable.md) | **A deleted trip is recoverable, and a trip is named for its destination**: prod — tapping "x" to clear one cancelled hotel booking hard-deleted (`ON DELETE CASCADE`) two unrelated live Southwest flight legs sharing the same trip shell; the itinerary was gone from Chord even though the flights still existed on Google Calendar (ADR 0158's mirror events are independent rows). `trips.deleted_at` (migration `076`, mirrors ADR 0168's `cancelled_at` shape) makes trip delete **soft**: `deleteTrip` sets it, `restoreTrip` clears it, children are NOT cascaded so restoring brings the whole itinerary back; Trips tab gets a third disclosure — **"Deleted trips · N"** with a DELETED badge + "↺ Restore". `findTripByDateRange`/`pickActiveTrip` exclude deleted trips (a rebooking after delete is a fresh trip, never a silent resurrection). New `removeTravelLeg`/`removeAccommodation` give the actual prod complaint ("drop the cancelled hotel, not the whole trip") a real scalpel — a hard per-row delete exposed as "Remove" on an already-`cancelled` row (no "Deleted leg" concept, a line item isn't worth its own undo UI) — and `maybeDeleteEmptyTrip` runs after every removal, soft-deleting the trip only when legs AND accommodations AND reservations are all now zero (a lingering dinner reservation keeps the trip alive even with every leg/stay gone). Second defect: `stageHotel`/`ensureTripForHotel` named a hotel-only trip after the PROPERTY, not the destination, so a rebooking at a different hotel forked a second differently-named trip for the same dates instead of extending the first. New optional `city` on the classifier's `Hotel` schema, read by the SAME LLM call that already reads `propertyName`/`address` (no second call); `hotelTripCity()` prefers it, falls back to a best-effort `address` parse, only falls through to the old property-name behavior when neither exists. The live bot-tool path (no structured classifier involved) gets the same rule as a **prompt instruction** on `create_trip`'s description in both tool catalogues — no server-side hook can tell a hotel-only `create_trip` apart from any other at call time, the ADR 0100-style "tell the model, that's the whole fix" case. Rejected: a confirm dialog on trip-level delete (doesn't fix a correctly-targeted tap on the wrong button), cascade-delete-but-tombstone (still loses the itinerary), a `deleted_at` on legs/accommodations too (no product need surfaced for undoing a single line-item removal), regex-only city extraction (address formats too varied), an executor guard rewriting a live-chat `create_trip`'s name after the fact (no reliable "this is hotel-only" signal exists yet when `create_trip` runs). **Addendum — `update_trip`**: recovering the motivating prod trip exposed that nothing could correct a trip's own name/destination/dates after creation (a wrong year from a source with no printed one, resolved against the wrong anchor); new bot tool mirrors `set_trip_passengers`'s `tripId`/`tripQuery` resolution, patches only the fields supplied, rejects an inverted date range — deliberately narrow, doesn't touch legs/accommodations/reservations/participants. |
| [0172](./docs/notes/0172-web-worker-process-split.md) | **Web/worker process split**: `server.ts` split into a **web role** (`entrypoints/web.ts`→`roles/web.ts`: the whole HTTP API + SSE subscribe routes + the connector registry, since inbound webhooks need an HTTP endpoint) and a **worker role** (`entrypoints/worker.ts`→`roles/worker.ts`: proactive scheduler, mail sync/IMAP IDLE/push-webhook renewal, outbox scheduler, `startPushPublisher`+`startTodosMirror`) — two systemd units (`chord.service`, `chord-worker.service`) in prod; `server.ts` itself becomes a thin dev/CI/sim-only entrypoint running both roles in one process against one shared `db`, so `npm run dev`/`npm run sim`/vitest need zero changes. **Deviates from ADR 0170's text on purpose**: `startOutboundPublisher`/`startProposalResolutionNotifier` are wired *inside* `ConnectorRegistry` itself (`registry.ts`, using the registry as their binding resolver), not standalone calls — splitting them out means splitting the registry's own construction (and its inbound webhooks, which only the web role can serve), a bigger design pass than "move a function call," so both stay in the web role for now; only the two consumers with no such coupling moved. `applyMigrations` gains a session-scoped `pg_advisory_lock` (not `_xact_` — the run spans many statements, not one transaction) since two processes now call it concurrently at boot — without it, a race on a fresh DB has both readers see an empty `_migrations` table and the loser crashes on the unique-constraint insert. New `DB_POOL_MAX` (default 10, unchanged) makes the per-process pool size configurable, since total Postgres connections are now (process count) × this value — the `db/index.ts` comment had anticipated this exact moment since ADR 0002. Purely structural, no user-visible behavior change; verified by running `web.ts`+`worker.ts` as two local processes and confirming a worker-originated broadcast reaches a web-subscribed browser via ADR 0170's Redis adapter. Rejected: moving all four `onBroadcast` consumers as literally specified (needs its own connector-registry design pass), one process silently owning migrations instead of locking (a rule to remember vs. a lock that's self-evidently safe), hardcoding a raised pool size (the right number depends on the VM's `max_connections`, unknowable from the repo). |
| [0173](./docs/notes/0173-two-emails-one-trip-even-before-either-is-confirmed.md) | **Two emails, one trip, even before either is confirmed**: a flight and a hotel for the same vacation routinely arrive as two separate emails, mined independently by the 5-minute sweep — `findTripByDateRange` (ADR 0158) only sees CONFIRMED trips, so if both get mined before either proposal is confirmed (the ordinary case), each stages its own `create_trip` and confirming both produces two trips for one vacation, exactly the fragmentation ADR 0171's prod recovery hit. `stageFlight`/`stageHotel` now also check `findPendingTripBundleId` — a PENDING `create_trip` proposal in the same group whose staged dates overlap — and attach the new email's children to that bundle instead. Needs no other wiring: ADR 0009's bundle-confirm already resolves every proposal sharing a `bundle_id` fresh at confirm time, whatever order they were added in. Checked only as a fallback after the real-trip match. Rejected: blocking the sweep until the first bundle is confirmed (unbounded wait, no clean resolution if the user never opens the app), merging two already-confirmed trips after the fact (strictly harder — reassigning rows between two real trips, one possibly already pushed to Google Calendar — to prevent a problem that's cheap to stop at staging time), widening `findTripByDateRange` itself (it has real-trip-only callers outside the email path, e.g. the live bot-tool `add_travel_leg`, which must never attach to a proposal nobody has agreed to). |
| [0174](./docs/notes/0174-proactive-scan-on-bullmq.md) | **Proactive scan on a BullMQ job queue**: `startProactiveScheduler`'s `setInterval`+`inFlight` pair (`proactive.service.ts`) is a single-process guard — N worker instances (ADR 0172) would each independently poll `proactive_messages` and race. New `proactive.queue.ts`: `REDIS_URL` set → `queue.upsertJobScheduler` (BullMQ **v6**'s Job Scheduler API — the older `add(...,{repeat})`+`removeRepeatable` this session first wrote doesn't exist in `bullmq@^6`, caught by typecheck against the library's real `.d.ts`, not assumed) registers a repeatable job (`every: PROACTIVE_TICK_MS`), a `Worker{concurrency:1}` processor calls the unchanged `drainOnce(db)`; unset → falls back to the original scheduler, same `REDIS_URL`-gated pattern as every other Phase 0-2 fallback. **A non-blocking session-scoped `pg_try_advisory_lock` (`withDrainLock`) wraps every drain pass regardless** — `concurrency:1` only prevents overlap *within one worker process*; the lock is the backstop once the worker role scales past one instance, since BullMQ's own atomic job-claiming stops two workers double-processing *the same* occurrence but not two *different* scheduled occurrences running at once. `db.transaction()` pins one Postgres connection for the lock's lifetime purely as a connection-pin, not for transactional semantics — `drainOnce` still commits its own writes independently. `stop()` deliberately does NOT call `removeJobScheduler` (would open a tick-gap on every ordinary systemd restart; `upsertJobScheduler` is idempotent so leaving the schedule in Redis is harmless). **Both new mechanisms verified against the real thing, not assumed**: `pg_advisory_lock` is scoped **per database, not cluster-wide** — confirmed empirically via `pg_locks.database` (a real OID) before writing `proactive-drain-lock.test.ts`, which is why that test opens two RAW connections to one shared schema-less database rather than using `openTestDb()`'s nested-clone escape hatch (a clone is a *different* database — locks would never contend) or a guessed timing delay (unreliable under real round-trip latency, first draft passed for the wrong reason and had to be rewritten to await B's non-blocking try-lock to completion before releasing A). The BullMQ wiring itself was smoke-tested against a real, disposable Redis (`redis-memory-server`, installed `--no-save`, never committed) — `getJobSchedulers()` showed `iterationCount: 8` over a 3s/500ms-tick window and `getJobCounts().active` never exceeded 1, both confirming the schedule and the concurrency cap work as designed. Rejected: trusting BullMQ's repeat/concurrency alone (the plan's own instruction was to verify first), removing the scheduler on every stop, the nested-clone or fixed-delay test designs (both tried, both wrong for reasons only discovered by testing). **Addendum 2026-10-03 (outage)**: the scheduler had no job options, so every completed tick was kept (5,760/day); 98k of them filled Redis to its 128 MB `noeviction` limit, the three workers then logged the refusal on every poll with no pause, and 23.5 GB of logs filled the disk and took both services down. The tick now carries `removeOnComplete: true` / `removeOnFail: {count: 50}` (`TICK_JOB_OPTS`), old ticks are cleared on start, and every worker `error` handler (and the SSE hub's Redis connections) goes through `queueErrorLogger`: first error at once, then one line a minute with a suppressed count and the error's MESSAGE (the ioredis object printed only the command). No disk or Redis-memory alert exists yet |
| [0175](./docs/notes/0175-a-published-calendar-file-is-not-an-invitation.md) | **A published calendar file is not an invitation**: a forwarded Expedia hotel confirmation, correctly classified `travel/hotels`, still never became a trip proposal — `inviteEventForMessage` runs BEFORE the ADR 0158 `travel/*` short-circuit and treated ANY successfully-parsed `.ics` MIME part as `isInvite: true`, with no look at the iCalendar object's own `METHOD`. Expedia — like airlines, venues, most booking confirmations — attaches a `.ics` so "Add to calendar" works, stamped `METHOD:PUBLISH` (RFC 5546: "here is a calendar object," not "please accept or decline"), so the confirmation got routed around the trip classifier into a bare, unenriched `create_event` — permanently, since the thread marks handled either way (ADR 0137). `probeResultFromIcs` now only treats the message as an invite for the iTIP transaction methods that actually ask something of the recipient — `REQUEST`/`CANCEL`/`REPLY`/`COUNTER`/`DECLINECOUNTER` — with `PUBLISH` **and no `METHOD` at all** (RFC 5545 gives the omission the same non-transactional meaning) falling through to the trip classifier / general magics extractor as if no `.ics` were attached. The `catch` branch (genuinely unparseable) is unchanged — still `isInvite: true`, "we don't know what this is" being a different posture from "we know exactly what this is, and it isn't asking to be answered." Outlook's own probe was checked and needed no fix — it gates on Graph's `meetingMessageType`, which Graph sets only for genuine meeting messages, never merely because a `.ics` is attached. Rejected: keying off `ORGANIZER`/`ATTENDEE` instead (a self-itinerary can list the traveller as their own organizer with no attendees; `METHOD` is the field RFC 5546 actually defines for this), treating method-absent as still an invite (would leave the Expedia-shaped case, and any hand-authored `.ics` omitting `METHOD`, unfixed). |
| [0176](./docs/notes/0176-mail-sync-on-bullmq.md) | **Mail sync on a BullMQ job queue**: `sweepMailboxes`'s sequential one-mailbox-at-a-time loop (`mirror-sweep.ts`) hands each candidate to a registered enqueuer when `REDIS_URL` is set, falling back to the direct sequential call otherwise. New `mail-sync.queue.ts`: `Worker{concurrency: MAIL_SYNC_QUEUE_CONCURRENCY}` (default 3) calls the ALREADY-EXISTING `syncAllUserMail` (`accounts.service.ts` — the Email tab's force-sync route already used it, and it's exactly what the sweep's old inline body duplicated; reusing it instead of re-inlining a third copy was a simplification found while implementing). `setMailSyncEnqueuer(fn\|null)` is injected, not imported — mirrors ADR 0118's `setMailboxSyncRunner` indirection exactly. **Corrects the original plan's assumption**: the plan claimed this queue would "replace" `accountSyncs` (per-account, ADR 0080) and the outer `mailSyncRounds`/`afterSyncRuns` (per-user, `gmail.service.ts`) — reading the real code first showed that's wrong and building on it would have quietly weakened live protection. Both existing mechanisms guard a WIDER axis (several call paths — this sweep, the Email tab's route, the daily-scan's own direct calls in `proactive.service.ts` — all reaching the same user/account at once); the new job-id dedup (`mailsync_${userId}`, user-scoped) only ever sees calls through THIS queue and solves a narrower problem (the sweep re-selecting a user whose previous sweep-triggered sync hasn't finished). The processor still calls `syncAllUserMail`, so all three existing coalescers keep doing their original job unchanged. **Caught for real, not assumed**: BullMQ v6 REJECTS a custom job id containing `:` outright ("Custom Id cannot contain :") — the first draft used this codebase's usual `:`-separated key style (`mailsync:${userId}`) and every `enqueue()` call would have thrown in production; caught by the same `redis-memory-server` smoke-test technique ADR 0174 introduced, fixed to `mailsync_${userId}`. The dedup/concurrency mechanics (active job blocks a duplicate, a different user runs concurrently, `removeOnComplete` frees the id for reuse) and the real wiring (`syncAllUserMail` completing through `QueueEvents`, not a count-poll that can race past cleanup) were both verified against real Redis+Postgres, never committed. Three new tests in `mirror-sweep.integration.test.ts` pin the enqueuer branch itself (delegates instead of syncing inline; falls back once cleared; one candidate's enqueue failure doesn't stop the batch) with no Redis needed. Rejected: an account-scoped job key (the plan's own flagged option — wrong granularity once the real coalescing picture was clear), re-inlining the sync body a third time, trusting `add()`'s dedup alone without checking job state first. |
| [0177](./docs/notes/0177-a-parsed-ics-is-deterministic-context-everywhere.md) | **A parsed `.ics` is deterministic context everywhere, not just for trips**: the real Vail booking still didn't become a trip after ADR 0175 — the classifier read the prose correctly except the departure YEAR, which the ticket never printed ("Thu, Feb 11 · 8:55 PM"), and `stageFlight`/`stageHotel`'s "nothing past ever surfaces" filter (ADR 0158 §4) silently drops a wrong-year-into-the-past guess as if the booking didn't exist. `InviteProbeResult.event` now populates regardless of `isInvite` (a PUBLISH/no-`METHOD` self-itinerary still parses to a real `ParsedIcsEvent` — only the ROUTING decision depended on `METHOD`); `magics.service.ts` hoists it into `icsDateHint` (fed to `stageEmailTripProposal` as before, unchanged) and a new `icsContext` block, both set together the moment a non-invite probe returns an event. **Generalized past trips on explicit owner instruction** ("for everything, not just trips"): `icsYearCorrection`/`shiftYear` (unchanged pure functions) now also run as a backstop against the GENERAL magics extractor's own `event.startsAt`/`endsAt`/`task.dueAt` output, and `icsContext` (title/start/end/location) is appended to `EXTRACT_SYSTEM`'s prompt as "Attached calendar file (authoritative for date/time)" — grounding, never a replacement; the model still reads the body for what a bare VEVENT can't hold (confirmation number, address, why the recipient must act). Zero cost/behavior change on the overwhelming majority of mail carrying no `.ics`. Rejected: fixing only the trip classifier (the ADR 0158 mistake restated — two pipelines read the same ambiguity and only one gets fed), feeding `icsContext` to `person-extraction.ts` (a VEVENT has no attendee list to ground a people-fact pass with — the mechanism generalizes to date-extracting passes, not every pass touching a `.ics`-bearing thread), replacing the LLM read with the `.ics` outright (ADR 0074/0158's reasoning — the calendar file is usually thinner than the body), an unconditional year correction with no month/day agreement check (a genuinely different date on the `.ics` must not overwrite the model's own read). |
| [0178](./docs/notes/0178-bot-turn-on-bullmq.md) | **Bot turns on a BullMQ job queue** (Phase 5, last of the scaling effort — deliberately done last as the highest-risk phase): `reactToMessage`'s per-group tail (ADR 0155) hands each turn to a registered enqueuer when `REDIS_URL` is set, falling back to the direct `runBotTurn` call otherwise. New `bot-turn.queue.ts`: `Worker{concurrency: BOT_TURN_QUEUE_CONCURRENCY}` (default 8) caps simultaneous LLM streams process-wide — today's fallback has no such cap, so a burst across many groups launches one concurrent stream per group unbounded. Job payload carries ids only (`{groupId, groupKind, messageId, tz, sourceBinding, chordVisible}`), never the live `Message` row; the processor re-fetches it and calls the UNCHANGED `runBotTurn`. **Deviates from the original plan**: the queue is started from `roles/web.ts`, not `roles/worker.ts` — reading `runBotTurn`'s silent-turn (`chordVisible: 0`, IM-bridge) delivery path first showed it calls `connectorRegistry.getForBinding(...).send(...)`, and the connector registry only exists in the web role (ADR 0172's own reason for pinning two of its four `onBroadcast` consumers there). Running the worker in the worker role would have silently broken every silent WhatsApp/Slack/Discord/Telegram reply the moment `REDIS_URL` was set. Given the worker stays co-located with the web role rather than moving to a separately-scalable tier, this phase's real win is the concurrency cap plus a queue seam a future connector-registry split can relocate, not moving AI work off the HTTP-serving process. **Deliberately at-most-once, not at-least-once**: BullMQ's `Worker` defaults `maxStalledCount` to 1 (a crashed worker's job is auto-redelivered once, independent of `attempts` — verified against the real installed `bullmq@6` types), which is wrong here specifically because a bot turn's ADR 0009 propose-mode tool calls have no idempotency key — redelivering a turn that crashed mid-way would rebuild LLM context from scratch (now including its own half-finished proposals) and risks duplicating them. `attempts: 1` + `maxStalledCount: 0` turn that off, matching today's existing at-most-once semantics (a mid-turn crash already just loses the reply, never retried) rather than silently upgrading to something that needs new idempotency machinery. `BOT_TURN_QUEUE_LOCK_MS` (default 5 min) must stay well past the slowest realistic turn (up to `MAX_ITERATIONS`=20 LLM round-trips) as a direct consequence — with redelivery off, an expired lock fails a still-running turn outright. **Per-group ordering (ADR 0155) needed no new mechanism**: since the worker runs in the same process as every `reactToMessage` call, the existing in-memory `groupTurnTails` tail already guarantees it — the tail doesn't advance until `enqueue()` resolves, which doesn't happen until the queued job finishes (`job.waitUntilFinished(queueEvents)`). The plan's proposed non-blocking `pg_try_advisory_lock`/re-enqueue-on-delay backstop (needed only if the worker could run on a different node than the enqueue call) was deliberately NOT built with nothing real to verify it against, on this migration's own established discipline of verifying lock behavior against real infrastructure before trusting it (ADR 0174's rewritten test is the precedent) — left as a named follow-up for whenever the web role scales past one instance or the connector registry splits out. **Verified against real infra**: a throwaway `attempts:1`/`maxStalledCount:0` worker configured to always throw was invoked exactly once, and `waitUntilFinished` correctly rejected rather than hanging or silently resolving; the full path (enqueue → real BullMQ worker → `runBotTurn` → mock LLM → tool execution → persisted reply + list row) was run end-to-end against a real disposable Redis and a real migrated Postgres test DB. Rejected: worker role placement per the original plan (breaks silent-bridge replies), splitting the connector registry now (bigger deferred design pass, mirrors ADR 0172's own deferral), building the advisory-lock mechanism with nothing to test it against, leaving BullMQ's default stalled-job redelivery on (the duplicate-proposal risk), and redesigning propose-mode tool calls for idempotency now (out of scope — "add the queue," not "redesign propose-mode"). |
| [0183](./docs/notes/0183-a-leg-can-move-without-its-trip.md) | **A leg/accommodation can move without its whole trip**: ADR 0171 made a whole-trip drag-to-merge mis-drop recoverable, but nothing let a user fix a mis-drop noticed later, or split ONE leg/stay out of a trip without dragging everything else along. `moveTravelLeg`/`moveAccommodation` (`trips.service.ts`) re-parent a single row onto a different, already-existing trip inside a transaction — strict group scope on BOTH trips, widen the TARGET's date range the same way `addTravelLeg` widens it for a fresh leg (source dates left alone), fold the source away via the existing `maybeDeleteEmptyTrip` if this was its last item (same recoverable soft-delete every other trip-emptying path uses); moving onto the item's own current trip is a no-op success rather than a special-cased error. New routes `POST .../legs/:legId/move` and `.../accommodations/:accomId/move` mirror the existing per-item DELETE routes' auth/broadcast shape exactly. Mobile gets a per-row long-press-drag beside the existing whole-card drag — since a row now sits INSIDE a card that's itself a long-press-pan drag target, the row's `Gesture.Pan()` calls `.blocksExternalGesture(cardPan)` (the card's own gesture object threaded down via a `rowDrag` prop bundle) so one touch is never read as both "move this leg" and "merge this whole trip"; row-dragging reuses the whole-card drag's `DropRect`/`hitTestRect`/`currentHit` hit-testing and TripsSection's `cardRefs`, refactored into a shared `measureCardRects(ids)`. New bot tools `move_leg`/`move_accommodation` resolve the item by the same descriptor shape `set_leg_passenger` already uses and the target trip the same exact-name-or-ask way `update_trip`'s `resolveTripId` already does; `findMatchingAccommodations` is new (mirrors `findMatchingLegs`, had no accommodation-side counterpart until now). Rejected: a small drag-handle icon per row (worse hit target than long-press-anywhere), one unified gesture branching on what it landed on (collapses two independently-reasoned actions into one state machine), shrinking the source trip's date range back down after a move (unnecessary — gaps are computed from what's actually placed on a trip, not the date range alone), a `move_reservation` tool/gesture (out of scope — reservations aren't part of the whole-trip merge's mental model the way legs/stays are) |
| [0180](./docs/notes/0180-a-read-email-earns-its-own-pass.md) | **A read email earns its own pass, whatever gate would have skipped it**: opening a thread (`GET /me/email/threads/:id`) now unconditionally runs one extraction pass on THAT thread alone, bypassing every background gate (bulk/discretionary/archived/unread-age/once-ever `magic` mark) — none of those gates' reasoning holds once a person already has the message open, and a phishing-shaped email is exactly the kind the bulk gate is likely to have skipped. New `runReadTimeEmailMagics` shares the real extraction+staging logic with the background sweep (`extractAndStage`, factored out of `runEmailMagics`'s per-thread body) rather than a second copy — same dedup (`shouldStageEventProposal`/`shouldStageTaskProposal`/`shouldStageListItemProposal`/`threadAlreadyProposed`), ADR 0085's own lesson. New suggestion kind `advisory` — nothing actionable, but the message itself is the concern (phishing/scam tell) — stored on the thread (`read_advisory_json`), rendered as a caution banner, never staged as a proposal, never co-occurring with an action suggestion for the same message; ordinary mail still resolves to `none` and renders nothing. **Once per thread, not once per open**: a new `read` extraction-mark kind (widens `email_extraction_marks.kind`'s CHECK, migration `077`) — independent of `magic`, since the two passes answer different questions and a thread the sweep skipped or already mined still gets its own interactive outcome; a later re-open re-serves the stored result rather than re-asking the LLM. Awaited inline in the thread-open route (ADR 0072's on-demand `summarize_email` latency shape), best-effort (a failure never fails the thread open), behind `EMAIL_READ_TIME_MAGICS_ENABLED` (default on) since — unlike every background pass — this is a real per-open cost by design. Rejected: loosening the background sweep's own gates instead (defeats 0109/0127/0143/0179, which stay correct for the unattended case), a simplified interactive-only extractor with its own staging (the 0085 mistake repeated), letting advisory co-occur with an action (a scam should not also get a task inviting compliance), re-running on every re-open (not a new signal — the `read` mark makes it idempotent like `magic`/`person`) **Addendum 2026-11-10**: it now runs ADR 0074's invite check first (`stageInviteIfInvite`, shared with the sweep) - relying on dedup let an Outlook invite with no time in its body get a model-guessed midnight card that then blocked the real one; a failed check is retried, and an invite card withdraws a model card from the same thread. It also sits behind AI consent and the allowance like the sweep (all doors: open, prefetch, post-sync warm-up), writing no mark when stopped |
| [0179](./docs/notes/0179-a-discretionary-broadcast-is-not-a-to-do.md) | **A discretionary broadcast is not a to-do**: a CVS coupon ("$4 off, redeem by Sep 16") became a `create_task`; an NYAC member newsletter (a page of dated club happenings) would stage a `create_event`. Neither is an obligation — nobody is waiting, the reader opted into nothing, ignoring it costs nothing — but the magics pipeline only ever tested "is there an imperative and a date". Two composed causes: the ADR 0127 bulk gate is **bypassed** when the classifier tags `action='to_do'` (its `to_do` definition — "an appointment to book, an RSVP" — is loose enough to catch a time-boxed coupon or "reservations recommended") or when the thread lands in `Events & invites` (`ALWAYS_MINE`); and `EXTRACT_SYSTEM` has no concept of "solicited" — a coupon expiry is a date, a club dinner series has fixed times, so both file. Fix is prompt + deterministic choke point (ADR 0100/0102 precedent): **(1)** the classifier's `to_do` bullet now calls a coupon / sale / un-registered event broadcast `informational` (so the gate works as designed and the ADR 0086 badge drops); **(2)** `EXTRACT_SYSTEM` gains a rule — emit a task/event ONLY when a specific person is waiting, the recipient has already committed (booking / registration / ticket / payment owed), or a stated deadline has a real consequence — with the coupon and club-calendar worked examples beside ADR 0132's permission-slip one; **(3)** `runEmailMagics` resolves each thread's **top-level** category (walking `parent_id`) and excludes any thread under `Coupons` or `Newsletters` from the pass entirely — not marked mined (ADR 0143: a policy change must revisit); **(4)** `retractDiscretionaryBroadcastProposals` runs in the per-pass sweep beside `retractPastEventProposals`, expiring pending `gmail:` `create_task`/`create_event`/`add_list_item` proposals whose thread is now under one of those buckets (retroactive, no migration, self-heals on reclassify). Rejected: prompt-only (0100 precedent — instruction alone insufficient), keeping the extractor call on `Coupons`/`Newsletters` to capture delivery/flight (a retailer's shipment is `Deliveries`, not `Coupons` — nothing to capture), blanket-suppressing `Events & invites` (a personal Evite genuinely lives there and is genuinely an RSVP — left to parts 1+2), a `basis` enum on every suggestion (more model burden than the two-line rule buys), a whole-pile sweep (ADR 0143 — no signal beyond the stored category). Residual: a promo misfiled into `Events & invites` is still exposed to the extractor; a genuinely actionable message misfiled under `Coupons`/`Newsletters` is now skipped (safe direction — the classifier does not route transactional mail there) |
| [0181](./docs/notes/0181-a-shared-event-invites-once-and-a-stale-calendar-goes-unnoticed.md) | **A shared event invites once, and a stale calendar goes unnoticed**: prod — "invite hannah, victor and scott" on a family-group meeting left a guest looking invited by a confusing "Family…" identity, and separately, moving that event to Personal in Google Calendar left a stale Family duplicate the user had to delete by hand. Two independent defects. **(1)** A fanned-out group event (ADR 0040 §7) gives every member their own row, all sharing one placeholder `ical_uid` until each is individually pushed (`markPushed` only stamps the real Google iCalUID onto the row being pushed, not its siblings); `create_event`'s invitee handling (ADR 0157) writes `event_attendees` keyed on that shared placeholder in the same request, well before any fire-and-forget push completes, and `pushDirtyEvents` attached that guest list to EVERY dirty row with no `role` check — so every family member's independent push re-sent the same invitation from their own account. Fixed by gating attendee attachment/`sendUpdates=all` on `row.role === 'organizer'`; a solo event's single row is always organizer, so its behavior is unchanged. **(2)** Two-way calendar sync (ADR 0040) only ever ran opportunistically (a bot call, a foreground refresh, the manual sync route) — unlike mail, which hit the identical shape and got a periodic sweep (ADR 0144/0159) — so an event edited directly in Google Calendar between sessions sat unreconciled until the user next happened to trigger one of those. New `sweepCalendars` (mirroring `sweepMailboxes`) runs the existing, idempotent `syncTwoWay` for stalest-first calendar-connected users on the same proactive tick, gated by `CALENDAR_SWEEP_{ENABLED,MINUTES,BATCH}` (`MINUTES` matches `MAIL_SWEEP_MINUTES` — no LLM-spend question to reason through, since calendar sync makes no LLM calls, only Google API quota). Rejected: refusing to push guest-bearing events to a shared calendar at all (a real product trade-off — a family member would lose visibility into the event on the calendar they check — not a pure bug fix), adding a Chord-side `update_event` calendar-move (bigger feature than this incident's evidence justifies), re-keying `event_attendees` by event id (doesn't address the actual leak, which is every sibling's push reading the same key). |
| [0182](./docs/notes/0182-an-unmatched-calendar-request-is-not-no-request.md) | **An unmatched calendar request is not no request**: prod — told to add an appointment "to your personal calendar," the bot replied that it did, and the event landed on Family instead. Two composed defects. **(1)** `resolveTargetCalendarId` treated a requested calendar name that matched NOTHING (not even ambiguously) the same as no name at all, falling through to the group's cached default — but a requested-and-unmatched name is a different situation than no request; the user expressed specific intent, and substituting a past request's cached calendar (Family) answers a different question. ADR 0137 already drew this line for the ambiguous (2+ match) case; the zero-match case fell through unhandled. Now returns `{calendarId:'primary', source:'primary', requestedNotFound: name}`, skipping the group/user default entirely — primary is always real and writable, the group default might be exactly what the user was avoiding. **(2)** `create_event`'s tool result never said which calendar was used AT ALL, successful or not — so the model had nothing but the user's own words to report back, the ADR 0102 failure on the success path instead of the failure path. New `calendarLabel` helper + a summary note (`· added to your <label> calendar` or, on a miss, `· could not find a calendar named "<name>" — used your <label> calendar instead`) with an explicit instruction not to substitute the user's own wording. Rejected: broadening `PRIMARY_ALIASES` to catch more phrasings (treats this one symptom, the next phrasing variant resurfaces it), asking rather than defaulting (nothing to enumerate — there's no candidate list, only "that name doesn't exist"), fixing only the resolution fallthrough without grounding the tool result (leaves the model ungrounded for any other path that reaches the same confabulation). **Addendum 2026-10-04**: the destination is only true if the event can get there - a user whose Google grant had been refused that morning was told "added to your primary calendar" for an event that was saved and queued (`local_dirty = 1`) but could not push. `googleCalendarConnection` (`calendar-write.service.ts`): `connected` keeps the note; `expired` (token + scopes cleared on `invalid_grant`, `google_connected_at` still set) says "saved in Chord ONLY", not on Google or the phone yet, reconnect in Settings; `never` says "saved in Chord" with no calendar claim and no urging. Found with it: 11 of 12 Google connections died exactly seven days after connecting (an OAuth consent screen left in "Testing"), and `maybeSyncCategoryLabels` now returns early without a Gmail grant instead of warning once per thread per pass (8,640 lines a day for one user) |
| [0185](./docs/notes/0185-a-flight-already-on-the-books-is-not-none.md) | **A flight already on the books is not `'none'`**: prod — three forwarded Air India eTicket/itinerary-change emails for one round-trip shared by two passengers. The attachment path (ADR 0071/0158) correctly auto-committed one trip with two `travel_legs`, one per passenger, and synced two calendar events — the user confirms this half worked. But each of the same three threads *also* staged its own standalone `create_event` proposal via the general email-magics extractor, all three confirmed, minting three MORE near-duplicate calendar events for the same flight (five total, tripping ADR 0042's conflict-nudge machinery). Cause: `runEmailMagics`'s `travel/*` short-circuit (ADR 0158) only falls through to the general extractor when `stageEmailTripProposal` returns `'none'` — but `stageFlight`'s matched-existing-trip branch (and `stageHotel`'s matched-accommodation branch) overloaded that SAME sentinel for two different meanings: "not recognized as travel" and "recognized as travel, and every leg/stay already exists on the matched trip" (`findExistingLeg`/`findExistingAccommodation` already found the duplicate and staged nothing new). Both branches now report the classification kind (`'flight'`/`'hotel'`) instead of `'none'` on a duplicate match — mirroring the file's own "already pending" dedup one level up (`if (already) return result.kind;`) — so `runEmailMagics` marks the thread handled instead of handing it to a general extractor that has no idea `travel_legs` exists. Rejected: teaching the general extractor about `travel_legs` (duplicates ADR 0158's own reason for keeping one trip vocabulary), threading `source_raw_context` through the attachment auto-commit path (real gap, but doesn't fix this bug alone — noted as a follow-up), a reason-carrying `'none:duplicate'` variant (wider than the one caller that needs the distinction). |
| [0188](./docs/notes/0188-a-request-with-no-matching-tool-is-a-question.md) | **A request with no matching tool is a question, not a guess**: the tool-firing prompt (`tools.ts`) maps specific phrasings onto a closed catalog (task/list/save/event/trip/…) but said nothing about a request that matches none of them ("add this to a spreadsheet", "build a tracker with columns for X/Y/Z") — and `bot.service.ts`'s imperative-verb retry explicitly forbids replying with prose only, so the only two outcomes were silence (nothing recorded) or the request forced into whichever tool loosely resembled it, reported as success — the ADR 0102 confabulation failure recurring one layer up, since the tool call itself "succeeds," just in the wrong shape. Fix is prompt-only (ADR 0100/0102 pattern): a narrow, explicit exception in `NATIVE_TOOL_PROMPT`, `TEXT_FENCE_TOOL_PROMPT`, and the STRICT MODE retry message — when nothing existing can represent the ask, name the closest primitive in one sentence and ask before acting, never force a mismatched write or stay silent, paired with an explicit "don't use this to dodge a tool call that does fit." `context.ts`'s system-prompt opening also gained a stated capabilities block (what Chord connects to and tracks; that it cannot make bookings/purchases or build a freeform spreadsheet) so the model — and a user who asks "what can you do" — has one honest, bounded answer instead of an improvised one. Rejected: a generic schemaless tracker entity (breaks the closed-typed-vocabulary design every primitive's dedup/archive/search/briefing behavior depends on — the ADR 0149 "second product" trap applied to the core data model), always forcing the nearest tool (today's failure, restated), leaving it silent (worse than asking). |
| [0190](./docs/notes/0190-a-trip-is-a-travelers-chain-not-a-date-window.md) | **A trip is a traveler's own chain of connected legs, not a shared date window**: a prod audit found both directions of `findTripByDateRange`'s defect — a 5-hop Kenya safari split into 5 trips (no date overlap between single-day hops) while a spouse's unrelated 3-day Amtrak trip silently absorbed into a 6-week in-law visit (its dates fell inside the visit's widened window). New rule, in order: **person** — a leg extends whichever of that traveler's own trips it connects to by CITY (`sameMetro`, promoted from `legGaps`-only to the actual matcher, strict `=== true` required — a false merge is worse than a missed one, the opposite asymmetry from `computeLegGaps`'s own tolerant check), then **group** as scope only, never a fallback guess. No identified traveler → live chat refuses and asks (explicit `tripId` overrides skip this — it's not a guess); the unattended email/attachment paths default to a new standalone trip + a "who's this for?" note, correctable via the already-shipped `merge_trips`/`set_trip_passengers`/`move_leg` tools and Trips-tab drag-to-merge (all pre-existing, previously undocumented). Stays have no traveler to chain on — matched by place (`findTripForStay`, new `city` field on `add_accommodation`) instead, one group only, never date alone. `add_travel_leg`'s result now surfaces any uncovered night on the affected trip as a nudge (`gaps`, already computed, previously `get_trip_status`-only) — never a gate, since a gap is sometimes correct (a kid with a bed in two cities) and is "dismissed" by any accommodation covering the span, no new state needed. Existing trips are NOT backfilled by this change — deferred, explicit follow-up once verified. Rejected: `findTripByDateRange` as a fallback for trip/stay identity (owner: "complete override" — kept only for reservation-into-known-trip placement and cancellation lookup), guessing a traveler from geography when unidentified, a parent/child hierarchy for a nested same-person detour (visually worse per the owner), actively searching for a candidate return leg on a manually-stitched one-way (owner: "just a nudge"). **Addendum 2026-09-19**: shared rules live in `trips/chain.ts` — travelers on the **same flight** (same route + same instant) share one trip (checked first, no name needed); a chain **closes** when the traveler returns to where it started; gap caps are onward 30d / return 90d (a 63-day-apart arrival + departure is two trips); one-time **`npm run regroup:trips`** (`trips/regroup.service.ts`) re-files existing trips by the same rules — plan-only unless `--apply`, per-group transaction + undo file, unnamed/cancelled legs pinned, duplicates reported not deleted, emptied trips soft-deleted **Addendum 2026-09-19 (2)**: stays match by **place AND dates** (`findTripForStay`); a **booking reference** is the booking (same reference = same trip; same route + new time = the flight *rescheduled*, updated in place by `rescheduleBookedLegs`, never duplicated; placeholders like `N/A` never count); `findExistingLeg` uses the matcher's name/route/instant rules; pending bundles join only by shared flight / booking reference / the traveler's own chain (or place + dates for stays); cancellations are found across all reachable trips by reference or route + day, and a flight two travelers are on with no reference cancels nothing; the regroup script joins on booking reference too. a **route change** under the same reference is detected exactly where the pairing is unambiguous (`reconcileBookedLegs`: unchanged → moved → replaced, one-for-one / shares-one-end / date-ordered equal counts; ambiguous is offered as an addition, an unmentioned leg is never removed; a lone leg from the bot tool still only detects a time move) |

| [0192](./docs/notes/0192-a-to-do-dies-with-the-event-it-was-for.md) | **A to-do dies with the event it was for**: the "prepare for the water shut-off *today*" briefing line survived 5391a3e because the scan was never reading the deleted event — it was reading an open **to-do** mined from the same email (both carry `gmail:<threadId>` provenance) that nothing retired when the event was removed. ADR 0143's rule, one hop over: every true event removal — `deleteEvent` (route, bot `delete_event`, legacy reverse-delete) and the Google pull's cancelled tombstone, full-sync absence and decline — calls `retireTasksForRemovedEvents` (`events/retire-event-tasks.ts`), which reads provenance + times BEFORE the row is hard-deleted and archives (not completes) the owner's own `open` to-dos from that thread that were **for** the event: undated, or due ≤ the event's end + 12h slack (a follow-up due days later survives); nothing retires if a live twin from the same email still starts within the slack window. `retireTasksByProvenance` gains `dueOnOrBefore`. Side effect fixed with it: the briefing's `RETIRED_TODOS` line ("more than a week past their date") now counts only overdue-sweep retirements (`due ≤ archived_at − TASK_OVERDUE_ARCHIVE_DAYS`), else any source-retired to-do — this one and 0143's — made it false. Not retroactive (no event row left to derive the link from), and a **rescheduled** event is not handled — its prep to-do keeps the old date (natural fix is shifting the to-do with the event; own decision). Rejected: render-time filtering (the event row is hard-deleted, nothing to compare), retiring every same-thread to-do regardless of due date (drops the permission slip when the field-trip event goes), a `source_event_id` column (wouldn't cover the to-dos already in the wild), prompt-side de-emphasis (the data was wrong, not the model) |

| [0193](./docs/notes/0193-a-token-counter-is-visibility-not-the-meter.md) | **A token counter is visibility, not the meter**: owner asked for a per-user Settings token counter (used/total) across bot turns and email AI passes, BYOK excluded. Checked first: nothing captured bot-turn tokens at all — `LlmStreamOptions.onUsage` existed but **no provider's `stream()` ever called it**, only `complete()` did (which is why the email passes, already on `complete()`, could be logged but bot turns never were). Owner call: **visibility only** — `getEntitlement`/`debitCredit`'s flat 1-credit-per-turn gate (ADR 0063/0079) is unchanged; this doesn't replace it. New append-only `token_usage` (migration `084`) — deliberately NOT `credit_ledger`-shaped (no `balance_after`, no enforced-unique `ref`): a duplicate row inflates a display number, not a double charge, so the idempotency machinery real money needs isn't worth it here. "Total" is `TOKEN_QUOTA_PER_MONTH` (static, display-only, not a real plan); "used" is a `SUM ... WHERE created_at >= period_start` computed on read, not a materialized balance that needs a monthly reset job — rejected that shape specifically because "who resets it and when" is the bug class ADR 0092 and ADR 0098 each already had to fix once. BYOK gets **no row recorded at all** ("their key, their cost" — not just hidden from display), gated on `llmTier` (not `entitlement.reason`, which is only computed when `BILLING_ENABLED` — using it would silently over-record BYOK users on billing-off deploys). Bot-turn capture needed new provider plumbing in all three real providers: OpenAI (`stream_options.include_usage`), Gemini (the aggregated final `response` after the stream drains, same `usageMetadata` shape `complete()` already reads), Anthropic (`message_start` for input tokens + `message_delta`'s cumulative `output_tokens`) — summed across every `llm.stream()` round-trip one bot turn makes (the native tool-use loop can iterate). Mock provider deliberately left untouched: its `complete()` never called `onUsage` either, so email passes already show 0 tokens under `LLM_PROVIDER=mock` — making only `stream()` fabricate numbers would make bot turns report usage mock never measured while email stayed silent, for no real gain. Email-pass wiring: `pass-cost.ts`'s `report()` becomes `async` and writes the row when given both `db` and `userId`; `llm-classifier.ts` already had `userId` in scope, but `magics.service.ts`/`person-extraction.ts`'s `trackPassCost()` calls live inside the injectable `EmailExtractor`/`PersonExtractor` seams whose input types deliberately omitted `userId` (test-stub-stability reason, per `pass-cost.ts`'s own comment) — fixed by adding `userId`/`db` as **optional** fields on `MagicThreadInput`/`PersonThreadInput`, populated only by the real callers, zero forced churn on existing test doubles. Surfaced via the existing `GET /me/billing` (`tokensUsedThisMonth`/`tokensQuotaPerMonth` on `BillingStatus`, omitted for BYOK) rather than a new route — the AI Plan Settings card gets one more line beside the credit balance. Rejected: replacing credits with real token-metered billing now (reshapes ADR 0063/0075/0079 for a feature that only needs visibility today), a `credit_ledger`-shaped resettable balance, requiring `userId` on the extractor input types. **Superseded same-day by ADR 0194**: the "visibility only, flat rate unchanged" line above no longer holds — the debit is now the turn's real token cost |

| [0194](./docs/notes/0194-a-turn-costs-what-it-actually-cost.md) | **A turn costs what it actually cost**: ADR 0193 gave Chord real per-turn token data and immediately surfaced the disconnect it sat on top of — `debitCredit` has always subtracted a flat **1 credit per turn** no matter how many tokens that turn spent; a three-word reply and a 20-iteration tool-use loop cost the same 1 credit. Owner call, same day: retire that abstraction, mean real AI tokens everywhere. `bot.service.ts` now debits `result.tokenUsage`'s actual `promptTokens + completionTokens` (the number ADR 0193 already computed, previously unused for billing) instead of `-1`; **`tokenUsage === null` (mock provider, or a stream that threw before reporting) skips the debit entirely** — owner call: unmeasured costs nothing rather than an invented worst-case estimate, which would drain a real balance for mock/dev turns and dress up a guess as a fact in the ADR 0102 sense. "Credits" retired as a *concept*: `debitCredit`→`debitTokens`, `grantCredits`→`grantTokens`, `getCreditBalance`→`getTokenBalance`, `Entitlement.reason`'s `'credits'`→`'tokens'`, `CREDITS_PER_BUNDLE`→`TOKENS_PER_BUNDLE` — all mechanical renames the compiler catches if a call site is missed. **The physical `credit_balance` column and `credit_ledger` table are deliberately NOT renamed** — every migration in this codebase to date is additive (`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`), nothing has ever renamed a column or table, and Postgres's `RENAME COLUMN`/`RENAME TO` have no idempotency guard, so doing it would be this codebase's first non-additive migration; ADR 0070 already decided this exact trade the other way for a bigger, more user-visible rename ("Catchup"→"To Do's" as a label only, route stays `/catchup/*`) — same shape here, rename what people read (functions, API fields, UI copy, the env var), leave the column a legacy internal name nobody outside `billing.service.ts` reads directly. `TOKEN_QUOTA_PER_MONTH` (ADR 0193, the Settings usage-counter denominator) is untouched and stays a separate concept — a flat display number shown even with no billing/balance at all, vs. the ledger's real prepaid amount that varies per user. No balance migration: confirmed with the owner that `BILLING_ENABLED=false` everywhere today and no real Stripe purchase has ever completed, so reinterpreting any existing `credit_balance` row's number as tokens-instead-of-turns costs nothing real. Rejected: keeping "credits" as a label backed by a fixed tokens-per-credit rate (adds a conversion constant nobody asked for), debiting a `MAX_TURN_TOKENS` estimate when usage is unknown, renaming the physical schema too, merging `token_usage` (ADR 0193, deliberately loose idempotency) into the billing ledger (deliberately strict) now that both mean the same unit |

| [0195](./docs/notes/0195-a-subscription-not-a-bundle-and-byok-still-pays-rent.md) | **A subscription, not a bundle — and BYOK still pays rent**: owner moved Stripe from ADR 0075's one-time `mode: 'payment'` bundle to a real `mode: 'subscription'` — a fixed token allowance that resets (never rolls over) every billing period. Second, sharper call: **BYOK now pays too** — ADR 0063 made BYOK unconditionally free reasoning only about LLM cost, but Chord's real cost shape is `fixed (hosting + WhatsApp, ADR 0139/0140) + K × tokens`, and a BYOK user paying $0 of the K term was also paying $0 of the fixed term. Two Stripe products, one mechanism: `STRIPE_PRICE_ID` (system-key plan) + new `STRIPE_PRICE_ID_BYOK` (base-only plan) — both $ amounts live in Stripe's dashboard as always; BYOK stays free (today's behavior) until the owner actually configures the second price ("X is a placeholder"). **`invoice.paid` is the single grant/renewal choke point**, not `checkout.session.completed` — Stripe fires an invoice for every period including the first, so one code path covers signup and every renewal with no separate branch to keep in sync or risk of double-granting the same payment twice; `checkout.session.completed` keeps a narrower job, linking `stripe_customer_id`/`stripe_subscription_id` to the user so `invoice.paid` (keyed by Stripe customer, not our user id) has something to look up. New `resetTokenAllowance` (`billing.service.ts`) **sets** the balance rather than adding — last period's leftover is discarded — idempotent by the Stripe invoice id like every other ledger write; new `credit_ledger.reason` value `subscription_reset`. **Found and fixed a real bug while wiring entitlement**: a leftover token balance after a subscription's `subscription_current_period_end` had passed would otherwise keep granting access forever (balance alone was the old gate) — `getEntitlement` now also checks the period hasn't lapsed for any user with `subscription_plan = 'system'` on record, while a purely promo/admin-granted balance (no subscription on record) is unaffected. BYOK's entitlement branch gets the identical period-end check when the base plan is configured, with a new `byok_unpaid` reason distinct from `none` so the UI points at the right plan. **Cancellation needed no event handler at all** — "keep working until period end" falls straight out of the period-end check already built for the trial: a cancelled subscription just stops producing new `invoice.paid` events, and a failed renewal (Stripe's dunning retries) behaves identically, so writing `customer.subscription.deleted`/`invoice.payment_failed` handlers would only add a second way to reach the same state and a second way to get it wrong. Migration `085` (four nullable `users` columns, additive per ADR 0194's own reasoning) plus widening `credit_ledger`'s `reason` CHECK constraint (dropped and re-added — Postgres's default name for an unnamed column-level CHECK, `<table>_<column>_check`, guessed and verified rather than left unconstrained). `TOKENS_PER_BUNDLE`→`MONTHLY_TOKEN_ALLOWANCE`: same consistency argument ADR 0194 made for "credits" — there is no bundle under a subscription, only a recurring allowance. One-time purchase code removed outright, not kept alongside (no stated need for two buy-token mechanisms). **Unverified against a live Stripe test-mode account** — same honesty posture as ADR 0118/0140: the invoice line-item shape read here (`lines.data[].price.id`, `lines.data[].period.end`) is Stripe's long-stable REST shape, but nothing has round-tripped a real webhook delivery yet. Rejected: handling `customer.subscription.deleted`/`invoice.payment_failed` explicitly (the period-end field already produces the wanted behavior for free), granting on `checkout.session.completed` instead of `invoice.paid` (would need a second code path for renewals, which have no Checkout Session at all), one Stripe price with a BYOK discount instead of two products (coupons are for promotions, not a durable second tier), keeping the one-time bundle as a fallback |

| [0196](./docs/notes/0196-usage-is-the-ledger.md) | **Usage is the ledger**: ADR 0193/0194/0195 built two numbers that looked related but weren't — `token_usage` recorded real cost across EVERY AI surface (bot turns AND all three email passes) but gated nothing, while `credit_balance`/`credit_ledger` gated bot turns but only ever tracked bot-turn spend — email passes checked `getEntitlement` as a binary yes/no and ran FREE if entitled, no debit at all. Owner call: unify onto one number — unless BYOK, real token count across every surface determines cutoff. `token_usage` becomes the sole source of truth: "remaining balance" is now COMPUTED, not stored — `MONTHLY_TOKEN_ALLOWANCE − SUM(token_usage) WHERE created_at >= subscription_period_started_at`. There is no more separate debit step: recording usage (which every consuming call site already did, for ADR 0193's visibility counter) IS the debit now. This deletes the ledger-write path outright, not just simplifies it — `debitTokens`/`resetTokenAllowance`/`grantTokens`/`getTokenBalance`/`insertLedger` all removed from `billing.service.ts`; `bot.service.ts` loses its post-turn debit call (`recordTokenUsage` is now the only write); the `invoice.paid` webhook loses its grant call too — a renewal's entire effect is updating `subscription_plan`/`subscription_period_started_at`/`subscription_current_period_end`, and the fresh allowance falls out automatically the moment the period boundary advances, because the SUM only ever looks at rows since that boundary. **Why this was safe in one pass**: every gating call site already went through `getEntitlement()` — `resolveTurnLlm`, the email `afterSync` gate (`gmail.service.ts`), and the proactive-scan gate all read `ent.reason`/`ent.balance`, none read `users.credit_balance` directly — so fixing `getEntitlement`'s computation was the ENTIRE change, and it's also why email passes are correctly included in the cutoff with **zero changes to `gmail.service.ts`**: its existing `if (!ent.entitled) skip` gate was always structurally correct, just fed a number that ignored half of what it was gating. New `subscription_period_started_at` column (migration `086`) — ADR 0195 only stored period END; Stripe's invoice line carries `period.start` alongside `period.end`, `parseInvoicePaid` now reads both. `credit_balance`/`credit_ledger` are now fully vestigial (not renamed — ADR 0194 already declined that — and now also never written); left in the schema since nothing forces dropping them yet. The two Settings cards (balance-remaining vs. used-of-total) stay visually separate but are now guaranteed consistent by construction: `GET /me/billing` derives "used" as `MONTHLY_TOKEN_ALLOWANCE − ent.balance` for an active subscriber (one query, can't drift), falling back to ADR 0193's original calendar-month display for everyone else (billing off, trial, no subscription — no real allowance to derive from). Trial and BYOK untouched: trial still outranks everything and its turns still write `token_usage` for visibility but `getEntitlement` never subtracts it from anything; BYOK still records nothing to `token_usage` and never touches this balance at all. Rejected: dual-write (keep `credit_ledger` strict, `token_usage` loose, written together) — keeps two numbers unified only by discipline at every call site, exactly the failure mode (one forgotten call site) that caused the bug this ADR fixes; unifying only the display side while leaving email passes unmetered (doesn't actually bound total AI spend, the whole problem statement); dropping `credit_balance`/`credit_ledger` from the schema now (no need forces it); a per-write idempotency key on `token_usage` now that it gates real access (a duplicate row still only costs a sliver of someone's allowance early, never a double charge — the loose-idempotency trade-off ADR 0193 chose still holds). **Addendum 2026-09-22**: new personal-only, read-only `get_billing_status` bot tool answers "do I have a subscription" / "how much have I used" in chat, per this file's every-UI-capability-needs-a-bot-tool-equivalent rule — the `GET /me/billing` assembly logic moved out of the route handler into a new `billing-status.service.ts` (`getBillingStatus(db, userId)`, sitting above both `billing.service.ts` and `llm/factory.ts` to avoid the import cycle those two already have with each other) so the route and the bot tool call the exact same function rather than risk two computations that merely agree |
| [0197](./docs/notes/0197-a-reply-is-written-for-you-and-sent-by-you.md) | **A reply is written for you, and sent by you**: a dashed "Ask Chord to write it · this part is never sent" block in the Email thread's reply box — the user types an instruction ("reply with times that work", "say yes, ask about parking") or taps a quick ask; `POST /me/email/threads/:id/compose` (`EmailComposeReplyRequest`) returns `{ body }` into the ordinary reply box and has NO mailbox side effect, so review-then-Send stays the user's. A second ask revises what is in the box (`draft`). Availability comes from the calendar, never the model: the next 10 days of `busyIntervalsInWindow` (same "busy" as `find_conflicts`) rendered per local day in `users.last_tz`, empty days said explicitly, no calendar/too many events said as UNKNOWN with a `[time]` placeholder instead of invented slots. The thread is fenced as data (instructions inside it ignored). Same model/gate/meter as other AI surfaces: `resolveTurnLlm`, `getEntitlement` → 402, `token_usage` source `email_reply` (migration `087`), BYOK unrecorded, ADR 0102 fixed failure sentence / ADR 0044 fix-your-key. Admitted under ADR 0149 because answering is the pipeline's response half and the calendar is what makes it coordination. Chat parity: `reply_to_email` plus a new read-only, personal-only `get_my_availability` tool returning the same rendering (`services/availability.service.ts`); `reply_to_email`'s description says to call it before offering times. **Addendum**: a bot-drafted reply card shows the whole draft and has an **Edit** button that rejects the proposal first (so it can't also be sent from the card) and then opens the thread with the draft in the reply box (`mobile/src/reply-draft.ts`). Rejected: sending directly, a calendar tool loop, always-on suggestions on open, a time picker instead of free text |
| [0198](./docs/notes/0198-an-inline-image-is-part-of-the-message.md) | **An inline image is part of the message**: "remote images don't load" was really `cid:` images (signature logos, pasted photos) — ADR 0120 left `cid:` unblocked but nothing ever resolved one, and Outlook's `hasAttachments` ignores inline parts so the part list was `[]`. `EmailAttachmentRef.contentId` (kernel `normalizeContentId`) is now captured for Gmail (Content-ID header; nameless inline parts count), Outlook (`isInline` then a per-part read, since `contentId` can't be `$select`ed on the base collection; parts listed whenever the body references `cid:`) and IMAP (mailparser `cid`). `GET /me/email/inline?k=&m=&c=` serves the part by Content-ID with the ADR 0136 image token, user-scoped, `image/*` only, pixel on failure. Not gated on the remote-image setting (the bytes are the reader's own mail). `getThreadWith` re-reads parts once for a body with `cid:` refs but no Content-IDs. Parts shown inline are hidden from the attachment chips. Rejected: filename matching, server-side `data:` inlining |
| [0199](./docs/notes/0199-a-read-state-is-delivered-not-dropped.md) | **A read state is delivered, not dropped**: prod's only mark-read failures were `graph setRead 404 ErrorItemNotFound` on archived Outlook mail — Graph re-issues a message's id when it moves folders (Chord does not request immutable ids), so the stored id was dead and every retry of it failed; Outlook/IMAP then showed it read locally and the next sync flipped it back, while Gmail threw and the open-thread auto-read hid it. Outlook writes (read + archive) now go through `withCurrentGraphId`: on 404 re-find the message by its RFC 822 Message-ID (`findIdByInternetMessageId`), write there, store the new id; found nowhere = deleted, nothing owed. A refused pure read-state change on ANY provider keeps what the user chose and is recorded in `email_read_writes` (migration `088`, one row per thread, latest intent wins); `recomputeThread` re-applies it so a sync can't flip it back; `flushReadWrites` (proactive tick, before the mail sweep) retries through the provider's own modify path with `fromRetry`, backoff 2min doubling to 6h, abandoned after 12 attempts (~26h) with an error log AND the mirror set back to the provider's actual state (Outlook delta never re-sends an unchanged message). Archive/labels still fail loudly. Rejected: immutable ids now (needs a `translateExchangeIds` migration of every stored id or delta re-imports duplicates), retrying the same id, reverting on first failure, in-memory retry |
| [0200](./docs/notes/0200-a-google-event-id-is-per-calendar-not-per-user.md) | **A Google event id is unique per calendar, not per Chord user**: ~7,900 warnings/day of `two-way pull item failed cal=en.usa#holiday@… duplicate key … "uq_events_gcal_event"` — that index was GLOBAL `(google_calendar_id, gcal_event_id)` while ADR 0040 gives every user their own row and `findLocalCopy` is owner-scoped, so the second subscriber to a public holiday calendar (11 users share it; 3 share a family calendar) could never insert its copy. Worse and silent: the pull saved `nextSyncToken` even when items failed, and a delta never re-sends an unchanged event, so two other subscribers had permanently lost every holiday with no log. Migration `089` (`scopeGcalIdentityPerOwner`) replaces it with `uq_events_owner_gcal_event` on `(owner_user_id, google_calendar_id, gcal_event_id)` and clears `sync_token` on every Google calendar with >1 subscriber (one idempotent full re-pull for the victims); `pullCalendarChanges` no longer advances the cursor when `failed > 0`. Rejected: one shared row per public calendar (contradicts 0040/0032), skipping read-only calendars (family calendars collide too), upsert-on-conflict (writes into another user's row), clearing every cursor |
| [0201](./docs/notes/0201-email-and-attachments-are-sealed-at-rest.md) | **Email and attachments are sealed at rest** (built in phases; phase 2 = files): per-owner AES-256 data keys wrapped by a KEK behind a `KeyProvider` (`local` \| `gcp-kms` over REST); self-describing `enc1.<owner>.<ctx>.…` text and `CHENC1` files, so sealed and plaintext coexist during rollout; **reads = one Kysely plugin that opens any sealed string in a result, writes = explicit `sealField`/`writeBlob`**, gated by `CONTENT_ENCRYPTION` (deploy readers, flip the flag, backfill). Key lookups use their **own 2-connection pool** because Kysely runs `transformResult` while still holding the query's connection (a key load through the app pool deadlocked a 1-connection pool, and would exhaust a busy one). An unopenable VALUE (no key/destroyed/altered) is returned as stored and logged; a key-PROVIDER outage throws; reading never creates a key. Erasure = destroy the owner's key (`content_keys` tombstone; account deletion does it after commit; files answer 410); an older backup keeps the wrapped key until it expires. Phase 3 = mail columns + a per-owner HMAC blind index (words with prefixes, trigrams for literals and codes) searched through one shared `findEmailThreads`, sender/recipients/labels deliberately readable; phase 4 = attachment text + chunks (same blind index, queried per OWNER), mail-derived suggestions, drafts and meeting summaries; confirmed shared family content is deliberately NOT sealed; rollout is deploy, flag on, backfill, verify, `CONTENT_LEGACY_TSV=false`, finalize |
| [0202](./docs/notes/0202-postgres-moves-off-the-app-vm.md) | **Postgres moves off the app VM** (tooling built and dry-run tested, not yet run against a real project): Cloud SQL, private IP, CMEK, PITR; runs AFTER the content is sealed so only ciphertext lands there; direct connections (session advisory locks forbid a pooler); driven by `scripts/deploy/cloudsql.sh` (`--dry-run`, `--yes` gate, never edits `.env.prod`); rollback = repoint `DATABASE_URL`; **phase 6** `retire-old` is the only command that deletes (old VM database, WAL, dumps, cron, optionally snapshots): report first, then `--yes` AND `CONFIRM_RETIRE=<instance>`, refused unless the app is on Cloud SQL and a managed backup exists; removing files does not overwrite blocks, so "no readable copy anywhere" means replacing the VM disk |

| [0204](./docs/notes/0204-the-load-test-found-the-database-doing-bookkeeping.md) | **The load test found the database doing bookkeeping**: a 500-user / 500-group harness (`server/scripts/load`, `docs/ops/load-test.md`; results and open items in `docs/perf/2026-09-24-load-test-500-users.md`) showed every one of ten pool connections busy while Node sat at 0.7 of a core, and most of the work was per-request overhead: the session looked up once per ROUTER (19 mounts of `loadSession`, now once per request), catch-up fan-out one insert per member (now one), the classification snapshot written a statement per thread (now chunked), a Google access token exchanged on every calendar call (now reused up to 10 min), an owner-tagged event copy broadcast to the whole group (10,000 socket writes per event at 100 members; now only to its owner), and blind-index lexemes and timezone formatters rebuilt per call (now memoised / cached). Measured, not assumed: `DB_POOL_MAX=25` is WORSE than 10 on the rig; session and `isMember` are deliberately NOT cached (authorisation decisions with a TTL); the personal-group id is not cached (a shadow merge deletes it). Left open and written up: sync and thread-open await LLM passes inline, ~10 statements per new thread in mail sync, Node's single core as the next ceiling. **Addendum 2026-10-23**: Outlook and secondary Gmail access tokens are reused the same way (`utils/access-token-cache.ts`, keyed on mailbox + a hash of the refresh token, so a reconnect misses and a rotated token stays a hit; concurrent misses share one exchange); every Outlook email open used to start with a ~300 ms Microsoft round trip |

| [0205](./docs/notes/0205-a-sync-returns-the-mail-and-the-ai-follows.md) | **A sync returns the mail, and the AI follows** (amends ADR 0144/0180): `POST /me/email/sync` (Email tab open, pull to refresh) and the per-account sync returned only after classification/magics/person extraction had run, so latency was the sum of every model call (3.9 s mean with a 0.5 s mock model). The route now runs at `mirror` depth (`withMirrorSyncThenAi`; `syncAllAccounts` takes a depth or a nested `full` round deepens the outer one) and returns; the AI layer starts as the response goes out and `email.synced` tells the client to refetch (it already did, on `email.synced` and `email.tiles.updated` — no client change). A mirror-depth caller no longer AWAITS an in-flight AI run in the post-sync coalescer (it used to join and wait — a second pull sat behind the first's model calls); its rows are covered by the trailing pass. Opening a thread waits for ADR 0180's one-time check only up to `EMAIL_READ_TIME_WAIT_MS` (1500), then shows the email and emits `email.thread.updated` when the check finishes (the open screen reloads on it). Cost stated: new mail is briefly unsorted after a pull, and a phishing-shaped message can show without its caution for up to the model's latency past the wait — hence the knob (0 = never wait). Tests that read pass output after a sync must `__settleAfterSyncForTests()`. **Addendum 2026-10-19**: the wait counts from when the open ARRIVED (not after the thread read) and the open's other lookups (provider link, images, unsubscribe, flight/parcel) run during it; an `email thread opened` log line gives each open's total and split; the reader's `?prefetch=1` of the next/previous email now STARTS its check in the background (`prefetchReadTimeMagics`, amends ADR 0218), an open during it joins the same check (one in flight per thread), same mail rule as the warm-up (unread or recent, not bulk/coupons/newsletters; old read mail still waits on open, ADR 0221), `EMAIL_PREFETCH_READTIME_ENABLED` |

| [0206](./docs/notes/0206-a-failing-mailbox-waits-instead-of-retrying-every-tick.md) | **A failing mailbox waits instead of retrying every tick** (amends ADR 0144): the sweep selects anyone whose `gmail_synced_at` / account `synced_at` is stale, and those move only on a SUCCESSFUL sync, so a failing mailbox was retried every 15-second proactive tick — prod: one Gmail user's per-minute quota 403 retried 271 times in an hour (each retry spending the quota it waited for) and the earlier missing-`CONTENT_KEK` outage logged ~12,600 warnings. A failed background sync now sets an in-memory wait (`email/sync-backoff.ts`): `MAIL_SWEEP_MINUTES` for an ordinary error, 10 min for a quota/rate-limit error, doubling per consecutive failure up to `MAIL_SYNC_BACKOFF_MAX_MINUTES` (60; `<= 0` = off), forgotten on the next success, keyed per mailbox (`gmail:<user>` / `acct:<id>`). Only the opportunistic paths (`maybeSyncGmail`, the `maybeSyncAccounts` loop) consult it; a sync the person asked for never does, though its outcome still updates it. Rejected: stamping `gmail_synced_at` on failure (makes a stale mirror look fresh), a persisted column (a migration for an hour of state), a fixed pause, parsing Gmail's retry-after text, slowing the fetch rate. **Addendum 2026-10-24**: a Gmail backfill (primary and attached, `runGmailBackfill`) is resumable. It stores the current historyId (`getProfile`) before page 1 and saves its page token after every page (`users.defaults` `gmail_backfill_progress` / attached `sync_cursor.backfill`; incremental waits while it exists). It fetches only messages not already mirrored, setting their UNREAD/INBOX from `is:unread`/`in:inbox` listings at the end (other labels from a dead-token gap stay stale). On a quota error it keeps what it fetched and returns `backfillDone: false` with `recordSyncPause`, not a throw. It fetches at most 4 at a time. The reverse-delete runs only on the finishing pass over a complete re-listed snapshot |
| [0207](./docs/notes/0207-who-touched-the-keys-and-the-vm-is-recorded-off-the-box.md) | **Who touched the keys and the VM is recorded off the box**: after sealing (0201) and Cloud SQL (0202) nothing recorded ACCESS — no Data Access audit config (KMS decrypts unlogged), no Ops Agent (VM logs editable by root), no `auditd`, SSH by metadata key. `scripts/deploy/audit.sh` (idempotent, `--dry-run`/`--yes`): Data Access logs for KMS + Cloud SQL; `chord-audit` log bucket (365 days, deliberately NOT locked) fed by a sink; two email alerts (a decrypt of the **content key** by anyone but the VM service account — scoped to that key because Cloud SQL's agent decrypts the `chord-db` key constantly; key disabled/destroyed/IAM-changed); Ops Agent shipping only `auth.log` + `audit.log` (never the app journal); `auditd` rules on `.env`, the data dir and psql/pg_dump filtered to logged-in people (`auid`), so the app's own reads don't bury them; OS Login (scripted, changes the app's Unix user — stops the app, chowns, needs an immediate deploy); `.env` written mode 600. **Testing found**: the project **Owner** role carries KMS decrypt (earlier docs said humans couldn't — wrong), and `ausearch` reads stdin when piped so over ssh it says "no matches" — always `--input-logs`. Not done: bucket lock, second-project sink, an IAM deny policy for Owner (`pgaudit` was already on for `ddl,role`). Root can still stop auditd (0203 is the answer) |
| [0208](./docs/notes/0208-an-event-read-is-a-window-around-today.md) | **An event read is a window around today, not the oldest N ever**: `listEventsForUser` read oldest-first with no start date and a 500 cap, so a user with 1,669 events since 2021 got only 2021 — the calendar screen, briefing and proactive scan never saw today (`listEvents`/`listEventsForUserInGroup` the same). Reads now take an `EventWindow` — default **7 days back / 30 forward** (bot, briefing, scan), overlap-based so an under-way multi-day event still shows; the **calendar screen always gets 730 days forward** plus `?pastDays=` grown by the app as it pages back (365 when an old build omits it). Every text-timestamp compare/sort goes through `server/src/db/text-instant.ts` (`textInstant`/`byInstant`: zoned cast, floating = wall time in the reader's zone). The device delete reconcile (ADR 0057) now asks `POST /me/events/live` about exactly the ids it wrote instead of diffing against a windowed list — the old `listAllEvents().catch(() => [])` also meant one network error deleted every device copy.
