Same-class sweep fixed: vacation responder cursor (re-read the same oldest 100 forever), list items (cap now drops oldest ticked items), title matching (nearest to now), `record_rsvp` default (`findNearestEvent`; `findLatestEvent` deleted — it picked the furthest-future event), task lists (live work first), person activity feed |
| [0209](./docs/notes/0209-the-app-vm-runs-as-its-own-least-privilege-account.md) | **The app VM runs as its own least-privilege account** (phase 8d): it ran as the default compute account holding project-wide `roles/editor` — root on the VM could edit or delete the Cloud SQL instance. The app's real needs, checked in code: decrypt on the content key (ADR 0201) and log/metric writing (Ops Agent, ADR 0207); Gmail/Calendar/Search use user OAuth or API keys, Cloud SQL a password on a private IP. New `chord-vm` account with exactly those (KMS grant **key-level**, not project), scope left `cloud-platform` (IAM is the limit). `scripts/deploy/vm-identity.sh` `create` → `switch` (stop/set-service-account/start, ~2 min, and **re-points ADR 0207's human-decrypt alert**, whose filter names the VM account — else every app decrypt pages) → `verify` (a Data Access log decrypt BY `chord-vm`, not just health) → `revoke-old` (default account loses the key grant and Editor; refuses while any VM still uses it) / `rollback`. Phase 8a/8b add their grants to this account. Decided alongside: 8a wraps `MASTER_KEY` under a separate key; Cloud SQL password to Secret Manager; break-glass still open (blocks 8a) |
| [0210](./docs/notes/0210-the-token-master-key-is-wrapped-not-stored.md) | **The token master key is wrapped, not stored** (Phase 8a): `MASTER_KEY` (seals refresh tokens, IMAP app passwords, BYOK keys, connector credentials) sat in `.env` beside the database. Same 32 bytes now live as `MASTER_KEY_WRAPPED` (Cloud KMS ciphertext); boot unwraps once (`db/crypto/master-key.ts` `bootCrypto()`, reusing `GcpKmsKeyProvider`) and `sealToken`/`openToken` stay synchronous. **The value does not change, so no blob is re-sealed.** Wrapped WINS and is never skipped: set-but-not-unwrapped makes sealing THROW rather than fall back to a plain key (a fallback would seal under a key the wrapped copy can't open and hide a half-done move). Two deploys, reversible until the plain key is removed; `npm run wrap:master-key` (on the VM) prints only ciphertext after proving it round-trips. Defaults to wrapping under the content key (no new key/IAM; cost: one shared kill switch, so disabling it also makes stored tokens unreadable — users reconnect); `MASTER_KEY_KMS_KEY` is bound in and must never change. Keep an offline copy of the plain key (lose the KMS key = unrecoverable). Runbook `docs/ops/master-key.md`. Not built: per-user token keys (re-seal backfill), a separate wrapping key |
| [0211](./docs/notes/0211-secrets-are-fetched-when-the-app-starts.md) | **Secrets are fetched when the app starts, not stored on the VM** (Phase 8b): `deploy.sh` pushed `.env.prod` verbatim, so every vendor secret sat on disk, in snapshots, and in one file. Now Secret Manager holds them and a **root-owned** `ExecStartPre=+` (`scripts/deploy/vm/fetch-secrets.mjs`, installed to `/usr/local/lib/chord`, NOT run from the app-writable `/srv/chord`) writes `/run/chord-secrets/secrets.env` (tmpfs, 600) as a second `EnvironmentFile` that wins over `.env` — **no app code change** (`env.ts` still reads `process.env`). **Fail closed**: a 404/403 stops the service (skipping `DATABASE_URL` would start on the local Postgres); transient errors retry; a restart during a blip may reuse this boot's last file, a reboot never can. Values byte-identical to the `.env` line (raw, single-line; a newline/NUL is refused). `SECRETS_MODE=fetch` (values also still in `.env`) then `only` (stripped from the VM `.env`); `deploy.sh` refuses unless `secrets.sh verify` passes. `chord-with-env <cmd>` gives operator commands (migrate, encrypt:backfill, wrap:master-key) the same env in the child's memory as you, not root. Names in `secret-keys.txt` (+ `local-only-keys.txt` for `MAXMIND_LICENSE_KEY`); `secrets.sh` never prints values and compares a truncated SHA-256 annotation, so push/verify don't trip the new "secret read by a non-app principal" alert. Runbook `docs/ops/secrets.md`. Not covered: root on the VM, values still in `.env.prod` on the laptop, no live reload (restart to rotate) |
| [0212](./docs/notes/0212-the-bot-asks-your-phone-for-one-contact.md) | **The bot asks your phone for one contact** (amends ADR 0065/0157): "what is Ben's email/number/birthday" is answered by `get_contact_info` (personal 1:1 only). It asks the phone FIRST, every time (saved People is only the fallback, per field or when the phone can't answer): it broadcasts `contact.lookup` on the personal chat's channel, and the app searches its own Contacts (`matchDeviceContacts` in kernel: every query word must match, ≤5 entries) and POSTs `/me/contact-lookups/:id`. Pending lookups are held in memory, answered once, and only the asker may answer; nothing is stored. No reply within `CONTACT_LOOKUP_TIMEOUT_MS` (8s) → `unavailable` (app closed/web/WhatsApp); single web instance only. `people.phone` (migration `094`) + `update_person` tool + `PATCH /me/people/:id` through one `updatePerson` (omitted = unchanged, ''/null = clear); birthdays still via `add_important_date`, written to Chord People only, never back to the device. The Contacts permission text no longer says names never leave the phone. Rejected: address-book sync, import-only, shared-group lookups, persisting lookups |
| [0213](./docs/notes/0213-a-package-is-tracked-on-its-carriers-page.md) | **A package is tracked on its carrier's page, and "which one" is a question**: new `suggest_external_action` kind `package_tracking`. A number the user gives → `trackingForStatedNumber` (`email/tracking.ts`): the request itself is ADR 0133's corroboration, spaces/dashes stripped, a shape two carriers share (20 digits: USPS/FedEx) → `ambiguous_carrier` + ask, a NAMED carrier taken at its word (even for an unfamiliar shape), an unknown carrier → no link. No number → `findDeliveriesForUser` over the asker's own `delivery_json` threads (45 days, deduped by number, `query.q` matches sender/subject) — **personal 1:1 only** (ADR 0084/0032; a given number works anywhere); several → candidates by sender + date **without numbers**, re-called with `q` (ADR 0093/0166). Carriers gain **Amazon** (`TBA`+12) and **UPU S10** by suffix (US→USPS, CA→Canada Post, GB→Royal Mail) gated on the **S10 check digit**; the new shapes feed email detection too. Link rendered, never fetched (0135). Rejected: OnTrac (legacy `.asp` URLs only), a 17track-style aggregator fallback, guessing USPS vs FedEx, mail lookup in shared groups |
| [0214](./docs/notes/0214-the-rest-of-email-is-sayable-in-chat.md) | **The rest of email is sayable in chat**: six bot tools closing the app-only email gaps, all **personal-only** (non-proposals also re-check `isPersonalTurn`). **`compose_email` / `forward_email` join `PROPOSABLE_TOOLS`** (nothing sent without a human tap, chord_email §3.4); commit goes through the composer's own `saveDraft` → `queueSend` (ADR 0113/0115), so bot sends get the undo window and a failed send returns to Drafts — unlike `reply_to_email`'s older direct send. `to`/`cc` validated as addresses (look up or ask, never guess). Forward's `threadSubject` resolved at propose from the DB and stripped from the model's schema (a model-typed subject could name another thread). Compose uses the default mailbox (primary Gmail if sendable, else oldest non-reconnect account). **`cancel_email_send`** undoes the latest queued message (no draft id in chat, ADR 0093); `cancelled:false` = already went. **`read_email`** newest N (≤20) messages, quotes folded (0121), 4k body cap, never marks read or runs the 0180 pass, bodies flagged as outside data. **`set_email_digest`** = `users.email_digest_enabled`; **`set_briefing_channel`** = `setBriefingChannelMuted` by group name, ask when ambiguous. Chat sends hold for **`EMAIL_CHAT_UNDO_SECONDS` (default 60s)**, not the composer's 8s (`queueSend` `holdSeconds`), since "unsend that" is typed; the outbox scheduler now stays on unless BOTH holds are 0 (else an app hold of 0 stranded chat mail), and a chat hold of 0 sends on confirm. Rejected: direct send on confirm, model-named forward subject, tools for 0107/0149 management verbs, marking read on bot read. **Addendum 2026-10-30**: an address the model wrote needs a SOURCE (`email/known-addresses.ts`, one rule): typed by the user in the conversation, in their own mail, on a saved person / family-plan entry, a chat-mate's account address, or returned by their phone's Contacts this turn; `compose_email`/`forward_email` refuse otherwise (`unknown_address`, no card), event invitees are created-but-not-invited (`unverifiedInvitees`), `add_family_member`/`add_person`/`update_person` save nothing; no person behind the call (confirm, ingest) = not checked |
| [0215](./docs/notes/0215-a-swipe-can-move-mail-to-trash.md) | **A swipe can move mail to Trash** (amends ADR 0107/0128/0149, owner exception to 0149's gate): a per-user `users.email_swipe_action` (`'archive'` default \| `'trash'`, migration `095`, Settings chips) picks what the leading swipe on a Traditional row does; the other swipe stays read/unread. `trash: true` on both modify requests (no `false` - restoring is the provider's). **Trash only, never permanent delete**: `threads.trash` is covered by `gmail.modify` (no new scope, no re-consent, same assessment); permanent delete would need `https://mail.google.com/`. Outlook moves each message to `deleteditems`, IMAP moves inbox copies to `\Trash` - and refuses (never fakes) when there is no Trash folder or the thread has left the inbox (a stored UID means nothing in INBOX after an archive). Mirror is identical whichever side trashed: every message gets `TRASH`, loses `INBOX` (`TRASH_DELTA`), so `recomputeThread` retracts the thread's suggestions/to-dos (ADR 0073/0143); the inbox predicate also excludes `TRASH`. Chat parity: `trash_email`, personal-only and **proposable** (tap to confirm; subject resolved server-side like `forward_email`). The swipe setting itself has no chat tool - it only changes a gesture. Rejected: permanent delete, a second trash swipe, per-device storage, direct chat trash. **Addendum**: web has no swipe, so Trash is also a button in the selection bar (confirm for several, via `confirmDestructive`) and in the open email; web rows show a checkbox on hover that stays **mounted** (the swipe gesture takes the pointer on press and fires a hover-out, so a checkbox that unmounted on hover-out vanished before the click) |
| [0216](./docs/notes/0216-an-update-is-offered-not-forced.md) | **An update is offered, not forced**: expo-updates' default only checks at a launch from scratch and applies at the NEXT one, so a user who never force-quits runs an old build for days. `mobile/src/update-banner.tsx` (root layout) also checks on return to the foreground, at most every 10 min from launch (`OTA_CHECK_MIN_GAP_MS`), downloads in the background, and on `useUpdates().isUpdatePending` shows "A new version of Chord is ready" with **Reload** (`Updates.reloadAsync()`) and **Later** (hides that update id only; a newer one asks again). Never reloads on its own (drops a half-written message). Phones only: web is fresh per load, dev has no update server. Pure decisions in `kernel/src/ota-update.ts`, tested. Rejected: auto-reload on foreground, silent apply-next-launch, a check on every foreground |
| [0217](./docs/notes/0217-a-repeating-event-is-a-series-of-rows.md) | **A repeating event is a series of rows** (reverses ADR 0040 decision 3): prod "add … weekly" made one event and the bot said "weekly recurring"; "this should recur weekly" made a SECOND one-off. Nothing could store a repeat and the tool reply did not say so. Also found: the pull matched Google instances on the series' SHARED iCalUID/chordUid, so every Google-made series collapsed to ONE row — `findLocalInstance` now matches an item with `recurringEventId` only by its own id (or adopts a Chord occurrence by master id + original start), and migration `096` clears Google sync cursors once so the full re-pull restores them. New `event_series` (rule, first start, IANA `tz`, `exdates`, Google master id, `series_uid` shared across family copies); occurrences are ordinary `events` rows with `series_id` + `original_start`, materialized to the 365-day pull horizon and topped up on the proactive tick (`topUpSeries`), so every reader works unchanged. Each occurrence has its OWN `ical_uid` (`<series_uid>:<local start>`) because `updateEvent`/`deleteEvent` fan out by it; pull/push never overwrite it with Google's. Push sends ONE master (`recurrence: [RRULE, EXDATE;TZID=…]`, named `timeZone`, `rruleForGoogle` turns a date UNTIL into end-of-local-day UTC); occurrence rows aren't pushed until adopted, and one edited before adoption keeps its edit and pushes to its instance after. Rules are expanded as wall-clock dates (`events/recurrence.ts`), so 3:30 stays 3:30 across DST. Chat: `repeat: {frequency, interval?, weekdays?, monthlyWeek?, until?, count?}` on `create_event`/`update_event` (never a raw RRULE); `update_event` + `repeat` converts an existing one-off in place, and `create_event` + `repeat` on an existing same-title/time event converts it too; a repeating event's edit/delete needs `scope: this|all`, else `scope_required` and the bot asks; title matches collapse a series to one answer; replies say "repeats weekly on Wed" or "one-off, does not repeat". Deleting one occurrence (chat or app) adds an EXDATE. Calendar shows "↻". Not built: "this and following", series-wide invitees, a repeat editor in the app |
| [0218](./docs/notes/0218-moving-through-mail-does-not-wait-on-the-mailbox.md) | **Moving through mail does not wait on the mailbox**: the Email list re-reads the mirror on focus (status + mirror in parallel) and only syncs when its last sync is >60 s old (pull / background return always sync) — returning from each email used to run a full multi-mailbox sync. `syncAllAccounts` runs attached mailboxes side by side (`allSettled`) and the Sync route runs primary Gmail beside them, so one failing mailbox no longer leaves the rest unsynced (their deletions never landed); first error still reported after. `aggregateThread` + the reader judge a thread by its non-TRASH messages when any exist (Gmail trashes per message; one trashed reply hid a live thread and showed its snippet). A shared **removing** set (thread-nav store) hides archived/trashed ids from every list write until the server answered, then re-reads (a refusal comes back); `email.thread.updated` patches one row instead of refetching the list (bulk Trash emitted one per thread and each refetch resurrected unprocessed rows), `email.synced` refetch coalesced 300 ms. Bulk modify runs per mailbox, 4 at a time (IMAP 1 — login rate, ADR 0080), results in request order. `users.email_after_remove` (`next` default \| `list`, migration `097`, Settings): reader Archive/Trash leaves at once to the next email (previous when last) and the write finishes behind it, refusal shown on the list. Reader prefetches next/prev with `?prefetch=1` (skips the ADR 0180 read-time pass; the real open runs it), auto mark-read patches in place. No bot tool (navigation-only, ADR 0215's reasoning) |
| [0219](./docs/notes/0219-preprocessing-the-read-time-pass-ahead-of-the-open.md) | **Preprocessing the read-time pass ahead of the open**: a live benchmark showed the ADR 0180 on-open LLM call, not the mail sync, is the actual latency a reader feels. New `preprocessReadTimeMagics` runs the identical `runReadTimeEmailMagics` earlier — right after sync, and opportunistically on every real thread-open (never a prefetch) — bounded to `EMAIL_PREPROCESS_READTIME_BATCH` (default 10) newest not-yet-`read`-marked threads per call, never every synced thread (the same unbounded-cost trap ADR 0109/0127/0143 avoid for the background sweep). **Self-advancing, no cursor**: the query is always "newest N with no `read` mark", so once the front N are marked the identical call naturally surfaces the next N. An in-memory per-user guard (mirrors `afterSyncRuns`/`mailSyncRounds`) stops two close-together triggers from double-paying for the same batch; `runReadTimeEmailMagics`'s own per-thread mark check bounds the race to wasted-not-wrong either way, so a DB advisory lock (ADR 0165's heavier tool) wasn't needed. Existing ADR 0218 tests asserting "nothing is read-marked until opened" had to disable this flag in their own setup — they test the on-demand path in isolation. Rejected: preprocessing every synced thread, a persisted cursor, a `pg_advisory_xact_lock` guard |
| [0220](./docs/notes/0220-a-bare-upload-still-lets-the-model-call-a-tool.md) | **A bare upload still let the model call a tool**: prod — a WhatsApp invitation photo with no caption. The deterministic attachment auto-filer (chat-upload sibling of ADR 0071/0158) correctly created the calendar event (Oct 28, exactly as printed), but the bot's own conversational turn, running concurrently, called `find_events`, got back an unrelated same-group event ("Annual Fund Kick-Off Breakfast", Oct 14 — also a breakfast, also 8 AM), and confidently reported THAT as if answering the upload — reading as "nothing happened" to the user, who resent the photo (no duplicate: the auto-filer recognizes an already-processed attachment) and then asked to "update it for October 28th", which `update_event` refused with "no matching event" since the only referent in the conversation was the wrong title the bot's own first reply had introduced. `bot.service.ts`'s `bareAutoFileUpload` suppression (ADR 0009's "must not also propose those tools") already exists and is gated on `!anyToolsExecuted` — but nothing told the model not to execute a tool in the first place; `context.ts`'s attachment block forbade re-`create_event`-ing the file's contents but said nothing about read-only lookups. `triggerIsBareUpload` (from the same `isBareAttachmentUpload` check, computed before the prompt is built instead of only after) now adds one explicit sentence for that case — call no tool this turn, nothing to check or answer, the filer already handles it silently — closing the gap at the source so the existing post-hoc suppression has nothing left to fail on. Rejected: suppressing the reply whenever an attachment is present regardless of whether a tool fired (breaks genuine "what does this say" questions), teaching every read-only tool to refuse on a bare upload (the model knows why it was called; the tool doesn't), fixing only `update_event`'s referent fallback (treats the symptom — the wrong referent only existed because of the first bug). **Addendum 2026-10-25**: none of this fired for app uploads — the app posts `📎 <filename>` and the bare check read the filename as words, so every app upload got a visible reply ("Importing the calendar…"); `isBareAttachmentUpload(body, filename)` now drops the file's own name first, as the app already does when hiding that caption |
| [0221](./docs/notes/0221-speculative-preprocessing-is-not-a-real-open.md) | **Speculative preprocessing is not a real open**: prod — a burst of 33 `create_task` proposals in one hour sourced from email threads as old as July, read and settled weeks ago, several already handled elsewhere. `preprocessReadTimeMagics` (ADR 0219, shipped the same session) warms ADR 0180's read-time pass by finding "the newest N threads with no `read` mark yet" and running the FULL extraction+staging pass on them, self-advancing with no cursor by design — but it also borrowed `runReadTimeEmailMagics`'s deliberate bypass of every background-sweep gate (ADR 0143's unread/7-day age rule, ADR 0127's bulk deny-list, ADR 0179's discretionary-broadcast exclusion), whose justification ("none of those gates' reasoning holds once a person already has the message open") is specific to a genuine human-initiated open and never applied to this fire-and-forget, nobody's-looking warm-up. Run on every sync (as often as every 5 minutes) plus every open, the self-advancing query eventually marched straight through a backlog of unread-but-settled mail with none of the gates that would have correctly left it alone. Fix: the preprocessing query now applies the SAME eligibility rules the background sweep uses (reusing `minableSince`/`minablePredicate`/`discretionaryBroadcastCategoryIds` directly, not reimplemented) — `runReadTimeEmailMagics` itself, the path a real open calls, is untouched, so ADR 0180's bypass stays correct for the case it exists for. Pinned by a new test (old+read excluded; recent+read and old+unread still included); confirmed to fail without the fix. Rejected: a bare age cutoff (duplicates a number instead of reusing the predicate, and misses the bulk/discretionary cases), requiring classification first like the background sweep does (needlessly delays warming a just-synced thread; the bulk gate already coalesces a null category safely), removing the self-advancing no-cursor design (overcorrects — recent mail warming was never the problem) |
| [0222](./docs/notes/0222-the-reader-is-for-reading-and-the-composer-is-a-screen.md) | **The reader is for reading, and the composer is a screen** (iPhone Mail layout, owner direction): the thread screen's top button row, always-open label card and docked reply box are gone; the mail has the whole screen and a floating pill holds trash / more / reply (`email-reader-bar.tsx`). Reply arrow opens Reply, Reply all, Forward, Suggested (+ "Continue draft"); More holds Archive, Mark read/unread, Share/Print, Unsubscribe, provider link; a Label button opens `email-label-sheet.tsx` (labels, new label, existing Chord labels, apply-category). The composer is a full-screen LAYOUT of the same screen (shares draft, autosave, recipients, attachments), with Cancel / attach / Send in the header, a body that takes the remaining height, and `keyboardVerticalOffset` = header height (its absence hid the box under the iOS keyboard). The bar answers the message opened last; per-message reply buttons and the side-by-side split are removed. **Plain Reply no longer drafts with the model**; only Suggested does (amends ADR 0197's auto-suggest). Adds `@expo/vector-icons` (one font, `MaterialCommunityIcons`; `expo-font` already linked in shipped builds and pinned, so OTA-safe). No server change, no new capability, so no bot tool. **Addendum 2026-10-02**: New message and Forward use the same layout (shared `email-compose-ui.tsx`); From is one line opening a list of every mailbox and verified send-as address; the ask bar works on a new message (`POST /me/email/compose`, `composeNew`); Cancel offers Save draft / Delete draft; a reply or forward draft is loaded back when its thread is reopened (`GET /me/email/threads/:id/draft`) with its files named; a send-as message is sent directly, not queued, because a draft has no send-as field. Rejected: a separate reply route, swipes inside an open email, emoji icons, the icon index import |
| [0223](./docs/notes/0223-the-same-event-again-updates-the-one-already-there.md) | **The same event again updates the one already there**: one flight became several calendar events (differently worded emails; one leg per passenger, ADR 0190/0185). `createEvent` now runs a deterministic `isSameEvent` check (`services/events/same-event.ts`): same owner, same start/end INSTANTS, not two explicitly different calendars, and the same subject (both flights without contradicting flight number/route, or titles sharing most words with non-disagreeing locations). A match updates the existing event (`mergeEventDetails`: fill missing end/location, merge description lines, one block per passenger (their confirmation beside the name, their itinerary number and email link bulleted under it), a title naming the flight replaces one that does not) and returns it with `merged: updated\|unchanged\|covered`; a Google-originated match is left untouched (`covered`). **One calendar event per flight**: found by leg-made + departure instant + route (`findFlightEvent`), so each passenger's leg adds to it; a passenger who moves comes off the old event. Suggestion staging uses the same rule; the conflict nudge fires only after a real create. Skipped: repeats, all-day, anything with its own identity (gcal/device pull, invite, `.ics`); a group event is compared only within its group. Existing duplicates left alone (owner). Rejected: asking the model, a time tolerance, requiring equal titles, editing Google's event, a stored flight key. **Addendum 2026-11-19**: a flight is on the calendar once - the calendar leaves out a trip leg when its own event is shown (`legsNotOnCalendar`, kernel); removing a leg deletes its flight event, or just that passenger when others are still on it (`releaseLegFlightEvent`, also used by the mailbox clean-up); the same traveler on the same flight number and route within 12 h, on any of the user's trips, is that flight moved (`rescheduleSameFlight`: email, uploaded ticket, `addTravelLeg`), unless the two booking references differ **Addendum 2026-11-10**: same instants + same people = one meeting whatever the titles or calendar (`samePeople`: every address on one side is on the other); a pulled event's guests + organizer are kept read-only in `events.guest_emails` (migration `145`, never pushed, ADR 0295 holds), a new event's people are its invitees; applied at staging, the covered sweep and `createEvent` (someone else's event = `covered`, no guest change, the chat note says it was already there) |
| [0224](./docs/notes/0224-a-thread-with-no-message-is-not-mail.md) | **A thread with no message is not mail**: prod listed emails that "don't exist" and opened to a blank screen - 47 `email_threads` rows with no message, left when a sync wrote the thread row (`ensureThreadRow`) and then failed to write its message (mostly 24 Sep, the missing content key). `reapEmptyThreads` removes them on every sync (older than 5 min, via `recomputeThread`, above the ADR 0079 gate); `getThreadWith` reads a message-less thread as not found and removes it; a Gmail body fetch that 404s drops that message (`BodyFetchOutcome.gone`) instead of skipping it; the reader says the email is gone; the recent-mail lines re-read on focus. Rejected: one transaction at nine call sites (does not clear existing rows), a one-off migration, an `EXISTS` in every list query, dropping an Outlook message on 404 (its id changes on a move, ADR 0199). The mail those rows stood for was never mirrored |
| [0225](./docs/notes/0225-an-image-is-fetched-when-the-mail-arrives.md) | **An image is fetched when the mail arrives, not when it is opened** (amends ADR 0136): the proxy hid who was reading but not that they read, since the fetch happened on open. `preloadEmailImages` (`email/image-preload.ts`, started un-awaited from `runAfterSyncPasses` above the ADR 0079 gate, one pass per user at a time) fetches every remote image of new messages at sync and stores it under `DATA_DIR/email-images/<user>/` via `writeBlob` (`email_image_cache` + `email_messages.images_preloaded_at`, migration `098`); `GET /me/email/image` answers from the store and falls back to the live fetch, so **the reader is unchanged** and a miss is never a broken image. All mail incl. bulk; never SPAM/TRASH (must not learn the address is live); nothing for a reader with `email_images_auto` off (left unmarked). Bounded: 20 messages/pass, 40 images and 5 MB per message, 30-day window and retention (`EMAIL_IMAGE_PRELOAD{,_BATCH,_MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES,_DAYS}`). Pass and reader name an image through one `absoluteEmailImageUrl`; the pass reads raw HTML by regex (`remoteImageUrlsInHtml`), and a disagreement with the sanitizer only means that image loads on open. **Also**: the reader's document now carries a content security policy (`emailBodyCsp`: images from Chord's origin or `data:` only, nothing else), and the sanitizer recognises seven spellings it missed (tab/newline in the scheme, CSS escapes, a comment inside `url()`, `image-set()`), dropping any style declaration that still names a remote host. Not checked on a device: the app-injected height probe should be exempt from the policy's script ban. Rejected: non-bulk only, rewriting stored HTML, retrying failed fetches, awaiting the pass, a cross-user store |
| [0226](./docs/notes/0226-what-is-kept-and-for-how-long.md) | **What is kept, and for how long**: after the 2026-10-03 disk-full outage (ADR 0174 addendum), nothing on the server had a limit on how MUCH it could keep, only how long. **Logs are capped by size**: a normal prod day writes 1.4-2.5 MB; each of the three logs keeps 30 files of 25 MB (`maxSize: '25m'`, `maxFiles: 30`; rotation is daily, so the count is ~30 days of ordinary running, while a flood day uses several files and pushes older days out; 750 MB a log, 2.25 GB at most; was 2 files, which was two days - addendum 2026-11-16; each log names its audit file so an options change no longer strands old files), and every deploy writes a journald drop-in capping the system journal at 200 MB (`JOURNAL_MAX_USE`). **Rows are removed after a stated window** by `pruneOldRows` (`services/retention.service.ts`, once a day from the proactive tick, in-memory guard): fired `proactive_messages` 30 days (an unfired row is never removed), rejected `proposals` 15, expired `proposals` 30 (also how long an unanswered suggestion counts as "still owed", ADR 0134), `token_usage` 365 (billing reads the current period only, ADR 0196). Kept on purpose: confirmed proposals (the record of what was approved) and `email_unsubscribes` (else the reader re-offers an unsubscribe already done). `RETENTION_ENABLED` / `RETENTION_*_DAYS` (0 = keep forever). Consequences: during an incident only the newest 750 MB of each log survives; the rotation library removes files only when a new one starts; files written before the named audit files (2026-11-16) are deleted once by hand; still no disk or Redis-memory alert. Rejected: age retention plus an alert, one shared cap, pg_cron, pruning confirmed proposals/mail/events. **Addendum 2026-11-02**: a phone (`device`) calendar no phone has sent for `RETENTION_DEVICE_CALENDAR_DAYS` (30) is forgotten with its `device:<calId>:` events (time, not missing-from-batch, since a second phone sends only its own); rows the Google/Outlook pull adopted (`google_calendar_id`/`gcal_event_id` set) are kept, rows are removed not tombstoned so nothing reaches a provider or the phone |
| [0227](./docs/notes/0227-a-crash-files-its-own-issue.md) | **A crash files its own issue, and asks for a fix**: the 2026-10-06 `chord-worker` crash (an unhandled imapflow `'error'` event; fixed by a listener on the session client in `imap-api.ts`) went unseen for two days — `/health` is the web role, the stack goes to the journal not winston, and the Ops Agent does not ship the app journal (ADR 0207). Both units get an `ExecStopPost` (not `OnFailure=`, which does not fire under `Restart=always`) that, on any result but `success`, starts the root-owned `/usr/local/lib/chord/crash-report.mjs` detached via `systemd-run`. It takes Node's fatal block from the stopped invocation's journal (app log lines dropped, emails/credentials/long tokens removed) and files a GitHub issue labelled `crash`: one per signature (error line + first five frames), repeats comment at most every 6h, a return after close files anew pointing back, at most 5 new issues an hour. Token = fine-grained Issues-only, in Secret Manager as `CRASH_REPORT_GITHUB_TOKEN`, read by the reporter itself, never in the app env or `.env.prod`. On only when `CRASH_REPORT_REPO` is set in `deploy.env`. `.github/workflows/crash-fix.yml` runs Claude on the `crash` label for a **draft** PR (cause, reproducing test seen failing, fix, pattern sweep) or a comment when the cause is unclear; never merges or deploys; the issue is read as data, not pasted into the prompt. Runbook `docs/ops/crash-monitor.md`. Does not catch hangs or caught-and-logged errors. **Addendum 2026-10-20**: the units run `node --enable-source-maps --import tsx src/entrypoints/<role>.ts`, not the `tsx` command, which SIGKILLed a busy child 60 ms into a stop and exited 143 (4 of 11 deploys would have filed a false crash); `stopOnSignal` stops a role once, exits 0, exits 1 if the stop throws; `unit-start-command.test.ts` guards the start line |
| [0228](./docs/notes/0228-outlook-calendars-through-the-same-sync-engine.md) | **Outlook calendars, through the same sync engine** (built; not yet run against a live mailbox; `OUTLOOK_CALENDAR_ENABLED` default off): `two-way-sync.service.ts` keeps every rule and loses its HTTP to a `CalendarTransport` (`google/calendar-transport.ts`: insert/patch/get/remove/listChanges, in Google's event shape). Google implements it with the calls it already made; Outlook over Graph (`outlook-calendar/transport.ts`) translating in `outlook-calendar/map.ts`. An Outlook calendar is `outlook:<accountId>:<calendarId>` in the `google_calendar_id` columns, so one push pass sends each event to its own provider and an unavailable provider leaves its rows queued. A mailbox carries a calendar when its grant has `Calendars.ReadWrite` (`email_accounts.calendar_enabled`, migration `099`); older connections are mail-only until Outlook is added again. Differences settled in the translation: no `sequence` (left undefined; engine compares by time, `remote.sequence ?? local.sequence`), no etag condition, UTC times restated in `users.last_tz`, RRULE rebuilt as Graph `recurrence` and each EXDATE deleted as its own instance, a delta occurrence filled from its series master, cursor replaced by a full listing every 12h, immutable ids, a real RSVP call tried before the iTIP email (ADR 0104). `primary` still means Google's; with no Google calendar it reads as the Outlook default. "Outlook" as a calendar name = the mailbox default; the same name on both providers is asked about (`(Google)`/`(Outlook)`). The server copy removes the phone mirror's copy of the same event. Disconnect removes pulled events, keeps Chord-made ones. Rejected: a second sync service, read-only first, full listing every pass, `is_primary` on the Outlook default, a separate calendar sign-in |
| [0229](./docs/notes/0229-a-repeat-nobody-asked-for-is-ignored.md) | **A repeat nobody asked for is ignored**: the model attaches a `repeat` to plain one-time requests (seen three times in prod). The earlier guard (`81713d6`) only ran when the spec FAILED validation, so a made-up VALID one (`{frequency:"daily"}`) made a daily series of "lunch tomorrow", and on `update_event` turned a one-off into a series or rewrote an existing series' rule. `repeatWasAsked` (`bot/executor.ts`) now asks the conversation before the spec is looked at: `no` (a triggering message, and no repeat wording in it or the last 8 messages / 30 min of that chat) ignores the `repeat` on create and edit; `yes` builds it as before (an inexpressible one still refuses and asks, ADR 0217); `unknown` (no message: ingest, proposal confirm) takes it as given. The window keeps "yes, that works" after a proposed pattern. `looksLikeRecurrenceRequest` gains plural day names, weekdays/weeknights/weekends, biweekly, fortnightly, nightly, quarterly, `each <day>`, standing, regularly. An ignored repeat is not mentioned in the reply. English only. Rejected: validation alone, a stricter prompt, checking only the triggering message, confirming every repeat. **Addendum 2026-10-25**: OpenAI models through OpenRouter fill every optional field, so "every Tuesday" arrived with `monthlyWeek: 1`, `count: 1` and a made-up `until` and was refused 4/4; `trimRepeatToRequest` (`events/recurrence.ts`) keeps a part only when the user's words support it (interval: "every other"; count/until: a stated end, never `count: 1` or an `until` before the start; monthlyWeek: monthly + "first/last…"; daily+weekdays → weekly only when days were named), on create and both update paths |
| [0230](./docs/notes/0230-google-sign-in-asks-for-mail-and-calendar-together.md) | **Google sign-in asks for mail and calendar together** (amends ADR 0062's separate Gmail consent): the sign-in consent requests `gmail.modify` beside `calendar` when the server reads Gmail (`/health.gmail` = `GMAIL_ENABLED`; absent = on), and the client starts a mail sync right after sign-in. No server change to the sign-in route (it already stores the granted scopes). Google's per-scope checkboxes mean a user can still untick either, so the Settings connect flows stay (also the only path for an Apple-only user). Every Google sign-in now counts towards the unverified app's 100-user cap. Rejected: a connect-mail screen right after sign-in (two steps), asking regardless of `GMAIL_ENABLED` |

| [0231](./docs/notes/0231-a-family-pays-once-and-shares-one-pool.md) | **A family pays once and shares one pool**: individual or family (4 seats incl. owner, one pooled 20M allowance), monthly or annual, sold by Stripe (web) or the App Store (via RevenueCat) onto the SAME `users` subscription columns so `getEntitlement` never knows the seller. An annual plan still gets a MONTHLY allowance (`allowanceWindowStart`). Family coverage is read from the owner's current period every call, never copied onto the member. `family_members` roster = `profile` (no seat) / `invited` (seat held) / `active`; invite links reuse `/join/<CODE>` with a `FAM`+12 code; Chordinator = the existing trust grant, applied on acceptance. Prices are config (`PRICE_*_USD`, `ANNUAL_FREE_MONTHS`) shipped on `BillingStatus.prices`. Single-use promo codes (`promo_codes`, `npm run promo:codes`) become a Stripe TRIAL (not a coupon - a 100%-off coupon makes an annual plan free for the year), burned only when the subscription starts; iOS uses Apple offer codes. Grandfathering = `users.grandfathered_at` set by `npm run grandfather:users -- --before <date> --apply` (not a migration), individual allowance free on a calendar-month window. One live subscription at a time (409; change via Stripe portal / Apple settings). Chat tools: `add_family_member`, `remove_family_member`, `list_family_members`, `get_checkout_link`. Unverified against live Stripe/RevenueCat/App Store **Addendum 2026-10-09**: `parseInvoicePaid` reads BOTH invoice shapes - Stripe's 2025-03-31.basil and later moved the line price (`pricing.price_details.price`), the invoice subscription (`parent.subscription_details.subscription`) and a line's `proration` (under `parent`), and a new webhook destination only offers current versions, so a handler that read only the old fields would grant nothing on a paid invoice. Not yet confirmed against a real delivery **Addendum 2026-11-05**: the family pool is `familyTokenAllowance()` = `FAMILY_SEATS` x `MONTHLY_TOKEN_ALLOWANCE` unless `FAMILY_TOKEN_ALLOWANCE` is set above 0 (default now 0), so raising the individual allowance raises the family's; enforcement and Settings read the same function |
| [0232](./docs/notes/0232-personal-first-fan-out-next.md) | **Personal first, fan-out next** (amends ADR 0040/0136/0182): a calendar named once is no longer remembered for the group (it sent every later event - flights included - to "Family", and gave every fan-out copy one calendar id): `resolveTargetCalendarId` is requested-name (that event only) → the user's own default → primary, `setGroupDefaultCalendar` deleted, `groups.default_calendar_id` inert, and each fan-out copy resolves THAT member's own calendar. A 1:1 event is one personal copy; `create_event.withGroupName` (personal chat only, exact-name-or-ask, via re-entering the executor so every guard runs) creates it in a group where it fans out. A personal event whose title names someone in one of the asker's shared groups (deterministic whole-word name match, incl. roster kids; names shown as the user sees them) returns a `fanoutSuggestion` the bot asks once - never shares itself; `share_event` is the yes (re-creates in the group then removes the personal original; refuses repeats and events with guests; remembered server-side in `pending-fanout.ts` and read back into the prompt, ADR 0166's reasoning). Shared Google calendar scratched. Trips/flights suggestion to a group NOT built (bundle consent surface has no group choice) **Addendum 2026-10-12**: prod refused "Move Car" four times on `withGroupName: "/personal"` and read the asker's whole chat list back as calendars. `isSelfReference` (`utils/self-reference.ts`) reads "/personal", "personal calendar", "my own default calendar" as the asker (group AND calendar names); `resolveGroupByName` returns near misses only, at most five, never the roster; a name that is no group no longer refuses - the event is created personally, says so, and near misses are offered via `fanoutSuggestion`/`share_event` (task/list/place: also personal, near misses named once as a suggestion; a later "yes, that group" adds a copy and keeps the personal one on purpose - the To Do's hub shows the copies as ONE row naming every group it is in) **Addendum 2026-10-25**: an item is created in the chat it was asked in, whatever the model sends - the model filled `withGroupName` with a real group nobody named ("Doe Family" 3/3 for "add milk to the Costco list" from the 1:1), so `withGroupName` is REMOVED from `create_task`/`add_list_item`/`save_place`/`create_event` (and `resolveWriteGroup`, its "no group is called" note, the near-miss offer); a personal item reaches a group only through `share_event`/`share_trip` after a yes to a server-computed offer **Addendum 2026-11-10**: `share_event` copies the whole event (the original calendar while still writable, else the usual pick and the reply says so; description passed explicitly so ADR 0162 does not recompute; zone via new `createEvent` `startTz`/`endTz`; all-day, free/busy, provenance; the family member re-found in the target group by People row then exact name). A video link cannot move (it belongs to the deleted provider event): a new one is asked for on the shared copy and the reply says the old link changed |
| [0233](./docs/notes/0233-a-trip-is-offered-to-the-family.md) | **A trip is offered to the family** (completes ADR 0232): a trip starts personal; sharing it with a group is a visible choice, never a default. Chat: `create_trip`/`add_travel_leg` in the 1:1 return a `shareSuggestion` (once per trip, live turn only) and `share_trip` is the yes - the offer lives in the same server-side `pending-fanout` record as the event offer (now `kind: event|trip`). Emailed trip: the Trips-tab bundle card gets the same Just me / group choice and `confirmProposal` honours a group on a TRIP bundle (every member runs in it; a screenshot bundle still ignores one). `shareTripWithGroup` moves `trips.group_id` plus what hangs off it - the flight's calendar event (found by group + uid, else a later leg duplicates it), the catch-up fan-out, and participants (user ids) not in the new group. Only the creator's personal trip, into a group they are in. Offered by name match on the trip, else to the asker's ONE shared group; several groups and no name = nothing. The family gets the TRIP, not the flight on their calendars. NOT covered: a trip filed from an uploaded document (the filer posts nothing and a bare upload's turn calls no tool, ADR 0220) |
| [0234](./docs/notes/0234-a-family-plan-comes-with-a-chat.md) | **A family plan comes with a chat**: accepting a family invite lands the person in the family's shared group - the first acceptance creates it ("<Surname> Family", marked `groups.family_owner_id`, migration 102, one per owner; an ordinary `kind=group` chat so fan-out, the bot and trip sharing need no special case), later ones join the same one, and removing a member or leaving the plan takes them out (the reverse is not true - the chat never decides coverage). Chordinator (ADR 0026 grant) is unchanged. `suggestTripShareGroups` offers the family chat first, even beside other groups. Forwarding is a deliberate post into a group the user is in: `message_group` and `share_email_with_group` (personal-only; no name = the family chat, a name = exact-name-or-ask), `POST /me/email/threads/:id/share`, and a "Share to family chat" row in the reader. An email goes as text (sender, subject, newest message with quotes folded, 1,500 chars; attachment names listed, files not copied). Rejected: a pair group per member (the 'exactly one shared group' fallback never fires), creating the chat at purchase, confirming each post, copying attachments **Addendum 2026-11-10**: the bot asks before it posts - `message_group` and `share_email_with_group` are in `PROPOSABLE_TOOLS`; staging resolves the group (exact or ask) and pins group id, `forUserId` and the email (subject, sender, message date, opening, file names) in fields hidden from the model; card Post/Share + Cancel (WhatsApp/Telegram via 0296); the tap shares the message the card showed; no pinned group = nothing posted. The reader's "Share to family chat" row stays direct |
| [0235](./docs/notes/0235-connect-openrouter-instead-of-pasting-a-key.md) | **Connect OpenRouter instead of pasting a key**: BYOK end to end through OpenRouter's OAuth (PKCE) flow, which hands the app a key the USER owns. `POST /me/model-key/openrouter/start` (session) mints a single-use `state` + PKCE verifier (`openrouter_connects`, migration 103, 10 min, one open attempt per user) and returns the openrouter.ai/auth URL; the browser returns to `GET /openrouter/callback/:state` (no session - the state is the credential), the SERVER swaps the code for the key at `/api/v1/auth/keys` and stores it through `saveModelKey` (the validate-then-seal rule extracted from `PUT /me/model-key`, now shared). The key never passes through the app and the verifier never leaves the server. The return destination comes from the `platform` the attempt started on (web = `/settings`, native = `chord://settings`), never from the request, so the callback is not an open redirect. Starts on the model the app runs on, spelled the OpenRouter way (`openRouterModelFor`: openai/..., google/..., anthropic/ with dotted versions), unless the user typed one. A valid key with NO credit is KEPT (`keepIfUnfunded`) and the user is told to top up - discarding it would mint a new key every retry; a rejected key or bad model is not kept and an existing key is untouched. `byokFailureMessage` takes the base URL so OpenRouter users are told about OpenRouter, not OpenAI, and a 402 now classifies as credit. Chat: `connect_openrouter` returns a link card and claims nothing. UNVERIFIED against live OpenRouter: the exchange sends no Authorization header (their docs disagree), and a custom-scheme return is untested. Rejected: client-side exchange, a pasted-key-only flow, discarding an unfunded key, trusting a returnTo from the request |
| [0236](./docs/notes/0236-cancel-is-one-click-and-ends-at-the-period.md) | **Cancel is one click, and it ends at the period**: `POST /me/billing/renewal {renew}` sets Stripe's `cancel_at_period_end` from a "Cancel plan" / "Resume plan" button on the plan card (and the personal-only `set_subscription_renewal` bot tool, one shared `setRenewal`), so cancelling no longer means leaving for Stripe's portal. The paid period is untouched - access is already period-based (ADR 0195/0196), so no entitlement change and no webhook handler. `BillingStatus.cancelsAtPeriodEnd` is read live from Stripe (3s timeout, 20s cache), not stored, so a cancel made in the portal shows too; unknown is omitted. App Store plans answer `apple` (409) and keep the App Store route; the portal stays as "Change plan or card". `?checkout=cancel` is stripped quietly like `success`. Unverified against live Stripe. **Addendum**: "Change plan" opens the other plans in the card and `POST /me/billing/plan` swaps the price on the existing item (`always_invoice`, `error_if_incomplete`, clears a cancel; same plan = resume or refused), with a proposable `change_subscription_plan` bot tool since it charges now; "Update payment card" is the portal's remaining job. Rejected: portal deep-link flow, immediate cancel, a stored column + `customer.subscription.*` handler, propose-then-confirm for the bot tool |
| [0237](./docs/notes/0237-an-invite-to-someone-already-here-just-seats-them.md) | **An invite to someone already here just seats them**: a family invite used to mint a link and leave delivery to the owner (nothing was ever sent) and made someone with an account find and accept it. Now the contact point is matched on PROOF the account gave - sign-in email, a connected mailbox, a verified phone (`family-contact-match.ts`; never a name; two different accounts = no match; a bare 10-digit number reads as +1) - and a match is seated at once through the same `seatInvitee`/`seatBlocker` as accepting (roster active, Chordinator grant, family chat, coverage still from the owner's plan). `claimFamilyInvitesFor` seats an invite sent before they had an account at sign-in and on phone verification. Chord EMAILS from the owner's own mailbox through the draft/outbox path (`delivery.email`: queued/no_email/no_mailbox/failed, so nobody claims a send that did not happen) and posts a Chord message in the new member's personal chat; it does NOT text - no SMS sender, and a first WhatsApp contact needs a template - so the text stays the owner's share sheet / `sms:` card. Rejected: sending texts from Chord, matching on name, email-the-link-only, seating silently |
| [0238](./docs/notes/0238-the-base-plan-is-a-subscription-in-both-stores.md) | **The BYOK base plan is a subscription in both stores**: ADR 0195's base fee existed in code and nowhere else - `STRIPE_PRICE_ID_BYOK` was empty (so BYOK stayed free by design), the card showed no price and no way to end it (the BYOK entitlement carried no `periodEnd`/`source`), and on iOS it would have opened Stripe's web checkout from inside the app. Now a fifth product: Stripe "Chord Base" $2.99/mo (own product, no annual), App Store `chord.byok.monthly` in the same subscription group (webhook maps it to `byok_base`), the BYOK entitlement returns interval/source/periodEnd, the card shows the price, buys per platform and gives the payer Cancel / Resume / Manage (a family-covered member gets none - not theirs to end), and `get_checkout_link` takes `plan: base`. `PRICE_BYOK_USD` is display only. Turning it on is the one `STRIPE_PRICE_ID_BYOK` line; prod had no BYOK users when written. Rejected: the web checkout on iOS for this plan, an annual price, a discount folded into the individual plan, free BYOK on iOS only |
| [0239](./docs/notes/0239-a-to-do-due-in-the-future-is-still-owed.md) | **A to-do due in the future is still owed** (amends ADR 0143/0092): future-dated to-dos were showing under Dropped because three automatic paths archived without checking the date, and swipe-to-trash (ADR 0215) made the first routine. `retireTasksByProvenance` called with no cutoff (source email trashed/deleted) now skips a to-do whose due date is ahead; `archiveStaleTasks` rules 2 and 3 skip one too (rule 1, a completed task being filed, unchanged). `updateTask`: a DROPPED to-do (`archived`, never completed) given a future due date becomes `open` and its `archived_at` is cleared, unless the caller also set a status - a new date is a new shelf life for the overdue sweep, and it cannot oscillate because rule 3 cannot match until that date is a week past. "Future" is decided on the parsed instant (`isDueInFuture`, ADR 0208). Still retires a future-dated to-do: a person dropping it, and its EVENT being removed (ADR 0192 passes a cutoff - extending the protection there would reopen the water-shut-off bug). Not retroactive. Rejected: blocking a manual drop, reviving on a past date, reviving completed tasks, keeping `archived_at` on revival |
| [0240](./docs/notes/0240-a-flight-time-is-on-the-clock-of-its-airport.md) | **A flight time is on the clock of its airport** (narrows ADR 0074 for travel): a Delhi 1:50am departure read by a user in New York was filed as 1:50am New York. Three paths did it: the trip classifier when the model omitted the optional `departZone`, chat `add_travel_leg` (no way to say whose clock, and the prompt stamps the user's offset on every datetime), and the general email extractor (its rule reads every time as the user's). `airportTimeZone` (`kernel/src/airport-zone.ts`, on `resolveMetro`'s keys) looks the zone up; `legDatetime` (`bot/trip_classifier.ts`) anchors each end: airport table, then the model's stated zone, then the reader's as before. A value already carrying an offset is moved ONLY when the offset is the reader's own; `Z` or any other offset is left alone. Called from the classifier's leg sites, the executor's `add_travel_leg` (before trip matching), and `anchorFlightTimes` in `magics.service.ts` (also moves the suggested event when it IS the departure/arrival). Existing legs are NOT corrected; an airport outside the table with no stated zone still gets the reader's zone. Not fixed, same pattern: restaurant/movie times and `add_reservation.dateTime` in another city (needs a venue's zone). Rejected: requiring the zone from the model, refusing an unknown airport in chat, re-anchoring every offset, model's zone before the table |
| [0241](./docs/notes/0241-on-an-iphone-billing-is-managed-in-the-browser.md) | **On an iPhone, billing is managed in the browser**: Apple billing is shelved (it needed the legal entity, a Paid Apps Agreement, five products, RevenueCat and a native build); Chord is an email reader, so the Netflix/Claude pattern applies - the AI Plan card REPORTS the plan (headline, renewal or cancellation date, spent allowance) and shows one **Manage billing** button that opens **Safari** (a Stripe subscriber lands on their billing portal, anyone else on the web app's Settings; never an in-app sheet). No prices, picker, promo, Cancel/Change/Update card on iOS; Connect OpenRouter also leaves for Safari and returns on `chord://settings?byok=` (the return handler now re-arms for a second connect). One switch, `IOS_BILLING_LINK_OUT`; false restores the dormant App Store path. The card's decision is `billingCardModel` in kernel, tested per state incl. "never a price". Review risk is Apple's (external-link text changed in 2025); text-only variant is the documented fallback. Rejected: text only first, region-gating (storefront unreadable OTA), Cancel kept in-app, deleting the App Store code |
| [0242](./docs/notes/0242-a-chat-message-can-be-deleted.md) | **A chat message can be deleted; in a shared chat, only for you**: `POST /groups/:id/messages/delete` takes `ids` (max 500) or `all: true` (clear chat: everything as of now, loaded or not). **Personal chat**: rows are removed (with a directive turn's hidden bot reply) and a `messages.deleted` event tells open devices. **Shared chat**: for the caller only (owner decision, replacing a first version that deleted for everyone) - any message can be chosen; selected ones are `message_hides` rows, clearing sets `group_members.cleared_at` (migration `104`); other members and the bot still see everything and no event is sent. One rule, `inViewOf(userId)` in `messages.service.ts`, is used by the message list, both search paths and the chat list's preview/unread count. The chat screen drops cached messages missing from the newest fetched page on every open (no replay, ADR 0002), which is also how the caller's other devices catch up. Files stay in Attachments; nothing is unsent from WhatsApp. App: header **Select** with checkboxes and a Select all / Delete bar, plus **Clear chat** in the chat's settings. Chat: proposable `delete_messages { scope: last\|all, count? }`, ids fixed when the card is staged, confirmable only by the asker. Rejected: delete for everyone, hiding in the personal chat too, a hide row per message for clear, a tombstone, deleting the file with its message |
| [0243](./docs/notes/0243-an-email-can-ask-for-more-than-one-thing.md) | **An email can ask for more than one thing** (amends ADR 0137): the email suggestion pass returned at most one suggestion per thread, picked arbitrarily, and the once-ever `magic` mark meant the rest were never looked at again. The extractor now replies `{"items":[…]}` with every SEPARATE actionable item, soonest due first, capped at `EMAIL_MAGICS_MAX_ITEMS` (4; 1 = old behaviour) by `capSuggestions` - the cap counts cards (each event, each to-do, each distinct list; a list holds up to 12 entries and is staged as ONE `add_list_item` proposal). An advisory is always alone (enforced in code). `threadAlreadyProposed` is asked without a tool: **a thread is staged from once**, so a re-mine adds nothing however it is reworded - which also means opening an email the background pass already made cards for no longer adds one. `needMore` may be set when the text stops partway through a run of dated items; reply cap 300 → 600 tokens. `EmailExtractor.extract` returns one, an array or null, so old stubs compile; a bare suggestion object is still parsed. Already-mined threads are not revisited. Rejected: telling the model how to pick one, no cap, keeping the per-tool guard, one confirm-all bundle per email, a second "anything else?" call |
| [0244](./docs/notes/0244-one-email-one-outcome-whichever-pass-reads-it-first.md) | **One email, one outcome, whichever pass reads it first** (amends ADR 0180/0219/0243/0197/0240): on a fresh prod account the read-time warm-up (0219, started in PARALLEL with the sweep) staged `create_event` "Flight UA 1234…" / "Hotel stay…" seconds before the sweep staged the same emails' trip bundle, and a list for the potluck email before the sweep's to-do. Both passes now run the travel short-circuit (`stageTripIfTravel`); **trip wins** - a claimed thread's pending `create_event`/`create_task`/`add_list_item` cards are withdrawn (`retractGeneralProposalsForThread`; confirmed ones left, ADR 0223 folds); the warm-up runs AFTER the sweep; `extractAndStage` re-asks `threadAlreadyProposed` and inserts under a per-thread `pg_advisory_xact_lock` (`withThreadStagingLock`, never held across a model call); "already staged" counts only the email's own ask (`EMAIL_SUGGESTION_TOOLS`), not ADR 0076 person proposals; the needs-someone-home delivery card is staged in the same locked step; a to-do naming >= 2/3 of a list's entries from the same email is dropped (`dropTasksRestatingLists`) and a plain comma series of 3+ becomes separate list entries (`splitListEntry`). **Offset of the date, not of today**: the model stamped today's -04:00 on a 20 Nov time; `reanchorNowStampedOffset` (utils/tz.ts) fixes a value carrying exactly today's offset when the zone's offset on that date differs - email events/due dates (not a flight's own endpoints, not when the mail names a zone: `namesATimeZone`) and chat `create_event`/`update_event`/`create_task`/`update_task` new-time fields (`validateNativeCall`); `legDatetime` accepts today's reader offset as the reader's; both prompts state the schedule (`offsetScheduleNote`). NOT in `normalizeDatetimes` (it also runs over stored other-place offsets at confirm). **Reply drafter answers the time asked about**: `mentionedDays`/`mentionedSlots` (utils/date-mentions.ts, a parser, month-first, no weekday names) find dates and date+time in the thread; `userAvailability` lists those days past its 10-day window (<= 5, <= 180 days) and gives each asked time a calendar-computed `FREE`/`BUSY` verdict (an hour assumed; neighbours listed when busy) - a live model misread an empty day when left to judge; prompt + a note beside the instruction: confirm and stop if free, decline with 2-3 alternatives if busy. Chat: `get_my_availability { dates }`. Rejected: warm-up skips unclassified mail, one shared mark, lock across the LLM call, fixing offsets in `normalizeDatetimes`, a 60-day window, the model picking the dates, changing the app's quick-ask text **Addendum 2026-11-06**: offered windows are read on their own clock - `mentionedSlots` parses ranges (`endMinutes`) and a US zone written after a time (`timeZone`, IANA; PT/ET/CT/MT/AKT/HT/UTC, uppercase, or the zone name); times listed together on a line go with one date and share a zone; `userAvailability` checks each window whole on the writer's clock, filed under the reader's day, earliest first, FREE / PARTLY FREE (30+ min stretches) / BUSY, and the label says "written as … PST" when the clocks differ; `MAX_ASKED_SLOTS` 3 -> 12; with several verdicts the drafter offers the EARLIEST free one (at most one fallback), on both clocks (prod: an 11/10 10:30 AM PT window was checked at 10:30 New York, and 11/12 was suggested instead). Also read: non-US zone abbreviations/names (CET, BST, IST = India, AEST, "Central European Time" before US Central, "3pm London time" via the place table), UTC/GMT offsets incl. "(UTC-08:00)", 24-hour ranges (hour past 12 or a leading zero; "3:00-4:00" stays unread), a zone written before the time ("PT: 10:30") and one stated for the whole email ("All times are in Pacific Time"; two different = none) **Addendum 2026-10-23**: every email card (incl. invite, delivery, ADR 0248 change cards) is staged under `withEmailStagingLocks` (thread lock, then a per-user lock) with all cross-thread duplicate checks INSIDE it, so two emails with the same ask read at once stage one card; the invite branch asks `threadAlreadyProposed`; a "someone must be home" delivery card is one per tracking number, newest email wins. Trip staging and person extraction take the same locks after their model call; a leg already in a pending trip bundle for the same traveler is not staged again |
| [0245](./docs/notes/0245-a-time-is-where-and-when-it-was-written.md) | **A time is where and when it was written** (amends ADR 0244/0240/0197). **Weekday names are dates**: `mentionedDays`/`mentionedSlots` resolve "today", "tomorrow", "Sunday"/"this Sunday" (next one after the day the email was SENT, in the reader's zone - never the server clock or the day it is opened); a bare weekday written on that weekday = no match; "next Friday" lists BOTH Fridays and gets no time verdict (except written on a Friday: +7); "last Friday" nothing; a weekday beside a written date is that date; abbreviations only capitalised where they read as a day; "at 3" is read only as 8-11 AM / 1-6 PM (reverses 0244's no-guess for those hours). **A time at a place is on that place's clock**: `placeTimeZone` (kernel; city, airport or street address against the metro table) + `placeDatetime` (moves only a bare or reader-stamped time); applied by `anchorModelTimes` - the one step BOTH tool paths share, so the text-fence path now gets 0244's offset fix - to `create_event`/`update_event` with a `location` and `add_reservation` with an `address`; `add_reservation` falls back to the stay it falls within, then the trip destination; also email events with a location and attachment-filed restaurant/movie times. Zone unknown = the reader's clock for that date, and the reservation result says so (`timeZone: null` + instruction; never called local time there). **Travelling: no in-person time unless the city overlaps**: `whereabouts.service.ts` derives away days from the user's own trips (leg depart-arrive = travel day; between legs = in that city; stay check-in..check-out; cancelled/deleted excluded; whose trip = participant, named on a leg, or creator when nobody is named); home = user-set weather place (source `user` only), else confirmed `home_airport`, else the trip's own first origin. `userAvailability` marks lines `| AWAY: in Denver`, leads an asked-about time's verdict with AWAY, adds a Travel sentence and the first two days back; `meetingHints` (a parser) reports whether the thread names that city / reads as a call / in person / does not say; unresolved = do not confirm in person, state the travel, offer a call or a day after return. Same in chat (`get_my_availability`) and the away days are skipped in the magics free-times line. Rejected: one reading of "next Friday", anchoring weekdays to today, geocoding unknown places, trip days as busy blocks, the model judging in-person/where, IP-derived home, the place rule in `normalizeDatetimes` |
| [0246](./docs/notes/0246-mail-questions-are-counted-on-the-server-and-answered-by-recipe.md) | **Mail questions are counted on the server and answered by recipe**: chat can now answer "which mailing lists am I on", "who emails me most", "who am I not answering", "what did I spend at X", "what am I paying for every month", "any coupons for X". Two general read-only tools do the counting - `query_mail_senders { rank: received\|written_to\|awaiting_reply\|gone_quiet }` (`email/sender-stats.service.ts`, SQL over the unsealed address columns, mass mail left out unless asked, rows carry `inPeople` so the bot can offer `add_person`) and `query_receipts { merchant?, after?, before?, groupBy?, recurring? }` (`email/receipts.service.ts`: `receipt_json` is sealed, so rows are read and worked in memory under a 2,000-row cap; one purchase counted once across its order/shipping emails; totals per currency, never summed across; `recurring` = one merchant, even spacing, 3+ charges, similar amount, not stopped) - and one block of recipes (`MAIL_QUESTION_RECIPES` in `bot/tools.ts`, appended on personal turns only) maps each kind of question to a call, so a new question is a new line, not a new tool. Mailing lists keep their own pair because one acts: `list_subscriptions` (senders whose mail carries `List-Unsubscribe`) and proposable `unsubscribe_from_sender { sender }` (exact-or-ask on the sender; a link-only sender gets a link card and no confirm step; ADR 0122's three outcomes kept apart) - the chat half unsubscribe never had. "Who emails me least" maps to `gone_quiet`, and says so. Coupons needed no code (ADR 0152 + 0126 already answer `coupons from:<store> newer_than:30d`); codes and expiry are not read (ADR 0179). The Finance tab (ADR 0070) is filled from the same service via `GET /me/email/receipts`. **Addendum 2026-11-05**: a list the user already unsubscribed from counts only mail sent >2 days after the request (`email/left-lists.ts`, in SQL before the limit), so asking again gets the NEXT lists; one that kept mailing comes back `stillMailing`/`unsubscribedAt` with that count, and the bot offers `trash_future_mail`; auto-trashed senders are left out; `listSubscriptions({ left: 'drop' })` for suggestions (tool, daily offer), default `keep` for the screen and the sender matcher **Coverage is stated everywhere**: a receipt exists only for mail the suggestion pass read since ADR 0160, so every total is "from the receipts Chord has" (tool result, tool description, and the tab). All personal-chat only; no migration, AI pass or env key. Rejected: skills only over `search`/`read_email` (a page is not the mailbox; slow, billed, wrong totals, every email through the model), one tool per question, a `load_skill` mechanism now, extracting coupon codes, contacts by city (no address data), summing currencies, two charges as a subscription |
| [0247](./docs/notes/0247-a-suggestion-can-go-to-several-groups.md) | **A suggestion can go to several groups at once**: the Action Items and trip-bundle cards take several groups (`targetGroupIds`, max 12); confirm runs the suggestion once per group in one transaction, each copy fanning out to that group (a trip bundle = one trip per group); every group is membership-checked before any write. Groups after the first run with `additionalGroupShare`, so `createEvent` skips the confirmer's own copy (`omitCreatorCopy`) and the second nudge. ADR 0150's rule offer only when exactly one group is picked. `GroupChips` + kernel `nextGroupSelection`: phone taps toggle, web plain click picks one and Ctrl/⌘-click adds or removes. Copies are independent (a later leg is on one trip only). No new chat tool (chat already targets one group per call). Rejected: one trip visible in several groups, a copy per group on the confirmer's calendar |
| [0248](./docs/notes/0248-an-email-can-change-what-you-already-have.md) | **An email can change what you already have**: a plain-text cancellation (NYAC: "You have successfully cancelled out of a reservation ... Squash Singles, Fri Oct 16, 5:30-6:15 PM", no `.ics`, not travel) reached neither the invite path (ADR 0074) nor the trip path (ADR 0168), so the event stayed on the calendar - and reschedules, bills paid, forms received have the same shape. **Code picks candidates, the model names one, code checks it, the user taps a card.** The ADR 0088 world block labels lines that came from a row (`e1` calendar, `t1` to-do, `p1` pending email suggestion; `formatLabelledWorld`, `email/magics-context.ts`), the label maps back to the row in code (no id in the prompt), plus up to 6 events per email on days it names or from its sender domain (`loadThreadEvents`), its own suggestion left out. New kind `change {target, action: remove|move|done|drop, startsAt?, endsAt?, dueAt?, why?}`, outside the ADR 0243 cap (max 4, one per target), advisory still alone; cancelled-but-not-listed = nothing (never an event for the slot). `applyEmailChanges` (`email/email-changes.service.ts`) drops and logs `unknown_label`/`wrong_action`/`gone`/`past`/`bad_time`/`card_exists` (a dismissed card is not re-offered); event remove -> `delete_event` card (scope `this` for an occurrence), move -> `update_event` (length kept), to-do done/drop -> `complete_task`, move -> `update_task`; a pending suggestion is withdrawn, or re-offered at the new time under its own email. Display fields (`eventTitle`/`eventStartsAt`/`taskTitle`) in `INTERNAL_TOOL_FIELDS`; cards in Action Items (no group chips) and docked beside the email (`EmailMagicSuggestion.kind` `change`, Apply), not in feeds/briefing; `email change staged|skipped|resolved` logs. Real model 12/12 on `server/scripts/eval-email-changes.ts` (invented label dropped by the check). Replaces this ADR's first version (a `cancelled` kind with a 15-minute/shared-word/sender rule matcher). Not in scope: flights/hotels (0168), trip reservations, already-mined mail. Rejected: the rule matcher, ids or titles as the target, changing without a tap, one kind per shape. **Addendum 2026-10-23**: a move to the time the item already has (`unchanged`) and an event on a calendar the user can only read (`read_only`, a subscribed feed) stage nothing **Addendum 2026-10-27**: no chat note any more - the card in Action Items / beside the email is the whole answer (`enqueueEmailChangeNotice` removed) |
| [0249](./docs/notes/0249-a-receipt-outlives-its-email.md) | **A receipt outlives its email, and Findings asks about your mail**: a receipt lived on `email_threads.receipt_json` and totals skipped trashed threads, so deleting the email (no thread row at all, ADR 0073/0224) dropped the purchase from every total. New `email_receipts` (migration `105`, one row per `(user_id, gmail_thread_id)` - the provider id, since the thread row goes with the mail), written by `keepReceipt` beside the thread column; holds total, vendor, order number, date, **payment method** (card type + last four or a wallet, sealed inside `receipt_json`; `normalizePaymentMethod` keeps at most the last 4 digits whatever the model returns) and **mailbox** (`account_id`, not an FK, + the address then). Totals/Findings read it with the thread left-joined only for a link (`threadId` null = "email deleted", no link). `copyThreadReceipts` copies existing ones (idempotent); encryption backfill + plaintext count cover the table. Deleted `RETENTION_RECEIPT_DAYS` (1095) after the email's date (a yearly charge needs 3 payments to count as repeating) and with the account (FK cascade + key destruction). The hidden Finance tab is shown as **Findings** (💡, key stays `finance`): "Ask about your mail" tappable questions (`findingsQuestions`/`topShops` in kernel - monthly payments, mailing lists, who emails most, who needs a reply, plus "how many orders from X last month" for the 3 most frequent shops; nothing needing whole financial history) that post to the personal chat and open it, then the receipts with card, mailbox and deleted marker. `query_receipts` rows carry `paidWith`/`mailbox`. Not in scope: deleting one receipt, delivery/flight facts (they describe the email and go with it), coupon codes. Rejected: counting trashed threads only, keeping the thread row for its receipt, storing a full card number, a separate answer screen |
| [0250](./docs/notes/0250-a-thread-opens-at-its-unread-mail.md) | **A thread opens at its unread mail**: the reader always started at the top of a thread, so the new reply at the bottom of a long thread took scrolling to find. `threadOpenPlan` (kernel `email-thread-open.ts`, tested): every unread message is unfolded and the reader scrolls to the earliest unread one (so several new replies read in order, as Gmail does); nothing unread = the newest message, as ADR 0121 had it. Decided once per thread from the state at OPENING, since opening marks it read and re-deciding after the refresh would fold the new mail back up. Positions are remembered as messages lay out, so the scroll happens whichever arrives first. No server change, no new capability, so no bot tool. Rejected: landing on the newest unread (skips earlier unread replies in the same thread), re-deciding on every refresh **Addendum 2026-11-10**: read message by message - opening no longer marks the thread; each open message is marked read once 60px of it is on screen (`kernel/src/email-auto-read.ts`), via `EmailModifyRequest.messageIds` (markRead only; Gmail batchModify, Outlook per message, IMAP per UID); a refused per-message write is retried on the next scroll, not queued (ADR 0199 is a thread intent); a message arriving after opening opens; Mark unread still sticks |
| [0251](./docs/notes/0251-byok-is-free-and-the-fee-waits-for-whatsapp-usage.md) | **BYOK is free, and a fee waits for WhatsApp usage** (amends ADR 0195/0238): bringing your own key works and nobody is asked for a base plan - done by CONFIG, `STRIPE_PRICE_ID_BYOK` blanked in `.env.prod` (no new flag; built, then removed). Nothing deleted: the plan, Stripe product, App Store product and webhook mapping stay dormant for a usage-priced WhatsApp add-on. `byokPriceUsd`/`appleByokProduct` are sent only while a base price is configured and their absence is how the card knows ("Using your own key - free", no Manage billing); the bot's trial-ended message and `get_checkout_link` say BYOK is free. Measured: ~80 outbound WhatsApp messages per user per month; Meta bills service replies at the utility rate since 1 Oct 2026 (US ~$0.0034, unconfirmed). The WhatsApp+BYOK price is left open |
| [0252](./docs/notes/0252-an-empty-key-is-said-once-where-the-user-is-looking.md) | **An empty key is said once, where the user is looking** (extends ADR 0044/0042): a BYOK key out of credit (or rejected) used to be reported only in a chat reply, while every background AI pass (email sorting, suggestions, people, briefing) failed with a log line. `buildByokLlm` now returns the LLM wrapped in `withKeyHealth`: a credit/auth failure writes `users.byok_key_issue` (migration `105`), a working call clears it - one choke point, nothing to remember per pass. The proactive tick posts ONE 1:1-chat nudge per outage (what is wrong, what is paused, a card to Settings → AI model; claimed atomically; a chat reply that already said it claims the outage first, so the sweep waits 45s); `EmailStatus.aiKeyIssue` drives a banner above the Email tab's to-dos and `BillingStatus.byokKeyIssue` a line in Settings → AI model. Saving or removing a key clears it. Rate limits and outages are deliberately not flagged. No bot tool (a status line, not a way to change anything) |
| [0253](./docs/notes/0253-say-where-whatsapp-is-from-config.md) | **Say where WhatsApp is, from config**: the app never said Chord could be reached on WhatsApp or at what number (Telegram has a Connected apps card; WhatsApp had a mute row that only appears once bound). `WHATSAPP_PUBLIC_NUMBER` (E.164, validated, empty = no entry) is served on `/health` and `/me/settings` and read at runtime, so changing the business number is a redeploy, not an app release. Three entries, all tap-to-open `wa.me`: a Settings → Connected apps WhatsApp card, a note on the phone-verify step, and a line on the sign-in page. A user's binding is keyed on THEIR number, so changing Chord's leaves bindings intact; the Meta phone-number id/token and the per-number 24h window move separately. No bot tool (information only). Rejected: baking the number into the app, an in-app Connect button (WhatsApp onboards by the user messaging Chord, ADR 0053), a card with no number |
| [0254](./docs/notes/0254-a-launch-opens-on-action-items-never-the-sign-in-page.md) | **A launch opens on Action Items, and never on the sign-in page**: tab bar is now Action Items, Email, Messages (centre), Calendar, Settings, and the root route redirects to `/catchup/actions` (a pending join code still wins; route names and the `chats` key unchanged). The sign-in flash was the root route rendering the marketing page + sign-in card from the first frame and only redirecting after `/health` THEN `/me` (sequential) answered. `bootScreen` (kernel, tested): signed in -> app; session token on the device and boot pending -> plain splash; no token -> landing at once; rejected token or unreachable server -> landing. `/me` now starts alongside `/health`. Rejected: faster redirect with the page still visible, skipping `/health` for returning users, optimistic routing from a cached user **Addendum 2026-10-27**: a launch lands on Email on every platform, web and phones (`launchTab()`, owner 2026-10-27; Action Items keeps its place in the bar); the Messages list hides its back button and tapping Messages inside a chat returns to the chat list |
| [0255](./docs/notes/0255-a-default-mailbox-is-set-in-chat.md) | **A default mailbox is set in chat**: new mail always left from the primary Gmail (else the oldest working attached mailbox), computed and never stored. `users.default_send_mailbox` (migration `107`, `'primary'` or an `email_accounts.id`, NULL = that rule). `resolveSendMailbox` (`services/email/default-mailbox.ts`) uses the stored one while it can still send, else the rule; `defaultSendMailbox` (chat compose, family invite) calls it and `EmailStatus.defaultSendAccountId` makes the New message screen open on it. `setDefaultMailbox` matches provider/address/local part/domain label/nickname, exact wins, else a unique partial; `ambiguous` and `needs_reconnect` store nothing; "" clears. Personal-only `set_default_mailbox { mailbox? }` (omit = read). Chat only, no Settings control (owner). Replies/forwards still go from the mailbox the email came to. Rejected: a Settings picker, storing the address. **Addendum**: the compose card shows "from <address>", fixed at staging (`fromMailbox`/`fromAddress`, hidden from the model) and used on confirm; a mailbox that stopped working refuses (`mailbox_unavailable`), never switches sender. **Every later per-user default goes in `users.defaults` jsonb (migration `108`) as key → value** via `defineUserDefault(key, zod)` in `services/user-defaults.service.ts` (`get`/`set`, null removes), never a new column |
| [0256](./docs/notes/0256-a-default-calendar-can-be-set.md) | **A default calendar can be set, in chat or in Settings**: `users.default_calendar_id` was read by every event create (ADR 0232's second step) but never written outside tests, so "my default calendar is Family" stored nothing.
