One writer, `setDefaultCalendar`: by NAME (chat) through `matchCalendarsByName` (exact or ask, no device calendars; `ambiguous`/`not_found` store nothing), by ID (Settings) only a Google/Outlook calendar of this user with owner/writer access (`not_writable`); the primary, "" or null store NULL. `getDefaultCalendar` reads through `resolveTargetCalendarId`. `GET`/`PUT /me/calendar/default`; personal-only `set_default_calendar { calendar? }` (omit = read); a "Default calendar" chip row in Settings › Calendars. Rejected: storing the name, remembering a per-event calendar as the default, the tool in shared groups |
| [0257](./docs/notes/0257-what-chord-pays-for-and-who-picks-the-key.md) | **What Chord pays for, and who picks the key**: the inventory of every outside service with a cost (metered: system-key LLM, Meta WhatsApp, Twilio Verify SMS, web search; per transaction: Stripe, Apple; fixed: GCP / Cloud SQL / KMS / Redis / developer programs / the annual Google CASA assessment; free today: Gmail, Calendar, Graph, IMAP, Telegram, Expo push) with who bears each for a BYOK user and whether billing guards it - a living list, new dependencies add a row. Compiling it found eleven call sites that asked for Chord's own model (`getLlm()`), so a BYOK user's email suggestions, people extraction, briefing, attachment indexing and memory extraction ran on and were paid for by Chord, and a dead key never showed on them. Now `getLlmForUser` is the only way to reach a model (user's key if they have one, else Chord's) and `one-key-chooser.test.ts` fails the build on a direct `getLlm()`. Licence findings (checked 2026-10-16): Open-Meteo's free API is non-commercial only (a subscription app is commercial, so a paid plan is needed before billing is on, and CC BY 4.0 attribution is missing from the app); GeoLite2 needs attribution (present) and replacing within 30 days of a new release (kept only by deploying). Airport/metro/zone/airline tables are curated in-repo and flight, map, restaurant and carrier sites are link-outs only, so none is licence-bearing. Open, unchanged: WhatsApp sends, SMS OTP and attachment indexing are not guarded by entitlement; the fixed costs are in no plan's price. Sets no price |
| [0258](./docs/notes/0258-we-ask-for-the-phones-location.md) | **We ask for the phone's location, and use it when it is given** (supersedes in part ADR 0091's "without a location permission", amends 0148): weather location tiers are now `user` (explicit, still wins - it must survive travel) > `device` (the phone's own fix, only if allowed) > `ip` (GeoLite2, now the fallback) > `tz`. A one-time card on Action Items explains and asks before the system prompt; Settings → Daily briefing has the switch. Least precise fix the OS offers, foreground only, at most every 30 min; the server rounds to ~1 km, keeps one current fix and no history; `PUT/DELETE /me/location`; a device fix follows the phone, never overwrites a `user` choice (it is remembered for 0148's moved-check, which now compares the phone first), and the IP lookup never overwrites it; a nameless fix (browser) is named by the nearby time-zone city else "Your location"; revoked in the OS → stops on next foreground. `expo-location` is native: lazily loaded so an OTA does not crash an older build (**addendum 2026-10-26**: a lazy `import()` in try/catch is NOT enough - Metro reports a module that throws while loading as fatal before the catch runs, which closed the iPhone app on the Email tab after the PR 171 OTA; load any optional native package through `importIfNative(nativeName, () => import(pkg))` in `mobile/src/optional-native.ts`, which checks `requireOptionalNativeModule` first), but phones need a native build + review, the iOS purpose string (added), Android `ACCESS_COARSE_LOCATION` only, the App Store privacy label and Play Data safety. Privacy screen/txt rewritten and Open-Meteo credited (CC BY 4.0). No bot tool for the permission (an OS gesture). Rejected: retiring GeoLite2, background location, device overriding an explicit choice, a location history |
| [0259](./docs/notes/0259-a-chat-can-be-left-or-deleted-from-the-list.md) | **A chat can be cleared, left or deleted from the Messages list, and by asking** (extends 0242, 0045): clear/leave/delete lived three taps deep in each chat's settings and the list had no menu. A long press on a row, or its ⋯ button, opens a sheet - Clear chat (all), Leave group (shared), Delete group (owner only) - each confirming in the same words as the chat's settings (`group-actions.ts`). The rules are now one pair of service functions, `leaveGroupAs` / `deleteGroupAs` (must belong; only the owner deletes; a personal chat is neither), used by the routes (same statuses and messages as before), the list and the bot. New proposable personal-1:1-only tools `leave_group` / `delete_group` stage a card and never claim it happened; the chat is fixed BEFORE the card by the exact-name-or-ask resolver (ADR 0164) with `groupId` + `forUserId` stripped from the model's schema, an owner-only delete is refused up front (`not_owner`), the tap re-runs the shared rules and only the asker's tap counts. Rejected: swipe, letting the bot act on the chat it is in, deleting without a card |
| [0260](./docs/notes/0260-a-signature-can-be-rich-and-built-in-one-place.md) | **A signature can be rich, and every send asks one function for it** (extends 0069/0131): the signature was appended in EIGHT places (compose + reply x Gmail, attached Gmail, Outlook, IMAP), copied from each other and drifting (spelling, HTML built once vs twice, columns read; Outlook sends HTML only). Now `withSignature(body, user)` (`services/email/signature.ts`) returns the text and HTML halves for all of them, and a test fails the build if any other file builds the `-- ` separator; a plain-text signature is byte-for-byte what it was. New `users.email_signature_html` (migration 110) holds cleaned HTML and `email_signature` is DERIVED from it (links keep their address), so the halves cannot disagree. Cleaned on the server on the way in with a `sanitize-html` allow-list (links, emphasis, lists, simple tables, https-only images, a short list of harmless styles; no scripts, handlers, `javascript:`/`data:`/`cid:`/http images, iframes, forms, `<style>`, `url()`/`expression()`); Settings reads back what was kept. Setting plain text replaces rich (`signaturePatch`, shared by the route and the bot). Settings: a rich HTML editor with live preview (`EmailBody ownContent`); chat: new `set_email_signature { text?, html?, clear? }` (personal 1:1). Not done: importing a Gmail signature, WYSIWYG, uploads (later done by ADR 0326), per-mailbox signatures. **Addendum 2026-10-22**: the signature is in the composer box, not added at send - new/reply/forward open with it (`bodyWithSignature`, kernel `email-signature.ts`), the send takes the body exactly (`withSignature` → `outgoingBodies`, rich HTML swapped in only for an unchanged signature), deleting it means none goes, a box with only the signature is empty; AI drafts revise the text above it; chat-drafted mail, family invites and vacation replies get it when made (`signedBody`) **Addendum 2026-11-06**: each mailbox may have its own signature (`users.defaults` `mailbox_signatures`, keyed 'primary' / account id; no entry = the shared one, both halves null = none); `mailboxSignatureRow` is read by every send path, `signedBody` and the vacation reply; one writer `setMailboxSignature` behind `PUT /me/email/mailbox-signature` and `set_email_signature { mailbox?, useShared? }` (mailbox honoured only when the conversation named it, ADR 0229); composers open with the sending mailbox's and a From change swaps an untouched one (`swapSignature`, kernel); Settings has a chip per mailbox; a shared signature never set (`email_signature` NULL) is `DEFAULT_EMAIL_SIGNATURE` "Get Chord at https://www.chordapp.ai" (`sharedSignature`), a cleared one is stored as '' and means none **Addendum 2026-11-13**: an older copy of the signature in the box (a draft saved before the signature changed) never got the rich version and nothing said so; reply/forward now swap an untouched copy when the saved one changes (`useSignatureFollow`), every composer shows a note with **Use saved signature** when the box ends in a `-- ` block that is not the saved one (`hasOutdatedSignature` / `replaceOutdatedSignature`, kernel), and the send logs a warning when it drops a rich signature that way; never replaced without asking. Settings edits a rich signature as it looks (`RichSignatureEditor`: editable page in a sandboxed iframe / WebView, kernel `signatureEditorDocument`), a tapped link opens a field for its address (`setSignatureLinkHref`), the HTML is behind **Edit HTML**, and the server cleans whatever is saved as before |
| [0261](./docs/notes/0261-an-email-has-a-tldr-button.md) | **An email has a tldr button**: under the subject of every open email (one reader for AI and Traditional), `POST /me/email/threads/:id/summarize` returns a few lines - what it is, who from, what it asks of you, dates/amounts/codes as written. Asked for, never automatic. Same model, gate and meter as the reply composer (shared `requireEntitled`/`write`), tokens under new source `email_summary` (migration `111`); thread fenced as data, quotes folded. Not stored (mail content is sealed, ADR 0201): in-memory cache keyed on the newest message id, so a repeat is free and a new reply re-summarizes. No bot tool (changes nothing; `read_email` covers chat). Also: Feed/Cards/Sections headings get a colour and emoji from the category's words (`emailHeadingStyle` in kernel; unknown = ✉️ + a colour fixed by name). Rejected: summarize on open, storing it, reusing `email_reply`'s source. **Addendum 2026-10-17**: tldr reads every message in full (was the first 3,000 characters of the newest 12, quotes always dropped); quotes fold only after the first message, so a forward keeps its content; only ceiling is `SUMMARY_MAX_CHARS` (20k chars, ~7 pages; was 160k until 2026-10-20 - a hostile email made each tap ~40k tokens), dropping whole older messages first. **Addendum 2026-10-21**: length follows what the email contains, not its size: 1-2 lines for a short email, else one line per action/deadline/event/amount/decision up to `SUMMARY_MAX_LINES` (12), never padded |
| [0264](./docs/notes/0264-a-category-is-walked-then-left.md) | **A category is walked, then left**: opening an email from a category line in the AI view (Feed, Cards, Sections) starts a walk - the tapped email, then the category's other UNREAD inbox mail newest first (`categoryNavOrder`, kernel), fixed at the start. Next past the last, or archiving/trashing the last, returns to the view it started from; removing mid-walk goes to the next, never back. Nav store gains `scope` (`list` \| `category`) and the AI view clears any order on focus, so a non-category open never borrows the Traditional list. Removed emails leave the category lines at once (`removedAt`). Reads the existing `?mode=category` list, up to 4 pages; no server change, no bot tool (navigation only). Rejected: walking read mail too, rebuilding the order each step |
| [0265](./docs/notes/0265-ci-runs-the-tests-a-change-touches.md) | **CI runs the tests a change touches**: lint, typecheck and build always; server tests picked by `server/scripts/ci/select-tests.mjs` - a changed test, the tests NAMED after a changed server/kernel file (else the ones NEXT to it), plus the two codebase-scan guards; mobile/docs run none. The WHOLE suite runs on the `full-ci` PR label, "Run workflow" on the Actions tab, or a change to something every test reads (lockfile, package.json, vitest config, test helpers, example.env, the workflow). Job limit 30 min. `vitest --changed` rejected: one kernel line selected 402 of 475 files. **Addendum 2026-11-18**: the whole suite AND a picked subset are split over up to 4 runners by each file's recorded time (`server/scripts/ci/test-shards.mjs`; ~180 s of test time per runner, a small subset stays on one); the `timings` job saves main's times to the Actions cache and `pick` reads them; before, a 299-file subset ran 477 s on one runner |
| [0266](./docs/notes/0266-a-user-can-order-their-email-categories.md) | **A user can order their email categories**: pinned categories come first in the AI view (Cards and Feed's recent-mail cards), in the user's order; the rest keep the default (unread, then newest). Set by press-and-hold then drag (the card and everything above where it landed become the pinned order, `pinnedAfterMove`) or the personal-only `set_email_category_order { categories?, mode: top\|replace, clear? }` (exact title match or ask; never guesses). Stored as titles in `users.defaults` key `email_category_order` (no migration); one writer `setCategoryOrder` for the route (`GET`/`PUT /me/email/category-order`) and the tool. Order only; does not change which emails go where. **Addendum 2026-10-19**: holding a dragged card near the top/bottom edge scrolls the view (`DragScrollView` + kernel `edgeScrollStep`, faster nearer the edge); Settings gets a Reset for the order; Cards keeps open cards open across navigation; Feed/Cards lines show a two-sentence snippet (`EmailTilePreview.snippet`) |
| [0267](./docs/notes/0267-a-user-can-tell-chord-how-to-sort-their-email.md) | **A user can tell Chord how to sort their email**: per-user `users.defaults` lists `email_sort_rules` (`{ from?, subject?, category, rule: always \| never }`; from = address or domain) and `email_user_categories` (`{ title, about }`), set in chat (`add_email_sort_rule`, `create_email_category`, `list_email_sort_rules`, `remove_email_sort_rule`, all personal-only). The LLM classifier is told them in its prompt AND applies them to its answer (`applySortRules`: always sets the path, never cuts that category and below; a thread left with no category is dropped from the carried-forward baseline), so they hold when the model ignores them. Adding/removing a rule re-queues the mail it covers (`classifier_version = 0`); a new category re-queues the last 30 days. `never` needs an existing category (else asks); `always` may create one. Only with `EMAIL_CLASSIFIER=llm`. Rejected: prompt-only rules, re-sorting the whole mailbox per change |
| [0268](./docs/notes/0268-a-model-reads-only-what-a-person-can-see.md) | **A model reads only what a person can see**: an email could show the reader one thing and the AI passes another - text/plain beat the HTML the reader renders at sync and in every pass, and `htmlToText` (regexes) kept `display:none`, zero-size, white-on-white, `<style>`-hidden, `<template>` and comment text. Now one rule in `utils/email-text.ts`: `modelBodyText` (sync producers, `fetchFullBodyText`) and `bodyForModel` (magics sweep + read-time, trip staging, delivery/flight, person extraction, classifier, composer, tldr, bot `read_email`, meeting context) = the HTML rendered to VISIBLE text when there is HTML, the plain part only when there is none. `htmlToText` is now `visibleHtmlText` (`utils/visible-text.ts`, `htmlparser2`): drops hidden elements (display/visibility (inherited)/opacity/font size <= 1px (inherited)/mso-hide/collapsed overflow boxes/off-screen indent or position/clip/scale(0)/`hidden`), comments incl. Outlook conditional blocks, text matching its painted background (unknown background = visible), and simple-selector `<style>` rules (`@media` ignored). The invisible-character strip adds bidi isolates, U+034F, Hangul fillers, U+180E and the TAG block, and covers subjects/previews in prompts; HTML attachments are read as rendered and all extracted text loses invisible characters (`stripInvisible` keeps tabs). `one-body-reader.test.ts` fails the build when a service picks the body itself. Not covered: hidden PDF text, complex-selector hiding; existing excerpts from text/plain stay until opened or re-synced. Rejected: trusting a plain part that "looks fine", more regexes, a headless browser, a mismatch advisory (later) |
| [0269](./docs/notes/0269-what-the-security-questionnaire-says-is-true.md) | **What the security questionnaire says is true** (CASA AL1 gaps, one change): `chord.sid` is `Secure` when `NODE_ENV=production` (`sessionCookieOptions()`, set and cleared the same way; not `req.secure`, which depends on Cloudflare's TLS mode); **`sessions.token` holds `h_` + SHA-256** of the client's token (`utils/session-token.ts`; sign-in stores the hash, `loadSession`/logout hash what they are shown; plain SHA-256 since the token is 144 random bits), migration `112` (`hashSessionTokens`, Postgres `sha256()`, skips rows already `h_`) keeps existing sessions and the web role re-runs it at boot for raw tokens the old process wrote between migrate and restart - **a test or fixture inserting a session must store `hashSessionToken(token)`**; `?token=` is accepted on the two SSE stream paths only (it was every route) and the debug request log runs through `redactUrl`; Graph `clientState`, the Gmail push token and Meta's verify token use `secretsEqual` (hash both sides, `timingSafeEqual`); pairing codes from `nanoid` `customAlphabet`, not `Math.random`; a BYOK `baseUrl` is screened on save AND per request (`assertSafeModelEndpoint` / `modelEndpointFetch`: https + public host in production, anything in dev for local Ollama, no redirects) and its validation failure no longer echoes upstream text, the custom IMAP host gets the same screen in production, and `utils/ssrf.ts` also blocks multicast/reserved/benchmark/TEST-NET/ff00::/8 and screens the IPv4 inside NAT64/6to4; `safeFileHeaders` serves inline only images (not SVG) and PDF, active types (HTML/SVG/XML/JS/unknown text) as `application/octet-stream` + `nosniff` (email attachment and group file downloads); WhatsApp zip import caps 10,000 entries / 512 MB declared and refuses `..`/absolute paths before writing; sign-out (and account deletion) removes every AsyncStorage key except layout prefs (`storageKeysToClearOnSignOut` in kernel, allow-list), resets native module caches (`mobile/src/sign-out.ts` `onSignOut`) and reloads the page on web; the error handler (`middleware/error-handler.ts`) answers a 5xx with `internal error` and keeps a deliberate 4xx message; nginx gets HSTS (1 year, includeSubDomains, no preload) and a query-free access log via `snippets/chord-security.conf`, included by `deploy.sh` into certbot's site file. TLS protocols/ciphers come from certbot's `options-ssl-nginx.conf` - check on the VM with `nginx -T`. **Addendum 2026-10-22**: CORS no longer echoes any origin with credentials - `buildCorsOriginCheck` allows chordapp.ai, www.chordapp.ai, `WEB_ORIGIN`/`SIGNUP_URL` and `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` (dev: localhost/LAN too), no-Origin requests still pass; `uuid` forced to 11 for `xcode`, and braces/image-size/node-forge (Expo build tooling only, no fixed release that works) suppressed with `docs/security/dependency-exploitability.md` |
| [0270](./docs/notes/0270-a-person-in-a-search-is-a-sender-not-a-word.md) | **A person in a search is a sender, not a word**: "emails from Bill" reached keyword search as `emails \| bill` (stopword `from` dropped), so promos and billing@ outranked Bill, and `from:` was a substring of only the thread's LATEST sender. `resolveCorrespondent` (`email/person-resolve.ts`) turns a name/address/domain into addresses from the user's own mail (senders + To/Cc) and own `people` only (ADR 0065/0114): whole-token match with nicknames (`kernel/src/nicknames.ts`; every word asked must match), grouped per person, `people` row = 25 messages, robots last; two near-equal people (< 3x) = `ambiguous` -> no mail query, the bot asks (exact-or-ask, ADR 0077/0094/0137); a shared robot address (noreply@) matches only with the sender's name via blind trigrams. `findEmailThreads` matches a sender on ANY message by exact address/domain, a recipient in To/Cc, new `actions`/`kind` (receipt/delivery/flight/invite) filters, dates as instants (`textInstant`). One preparation for both surfaces (`email/mail-query.ts`). Bot `search` gains `from`, `to`, `after`/`before` (local days), `hasAttachment`, `emailAction`, `emailKind`; `query` may be empty. `parseMailIntent` (kernel) is the plain-language fallback; an unmatched name goes back into the text. Changes ADR 0126: `from:` resolves first, substring only when nobody matches; `to:` is new; an unmatched `to` returns nothing. Eval set `services/search/eval/` (sealed mailbox): person hit@3 0.17 -> 0.83, MRR 0.55 -> 0.82. Migration `113` (sender index). **Addendum 2026-10-23**: one word that is the domain name of every match ("deloitte") is that domain, not a person to ask about; `resolveCorrespondent` takes `direction` so "from" offers only people who sent the user mail and "to" only people who received some (People rows with no mail stay); `askWhichPersonInstruction` writes the options out and shows a To/Cc-only address as "address (name not known)", since the bot made a name up from `andrewallen5@` |
| [0271](./docs/notes/0271-chat-search-orders-mail-by-how-well-it-matches.md) | **Chat search orders mail by how well it matches** (amends ADR 0152): the bot path sorted by date over OR'd words, so a newer promo with ONE word beat the thread matching all of them. Now `order: 'rank'`: a candidate window (8x cap, min 80) re-scored `(coverage x 4 + ts_rank_cd x 10 + 6 in named category) x (1 + 0.15 e^(-age/90d))`, coverage = each query word (up to 8) x2 in subject/snippet/sender, x1 body-only; filter-only queries newest first. A named category is ADDED and boosted, not swapped in; outside it a one-word body-only match is dropped (keeps 0152's newsletter fix); category match stays whole-query (per-word would drag in a Trips category). Up to 5 candidate windows; legacy tsvector rank only for rows with no blind index. Mail filters narrow FILES to attachments from the matching threads (`origin_email_thread_id`, ADR 0071) instead of hiding them, each with the email's subject/sender/link. Eval MRR 0.82 -> 0.89 |
| [0262](./docs/notes/0262-what-chord-cannot-do-gets-a-search-not-a-stand-in.md) | **What Chord cannot do gets a search, not a stand-in** (extends 0188/0153): prod "generate a picture of a boy running" → "yes" got a Google **Maps** card, because `map` was the only `suggest_external_action` kind taking free text. New catch-all kind `search` (`query.q` + `searchType` web\|images\|videos\|news\|shopping → a Google search URL; no terms = no card, `no_query`). One prompt rule (both catalogues + the `context.ts` capabilities block): for something Chord cannot do (make a picture, play music/video, call, text, buy, browse), say so in one line and attach the search card in the same reply, without asking first, called what it is, never "I found"/"I generated"; `map` is for a real place only. Prompt-only (no reliable "is a place" test). Rejected: one kind per tab, asking first, image generation |
| [0263](./docs/notes/0263-android-is-brought-level-with-the-iphone-app.md) | **Android is brought level with the iPhone app** (app and build config only; not yet run on a device): the AI Plan card on Android reports the plan and sends billing to the browser like ADR 0241 (`ANDROID_BILLING_LINK_OUT`, separate from the iOS switch; both feed `BILLING_LINK_OUT`, which Connect OpenRouter also follows) because Play requires Play Billing for anything sold in the app; `ensureContactsAccess(what)` (`mobile/src/contacts-access.ts`) shows an in-app explanation before the Android contacts prompt (bot contact lookup, invite-a-guest picker; iOS unchanged); public `/delete-account` page for Play's Data safety form; unused permissions removed from the manifest (`RECORD_AUDIO`, `WRITE_CONTACTS`, legacy storage, and `ACCESS_FINE_LOCATION` since ADR 0258 asks for approximate only); adaptive + notification icons; `eas.json` Android profiles (`preview` APK, `production` bundle, submit to the internal track as a draft with the ignored `mobile/play-service-account.json`); `app.config.js` reads `google-services.json` (or EAS `GOOGLE_SERVICES_JSON`) when present, without it Android has no push. Owner's side: signing fingerprints in `assetlinks.json` (sign-in fails without, ADR 0081), Firebase + FCM key, Play service account, package name decision. **Addendum 2026-10-17 (parity pass)**: Apple sign-in on Android via the web flow in a Custom Tab (state prefixed `android.` -> callback returns to the `/nativeauth` App Link, `appleReturnPath`); `KeyboardAvoidingView` uses `padding` on Android too (edge-to-edge means the window never shrinks); tab bar + bottom sheets pad for the Android nav bar (`useAndroidBottomInset`); the 8-button task alert becomes an `ActionSheet` on Android (it drew 3); `isGoogleBackedSource` / `pickAndroidWriteCalendar` in kernel recognise Android's `com.google` calendars (wrong write target, doubled Google events before); pushes name a HIGH channel at `priority: high`; files open in a viewer via `expo-intent-launcher`; card app links (`sms:`) are tried directly on Android. Still iOS-only: Reminders sync, the EventKit external-id helper, the dormant App Store path. Not yet run on a device. Rejected: Play Billing, keeping in-app Stripe checkout, one switch for both stores **Addendum 2026-11-23**: Android release builds run R8 (`expo-build-properties` `enableMinifyInReleaseBuilds`; RevenueCat kept, line numbers kept; resource shrinking off since resources are found by name at run time); mapping ships in the bundle and as an EAS artifact. Play's edge-to-edge warning comes from React Native, react-native-screens and expo-image-picker code, not Chord's config; it clears with a library/SDK upgrade |
| [0272](./docs/notes/0272-birthdays-lead-the-briefing.md) | **Birthdays and anniversaries lead the briefing**: a code-built `🎂 Birthdays & anniversaries` section sits at the very top of the morning message (above the weather), from saved People dates, calendar events, open to-dos (title says birthday/bday/anniversary, next 14 days or undated) and the last 14 days of non-bulk, non-archived email by subject (`services/briefing/celebrations.ts`; subjects sealed, so matched in code); same day merged (to-dos/emails attach as notes by first name); the scan prompt is told not to repeat them; the WhatsApp heads-up slot and gated teaser lead with the same list. English keywords only. Rejected: prompt-only placement, creating People rows, an LLM pass over bodies |
| [0273](./docs/notes/0273-a-date-field-opens-a-calendar.md) | **A date field opens a calendar**: every typed `YYYY-MM-DD` field (event edit, People dates, persona birthday add/edit, task due date via a new 📅 Pick date button) gets a pure-JS calendar (`mobile/src/date-field.tsx` `DateField`/`CalendarPicker`, logic in `kernel/src/date-picker.ts`): month arrows, a year list, Today/Cancel, and `allowUnknownYear` → `0000-MM-DD`. Typing still works; time fields stay typed. Rejected: the native datetimepicker (needs a build, not OTA-safe), web-only `<input type=date>` |
| [0274](./docs/notes/0274-a-swipe-says-which-way-and-tldr-reads-the-newest-email.md) | **A swipe says which way, and tldr reads the newest email** (amends 0128/0215/0261): the list swipe did the opposite of its label (the library names a swipe by where the ROW went); now finger left = archive or Trash (Settings), finger right = read/unread, opaque row, fixed full-height coloured panels whose icon grows with the drag, archive fires on will-open, read springs back closed. tldr on a thread reads the NEWEST message in full with its quoted history as the whole conversation (the mirror often lacks the older messages), falling back to message-by-message only when it quotes nothing. Flag-on-swipe not built (provider-side, ADR 0107) |
| [0275](./docs/notes/0275-the-dentist-is-a-role-not-a-name.md) | **"The dentist" is a role, not a name** (extends ADR 0270): a name that matches nobody and is a ROLE (`kernel/src/roles.ts`, ~20 roles with specific whole-word terms; loose ones like health/care/electric left out) is resolved, strongest first, from People notes/name, then saved `contacts` org/note/tags (resolved via the contact's NAME, no email there), then senders whose own name or local part says it (robot addresses only under a name that says so; the DOMAIN is not read, `dentalplansweekly` vs `brightsmilesdental`). A role never asks "which one" (up to 10 addresses). If the server finds none, the search tool asks the phone (ADR 0212 lookup, personal chat, with `role` terms; the app reads Company + Job Title, sends only matching entries, `matchDeviceContactsByRole`); their emails become the filter, and the bot offers once to save the contact to People with the role as its note (`add_person`), so step 1 finds it next time. Old apps ignore `role`. Eval person hit@3 0.83 -> 1.00 |
| [0276](./docs/notes/0276-search-matches-by-meaning-on-sealed-vectors.md) | **Search matches by meaning, on sealed vectors** (supersedes ADR 0006's no-embeddings for search; ships OFF, `EMBEDDINGS_ENABLED`): one vector per email thread (subject, people, category path, receipt/delivery/flight facts, first + latest message via the ADR 0268 body reader; no new summary) and per file (name + summary + first 6,000 chars, ~two pages), sealed per owner (`sealBytes`) as int8 + scale (516 bytes @512), `email_thread_embeddings` / `attachment_embeddings` (migration `114`) with `model` + content hash (unchanged text never re-paid, model change re-embeds). Compared in the app (per-user LRU cache, `EMBED_CACHE_USERS`), only rows of the user's current model, AFTER ADR 0270 person/date/kind filters; blended with keywords by reciprocal rank fusion (k=60), `SEARCH_SEMANTIC_MIN_SIM`, meaning-only hits carry `matchedBy: 'meaning'`; files the same. `getEmbedderForUser` (`llm/embeddings.ts`, the only builder, guarded like `getLlm`): no key = Chord's (metered, `token_usage` source `embedding`); own OpenRouter/OpenAI = theirs, same model id (vendor prefix dropped); own Gemini = theirs, `gemini-embedding-001@768` via REST; own Anthropic/Ollama = Chord's unmetered (`EMBEDDINGS_BYOK_FALLBACK=chord`) or keyword-only (`none`). Chord's embedding key is separate from its chat model (`EMBEDDINGS_BASE_URL/API_KEY`, default `OPENAI_*`); none = meaning search off, logged once. OpenRouter calls send `data_collection: deny`. Backfill newest-first, bounded, after the gated after-sync passes AND a proactive-tick sweep. Privacy text updated. Stand-in-embedder eval = pipeline proof only; `npm run eval:email-search` measures the real model: first live run MRR 0.89 -> 1.00 on the 19-thread corpus |
| [0277](./docs/notes/0277-findings-has-its-own-chat.md) | **Findings has its own chat** (amends ADR 0249): a sample question no longer posts into the personal chat and jumps there; it is answered in a small chat inside the Findings tab (`mobile/src/findings-chat.tsx`, chips + text box + follow-ups + New chat). `POST /me/findings/chat` takes the whole conversation from the client (≤40 messages of ≤4,000 chars, last = user) and stores nothing: no message row, no SSE, no proposal, so the personal chat's history neither reaches the model nor is added to. Read-only mail tools only (`query_mail_senders`, `query_receipts`, `list_subscriptions`, `search`, `read_email`, under the personal chat's scope); a real write tool is refused. A change (unsubscribe, to-do, event, reply) goes through `hand_off_to_chat { request }`, a side tool only this surface has: one stand-alone first-person message naming exactly what (sender name + address, email subject/sender/date, to-do title/date/amount), returned as `handoff: { message }`; the app posts it to the personal chat as the user and opens it, so the bot there acts at once. Same tool loop via `runSideChatTurn` (turn options `onlyTools` + `quiet` + `sideTools`); own prompt (persona, local time, ADR 0246 recipes); entitlement 402, tokens as `bot_turn` unless BYOK. Conversation ends when the tab is left. No bot tool (a place to ask, not a capability). Rejected: post without navigating, a hidden second chat group, write tools here. **Addendum 2026-10-30**: `hand_off_to_chat` refuses a request naming an address the user did not type in this conversation and that is not a sender/recipient in their own mail (`unknownAddresses`; prod handed over twelve lists as `name@example.com`); an assistant message is not a source; side tools may be async **Addendum 2026-11-01**: the hand-off is never posted unasked - Findings reads mail, so an email's instruction could be copied into it and run as the user; the app shows the exact message as a card with Send to chat / Cancel and posts it only on Send (tool answers `sent: false`); a throwing side tool returns the ADR 0102 failure sentence instead of ending the turn |
| [0278](./docs/notes/0278-every-calendar-has-its-own-color.md) | **Every calendar has its own color, and the user can change it** (extends 0072): a calendar shows the user's pick in Chord, else its provider's color (Google/Outlook/phone), else the next free color of Chord's palette (the app icon's five colors, then darker/lighter sets, then slighter shades) - one kernel function, `assignCalendarColors`, for Settings and events. A pick is kept exactly (two calendars may share one); a provider color already in use shifts to a slight shade; claimed primary first then by `first_seen_at` so a new calendar never repaints the others. `calendar_sources.color_choice` (migration `115`, separate from the sync-written `color`), display only, never written to Google/Outlook. One writer `setCalendarColor`: Settings (`PATCH /me/calendar/sources/:id { colorChoice }`, now a partial patch) and personal-only `set_calendar_color { calendar?, color? }` (name or hex, empty clears, omit calendar = read; exact-or-ask over all calendars incl. read-only). Events carry `color` + `calendarName`; web hover tooltip names event, time and calendar; tapping opens the event sheet naming its calendar; month dots per calendar; Settings swatch opens the color choices. **Mailboxes too**: same rule (pick, else palette), picks in `users.defaults` key `mailbox_colors`, `PUT /me/email/mailbox-color`, personal-only `set_mailbox_color`; Traditional chips + row edge/tag in the mailbox color. Email actions get fixed colors (`ACTION_COLOR`): solid To do/Reply badges, matching filter chips |
| [0279](./docs/notes/0279-a-search-filter-nobody-asked-for-is-dropped.md) | **A search filter nobody asked for is dropped** (ADR 0229's rule applied to search): "show me emails from mallory" came back empty because the model filled every optional search filter (`emailKind: "delivery"`, a six-week window, all four actions, `hasAttachment: false`, `to: ""`). `dropUnaskedMailFilters` (`bot/search-filter-guard.ts`) keeps a filter only when the user's own recent words (triggering message + up to 8 earlier in 30 min, bot messages excluded; the side chat passes `userText`) could have asked for it: a kind word, an attachment word, a reply/to-do word, a named time. Empty `from`/`to`, `hasAttachment: false` and all four actions are never a filter. No message to read = taken as given. Descriptions say leave out unless asked; dropped filters are logged by name. Rejected: prompt only, retry without filters on an empty result |
| [0280](./docs/notes/0280-a-second-copy-of-a-role-is-safe.md) | **A second copy of a role is safe**: two web or two worker copies used to duplicate WhatsApp/Telegram sends, approval notices and pushes (every onBroadcast listener runs on every copy via the ADR 0170 replay), double-poll Telegram and double-open IMAP IDLE, reorder a group's bot turns (ADR 0155's tail was in memory) and forget what the bot just asked. One owner per duty via a Postgres session advisory lock on a dedicated connection per process (`services/cluster/leadership.ts`; duties `im` = forward/notify/Telegram poll/Discord handling, `worker` = push/To Do's mirror/IMAP IDLE/push renewal; never started = everything held, so tests and dev are unchanged; ~5 s takeover, sends in the gap are lost like a restart). Bot turns take a `bot_turn_tickets` row (bigserial per group, 15 s heartbeat, 60 s stale) and wait to be head, before the queue so a waiter holds no slot or connection. Follow-up window, numbered list, pending invitees and share offer move to `bot_pending_state` (Postgres, not Redis: Redis is optional, so it would need a second in-memory copy; expired rows pruned by the ADR 0226 pass); per-turn allowances stay in memory. The phone's contact answer (ADR 0212, nothing stored) is forwarded to the waiting copy over Redis pub/sub (`sseHub.sendToPeers`). Migration `116`. Still per-process (waste, not wrong): read-time/preprocess de-dupe, image preload guard, mail-sync coalescers, caches |
| [0281](./docs/notes/0281-chord-says-what-it-remembers.md) | **Chord says what it remembers, and can be told to forget**: a NEW fact from extraction gets a `memory` card (`BotCard.kind 'memory'`, `memoryId`/`memoryStatus`/`memoryForUserId`) under that turn's bot reply, or, with no visible reply (passive, silent bridge), in the sayer's personal chat as a `system` message (no push, not bridged, out of model history); a fact already held (same subject + key, value equal ignoring case/spacing, confirmed OR proposed) gets no row and no card (`writeMemory` → `created`; fixes restating a saved fact opening a new pending card each time). Card states proposed (Keep · Not right) / saved (Forget) / replaced (shows new value) / removed, buttons only for the sayer (kernel `memoryCardActions`); `memory_notices (memory_id, message_id)` (migration `117`, no FK to memories so a deleted fact's card can say "removed") and `refreshMemoryNotice` rewrite the stored card on every change (confirm/reject/decay routes, family-profile edit/delete, tools, clean-up). `remember_fact` returns the same card. New tools `correct_memory { memoryId? | match, value }` (new confirmed row supersedes the old; a proposed one is replaced) and `forget_memory` (reject proposed / delete confirmed), acting only on facts `list_memories` can show (`visibleMemoriesForTurn`), match = every word in subject/key/value, one acts / several `ambiguous` + candidates / none `not_found` (`updated`/`deleted: false` so the pill reads declined). Extraction is told the facts already held so it reuses keys. Daily `maybeCleanupMemories` (proactive tick): drop proposals repeating the saved value, keep newest proposal per fact, drop unanswered proposals older than `MEMORY_PROPOSED_TTL_DAYS` (30) not on the lighter-bar timer; then for chats with confirmed facts newer than `groups.memories_reviewed_at` (≤ `MEMORY_CLEANUP_GROUPS_PER_RUN`), the model names same-subject facts saying the same thing and which to keep, code validates ids (local `fN`, same subject, once each) and supersedes, never writing a value; entitlement-gated, tokens as `memory_cleanup` unless BYOK. Not done: contradictions, bridged-chat notices, family-profile routes still let any member edit a member's fact. Rejected: file per fact, one summary notice per turn, all notices to the personal chat, the model rewriting values, soft forget |
| [0282](./docs/notes/0282-dark-mode-follows-the-device.md) | **Dark mode follows the device, and can be forced**: `theme.ts` has a light and a dark palette; `colors` is a set of getters on the active one (new `onAccent` for text on accent fills, `warnBg`/`redBg`/`greenBg`/`amberBg` tints). **Use `themed((colors) => ({...}))` instead of `StyleSheet.create`** (built once per scheme, read through getters, so `styles.x` call sites are unchanged) and `colors.onAccent` instead of `'#fff'` on an accent fill; a color copied into a module-level constant does not follow. A scheme change remounts each screen's content through the root Stack's `screenLayout` (a fragment keyed on an epoch); keying the Stack itself breaks on web ("Another navigator is already registered"), and this way history and the current route survive. Settings → Appearance → System / Light / Dark, per device in AsyncStorage `app.colorScheme` (kept on sign-out); native also calls `Appearance.setColorScheme`; System mode applies OS changes only while the app is active (iOS flips it for background snapshots); web reads the choice synchronously from localStorage and follows `prefers-color-scheme`. Status bar set from the resolved scheme. Email HTML stays on a white card in dark mode (written for white paper); plain-text mail follows the theme. No bot tool: a per-device display preference the server never sees. Rejected: a `useTheme()` hook in every component, reload-on-change, inverting email HTML |
| [0283](./docs/notes/0283-ai-sees-your-data-only-after-you-agree.md) | **The AI sees your data only after you agree** (Apple 5.1.2(i)): one decision per user in `users.defaults.ai_consent` (`granted\|declined`, when, the recipient sentence shown, age statement). `hasAiConsent` gates the model choke points — `getLlmForUser`/`resolveTurnLlm` return a model that throws `AiConsentRequiredError` before sending anything, `getEmbedderForUser` returns null — and the entry points check first so they skip quietly: bot turn, email AI passes after sync (mirror still syncs), embed sweep, briefing (no-model teaser), other model proactive kinds (skipped), reply composer/tldr/Findings chat (403), attachment auto-filers (text + keyword index still run), WhatsApp import extraction. No user = system work, unaffected. App: a signed-in user with no answer lands on `/ai-consent` (names the real recipient via `aiRecipient`, e.g. "OpenRouter, which passes it to OpenAI (…)", lists what is sent; Allow needs the "18+ or a parent/guardian agreed" box, Not now never does) and Settings → AI model has the switch. Chat: without consent a turn posts a fixed message; AGREE / NO alone in the personal chat is read in code and recorded; `withdraw_ai_consent` bot tool (personal-only). Existing users are asked too, not backfilled. `AI_CONSENT_REQUIRED` (default true; tests run with it off). Policy + Terms: any age with a parent's or guardian's consent; under-13s only via a Family Link-supervised Google account. Not done: filtering a non-consenting member's messages out of a shared group's context |
| [0284](./docs/notes/0284-a-fact-is-found-by-meaning-and-confirmed-before-it-changes.md) | **A fact is found by meaning, and confirmed before it changes**: facts are embedded when a search needs them (no background pass; a chat holds a few hundred one-line facts), in `memory_embeddings (memory_id, model, content_hash, vec)` (migration `118`, one row per fact per model, `ON DELETE CASCADE`, not sealed since facts are not), text = `<who> · <key>: <value>`, re-embedded when the hash changes; embedder = the asker's via `getEmbedderForUser`, Chord's key metered as `embedding`; none = words only. `correct_memory` / `forget_memory` still act on one WORD match; with no word match the 3 closest by meaning (≥ `SEARCH_SEMANTIC_MIN_SIM`) come back `suggested` and nothing changes until the user says yes. `list_memories { query? }` merges word and meaning matches by rank (`fuseRanks`); `matched: false` returns the plain list. `queryVector` exported from `search/semantic.ts`. Not done: a facts category in the unified `search` tool. Rejected: background pass, acting on a confident meaning match, sealing fact vectors, one vector regardless of model |
| [0285](./docs/notes/0285-a-retired-to-do-is-named-in-the-briefing.md) | **A retired to-do is named in the briefing, with why** (extends 0092/0143/0192): `tasks.archived_reason` (migration `119`, not backfilled) is set by every writer — the sweep (`overdue` / `expired` for a passed `live_until` / `completed` for a done task filed), `retireTasksByProvenance` (`event_gone` with a cutoff, else `email_gone`), `updateTask` (`dropped` into archived, cleared out of it and on ADR 0239 revival). | [0286](./docs/notes/0286-a-fetch-is-only-as-fresh-as-the-write-it-followed.md) | **A fetch is only as fresh as the write it followed**: reading down a category in the AI view (Feed/Cards) and putting each email away left the view still listing the deleted ones. ADR 0218 navigates before the mailbox answers and `applyThreadModify` writes the mirror only AFTER the provider round trip, so the walk's focus refetch (ADR 0264) was issued milliseconds after the last taps and well BEFORE their writes - and the filter was `removedAt >= askedAt` with `removedAt` stamped only at the tap, so that answer looked newer than every removal while predating their writes, carried the threads, and nothing refetched again (the AI views hear `email.synced` / `email.tiles.updated`, never the `email.thread.updated` the Traditional list converges on). One rule in `kernel/src/email-removals.ts` (`removedSinceFetch`, pure + tested - `mobile/` has no harness, ADR 0120/0121): an unanswered removal hides the row outright (the `removing` set the recents filter never consulted), an answered one hides it in any data fetched before the **confirmation** - so `doneRemoving` re-stamps `removedAt` when the mailbox answers, not only `startRemoving` at the tap. A REFUSED removal deletes the stamp so the row comes back everywhere at once (ADR 0218), every caller now naming its failed ids (the batch path from `EmailBulkModifyResult.failed`, so a half-worked batch releases exactly the half that did not change). `fetchPredatesRemoval` drives ONE coalesced reload of known-stale data, debounced and only while the view is in front, so a walk does not refetch the tile tree per email - what makes the view right rather than merely not-wrong, and what closes the 10-minute `removedAt` pruning window. `openFromCategory` takes the same rule in place of its `removing`-only filter (a walk started right after deleting from that category was handed ids whose writes were in flight), and `reload` gains a sequence guard so an older answer in flight cannot replace the fresher list behind it. Rejected: awaiting the write before navigating (undoes 0218), patching rows from `email.thread.updated` in the AI views (removes what the filter already removes, brings nothing back, and the stream is closed during the walk anyway), shortening the recents cache (a tuning answer to a staleness rule), caching an archived flag client-side |
| [0287](./docs/notes/0287-unread-is-the-band-the-user-orders-within.md) | **Unread is the band, the user's order governs within it** (amends 0266): `applyCategoryOrder` is absolute - pinned first, everything else after - so a pinned category already read through sat above an unpinned one holding unread mail, and neither AI view said how much unread it held. `applyUnreadFirstOrder` (kernel, beside `applyCategoryOrder`, which it still uses for each half) splits the cards on "holds unread mail" and applies the user's order to the two halves separately: a pinned category with unread is at the very top, an unpinned one with unread still beats everything read, and a read category falls back to EXACTLY where the user put it (with nothing unread the result is `applyCategoryOrder`'s, unchanged). In kernel because `mobile/` has no harness (0120/0121) and because Feed and Cards must order the same way; `items` arrives in the view's own default order (most unread, then last mailed), which the unpinned tail of each band keeps. One shared `UnreadBadge` reads `3 unread`, not a bare number - a Cards header also carries a decisions count in the category colour, so the badge says the word and uses the accent. Sections is untouched (0147 groups it by decision KIND, so it has no category cards). Consequence: a card dragged below unread rows records them as pinned too (0266 takes everything at and above the drop), and a read card dragged to the very top shows at the top of the READ band until the unread is read - the pin is recorded either way. Rejected: most-unread-first over the whole list (removes the ordering 0266 exists for), a separate "Unread" section (two lists, and a card jumps between them as it is read), a bare number, banding Sections, badge-only with no reordering (a badge three screens down is still three screens down) |

Now `selectExcerptsForTurn`: a file with a vector of the user's embedding model (ADR 0276) comes in only at `RETRIEVAL_SEMANTIC_MIN_SIM` (0.35; may come in sharing no word, on its summary); without one, `wordsQualifyFile` judges each message of the window as whole words (`utils/whole-words.ts`) - a 4+ letter word of the filename, or at least two (and half) of the message's words in its matching chunks. Pinned unchanged. `citedFiles` (`bot/file-citations.ts`) names a file only when `look_at_attachment` / `reference_file` / `send_file` reached it this turn (`usedFileIds` on the turn result) or the reply repeats 5 consecutive words (3 real) from its injected excerpt or full text; group-visibility rule and cap of 4 kept. Dead `scoreRow` / `selectAttachmentsForTurn` / `searchUserOtherGroups` removed. Rejected: tighter substring checks, asking the model, citing every injected file, one floor for search and injection. **Addendum 2026-10-25**: the phrase rule is gone - a `/plan` reply repeating a to-do read with `list_tasks` was footed with two club statements holding the same words; `citedFiles` now names only a file a file tool reached this turn (reply text no longer an input, `repeatsPhrase` deleted); a missing row is acceptable, a wrong one is not; retrieval unchanged |

Now a clash needs the time AND a person on both (`trips/trip-conflict.ts`): trip travellers = `trip_participants` + live legs' `persona_id`/`passenger_name` (never the booker); an event's people = its assignee (member or managed persona, ADR 0151), else the copy's owner, plus any traveller whose FIRST name is a whole word of the title (never a surname). `ticketsForOthers` keeps someone else's ticket out of `computeConflicts`, `busyIntervalsInWindow` and the event-created nudge; a ticket booked for someone else is stored and pushed as FREE on the booker's calendar (`trips/ticket-free-busy.ts`: on ticket create/update + hourly sweep, both ways; phone mirror sets availability) **Addendum 2026-10-24**: an entry with no leg uid departing within 10 min of a live leg on a trip its owner can see, and naming that leg's number or destination (origin alone loses to a destination match; a tie = ordinary), is that ticket (`ticketTrips`/`legMatchStrength`), and the match is STORED (`events.trip_id` + `trip_link` 'auto'|'user', migration `122`; the user corrects it in chat with `set_event_trip`, incl. `notTravel`, and a 'user' answer is never re-guessed; a traveller's first name in the TITLE no longer makes the event theirs ("Send Ben Money" is the parent's errand) - the event's maker joins its owner instead; events of 5 min or less are free (was <5) and the briefing treats all-day events as all-day **Addendum 2026-11-11**: anyone can be a traveller - family or a friend, with or without an account (`trip_named_travelers` by name + optional persona, migration `147`, `trips/travelers.ts` `resolveTripTraveler`/`addTripTravelers`, on the trip itself even with no tickets); a new trip naming nobody asks "Who is going on ...?" instead of a conflict check, kept as `bot_pending_state` `trip_travelers` and re-checked once answered; on a personal turn `resolveTripId` sees trips in every group the user is in |

| [0293](./docs/notes/0293-sign-in-with-microsoft-connects-the-mailbox.md) | **Sign in with Microsoft, and the mailbox comes with it** (amends 0014/0156/0080): `POST /api/v1/auth/microsoft` takes the same PKCE code as the Outlook connect; ONE exchange (`exchangeOutlookCode`, now also returning the `id_token`) gives the identity and the mailbox, attached through `attachOutlookMailbox` (shared with Settings) with no second consent. Button on web, iOS and Android, same expo-auth-session flow and redirect as the Outlook connect (`mobile/src/microsoft-auth.ts`; Microsoft accepts `chord://`, so no App Link). **An attached Outlook mailbox is NOT a Microsoft login** (owner decision): a Google/Apple user who attached Outlook in Settings and then uses Sign in with Microsoft gets a NEW account unless the email is proven; `microsoft_sub` is written only by Sign in with Microsoft, never by a mailbox attach (a child's school mailbox must not open the parent's account) - say so whenever this is asked. Only `microsoft_sub` moves, so a Settings-attached mailbox still never becomes a login. | [0294](./docs/notes/0294-is-the-sender-who-they-say-they-are.md) | **Is the sender who they say they are?** (amends 0180, 0201): a prod "Robinhood" phish passed the provider's filter, and the read-time pass (ADR 0180) was told to catch impersonation but its prompt never had a From line. New subtree: pure checks in `kernel/src/sender-check/` (`checkSender` → `SenderCheck {level, signals}`; curated `BRANDS` table + domains it sends from; look-alike folding/edit distance; RFC 8601 parser) and loaders in `server/src/services/email/sender-check/` (`annotateSenderChecks` on every thread open, `senderContextBlock` for the read-time prompt). Signals: brand name on a non-brand or freemail domain, look-alike domain, display name showing another address, a People full name from an address the user doesn't have for them, DMARC fail / no auth, Reply-To elsewhere, IP/punycode links, mixed-script name, executable/HTML attachment, first contact, not addressed to you. Non-distinctive brands (Chase, Apple, Target) count only with service words ("Chase Alerts", never "Chase Miller"); a brand not listed is never claimed; never says "safe". Only the TOPMOST `Authentication-Results` is read (`firstHeader`; a lower one may be the sender's), and DMARC pass proves only the sender's own domain. Migration `121` adds plaintext `email_messages.reply_to` + `auth_results` (parsed), undefined-leaves-alone like list-unsubscribe; Gmail/IMAP free at sync, Outlook written on thread open (that fetch already had the headers and dropped them); no backfill ("not available"). Reader: tap name → sender box, tap To → recipients box, a warning line above the body + ⚠ by the name. Chat: `read_email` carries `senderWarnings`. Deferred: RDAP new-domain age, QR codes, reply-chain hijack, brand-in-body. Rejected: LLM-only, a public-suffix list, header fetch on open for old mail, link-text-vs-href (ESP tracking). **Addendum**: look-alikes compare what the reader SEES - punycode decoded in plain TS (`confusables.ts`), NFKC, accents dropped, Cyrillic/Greek look-alikes mapped to Latin, then 0→o / 1,I,i→l / rn→m folded - against `BRANDS` AND `knownDomains` (domains the user has SENT to or has in People; never received mail, or a repeat phisher becomes "known"; a domain the user writes to is never a look-alike). A `danger` message renders as plain text (no formatting, images or tappable links; a note lists where links go; "Show original" keeps images off), and the ADR 0225 image preload skips it like spam **Addendum 2026-10-27**: on a likely fake (any other danger signal, or brand_mismatch / look-alike / auth_failed / auth_weak / name shows another address) the Reply-To signal becomes `danger` and says the email asks for replies at X, which is not the brand, and not to reply or share account details (`escalateReplyTo`); `replyToWarning(check)` in kernel drives a red Reply-To line of its own in the reader, a Cancel-default confirm before Reply / Reply all / Suggested, and the `reply_to_email` card + `senderWarning` result (stripped from the model schema); ordinary Reply-To elsewhere keeps the gentle line. **Addendum 2026-10-28**: nothing is suggested from a `danger` sender - the suggestion sweep, the read-time pass and person extraction ask `isDangerousSender` before any model call, stage nothing and withdraw pending cards from that email (a fake Robinhood email got a "call this number" to-do); the brand check reads the display name through `latinSkeleton`, so a Cyrillic о no longer hides the brand claim Chord's reply now goes to the Reply-To (`replyAddressOf` in kernel: composer, bot card and every provider's no-audience send; not when it is the user's own address; the vacation responder still answers the sender, RFC 3834); found with it: Outlook replies naming recipients dropped their attachments, now merged; a suspect message's plain-text notice offers **Show original with images** (its HTML with images) for that message only (never remembered for the sender, warnings stay) **Addendum 2026-10-29**: any invisible character in a display name is `danger` (`hidden_characters`, owner rule; only an emoji's own selector/joiner exempt) - a Hotmail phish wrote U+E0139 between every letter of "Coinbase" from a DMARC-passing domain; `latinSkeleton` drops marks and default-ignorables, and a distinctive brand in pieces or glued to a service word counts ("Coin base", "Coinbase.com", "CoinbaseSupport", "C0inbase"); the ADR 0268 strip also drops variation selectors. A thread whose every message is in Spam/Junk (or Trash) withdraws its suggestions and archives its open to-dos as `email_spam`, even ones dated ahead (amends 0073/0143/0239); Outlook's probe reports `junk`, and a stored id that 404s is looked up by Message-ID before it counts as a delete (Graph re-issues ids on a move, so archives read as deletes too) |
| [0295](./docs/notes/0295-guests-live-on-the-calendar.md) | **Guests live on the calendar** (amends 0157, keeps 0181): Chord kept its own guest list (`event_attendees`, keyed on `ical_uid`) and re-sent it on every push - the first push replaced `ical_uid` so a second guest was sent alone and Google (which replaces `attendees` on a patch) un-invited the first, a time change told nobody, a guest removed in Google came back, a guest added to a Google-made event was never sent while the bot said "invited", and nothing could remove a guest. Now Chord keeps NO guest list: `changeEventGuests` (`services/events/guests.service.ts`) reads the provider event, changes its `attendees` (everyone listed sent back unchanged, minus removed, plus added - `nextAttendees`) and patches with notifications on; only the organizer (own copy, and the provider agrees) may, else `not_organizer`; not on a connected calendar = `not_on_calendar`. The push never sends `attendees` and notifies on every patch except a member's reflection. Bot: `update_event.removeInvitees`; replies say invited/removed only when the provider took it, else "NOBODY was invited" + reason. Outlook maps Graph `attendees`/`isOrganizer`. `share_event` reads guests from the provider. `event_attendees` is no longer written for guests (old rows stay, unread) |
| [0296](./docs/notes/0296-a-card-on-whatsapp-is-answered-with-a-button-or-a-word.md) | **A card on WhatsApp is answered with a button or a word**: chat proposal cards ("tap to send/confirm") had no buttons, so WhatsApp got plain text with nothing to tap and a typed "yes" reached the model, which cannot confirm. Every staged proposal card now carries `p+`/`p-` buttons (`Send`/`Move to Trash`/`Unsubscribe`/`Confirm` + `Cancel`; the app ignores them); WhatsApp and Telegram renderers read buttons from any card kind, drop the "tap …" hint (`renderers/proposal-answer.ts`) and, on WhatsApp, show the card's text above the buttons. Over three buttons (WhatsApp's cap) the text ends "Reply YES to confirm, or NO to cancel." and `connectors/reply-answer.ts` makes that true: a bare YES/NO resolves the sender's own pending cards staged since their previous message on that app (max 30 min), one per bundle, via the button handler; nothing matching = normal bot turn. Upload announcement and ADR 0248 note reworded. English only |
| [0297](./docs/notes/0297-a-time-is-picked-on-wheels.md) | **A time is picked on wheels, in a time zone, and the end follows the start** (amends 0273): every typed time box (event start/end, Settings briefing time and quiet hours) is a `TimeField` (`mobile/src/time-field.tsx`) - hour / minute / AM-PM wheels, pure JS (OTA-safe, same on web), no keyboard; optional fields offer "None". The event editor gets a `TimeZoneField` (device zone default, searchable common list); times are clock times in that zone, converted by `instantOfWallTime`/`wallTimeIn` (`kernel/src/time-picker.ts`, Intl only), server unchanged. Moving the start moves the end by the same amount (`moveEndWithStart`, overnight measured as save reads it). An event opens in its own zone (`GroupEvent.timeZone` = `events.start_tz`, else a common zone matching the time's offset, `zoneForEvent`) and saving writes `start_tz`/`end_tz` (`UpdateEventRequest.timeZone`), so Google gets it; chat: `update_event { timeZone }`, its times put on that zone's clock by `anchorModelTimes`. **No text box is left behind the keyboard** (`mobile/src/keyboard.tsx`): put a screen's scroll in `KeyboardScrollView` (pads for the keyboard; any `@/text-input` box inside reveals itself on focus, `keyboardRevealDelta` in kernel) and a text-entry Modal's content in `KeyboardModalBody` - a new screen or popup with a text box uses these. Rejected: native datetimepicker, a looser parser, always the device zone, `react-native-keyboard-controller` (native) |
| [0298](./docs/notes/0298-a-blocked-person-is-hidden-from-you-everywhere.md) | **A blocked person is hidden from you, everywhere** (App Store 1.2; amends 0067): the `blocks` row is now a user-level block. The blocker no longer sees the blocked person's messages in any shared chat (`inViewOf` gains the rule, so list/search/delete-last-N follow; same SQL in the chat list preview/time/unread; the chat screen drops live SSE messages from its own block list), gets no pushes for them (`selectRecipientTokens`), and neither can start a direct chat via `message_user` (new `you_blocked`). The blocked person is told nothing; the bot still reads them; nobody leaves a chat; Unblock shows everything again. Only someone you share a chat with can be blocked (else 404, so ids cannot be probed). `GET/POST /me/blocks`, `DELETE /me/blocks/:userId`; chat: long-press or tap a name on someone else's message → action sheet (Copy / Block) → confirm; Settings → Blocked people with Unblock; personal-only `block_user`/`unblock_user`/`list_blocked_users` (exact-or-ask names). Reporting content (the other half of 1.2) NOT built. Rejected: hiding for everyone, telling the blocked person, server-side per-subscriber SSE filtering, leaving a pair chat on block |
| [0299](./docs/notes/0299-a-skill-is-a-set-of-questions-the-server-remembers.md) | **A skill is a set of questions the server remembers**: guided chat skills (`services/bot/skills/`, one code file each: `id`, `title`, one-line `when`, `instructions`, `steps`, `finish`). A step = `ask`, optional `choices`, `saveWith` (EXISTING tools that save the answer; the skill stores no answers), optional `known(ctx)` code check that the answer is already on record, so it is never asked. Progress in `skill_runs` (migration `124`, one row per user+skill, step ids only, cascades with user/chat; a table because `bot_pending_state` expires). Personal-only tools `start_skill { skill, restart? }` (starts/resumes, returns instructions + first question, saves nothing) and `skill_step { action: answered\|skip\|pause\|stop, stepId? }` (after the answer is saved; a stepId that is not the open question is refused `wrong_step`). Next question computed in code (first not answered/skipped whose `known()` is false). Prompt: one line per skill with the user's progress; while one runs, its instructions + open question repeat every turn, last in the prompt (ADR 0166's pattern). One skill per chat (starting another pauses it). Questions with `choices` attach a `BotCard` kind `choices` (`replies`): app chips post the text as the user's reply, newest message only; bridged surfaces get `subtitle`. Skills: `onboarding` (family, birthdays, schools + offer to route school mail, home city/airport, briefing time, connect accounts, WhatsApp) and `plan_my_day` (read events/to-dos/free time, ask what else, lay out the day, add items only on a yes). **Addendum 2026-10-25**: the household question saves through `add_family_member` (roster + People), and everyone on the roster WITHOUT an account is a managed persona in the family chat (ADR 0151/0234; `placeRosterProfilesInFamilyGroup`, role read from the relationship, linked by `personas.linked_person_id`), handed to their account when they accept (no second persona; a returning leaver keeps their own and the stand-in goes), removed with the roster entry; no family chat = roster + People only. New personal-only `verify_phone { phone?, code? }` (same service as Settings → Phone; number kept in `bot_pending_state` kind `phone` 15 min; bare 10-digit = +1 per ADR 0237) and an onboarding phone question, skipped once verified or with no SMS provider. **Onboarding starts on first use, no button** (`skills/auto-start.ts` `maybeStartOnboarding`): on AI-consent grant (app or AGREE in chat) the opening question is posted with chips; where consent is off, the first personal-chat message starts it and that turn asks. First use = not a shadow, never started, at most one own message in the personal chat; the bot no longer offers skills itself. **Slash commands** (`Skill.command`): `/onboard`, `/plan`, read in code before any model; resumes paused/running, restarts done/stopped; `opensWithQuestion` skills post the question as the turn's reply (same message id), others start and let the turn run. Not built: mail recipes as a skill, user-written skills, skills in shared groups. **Never rename a shipped step id**. **Addendum 2026-10-25 (guided run)**: the SERVER moves a skill on - while one runs, the user's next message is the answer to the open question (`handleSkillReply`, `skills/reply.ts`, recorded before any model), and the model is told what was answered and what to ask next or how to finish (`skillTurnBlock`); a bare skip/later/stop is handled in code; `skill_step` is only `reopen\|pause\|stop` (reopen = that message was not an answer); a skill turn is always a bubble, with the open question's chips attached by the server. In prod the model asked follow-ups after "lunch and squash" instead of calling `skill_step`, and `/plan` never planned. **Addendum (plan starts now)**: a plan for today starts at the time asked (past events/free time left out; little day left = offer tomorrow); today's availability line reads "rest of today, from 7:40 PM" and a time asked about earlier today is `ALREADY PAST`, never `FREE` (shared with the reply composer) |
| [0300](./docs/notes/0300-a-skill-can-be-added-without-a-deploy.md) | **A skill can be added without a deploy** (extends 0299): a skill can be a JSON row in `skill_definitions` (migration `125`, each save also in `skill_definition_versions` for rollback), same fields as a code skill; built-ins stay in code and their ids/commands cannot be taken. Checked on save (`checkSkillDefinition`: shape/lengths, id or `/command` taken, `saveWith` naming a tool that does not exist, `known` naming a check that does not exist, duplicate step ids) and again on load (a failing row is skipped and logged, never hides the rest). `known` is a fixed menu in `skills/checks.ts` (`KNOWN_CHECKS` + `has_fact:<key>`), shared with onboarding. The web role re-reads the table every 30 s (`startSkillRegistry`); every lookup reads `allSkills()`; `start_skill.skill` is a plain string checked at call time. A run on a question that no longer exists moves to the next one. Written only by the owner with `npm run skill --workspace=server -- check|put|list|show|disable|enable|rollback` (on the VM via `chord-with-env`); format in `docs/ops/skills.md`. `tools` / `checks` list what a skill may name (and which stored skills use each tool). **Every deploy runs `skill verify --fix` with the new code, after migrations and before the restart**: a renamed tool/check (`TOOL_RENAMES`/`CHECK_RENAMES` in `definition.ts`, added in the same change as the rename) is saved under its new name; an enabled skill naming a REMOVED one stops the deploy, naming the skill and tool, old services left running (`SKIP_SKILL_VERIFY=1` overrides). | [0301](./docs/notes/0301-a-report-reaches-a-person.md) | **A report reaches a person** (App Store 1.2, with ADR 0298): a message (or a file posted as one) another member posted in a SHARED chat can be reported; not the personal chat, bot replies, your own messages, or email. `content_reports` (migration `130`, one per reporter per message) keeps the text (2,000 chars), file name, sender, chat and reason; `reportMessage` (`services/reports.service.ts`) answers `not_found` for anything the reporter cannot see. The row is saved, then emailed in the background to `REPORTS_EMAIL_TO` (default hello@chordapp.ai) over `REPORTS_SMTP_URL` (nodemailer); a failed send is retried by the proactive tick (8 tries, 10 min apart, 3 days), and with no SMTP URL it is saved and logged only. `POST /me/reports`; chat sheet **Report message** -> a reason list -> offer to Block; personal-only `report_message { groupName, person, reason? }` reports that person's newest message (exact-or-ask). The person is not told and nothing is hidden. Acting on reports is by hand from the email; filtering not built. Env keys not yet in `example.env` (it runs the whole CI suite) |
| [0303](./docs/notes/0303-a-draft-is-also-in-the-mailboxs-drafts.md) | **A draft is also in the mailbox's own Drafts folder** (extends 0113): Chord's `email_drafts` row stays the source; the composer saves it after 1.5s quiet OR 30s after the oldest unsaved change (`draftAutosaveDelay`, kernel - a pure debounce never saved a non-stop typist), and a `worker`-leader pass copies changed rows every `EMAIL_PROVIDER_DRAFT_SECONDS` (30) to Gmail `drafts.create/update` (threaded, reply headers), Outlook `POST /me/messages` or `createReply` + `PATCH`, IMAP `APPEND` to `\Drafts` + delete the old UID - updated in place, text and recipients only (files stay on the Chord draft). Backoff 1 min doubling to 6h, reset by a local save; no Drafts folder = `provider_error`, not retried. One direction only. Gmail `DRAFT` messages are skipped by sync (primary + attached) so the copy never shows as a message in its thread; a draft sent from Gmail is fetched when it loses `DRAFT`. Migration `131`. Not run against live providers; IMAP needs UIDPLUS. **Addendum 2026-11-10**: a deletion is the one thing read back - a copy deleted, sent or moved out of Drafts in another mail app deletes the Chord draft too (nothing queued), never re-created; found when the next copy finds it gone (`writeProviderDraft` GONE, IMAP `saveDraft` → `{ gone: true }`) and by a check of untouched drafts every 5 min (`provider_checked_at`, migration `144`, per-mailbox `missingDrafts(refs)`); a failed check is not a deletion; only the version looked at is deleted. Same rule for labels and folders: filing rules (0309), Chord label push (0142) and the category label sync make one only the FIRST time (`users.defaults` `mailbox_folders_seen`, `email/mailbox-folder-memory.ts`, per mailbox + name); once seen, missing = the user deleted it and Chord stops using it there; a new filing rule, switching push/sync back on, or an explicit apply re-arms it; Outlook categories / IMAP keywords unchanged (no deletion to see). | [0302](./docs/notes/0302-two-weeks-of-mail-live-on-the-phone.md) | **Two weeks of mail live on the phone** (amends 0218/0269): native only. Every inbox thread whose newest message is within 14 days is kept as a file (list row + messages' text/HTML; NOT images or attachments; image token stripped) under the app's CACHE directory (not documents: iOS backs that up to iCloud), one folder per user + `index.json` + `list.json` (AsyncStorage rejected: Android's 6 MB cap). Rules pure in `kernel/src/email-device-cache.ts`, file I/O in `mobile/src/email-device-store.ts`. A pass runs after each Email-tab sync / `email.synced` / focus (one at a time, at most 1/min): reads the inbox back to the 14-day line (<= 6 pages), saves the list, fetches new or changed threads newest first, 3 at a time, <= 40 per pass, skips a thread over 1 MB, keeps <= 100 MB / 1,000 threads (oldest go). Background fetches use **`?prefetch=cache`** (no read-time check, unlike `?prefetch=1`). Reader paints the stored copy, then the real open runs (check + mark-read) and replaces it; offline it says so. Leaves the store: past 14 days, `email.thread.updated` archived/trashed, a confirmed removal from this device, a 404 open (ADR 0224), and any stored thread a fresh list shows is no longer in the inbox (`goneFromInbox`). **Always encrypted, no setting** (owner): AES-256-GCM (`@noble/ciphers`) under a key in Keychain/Keystore (`expo-secure-store`, this-device-only, after first unlock, no passcode/Face ID prompt), every file incl. index/list; an unsealed or unopenable file is treated as missing. `expo-secure-store` is lazy-loaded: a build without it keeps NO mail (behaves as before, short AsyncStorage list); with it, the plain AsyncStorage list is deleted. Store needs the next native build. Sign-out deletes the folder and the key (`onSignOut` resets may now be async and are awaited); opening as another user deletes other users' folders. Web keeps today's behaviour. No bot tool (per-device storage, ADR 0282) |
| [0305](./docs/notes/0305-a-pdf-opens-inside-chord-on-the-web-too.md) | **A PDF opens inside Chord on the web too** (extends the phone viewer, keeps ADR 0269): web used to `window.open` a `blob:` of the authed fetch, and a blob carries only its TYPE (no `Content-Disposition`), which for email is the sender's label - often `octet-stream`, so Chrome downloaded it; Firefox opened a new tab. Now `showPdfOnWeb` (`mobile/src/api.ts`) checks the bytes start like a PDF (`looksLikePdf`), re-wraps them as `application/pdf`, and pushes `/file-viewer`, whose web branch shows the browser's own PDF viewer in an `<iframe>` with X and Download, revoking the blob on close (a reload says to tap the file again). Which files go there: kernel `opensInPdfViewer(mime, name)` (PDF type, or a generic type with a `.pdf` name; a named other type never), decided before the fetch so other files keep the popup-safe new tab; a file that only claims to be a PDF is downloaded. No server change, no token in a URL. Not covered: Chrome's "Download PDFs" setting; Mobile Safari draws only page 1 in an iframe. Rejected: retyping on the server by name, pdf.js on web, a sandboxed iframe (Chrome won't draw a PDF in one) |
| [0306](./docs/notes/0306-reply-all-to-an-event.md) | **Reply all to a calendar event opens the composer**: the event sheet (tap, or double-click on web) shows `✉ Reply all (N)` when anyone else is on it and opens `/email/compose?to=&subject=` (composer reads these only for a new message, never over a resumed draft). Recipients = `eventReplyAllRecipients` (kernel): organizer first, then guests, lowercased, once, never the writer; `GET /events/:id/guests` now returns `organizer` (null = the calendar owner; Google `organizer.email`, Outlook `organizer.emailAddress` now mapped). Chat: read-only, personal-only `email_event_guests` returns `recipients` + an `email` card linking the same route; dictated text goes through `compose_email` with those recipients. No new send path |
| [0304](./docs/notes/0304-every-unread-email-is-fetched-before-it-is-opened.md) | **Every unread email is fetched before it is opened, on the web too** (amends 0302/0218): the ADR 0302 store keeps every unread inbox thread **at any age** (newest 300 unread; read mail still 14 days) and a pass reads the unread-only list too, fetching **unread first** then newest, 3 at a time, 40 per pass, re-running itself 5 s later until done, always `?prefetch=cache` (no model call). Under size/count pressure read mail goes before unread. Stops while the app/tab is hidden or offline (3 failures in a row), resumes on sync, focus, or web visible/online. **Suspected spam is never kept** (owner): a `SPAM` row is never fetched, a fetched thread with sender check `danger` (0294) or a read-time caution (0180) is not stored and not re-asked until a new message (`suspectedForDevice`). An old unread email leaves when read elsewhere (`unreadReconcile` against the unread list, or `email.thread.updated`); a removal after a fetch started wins (0286). **Web**: the same store with a memory backend (`Map`, 40 MB / 400 threads), gone on reload; **not IndexedDB** - browsers have no key store, so it would be readable mail on disk on shared computers (0269). Reader paints the stored copy then runs the real open, as on phones. Rules in `kernel/src/email-device-cache.ts`. Phones still need the `expo-secure-store` build. No bot tool (per-device storage) |

After each sync `email/unsubscribe-offers.ts` stages one ordinary `unsubscribe_from_sender` card for an ignored one-click/mailto list, beside its newest email, once per sender ever (`users.defaults` `unsubscribe_offered`), at most one a day, no AI call; stored `why` hidden from the model. Several lists, one tap: proposable `unsubscribe_from_senders` (one card naming every list) and the screen's "Unsubscribe from all" both call `unsubscribeFromSenders` (`POST /me/email/subscriptions/unsubscribe`), one list at a time with a result each, deliberately NOT an all-or-nothing proposal bundle (a sent request can't roll back); stages nothing if any named list can't go on the card and says which. | [0308](./docs/notes/0308-a-list-chord-cannot-leave-is-the-users-to-finish.md) | **A list Chord cannot leave is the user's to finish, or its mail goes to Trash** (extends 0307/0122): an unsubscribe result where nothing was done (refused one-click, web-page-only, already asked) carries `needsYou`; a failed one-click returns the email's own unsubscribe link as `url`. Chat: proposable `trash_future_mail { senders }` (a list the user is on, or an exact address they have mail from; else stages nothing) and immediate `stop_trashing_mail`; screen: "Send future mail to Trash" (confirm) / "Stop", via `setAutoTrash` (`POST /me/email/subscriptions/auto-trash`); `autoTrash` on list rows and results. | [0309](./docs/notes/0309-a-filing-rule-is-chords-and-the-folder-is-the-mailboxs.md) | **A filing rule is Chord's; the folder it files into is the mailbox's** (owner exception to 0107/0123/0149, like 0215/0308): "send emails from X (person) or company.com to folder Z". `users.defaults` `email_filing_rules` (≤100: `{id, kind: sender\|domain, value, folder, since}`); `email/filing.service.ts` `applyFilingRules` runs after each sync (after auto-trash, above the AI/billing gates, ≤25 threads a pass): inbox mail matching a rule and newer than `since` leaves the inbox into Z on the user's own mailbox, made if missing - Gmail (primary + attached) a label via `findOrCreateGmailLabel` + INBOX removed (nesting `A/B` allowed), Outlook a top-level folder (`ensureFolder`/`moveToFolder`, `fileOutlookThread`), IMAP a mailbox (`moveToFolder`, `fileImapThread`); `/` refused on Outlook/IMAP. Sender beats domain (domain includes subdomains), then oldest; a Trash-ruled sender is not filed; Inbox/Trash/Spam/Sent/Drafts/Archive refused. Only new mail unless `alsoExisting` (since = epoch). Chat: proposable `add_mail_filter { from, folder, alsoExisting? }` (address/domain as typed, a name via `resolveCorrespondent` - ambiguous = ask), read-only `list_mail_filters`, immediate `remove_mail_filter { from?, folder? }`; `GET/POST /me/email/filters`, `DELETE /me/email/filters/:id`; Filters screen (folder sheet → Filters).
