Android still hands off PowerPoint/.doc/.rtf/iWork/media and web downloads them. **The server reads Office text** (`services/office-text.ts`, adm-zip + htmlparser2): docx (+ footnotes), xlsx (all sheets, date-formatted cells as dates), pptx, odt/ods/odp, rtf; archive caps 5,000 entries / 30 MB a part / 60 MB in all, from the headers; the text takes the PDF path (sealed, indexed, summarised, auto-filer -> action cards). Not read: binary .doc/.xls/.ppt, iWork. No bot tool (read-only). Rejected: Google/Office online viewers (send the file out), Quick Look now (native build), LibreOffice conversion now, bundling the libraries, rendering HTML attachments |
| [0311](./docs/notes/0311-offline-shows-what-was-saved.md) | **Offline, the app shows what it saved and stops asking**: airplane mode at launch put a signed-in user on the sign-in page (`/health` + `/me` failed, launch state `offline`, `bootScreen` → landing, nothing saved on the phone, check never re-ran), and inside the app every stream re-polled forever and every focus/foreground/pull fired a failing request. One answer to "can we reach the server?" in `mobile/src/network.ts`, decided by what requests do (no NetInfo: native-only, and "has Wi-Fi" is not "server answers"): every server call goes through `apiFetch` (`api.ts`) - offline it throws `OfflineError` without touching the network; a request that never reached the server (`isNetworkFailure`, kernel) asks for ONE `/health` check and only a failed check goes offline; any answer incl. error statuses = reachable; offline and in front, a check after 5s doubling to 60s (`offlineProbeDelay`), immediately on foreground/visible/`online`. Offline: streams park like background on every platform incl. web (stream errors ask for a check, max every 15s); `useForegroundRefresh` skips and reloads the focused screen on reconnect, a pull runs the check; the ADR 0304 device mail pass waits; screen loads failing with `OfflineError` show no error (`loadErrorText`: Email, Calendar, To Do's, chat list, chat), writes say "You're offline" (nothing queued); a strip above every screen (`OfflineFrame`, `offline-bar.tsx`) - not a floating pill (covered the calendar date), takes the status-bar inset and gives screens top 0, same tree shape either way or the navigator remounts. **Saved user**: every server-confirmed user saved to `chord.savedUser.v1` (removed by sign-out and a rejected session); `getMeOutcome` splits 401/403 `rejected` from `unreachable` (`meOutcomeForStatus`); `enterWithSavedUser` (kernel) opens the app offline when the token is on the device and a saved user exists, `/me` re-asked on reconnect (fresh replaces, rejected → sign-in); an offline landing re-runs its check on reconnect. Phase 2 not built: saved chat list/messages/To Do's in the encrypted device store; NetInfo later via `importIfNative` **Addendum 2026-10-30 (chat queue)**: a chat message sent offline waits in ONE send queue shared with email (`mobile/src/send-queue.ts`, kernel `send-queue.ts`): kinds register a sender, oldest first on reconnect, a new send queues behind a waiting one of its kind (FIFO, bot reads in order), 4xx = failed with reason + Retry/Delete, offline/5xx stops and keeps order; stored SEALED ONLY in the ADR 0302 device folder (`readDeviceJson`/`writeDeviceJson`), memory-only on builds without the key store; shown dimmed "Waiting to send" in the chat; files not queued; a lost response can send twice (no client id yet; later fixed by the offline-edits `Idempotency-Key`). **Email outbox addendum**: offline Send in every email composer queues kind `email` with the whole send (`EmailOutboxPayload`, `mobile/src/email-outbox.ts`) and a new Send joins the line while an earlier email waits; sent later with the same direct call the composer makes (`sendEmail`/`replyEmailThread`/`forwardEmailThread` + draft id), NOT the ADR 0115 server queue (no composer to undo from; a reply must keep the message it answered); a payload missing what it needs is refused like a 4xx. Autosave keeps a draft whose save stops (offline/5xx) in sealed `email-phone-drafts`, restores it first, uploads on reconnect (open composer, or `uploadPhoneDrafts`); Save draft offline says "Saved on this phone". Outbox screen `/email/outbox` (folder sheet "Outbox (N)"). Tested rules in kernel `email-outbox.ts` (`outboxSendPath`, `outboxItemProblem`) **Addendum 2026-10-31 (saved copies)**: `readWithSavedCopy` (kernel `saved-read.ts`) keeps a sealed copy of `getEmailStatus`, `listEmailTiles`, `listEmailDecisions` and `listAllProposals` and answers from it ONLY on `OfflineError` (never on a refusal), so offline the AI view, Action Items and the compose button (drawn on `hasAnyMailbox(status)`) have data; stored as `read-<name>` via `readDeviceJson`/`writeDeviceJson` (the store registers with `setSavedCopyStore`); the offline strip reports its height (`useOfflineStripHeight`) and iOS `KeyboardScreen` adds it (`keyboardOffset`) - it had put the chat box, composer and reader ~70pt under the keyboard **Addendum 2026-10-31 (To Do's)**: the other To Do's tabs and the chat list are saved the same way - tasks, lists, saves, facts, trips and files through `withSavedCopy` in the screen's `LOADERS` (NOT in `listAllTasks`/`listAllLists` themselves: the Reminders sync reads those and must never act on a saved copy), and `listGroups` (names the group chips and the chat list) and `getEmailReceipts` (Findings) in `api.ts`; before, only Action Items' suggestions were kept, so on a cold start offline every other tab showed a spinner **Addendum 2026-10-31 (offline edits)**: to-do and list-item changes (add, tick, drop, delete, edit title/notes/due/assignee; item add/tick/edit/delete) go through the send queue as kind `todo` to `POST /me/todo-sync` (one `TodoOp`, kernel `todo-sync.ts`), online too; each carries the values it expected and the server (`todo-sync.service.ts`, row locked, same task/list service functions) writes a field only if it still holds that value or already holds the new one; a field someone else changed returns as a conflict (`partial`/`conflict`), a deleted target as `gone`, and the To Do's screen lists them under **Couldn't sync** with Keep mine (resends only the lost fields with `force`) / Keep theirs; deleting something changed since asks; a tick never conflicts; no version column (tasks/items have no `updated_at`). Waiting changes are laid over the saved copy (`applyPendingTodoOps`); something made offline has a `tmp_` id swapped for the real one before later changes send. List create/rename/delete/move-to-Saves are still online-only. **Every queued send carries an `Idempotency-Key`** (the queue's id, made before the first attempt; composers reuse it for their Outbox fallback): `client_requests` (migration `136`, per user, kept 7 days) returns the first answer to a repeat, 503 while the first is running, forgets a 5xx; on to-do sync, chat post, email send/reply/forward. Tested with writers at the same moment (`docs/ops/concurrency-tests.md`): `addItem` now takes ADR 0165's advisory lock on list + normalised text, since two adds of one item at once made two items **Addendum 2026-11-01**: every To Do's copy (tasks, lists, saves, facts, trips, files, suggestions, chat list) is refreshed at sign-in, reconnect and foreground, at most every 5 min (`mobile/src/todo-copies.ts`, `shouldWarmSavedCopies`), not only when its tab opens - a tab never opened showed nothing offline; ✓/✕ on a suggestion goes through the send queue as kind `proposal` (`mobile/src/proposal-answers.ts`, confirm/reject routes take the `Idempotency-Key`), shown answered until sent (`applyPendingProposalAnswers`, also over email decisions), a refusal listed under "Couldn't send" with Retry/Discard; used in To Do's, email cards, urgent strip, batch card, calendar; invitation Yes/Maybe/No still online-only (ADR 0104) **Addendum 2026-11-13**: every launch with a session and a saved user opens from the saved user at once (`openFromSavedUserAtLaunch`), `/me` checks in the background (rejected = sign-in page); the consent check is skipped when this device saw the same user agree (`aiConsentKnownAtLaunch`, `chord.aiConsentAgreed.v1`) **Addendum 2026-11-15**: screens paint their saved copy first and the server's answer replaces it (kernel `showSavedThenFresh`, `readSavedCopy` in `api.ts`): To Do's tabs, chat list + group chips (store filled at sign-in), each chat's newest 50 messages (`messages-<id>`), People (`people`), Email AI view (status, decisions, tiles); phones with the key-store build only, web reload still waits |
| [0312](./docs/notes/0312-mail-stays-with-its-mailbox.md) | **Mail stays with its mailbox** (amends 0080/0104/0228/0232): an email event used the user's default calendar and an invitation was answered by whichever Google/Outlook calendar had it, with the iTIP fallback always from the primary Gmail - so a work-Outlook meeting landed on the Family calendar and a work invite was answered from the personal address. `email/source-mailbox.ts` reads the mailbox off the `gmail:` provenance (thread row's `account_id`, else the `scopedId` prefix, else primary) and names its calendar: primary Gmail = the user's default when it is a Google calendar, else Google `primary`; Outlook with its calendar (0228) = that mailbox's own calendar, whatever the user default (owner rule); IMAP, secondary Gmail, Outlook without calendar = **the card asks** (`Proposal.calendarChoice` `auto`|`ask`, options = writable calendars); no writable calendar anywhere = saved in Chord as before. Confirm takes a checked `calendarId` (`isWritableCalendar`); an ask with none is refused `calendar_required` (422, proposal stays pending), never guessed; chat requests unchanged. `respondToInvite` takes `provenance` and answers ONLY through that mailbox: primary = Google patch then iTIP from the primary; Outlook = that mailbox's calendar answer then iTIP from it (`OutlookApi.sendMime`); secondary Gmail / IMAP = iTIP from that address (`ImapApi.sendRaw`); never another account. **Disconnecting a mailbox removes what its mail made** (`email/mailbox-purge.ts`, run by `disconnectGmail`/`disconnectAccount` before the threads go; amends 0249): its legs + their flight events, stays, reservations, trips left empty, people it introduced (new `people.provenance`, migration `133`), relationships, facts (a typed fact they replaced comes back), receipts - matched by `gmail:` provenance (`gmail:<accountId>:…` attached, bare id = primary; attachment-filed trips via `origin_email_thread_id`); pending cards, to-dos, older untagged people/facts and imported attachments stay; a sign-in fold's mailbox move keeps all (`keepDerived`). A flight from email goes on its mailbox's calendar (`calendarForBooking`), the user default only when that mailbox has none. Not built: secondary-Gmail calendars (own session); meeting context (0163) still reads every mailbox |

Also: a pull leaves a row or series on ANOTHER account's calendar alone (`onOtherAccount`), else the login pull of a shared second-account calendar took the event over by `chord_uid` and wrote it with the login token **Addendum 2026-11-09**: `createEvent`/`updateEvent`'s own push (`firePush`) and the series push did the same on a transaction (confirmed email event card, trip bundle, `addTravelLeg` flight event): claimed in the transaction, Google id written after commit and refused, so the event was never sent; now one mechanism in `db/after-commit.ts` - `runAfterCommit` (now at once, or to the transaction's bound sink), `transactionThen` (a transaction that starts collected work after commit), `bindAfterCommit` (confirmProposal's buffer) - and `startAfterWrite` uses it; a transaction with no sink leaves the event dirty for the next sync. **A new transaction that can write an event uses `transactionThen`** |

| [0316](./docs/notes/0316-the-web-app-never-holds-its-own-session.md) | **The web app never holds its own session, and email is drawn in a sealed frame** (amends 0269/0136/0294): on web the session is ONLY the httpOnly `chord.sid` cookie - no stored token, no `x-chord-token`, no `?token=` on the two streams (same origin); "signed in" is decided by asking `/me` (kernel `deviceMayHaveSession`, `web` flag on `bootScreen`/`enterWithSavedUser`), offline saved user unchanged; `POST /api/v1/session/adopt` moves a stored token to the cookie, then deletes it (a failure keeps it, nobody signed out). **A write authenticated only by the cookie must carry `x-chord-csrf: 1`** (`CSRF_HEADER` in kernel) or runs signed out (403 on `requireSession`); header/bearer clients (phones) unchanged. | [0318](./docs/notes/0318-a-chat-invite-is-an-email-address.md) | **A chat invite is an email address** (amends 0047/0101): "Message someone" (Messages screen) and Invite (group settings) take an email + optional first message; `POST /api/v1/chat-invites` (`inviteByEmail`, `services/chat-invites.service.ts`): an account matched on proof (ADR 0237) that is CONNECTED (an accepted `chat_invites` row either way) is added now (`ensurePairGroup` or the group); on Chord but not connected = an invite to accept/decline (top of their Messages list, `GET /me/chat-invites`, `POST …/:id/accept\|decline`, note in their 1:1), nothing changes until they answer (owner: accept first); not on Chord = email from the inviter's own mailbox with the chat's `/join/<code>` link (direct invite makes its chat up front, `Chat with <email>`, message posted in it), following the link accepts (`noteJoinedViaLink` on the join route; direct chat becomes the pair chat), signing up with the address without the link leaves it waiting in the app (`claimChatInvitesFor` at sign-in); `no_mailbox`/`failed` return the link to copy, never "sent". Code stays as the mechanism but is never typed; link/code kept in group settings. Blocked inviter (0298) gets the same `asked` answer and nothing is sent. Migration `139`. | [0319](./docs/notes/0319-a-video-call-follows-the-calendars-provider.md) | **A video call follows the calendar's provider** (amends 0317): one kernel rule, `videoCallDefaultFor` (`kernel/src/video-call.ts`) - a Google-driven calendar (the login account's own, an attached Gmail's `gacct:`) gets a Google Meet, an Outlook / 365 `outlook:` calendar gets none (Chord cannot make a Teams meeting), a phone-mirrored calendar and a mailbox with no calendar of its own get none, and any other prefixed key gets none rather than inheriting Meet. A `videoCall: true` nobody asked for IS the default (models fill every optional field, ADR 0229) and is followed only where the calendar has one, writing nothing and saying nothing elsewhere; an explicit ask (`looksLikeVideoCallRequest`, generous on purpose) is honoured or refused in plain words naming Teams (ADR 0102). The "was it asked" window is now one reader, `askedInConversation`, shared with `repeatWasAsked`. `eventVideoCallOption` is the single lookup (a Google-shaped key also needs `hasCalendarWriteScope`, since an IMAP mailbox carries `primary` with its events in Chord alone), read by the executor and by the guests route as `EventGuestsResponse.videoCall`, so the editor draws "Add Google Meet" only where it works. Nothing stored. Rejected: shape alone (a phone id looks Google), Meet for an unknown prefix, a per-user setting, a `calendarProvider` on every `GroupEvent`, building Teams (its own ADR - checked: Graph needs only `isOnlineMeeting` + `onlineMeetingProvider` on the event and no new scope, but it changes the rule's type and a personal Outlook account has no Teams). **Addendum 2026-11-05 - `confirmPast` is the user's answer, not the model's**: the same bug class, and the one flag that switches ADR 0100's past-date refusal off, so a filled-in field filed a stale-year event silently. Every refusal now records the local day it refused (`bot/pending-past-dates.ts`, `bot_pending_state` kind `past_date`, 1h) and `confirmPast` is believed only against one of those, from a DIFFERENT `turnId` (the ADR 0005 loop re-streams up to 20 times, so a same-turn retry is the model answering its own question) and for the SAME day (the user confirms a date, not a flag). Scoped to the chat; shared by both tools (one question about a date, not about which tool writes it); kept after a successful write (a confirmed date re-sent after an unrelated refusal must not be asked about twice). NOT stripped from the model's schema - passing it is how the conversation ends. ADR 0100's exemptions (no `turnId`, any `externalUid`) untouched. One existing test FLIPPED: ADR 0155's file path records nothing and so never honours `confirmPast`, since it tells the model not to ask - thirty refusals in one turn would otherwise license thirty history rows. Residue: the licence cannot read the answer, so an off-topic reply on the confirming turn still counts. **Addendum 2026-11-05 (2)**: the sweep's last two fields, each needing a DIFFERENT rule (same class, several right answers). `create_event.allDay` is honoured only when it AGREES with the time - `true` beside a 3pm start filed the meeting as an all-day non-blocking row, so on a turn it is believed only at midnight in the value's own offset and anything else falls back to `createEvent`'s sniff; ingest/.ics/proposal confirm (no `turnId`) are taken as given. NOT stripped from the schema, which was the first attempt and wrong: `applyToolCall` runs `normalizeDatetimes`, which stamps the user's offset onto the floating midnight the prompt asks for, so the sniff can no longer see it and the FIELD is the model's only way to say "all day" (a probe found this; a test pins the offset-qualified start so the reason cannot be refactored away). `add_mail_filter.alsoExisting` is gated on the user's own words (`looksLikeExistingMailRequest`, `email/filing.service.ts`, through the shared `askedInConversation`), falling back to ADR 0309's new-mail-only default; decided at staging so the card states what happens. Both claims in the first addendum were wrong and are corrected there: `allDay` is not ingest-only, and both cards DO say "new and existing mail" (which makes `alsoExisting` the weakest of the three - the tap rides a main sentence that is right while the quiet half moves the inbox) |
| [0320](./docs/notes/0320-the-list-stays-while-you-reply.md) | **The list stays while you read and reply, and a connect that added nothing says so** (amends 0222): on a window >= 900 the mail list keeps a draggable half beside the email - on `/email` ITSELF in Traditional mode (owner's call: the reading pane IS the screen, a row tap selects instead of navigating, nothing selected at first since opening marks mail read) and beside the reader opened by link, composer included since that is a layout of the same screen; `SplitFrame`/`EmailSplit` (`mobile/src/email-split-pane.tsx`), the reader taking `embedded` to draw its own `PaneHeader` (two `Stack.Screen` option sets in one route fight) with next/close as the parent's selection. Widths in `kernel/src/email-split.ts` (default half, per-device `email.listPaneWidth`, `READER_MIN` 420 is what never gives way when a window shrinks). The pane draws the Traditional list's OWN rows, now published to the nav store beside the order and `loadMore` it already published (ADR 0119), filtered by that store's `ids` so removals follow one rule (0218/0286) - no fetch, except a walk published with no rows (an AI-view category walk), where it loads one inbox page and publishes it as the walk; a row tapped there is `router.replace`. **Reply all lives on the Cc line** of the open composer (owner: "tap reply all and cc becomes reply all"), so that line shows - empty - whenever reply-all would widen the audience (`replyAllAdds`, kernel, derived from `replyRecipients` so it cannot disagree with Send); Bcc stays behind the `Cc/Bcc` link. It ADDS exactly the people a plain reply misses and removes exactly those again (`applyReplyAll`), never re-deriving the fields the way the menu's `pickMode` does - mid-reply that would silently drop a recipient the user typed or restore one they deleted ("it should be allowed even after pressing reply, so progress is not lost"); To is untouched, since everything reply-all adds is a Cc address. **Outlook "+ add" re-attached the mailbox already there**: the connect asked Microsoft for `prompt=consent`, which never shows the account picker - now `select_account` (`MS_ADD_ACCOUNT_PROMPT`), the value the secondary-Gmail connect got in 0080 and Sign in with Microsoft already sends; and a connect that replaced an existing row reports `EmailAccountSummary.reattached` (connect responses only) so the ADD door says nothing new was added while the reconnect door stays silent. `insertAccount` also lowercases and matches case-insensitively - no reachable caller today, but a mixed-case address would attach one mailbox twice |

| [0322](./docs/notes/0322-a-chat-turn-is-sent-the-tools-it-needs.md) | **A chat turn is sent the tools it needs** (amends 0005): measured in prod, one personal round trip ~43k prompt tokens, the median turn (two) ~91k, the 6-tool Findings chat ~8k, so ~35k of every round trip was the 121-tool catalogue. `services/bot/tool-groups.ts`: 28 core tools always (event create/update/delete/find, tasks, lists, saves, search, search_web, suggest_external_action, facts, files, skills) + groups `calendar`/`trips`/`email`/`email_settings`/`people`/`chats`/`account`/`forms`, chosen by words in the last 4 user/bot messages (the addendum: user messages only), a running skill's saveWith tools and a pending share offer; `more_tools { groups }` (sent every turn, names every group and tool) loads more from the next round trip and is not counted as a tool attempt. **Not a guard**: a tool called by name without being sent still runs and its group is sent after. **A new tool must be placed in CORE_TOOLS or one group** (a test fails otherwise). Order core, more_tools, groups (cache prefix). Findings (`onlyTools`) and text-fence unchanged; `BOT_TOOL_SUBSET` (default true); `bot turn tools` log line per turn (groups, loaded later, tools sent, round trips, prompt tokens). Grocery turn = 37% of the catalogue. Fixed with it: the strict retry's tokens are now recorded (`recordRun`); other model calls that record nothing (briefing scan, memory extraction, attachment summaries/classifiers, query understanding, forms, meeting context, WhatsApp import) are not **Addendum 2026-11-10**: prod turns still started with 4-8 groups (~88k prompt tokens a turn): the words were read from bot replies and matched ordinary talk (`plan`, `week`, `number`, `message`, `phone`, `order`, `cancel`). Starting groups now come only from the triggering message plus the same user's previous message within 10 min (`turnToolWords`; bot replies, other members and the system prompt never read), and each group's words name the group (email/inbox/reply to, flight/trip/hotel, am I free/invite/rsvp, birthday/phone number/contacts, the chat/tell her, tokens/subscription/whatsapp/briefing); core-tool asks ("add dinner Friday", "what's on this week") start none. 25 sample turns: 1.44 → 0.40 groups, ~19.1k → 15.7k tool tokens a round trip. A missed group costs one `more_tools` round trip |
| [0323](./docs/notes/0323-a-teams-meeting-is-what-outlook-hosts.md) | **A Teams meeting is what Outlook hosts** (amends 0319): `videoCallDefaultFor` returns `'teams'` for an `outlook:` calendar (`VideoCallKind` widens to `'google_meet' | 'teams'`), so a 365 user asking for a video call gets the link Outlook's own Teams toggle would make. 0319's other half is unmoved: the kinds are NEVER crossed (no Meet on a 365 event, no Teams on a Google one) and a phone-mirrored calendar still gets neither. Re-verified against Graph v1.0 first, and two findings the plan lacked changed the design - `onlineMeeting` is READ-ONLY (Graph initializes it from the two writable fields `isOnlineMeeting` + `onlineMeetingProvider: "teamsForBusiness"`; the link is `onlineMeeting.joinUrl`, `onlineMeetingUrl` is deprecated), it can never be changed or turned off once set, and the calendar's own `allowedOnlineMeetingProviders` is Microsoft's documented test for whether Teams is possible at all. No new scope (ADR 0228's `Calendars.ReadWrite` covers it). **One transport verb both providers implement**, `addOnlineMeeting` - not a Google-shaped `conferenceData` body translated in `map.ts`, which cannot carry the calendar pre-check at all, and not an Outlook-only extension beside an inline Google patch. `GoogleEventResource.hangoutLink` -> provider-neutral **`videoCallLink`** (Google's transport from the wire field, `graphEventToResource` from `onlineMeeting.joinUrl`): the only reader asks "does it already have one", and on Outlook that answer must be right because Graph refuses a second write. **The write is VERIFIED, not trusted** - Graph is reported to answer 200 with `isOnlineMeeting: false` where the provider is not allowed (the ADR 0137 shape), so a response with no join link is re-read once then REFUSED, never passed on as pending; a Teams link arrives with the write, so "not returned yet" is reachable only for Google. A calendar that cannot be READ is not taken as a yes. Refusals: `not_google` -> **`no_video_call`** (its copy named Teams, now false; the name was already a near-lie for a phone calendar) and new **`teams_unavailable`** (personal account has no Teams for Business / tenant needs the licence), which tells the bot explicitly NOT to offer a Meet instead. An unasked default that FAILS now says nothing either (0229/0319's posture - an unlicensed tenant must not produce a complaint about a meeting nobody asked for). The editor's button is NAMED from `EventGuestsResponse.videoCall` and the route returns `kind`, so no surface derives the label twice. Consequence stated not hidden: `eventVideoCallOption` asks no provider (it runs on every guests read - the ADR 0204 cost), so the editor can offer "Add Teams meeting" on a personal Outlook account where the tap honestly refuses; caching `allowedOnlineMeetingProviders` on `calendar_sources` would close it (one `$select` on a call the sync already makes) but means a migration on an unverified path. **Not round-tripped against a live Microsoft tenant** (0118/0140/0228's posture); the failure paths are the tested ones. Four tests FLIPPED, not deleted (0108/0139). No migration, nothing stored, no platform-specific code. Rejected: leaving Outlook with none, a Meet on a 365 event, the `conferenceData` smuggle, keeping `hangoutLink` shared, asking Graph in `eventVideoCallOption`, trusting a 200, reporting a failed unasked default, a user-picked provider, and Skype (superseded by Teams, and every kind added is one more every surface must name) |
| [0324](./docs/notes/0324-every-model-call-for-a-user-is-counted.md) | **Every model call made for a user is counted** (amends 0193/0196/0257): `token_usage` is the allowance, and only chat turns, email passes and a few hand-recorded paths wrote rows - the briefing scan (the biggest), nudges, memory extraction, attachment text/vision/summary, upload content + trip classifiers (incl. email trip staging), `look_at_attachment`, form filling, meeting context and WhatsApp import were free to the user. `getLlmForUser(db, userId, source)` now REQUIRES a `token_usage.source`; on Chord's key it returns the model wrapped in `withUsageRecording` (`llm/usage-recording.ts`: hooks `onUsage` on every `complete`/`stream`, still calls the caller's, one row per call when it ends, nothing when the provider reports nothing, a failed insert only warns); on the user's own key nothing is recorded (ADR 0193); no user = unwrapped. New sources (migration `140`): `briefing`, `nudge`, `memory_extract`, `attachment_read`, `content_classify`, `trip_classify`, `form_fill`, `meeting_context`, `whatsapp_import`. Email passes moved onto the wrapper and `trackPassCost` only logs - it had written rows for BYOK users too. `resolveTurnLlm` paths (chat, reply composer, tldr, Findings, memory clean-up) still record themselves. `every-call-records.test.ts` lists every file calling `.complete(`/`.stream(` with how it is recorded and its exact call count, so **a new model call fails the build until it is listed**; `__setLlmForTests` swaps Chord's model for one that reports usage. Not backfilled |
| [0325](./docs/notes/0325-a-free-allowance-is-tokens-not-days.md) | **A free allowance is tokens, not days** (supersedes ADR 0079's trial; the gate stays): every account gets `FREE_TOKEN_ALLOWANCE` (default 10M) tokens ONCE, counted from sign-up with no time limit; `used` = `SUM(token_usage)` since `created_at` (trial-era and paid-plan usage included, BYOK records nothing), so accounts whose 30-day trial ended with < 10M used get AI back on deploy. Ranked where the trial was: a paid / family / grandfathered allowance with room wins and is reported, free is the fallback before `none` (a spent plan stays on the card). | [0326](./docs/notes/0326-a-signature-picture-is-kept-by-chord.md) | **A signature picture is kept by Chord, and a pasted signature keeps its pictures** (amends 0260): Outlook/Apple Mail/Word put a signature's logo inside the copied HTML (`data:`) or refer to a clipboard-only file (`file:`, `cid:`), which the cleaner dropped silently, and pasting a rendered signature into the HTML box brought only its text. `signature_images` (migration `141`, 24-char random id, dedup by SHA-256 per user, 100 per user, FK cascade, NOT sealed - it is mailed to strangers); `POST /me/email/signature-images` (session, base64) answers `{url}`, type read from the bytes (PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP, never SVG), wider than 800 px or over 300 KB drawn smaller with `sharp`, over 1 MB refused; public `GET /api/v1/signature-images/:id` (no session, nosniff, `default-src 'none'`, immutable cache, mounted before the general rate limit). | [0328](./docs/notes/0328-a-group-event-invites-the-family.md) | **A group event invites the family** (built, behind the flag; amends 0040 §7, 0232, 0295): a fanned-out copy is a silent write, so a member is never told the event moved and cannot answer. Guests are notified only for a time/place change or cancel (narrows 0295 for all events; Google's delete now sends the cancellation; Outlook still mails every organizer change). `share_event` moves the event and adds the family instead of re-creating (`moveEventIntoGroup`; repeats and events for someone else keep the 0232 re-create); replies say who was invited vs only added. Answers are kept on the organizer's row (`guest_answers`, since a member who declines loses their own row) from the organizer's calendar sync, the guest change, and mailed iTIP `REPLY`s; the guests endpoint returns `answers` for any copy, the editor shows Yes/Maybe/No/Not answered, the bot context lists them. | [0335](./docs/notes/0335-chord-is-a-connector-in-claude-and-chatgpt.md) | **Chord is a connector in Claude and ChatGPT, and an outside app only suggests** (built, `MCP_CONNECTOR_ENABLED` default off): remote MCP at `POST /api/mcp` (Streamable HTTP, stateless, JSON responses, hand-written; GET/DELETE 405; `initialize`/`ping`/`tools/list`/`tools/call`). Chord is the OAuth 2.1 server on its own users: RFC 9728/8414 discovery under `/.well-known/oauth-*` (new nginx snippet `chord-mcp.conf`, installed by `deploy.sh`), DCR at `/api/oauth/register` (https or loopback http redirects), `/api/oauth/authorize` (exact redirect match or an error PAGE, S256 PKCE required) then a consent page in the web app (`/connect?request=`, session + CSRF, names app, scopes, redirect host), `/api/oauth/token` (code 5 min single use, reuse revokes the grant; access 1h; refresh 30d single use, a spent one revokes the grant), `/api/oauth/revoke`. Every secret stored `h_<sha256>` (ADR 0269); tables `oauth_clients`/`oauth_requests`/`oauth_grants`/`oauth_tokens` (migration `148`). Scopes `read`/`suggest`. Tools = bot registry tools run through `runToolForAiApp` (validateNativeCall + executeAndBroadcast in the user's personal chat): reads search, read_email, list_tasks, get_list, search_saves, find_events, get_my_availability, query_mail_senders, query_receipts, list_subscriptions + connector-only `list_action_items`; suggest create_event, create_task, add_list_item, reply_to_email, trash_email, unsubscribe_from_sender, ALL staged as proposals via new executor `stageWrites` (provenance `connector:<grantId>`, sealed, shown in Action Items as "Suggested by <app>"). Revoke: Settings → Connected AI apps (`/api/v1/me/ai-apps`) or personal-only `list_connected_apps` / `disconnect_app`. Connector routes mount before the per-IP limiters under `connectorLimiter` (keyed per token/client): Claude's and ChatGPT's servers share IPs. Directory submission steps: `docs/ops/mcp-connector.md` **Addendum 2026-11-12 (getting started)**: signed out, the sign-in page says connecting creates a free account and skips the intro on the way back; the authorization request lasts 30 min; the consent page is three steps (account ✓, mailboxes with "Connect a mailbox" -> `/settings?open=mailboxes`, new tab on web, never blocking Allow, then Allow) plus things to ask; connector tool `get_started` (every grant) returns the steps, `nextStep`, mailboxes, link and `tryAsking`, server `instructions` say to walk it one step at a time; mail tools add `noMailbox` when none is connected; the things to try are one kernel list `AI_APP_TRY_ASKING` checked by a test against the tools at each scope; `/api/mcp` refuses a browser `Origin` that is not Chord's own or a known AI-app host (MCP spec DNS-rebinding rule) |

Opens the way the answered email was written (HTML = Rich), new = Rich, a draft = as saved (`defaultComposeFormat`; forward reads `EmailForwardPreview.originalIsHtml`). The text body stays the source of truth (autosave, empty check, AI ask, outbox) and is kept in step (`richToPlain`); a change not made by the page rebuilds it (`plainToRich`). Wire: `format` + `bodyHtml` on reply/send/forward/draft/outbox, absent = old app (old `outgoingBodies` rule); one server step `composedBodies` (cleaned with `sanitizeQuotedHtml`) for every send path and the Drafts copy. **Write a new ADR when** you chose between multiple viable alternatives,
the decision isn't obvious from code, or future readers might ask "why
this and not X." Template in [`docs/notes/README.md`](./docs/notes/README.md).

## Current state

Committed phases:

- **Phase 0** — scaffold, sessions
- **Phase 1** — groups, messages, SSE
- **Phase 2** — file upload, indexing, bot replies
- **Phase 3a** — CRUD for tasks, lists, saves, events, places, contacts, media
- **Phase 3b** — bot tool use via text fences, mock provider

**Phase 4 has landed**, scoped by ADRs 0004–0007:
1. Identity + scope (ADR 0004 + 0007) — shipped; strict per-group scope (ADR 0032)
2. Retrieval (ADR 0006 → 0021) — shipped; Postgres `tsvector` FTS (ADR 0038)
3. Iterative tool use (ADR 0005) — shipped; native function-calling loop on OpenAI, Anthropic + Gemini

Treat `docs/tasks/README.md` as the checklist for any remaining follow-through work.

## Running things

```bash
# first run
npm install
cp example.env .env
# Postgres setup is one-time per machine — see docs/ops/postgres.md
# (apt install postgresql; createuser chord; createdb chord; createdb chord_test).
npm run migrate        # applies the squashed schema to $DATABASE_URL

# dev
npm run dev            # server + Expo dev
npm run dev:server     # server only (http://127.0.0.1:4000)
npm run dev:mobile     # Expo only (interactive platform picker)
npm run dev:web        # server + `expo start --web`

# checks
npx tsc -p server/tsconfig.json --noEmit
cd mobile && npx tsc --noEmit
cd mobile && npx expo export --platform web --output-dir .expo-web-test
```

The dev server anchors `data/` (uploaded files) and `logs/` at the repo
root regardless of CWD (see [`docs/ops/storage.md`](./docs/ops/storage.md)
and [`docs/ops/postgres.md`](./docs/ops/postgres.md)). The DB lives in
Postgres, not under `data/`. Reset local state with
`dropdb chord && createdb chord && rm -rf data logs && npm run migrate`.
To point file storage at a different location (shared volume, external
drive), set `DATA_DIR` / `LOGS_DIR` in `.env.local` — not symlinks
committed at those paths.

## Testing the bot flow without an API key

```bash
LLM_PROVIDER=mock npm run dev:server
# Another shell:
BASE=http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1
TOK=$(curl -s -X POST $BASE/session -H 'content-type: application/json' \
      -d '{"username":"demo"}' | node -e "console.log(JSON.parse(require('fs').readFileSync(0,'utf8')).token)")
G=$(curl -s -X POST $BASE/groups -H 'content-type: application/json' \
      -H "x-chord-token: $TOK" -d '{"name":"Test"}')
GID=$(node -e "console.log(JSON.parse(process.argv[1]).id)" "$G")
curl -s -X POST $BASE/groups/$GID/messages -H 'content-type: application/json' \
     -H "x-chord-token: $TOK" -d '{"body":"@Chord add to Costco list - milk, bread"}' >/dev/null
sleep 1
curl -s -H "x-chord-token: $TOK" $BASE/groups/$GID/lists
```

You should see the Costco list with milk + bread.

## Conventions

- **Commit style**: subject line explains the *what* in terse form; body
  (wrapped at ~72) explains the *why*. Claude may sign its work: end commits
  with the `Co-Authored-By: Claude …` trailer and PR bodies with the
  `Generated with Claude Code` line (owner, 2026-11-20).
  Don't `--amend` already-shared commits; make a new one.
- **Comments in code**: default to none. Only add when the WHY is non-obvious.
  Never explain what well-named code already shows. Never reference the
  current task in a comment ("added for X") — that belongs in the commit body.
- **Tests**: Vitest lives in the `server` workspace. Run `npm test` from the
  root (or `npm test --workspace=server`). Put unit tests under `__tests__/`
  next to the code they cover. `openTestDb()` provisions a per-test
  Postgres database cloned from a template built once at vitest globalSetup.
  Cloning via `CREATE DATABASE … TEMPLATE` is a fast file-level copy of the
  migrated catalog, so each test pays a CREATE-DATABASE round-trip rather
  than re-running every migration.
  **Two checkouts can run tests at once.** The template is named after the
  test database (`<TEST_DATABASE_URL db>_template`), so pointing a second
  worktree at its own `TEST_DATABASE_URL` in `.env.local` gives it its own
  template and the two never collide. Without that they share one, and the
  loser of `CREATE DATABASE … TEMPLATE` reports hundreds of failures whose
  stacks all end in `closeTestDb` — real-looking, and entirely spurious.
  **CI runs only the tests a change touches** (ADR 0265): name a new test after
  the file it covers so CI finds it, and add the `full-ci` label to a PR that
  touches shared code.
  **Wait for events, not milliseconds.** A test never sleeps a guessed
  number of milliseconds and then asserts, and never asserts how long a
  request took: both pass on a fast machine and fail on a loaded CI runner.
  Start un-awaited work with `runInBackground` (`server/src/utils/background.ts`)
  and have the test `await settleBackgroundWork()`; poll a condition with
  `waitFor` (`__test-helpers__/wait.ts`); prove "did not wait" by holding the
  slow part shut and racing the request against a long guard. **A test must
  not depend on today's date**: compute offsets for the test's own date, or
  run the suite with `CHORD_TEST_NOW=<ISO instant>` (moves every worker's JS
  clock, not Postgres `now()`) at an awkward moment — the night before a
  clock change, 23:30 in New York on 31 December — before relying on it.
  **Writes at the same moment need a real pool.** The `openTestDb()` handle
  has one connection, so requests in a test take turns and a race test on it
  passes with or without the fix. A change to a write path two people or two
  devices can reach at once gets an overlap test with its app on
  `openParallelHandle(db)` (sends via `Promise.all`, assertions true whichever
  request lands first, checked against the unfixed code more than once) and a
  scenario in `server/scripts/concurrency` (`npm run concurrency
  --workspace=server` against a dev server with `RATE_LIMIT_ENABLED=false`).
  See [`docs/ops/concurrency-tests.md`](./docs/ops/concurrency-tests.md).
  **Timing tests run apart.** Files named `*perf*.test.ts` (sealed-vs-plaintext
  search, sync throughput, the key cache) are excluded from `npm test` and run
  serially, one worker, two retries, by `npm run test:perf --workspace=server`;
  `test:all` runs both. Beside four other workers they measure the neighbours,
  not the code. A full parallel run can still abort with `ERR_IPC_CHANNEL_CLOSED`
  and zero assertion failures (see the comment in `server/vitest.config.ts`);
  it has been seen repeatedly on the encryption branch. Running the folders in
  two or three chunks (`npx vitest run src/services/bot src/services/__tests__`,
  then the rest) completes reliably.
- **Before starting to fix a bug, log it.** Write it down first, before any
  code is edited: what goes wrong (the symptom, as reported or observed),
  the cause (traced in the code, with file:line, not guessed), and the steps
  that reproduce it. Say whether it traces to an earlier change or reopens
  an earlier bug. The same write-up goes into the commit body or PR, and the
  reproduction steps become the test below.
- **Every bug fix gets a test that reproduces the actual failure, not just
  a test that exercises the fix.** Build the test from the real failing
  shape — the same data pattern, same code path, same scenario that broke
  in prod or was reported — not a generic case that happens to pass once
  the fix lands. If a prod incident supplied real values (an email
  subject, a PNR, a thread id, an event id), use them; a synthetic
  approximation can miss the exact edge that mattered. **Verify the test
  actually catches the bug**: temporarily set the fix aside (a WIP commit,
  or `git stash push -u -m "<tag>"` per the stash-safety rule below — never
  bare `git stash`) and confirm the new test fails with the same symptom as
  the original bug, then restore the fix and confirm it passes. A test that
  was never run against the broken code is not evidence the bug is fixed —
  it only proves the test compiles. This is what proves the bug won't come
  back, not just that it's gone today.
- **Every bug found is a bug class to sweep for.** Once the root cause is
  known, name the pattern (e.g. "a text timestamp in a local offset compared
  as a string against a UTC bound", "a loose any-token name match where
  family members share a surname") and search the whole codebase for other
  instances before calling the fix done. Fix the ones with real impact in
  the same change (each with its own reproducing test); list the rest, with
  file:line and why they're harmless or deferred, in the commit body or PR.
  A fix to one call site that leaves its twins in place is how the same bug
  comes back from a different screen.
- **A new door to an existing job inherits the old door's fixes.** When a
  change adds a second path that does what an older path already does (a
  new pass over mail, a new way to create an event, a new chat surface, a
  new entry point to a model), list the guards the earlier ADRs put on the
  old path and route the new one through the same shared step, not a copy.
  Say in the commit/PR which guards it inherits and which it skips on
  purpose (with the ADR that allows it). "The dedup will catch it" is not a
  guard: ADR 0180 relied on that, skipped ADR 0074's invite check, and an
  Outlook invite got a model-guessed midnight time that then blocked the
  real one.
- **No CJS**. All project code is ESM TypeScript.
- **A new ADR says whether it goes on the site.** In the same change, add
  its number to the `basis` of the feature in `scripts/site/content.mjs` it
  supports (and the claim to that feature's `points`), or to `NOT_ON_SITE`
  with the reason, then `npm run site:build` (rewrites the site pages AND
  `README.md`). CI fails otherwise (ADR 0349). Never edit `README.md` by
  hand: change `README.template.md` or `content.mjs`.
- **A real person's address, number or name in a test goes in the scrub map.**
  The bug-test rule still says use the real values; the weekly open-source
  export swaps them out. When you add one, add it to
  `scripts/public/scrub-map.json` in the same change: an address or number in
  `replace` (keep the domain, keep the length if a test checks it), a real
  person's name in `names` (made-up name, swapped as a whole word). A missed
  address or number makes the next export refuse (ADR 0350); a missed NAME is
  published, since nothing can scan for names. A made-up fixture at
  gmail/outlook/yahoo goes in `allow`.
  `npm run public:export` writes the public copy to `../chord-public` to check.
- **A new per-user default is a key, not a column.** Declare it with
  `defineUserDefault(key, zodSchema)` in
  `server/src/services/user-defaults.service.ts` (stored in `users.defaults`
  jsonb, ADR 0255 addendum). Only `default_calendar_id` and
  `default_send_mailbox` predate this and keep their columns.
- **Worktree sessions rename themselves.** Whenever you create a worktree
  (via `EnterWorktree`) for the session's own work, immediately set this
  session's title to match the worktree/branch name (`set_session_title` with
  `session_id: "self"`), so the session list stays legible across several
  concurrent worktrees instead of a pile of generic titles.
- **Forked work gets a nested session, not a rename.** When work forks off
  what a session is already doing (a follow-up PR, a version bump, a fix
  found along the way, while that session's own work goes on), start a
  separate session for it in its own worktree (`start_session`,
  `use_worktree: true`) instead of moving this session with `EnterWorktree`.
  The new session names itself after its worktree; the original session keeps
  its title, because renaming it to each fork loses track of which session
  owns which work, worktree and branch (owner rule 2026-10-23).
- **Never touch another session's worktree.** A session may change only the
  worktree it created itself (or the primary checkout, if it works there).
  Every other folder under `.claude/worktrees/`, and the branch checked out
  in it (local or `origin/`), belongs to another session, so never edit,
  commit to, reset, rebase, stash, check out, move, lock/unlock, remove or
  `git worktree prune` it, and never delete its branch. This holds even when
  the folder looks abandoned or merged, and for cleanup tools such as
  `clean_up_worktrees`. Reading it (`git log`, `git diff`, opening a file) is
  fine. **One exception: an empty leftover may be removed** — a folder that
  `git worktree list` does not show and that holds no files (at most links
  to folders that no longer exist). Unlink each link first (`rmdir` on the
  link, never a recursive delete through it), and leave the folder if
  Windows says it is in use. If something there needs doing, tell the owner what and where and let
  them, or that worktree's own session, do it.

## Environment variables

All declared in `example.env`. Loaded via `dotenv/config` in
`server/src/config/env.ts`. Never read `process.env` directly from business
code — use the `env` object. Add a default there when adding a new var, and
document it in `example.env` — **appended to the `NEW KEYS` section at the
BOTTOM of the file**, dated, with a when-you-need-it / why-it-exists note
(owner convention 2026-07-19: a post-pull read of that section must answer
"what do I add to my .env now"). Fold entries up into their topical section
once they've aged.

## Dangers / pitfalls

- **Never** add `.js` extensions to internal `kernel/` imports. Metro will fail.
- **Never** give `kernel/package.json` a `name` field. npm will try to
  install it from the registry and fail.
- Data dirs live at **repo root**, not in the workspace that launched the
  server. Don't `rm -rf` without understanding which `data/` you mean.
- **`data/` no longer contains the DB.** Postgres lives wherever `DATABASE_URL`
  points (default: same-VM `127.0.0.1:5432`). `rm -rf data` only wipes
  uploaded files; `dropdb` is the equivalent for the database.
- SSE hub fans out over Redis pub/sub when `REDIS_URL` is set (ADR 0170),
  and the server splits into a web role + a worker role in production
  (ADR 0172). Either role can run as several copies (ADR 0280): a duty only
  one copy may perform (forwarding to WhatsApp/Telegram, the Telegram poll,
  pushes, IMAP IDLE, the To Do's mirror) checks `isLeader('im'|'worker')` or
  runs under `runWhileLeader` (`services/cluster/leadership.ts`); a group's
  bot turns are ordered by `bot_turn_tickets`; and anything the bot is
  waiting on an answer to goes in `bot_pending_state`, never a module-level
  Map. A new `onBroadcast` listener that SENDS something must check
  `isLeader` — every copy hears every broadcast. Several copies need
  `REDIS_URL`.
- The bot-turn queue (ADR 0178) is intentionally at-most-once: BullMQ's
  default stalled-job redelivery is turned OFF (`maxStalledCount: 0`)
  because a redelivered turn could duplicate ADR 0009 propose-mode tool
  calls. Don't flip that default back on without also giving proposal-
  creating tools a turn-scoped idempotency key.
- **No Postgres `LISTEN`/`NOTIFY` in business code.** It doesn't survive
  transaction-mode connection pooling (the path to managed Postgres later).
  See ADR 0038 §"Future: managed Postgres".
- **No `IS TRUE` / `IS FALSE` against boolean-shaped integer columns.**
  Booleans are still stored as `integer 0/1` post-migration. Compare with
  `= 1` / `= 0`. (ADR 0038 type-mapping rules; cleanup deferred.)
- **Never compare or sort a time column as a string.** `starts_at`,
  `ends_at`, `depart_at`, `due_at`, `remind_at`, `date_time` are text in
  mixed shapes (UTC `…Z`, the value's own offset, floating all-day), so text
  order is wrong by up to the offset. Use `textInstant()` in SQL and
  `byInstant()`/`instantMs()` in JS from `server/src/db/text-instant.ts`
  (ADR 0208). And never read "the first N" of a table that only grows —
  bound it by time around now, or order it so the cap drops what nobody needs.
- **`LIKE` is case-sensitive in Postgres.** Service code that compares user
  input must lowercase both sides or use `ILIKE`. Existing call sites
  already do this; don't regress.

## When in doubt

Read the subsystem doc for the area you're touching before writing code.
If an ADR exists for the decision space, respect it. If the design you need
isn't in an ADR, write one *before* writing the code, so the reasoning is
durable.
